P·L EVANGELIUM SECUNDUM MATTHAEUM 114

Patrologia Latina · Vol. 114 ·

EVANGELIUM SECUNDUM MATTHAEUM

One book of the Glossa ordinaria — the whole gloss, book by book.

PL 114, coll. 63–178  ·  Latin from the Migne printing  ·  New English translation  ·  column numbers follow the original plates, not the Garnier reprint

Anselm of Laon and his school

Anselmus Laudunensis et schola

twelfth century

The masters of the cathedral school of Laon, gathered around Anselm (c. 1050–1117), to whom the making of the Glossa ordinaria on the Bible is traditionally credited. The gloss is a school's work rather than one man's: it draws its comment from the Fathers — Rabanus, Isidore, Bede, Jerome, Gregory — and names them as it goes, and it grew by accretion over generations. Migne prints it under the name of Walafrid Strabo, an ascription now known to be mistaken.

PROLOGUS.

  • 63A

Matthaeus cum primo praedicasset Evangelium in Judaea, volens transire ad gentes, primus Evangelium scripsit Hebraice: quod fratribus a quibus ibat, ad memoriam reliquit. Sicut enim necesse fuit ad confirmationem fidei Evangelium praedicare, sic et contra haereticos scribi. Cum autem plures Evangelium scripserint, quatuor tantum habent auctoritatis 63Btestimonium: quia per quatuor mundi partes fidem nuntiant Trinitatis, et sunt quasi quatuor rotae in quadriga Domini, quae vehit eum per praedicationem Evangelii, et genus humanum quadrifida 64Amorte peremptum eorum erat praedicatione vivificandum: unde et aliorum evangelia deciderunt, nec recepta sunt: quia nolebant praefinitum numerum cessari propter virtutem sacramenti. Designantur etiam evangelistae quatuor figuris, quae non sunt deceptoriae, sed jucundi mysterii sibi consciae. Matthaeus in homine intelligitur: quia circa humanitatem Christi principaliter immoratur. Marcus in 64Bleone, quia agit de resurrectione. Lucas in vitulo, agens de sacerdotio. Joannes in aquila, scribens sacramenta Divinitatis. Christus vero quem describunt, homo fuit de virgine natus: vitulus in immolatione, 65Aleo in resurrectione, aquila in ascensione. Vel in homine humanitas, in vitulo sacerdotium, in leone regnum, in aquila exprimitur Divinitatis sacramentum.

Incipit Evangelium secundum Matthaeum.

PROLOGUE.

  • 63A

Matthew, when he had first preached the Gospel in Judaea, wishing to pass over to the Gentiles, was the first to write the Gospel in Hebrew: which he left for a remembrance to the brethren from whom he was going. For as it was necessary that the Gospel be preached for the confirmation of the faith, so also that it be written against the heretics. But although many have written a Gospel, only four have the 63Btestimony of authority: because through the four quarters of the world they announce the faith of the Trinity, and they are as it were four wheels in the Lord's chariot, which bears him through the preaching of the Gospel, and the human race, destroyed by a fourfold 64Adeath, was to be quickened by their preaching: whence also the gospels of the others fell away, nor were they received: because they were unwilling that the predetermined number should be brought to an end, on account of the power of the sacrament. The evangelists are also designated by four figures, which are not deceptive, but conscious to themselves of a delightful mystery. Matthew is understood in the man: because he dwells principally upon the humanity of Christ. Mark in the 64Blion, because he treats of the resurrection. Luke in the calf, treating of the priesthood. John in the eagle, writing the sacraments of the Divinity. But Christ, whom they describe, was a man born of a virgin: a calf in the immolation, 65Aa lion in the resurrection, an eagle in the ascension. Or in the man is expressed humanity, in the calf priesthood, in the lion kingship, in the eagle the sacrament of the Divinity.

Here begins the Gospel according to Matthew.

CAPUT PRIMUM.

VERS. 1.-- Liber generationis. (HIER.) Hebraei voluminibus suis a principiis nomen imponunt, ut liber Genesis, etc., usque ad sed hic mos est in multis, maxime in prophetis, ut visio Isaiae, subaudis haec est.

Filii David. Ordo praeposterus ne praemisso Abraham, generationis contextio interrumperetur. Horum 65Bduorum specialiter dicitur Filius: quia unus primus inter patriarchas, alter inter reges ad quos facta est de Christo promissio, ut Judaeis Christum ex lege venturum aperiret, in quo viderent vaticinia impleri.

Filii David filii Abraham. Hos duos de omnibus elegit quorum Filius dicitur Christus, ut Judaeis quibus scribit, scientibus ex lege Christum venturum, appareret hic esse qui diu erat promissus, in quo viderentur impleri omnia vaticinia; his enim solis in Veteri Testamento legitur facta promissio Abrahae, et benedicentur in semine tuo omnes gentes terrae (Gen. XXII).

Abraham et David sunt duae columnae versantes in lege quasi in vestibulo ante ostium quod est Christus: 65Cquas ambit funiculus duodecim cubitorum, id est fides apostolorum. Amplectitur hos in genealogia patres, de quorum medio ostium Christus aperitur credentibus.

VERS. 2.-- Abraham genuit Isaac: Isaac autem genuit Jacob. Ab eo congrue sit generationis exordium cui promissus est primum, in quo est benedictio omnium.

Matthaeus generationem incipit ab exordio promissionis. Et etiam usque in finem libri haec agitur, ut qui ex hac generatione natus est, Deus et homo intelligatur.

Ab initio ab eo cui primum facta est promissio fidei incipit, ut in hoc patre discant cui primum facta est promissio, qui et quales esse debeant qui 65Dvolunt in ejus semine benedici. Matthaeus generationem descendendo incipit vel computat: quia humanitatem Christi ostendit, per quam Deus ad homines descendit. Lucas ascendendo referens formam sacramenti aperit. A baptismo enim incipiens usque ad Deum ascendit, ostendens baptizatos ascendere ad hoc, ut sint filii Dei.

Genuit. Matthaeus ponit, genuit: qui naturae filios tantum numerat. Lucas: Qui fuit: qui aliquos secundum legem vel per adoptionem filios interponit. Matthaeus ponit genuit, et non generavit, forsitan ut ille cognosceretur in fine ostensus de quo dicitur: Ego hodie genui te (Psal. II).

Judam et fratres. Ideo solus Judas nominatim exprimitur, 66Aut eum cognosceres qui ex ejus stirpe fuerat promissus.

Fratres Judae memorat praetermissis fratribus Isaac et Jacob: quia illi a populo Dei quasi alieni sunt repulsi: hi velut haeredes in libro vitae scripti, et quasi patriarchae duodecim, in quibus et numerus apostolorum signatur. Sed nominatim non exprimuntur, ne ultra numerum sacramenti generatio extendatur.

In semine praedictorum patrum Christus intravit in Aegyptum quod praesagiens inimicus populum premebat, et non feminas, sed mares interimebat, ut eum exstingueret.

De Juda Christus quem omnes gentes susceperunt: quo veniente jam Herodes alienigena principabatur.

66BVERS. 3.-- Judas autem. Judas genuit Phares et Zaram antequam intraret Aegyptum in quam ambo postea cum patre transierunt.

Phares autem genuit Esrom. In Aegypto genuit Phares Esrom, et Esrom Aram, et Aram Aminadab, et Aminadab Naasson: et tunc Moyses eduxit eos de Aegypto. Naasson fuit dux sub Moyse in tribu Juda per desertum, in quo genuit Salmon. Iste Salmon fuit princeps in tribu Juda, qui cum Josue in terram promissionis intravit. Iste Salmon in terra promissionis genuit Booz de Raab.

Christus est Phares, id est divisio, quia separabit oves ab haedis. Ipse est Esrom, id est sagitta vel atrium, quia penetrat corda auditorum. Habet etiam 66Clatitudinem charitatis, qua et inimicos dilexit.

VERS. 4.-- Aram autem. Aram Christus electus vel excelsus interpretatur: Electus de quo propheta: Ecce puer meus quem elegi (Ose. XI). Et alibi: Excelsus super omnes gentes Dominus (Psal. CXII). Ipse est Aminadab, id est voluntarius, qui dicit: Voluntarie sacrificabo tibi (Ibid. XXXIII). Idem est Naasson, id est augurium, qui novit praeterita, praesentia et futura. Vel Naasson serpentinus, quia Christus more serpentis omnes cavit insidias.

VERS. 5.-- Salmon autem genuit Booz. Christus est Salmon, id est, sensibilis, quia omnia sentit. Ipse accepit Raab, id est Ecclesiam de gentibus. Raab, fames, vel beatitudo, vel impetus: quia Ecclesia gentium esurit et sitit justitiam, et dilatata per 66Dorbem terrarum impetu doctrinae philosophos et reges convertit. Raab ostendit coccinum in fenestra, id est in ore per confessionem Christi passionem declarat. Raab meretrix quae nuntios Josue suscepit, quam Salmon princeps tribus Judae sibi copulavit, significat Ecclesiam de gentibus, quae principi ex Juda, id est Christo copulatur. Et sola cum suis salvatur, dum Jericho, id est praesens saeculum, septem muris vitiorum vallatum, septem dierum tempore tubis praedicationum circumvallatur, donec in novissima tuba omnes muri corruant, et novissima mors destruatur.

Booz autem. Booz Christus de quo dicitur: Ecce Dominus Deus in fortitudine veniet (Isa. XL). Nota 67Anullam de sanctis feminis in genealogia Christi assumi, sed quas Scriptura reprehendit. Debuit enim de peccatoribus nasci, qui pro peccatoribus venerat, ut discerent sui peccata patrum sibi non obesse. Unde agnus in Pascha immolandus jussus est assumi ex capris et ovibus id est ex justis et peccatoribus generandus

Ex Ruth. Moabitide, ut ostendat impletum vaticinium Isaiae: Emitte agnum, Domine, dominatorem terrae (Isa. XVI). Per hanc semen Abrahae ad Christum deducitur. Ecclesia de gentibus per Ruth significata, priusquam ad Booz, id est ad Christum veniret, ex patre diabolo erat.

Ruth significat Ecclesiam quae est de Moab, id est de gentibus, quae ex patre diabolo et petra deserti 67Bpropter idololatriam de qua Deus suscitavit filios Abrahae: oblita prioris gentis venit ad Booz, qui fortis dicitur, id est ad Christum, qui suscitat semen fratris sui, id est Moysi qui obiit sine liberis, quia nihil ad perfectum adduxit lex (Hebr. VII). Ruth videns vel festinans; quia Ecclesia puro corde Deum videt quae festinat ad bravium supernae visionis et diffinit perseverare in fide et dilectione.

Jesse autem. Pater David saepius nominatur Isai, sed binomius fuit. Hic autem ideo dicitur Jesse: ut sciret eum venisse; de quo Isaia: Egredietur virga de radice Jesse (Isa. XI), etc. Hic est flos quem odorat Isaac, dicens: Ecce odor filii mei sicut odor agri pleni (Gen. XXVII), quia Spiritus eum abundantissime replevit.

67CRegem. Non quod solus in hac genealogia rex, sed quia primus rex de Juda et regum principium, propter quem et caeteri in solio regni servantur: et per istum ordo generationis decurrit ad Christum qui habet regnum David.

VERS. 6.-- David autem rex. Mystice David, id est Christus qui Goliam, id est diabolum superavit. Ipse est manu fortis, vel visu desiderabilis. De eodem dicitur: Dominus fortis et potens (Psal. XXIII), etc. Et iterum: Speciosus forma prae filiis hominum (Ibid. XLIV).

Uriae. Uria, lux mea Dei scilicet, id est, diabolus, qui dixit: Similis ero Altissimo (Isa. XIV): cui Ecclesiam conjugatam Christus de solario paternae majestatis adamavit, et pulchram factam sibi matrimonio 67Dcopulavit. Urias qui est figura diaboli, lux mea Deus interpretatur, qui adhuc transformat se in angelum lucis, et lucem Dei se facit.

VERS. 7.-- Salomon autem. Christus est Salomon, id est pacificus, quia ipse est pax nostra qui fecit utraque unum. Ipse est Roboam, id est impetus populi, qui velociter populos convertit ad fidem. Roboam. De isto dicitur: Dabo ipsi Roboam tribum Judam, ut remaneat lucerna David cunctis diebus in Israel (III Reg. XI). Quod ad litteram non potest accipi, quia nec Israel cunctis diebus exstitit, nec de David post Christum dux aliquis in Israel fuit. Sed Christus lucerna est aeterna in coelesti Israel, et ideo servatur semper. Super solium autem David dicitur 68Asedere Christus, quia regnum David temporale figuravit aeternum Christi regnum. Abias. Pater Dominus, id est, Christus, qui dicit: Ego ero illi in Patrem, quia tanquam Dominus omnia quaecunque voluit fecit (Psal. CXIII). Qui etiam est Asa, id est, attollens, quia ipse abstulit peccata mundi.

VERS. 8.-- Josaphat, etc. Judicans vel judicium Dei, scilicet Christus qui judicat orbem terrae in aequitate. Joram autem genuit. Christus est Joram, id est excelsus, qui ait: Nemo ascendit in coelum nisi qui descendit de coelo filius hominis qui est in coelo (Joan. III).

VERS. 9.-- Ozias autem. Sub Ozia, Joathan, Achaz et Ezechia, prophetavit Isaias de Christo. Propter peccata principum claudebantur ora prophetarum. 68BEzechias. Fortis Dominus, ipse est qui dicit apostolis: Nolite timere. Ezechiae cum esset sine liberis dictum est: Dispone domui tuae, quia morieris tu et non vives (Isa. XXXVIII). Ideo flevit non propter longiorem vitam, cum sciret inde placuisse Deo Salomonem, quia non petiisset ampliores annos: sed quia dubitabat ne promissio Dei impleretur cum se sciret esse de genere David per quem oportebat venire Christum: et ipse erat sine liberis unde et dicebat (Ibid. XXXVIII): Non videbo Dominum Deum, item, non aspiciam hominem ultra, etc. E contra credebat, quia fidelis est qui promiserat. Deus autem sententiam quam dederat, ad probationem fidei immutat, et spatium generandi dat.

VERS. 10.-- Manasses. Obliviosus. Iste est qui 68Cquasi converso peccatori dixit: Omnium iniquitatum ejus non recordabor (Ezech. XVIII). Amon, id est fidelis vel nutritius, significans Christum de quo dicitur: Fidelis Dominus in omnibus viis suis, qui convocat filios, ut gallina pullos suos (Psal. CXLIV).

VERS. 11.-- Josias, id est salus Domini vel incensum. Ille est qui dicit: Dirigatur oratio mea sicut incensum (Psal. CXL), etc.

Josias, rex justus, quod non filii ejus quos genuit, non in transmigratione, quia nunquam transmigravit: sed secundum praedestinationem Dei ad transmigrandum, Jechoniam et fratres ejus simul ponit, ut quorum est communis iniquitas similis sit et miseria. Jechonias praeparatio Domini, quia tam patrem quam filium Dominus ad transmigrandum praeparavit.

68D(ISID.) Veritas historiae habet, quod duo fuerunt Jechoniae, ut unus scilicet pater sit in fine praecedentis tesseradecadis, alter in principio sequentis Sed mystice secundum Augustinum unus et idem est in fide praecedentis et in principio sequentis. Sed quare praetermissus est Joachim pater Jechoniae? forsitan ut typum Christi faceret Jechoniam bis numeravit, et Joachim ne numerus augeretur praetermisit.

Omnes qui a David usque ad transmigrationem Babylonis numerantur, reges fuerunt.

Jechonias. Prior Jechonias, resurrectio Domini, sequens Jechonias praeparatio Domini dicitur. Utrumque 69Acongruit Christo, qui est resurrectio et vita (Joan. XI).

VERS. 12.-- Salathiel. Petitio mea Deus, Christo convenit qui dicit: Pater sancte, serva eos quos dedisti mihi, ut sint unum sicut et nos (Joan. XVII).

VERS. 13.-- Zorobabel autem genuit Abiud. Magister Babylonis, id est confusionis. Illi convenit qui mundum ab errore idololatriae ad viam veritatis revocavit. Fuerunt viri studiosi ex Judaeis dicti Heriles propter propinquitatem generis Christi, erantque Nazaraei, qui ordinem genealogiae Christi partim memoriter, partim ex libris Dierum, partim ab avis et proavis retinentes secundum ordinem scripserunt. Abiud, id est, pater meus. Iste Christo convenit, de quo dicitur: Ipse invocavit me: pater meus es tu 69B(Psal. LXXXVIII), etc. Eliachim. Dominus resuscitans. Ille qui dicit: Omnis qui videt Filium et credit in eum, habebit vitam aeternam, et ego resuscitabo eum (Joan. VI), etc.

VERS. 14.-- Azor. Adjutus. Ille est qui dicit: Ecce Deus adjuvit me, Dominus susceptor est animae meae (Psal. LIII). Sadoch. Justus, id est Christus, de quo dicitur: Justus Dominus et justitias dilexit (Ibid. X). Achim, id est, frater meus iste. Quis alius quam ille qui homo fieri voluit, ut possit habere fratres, de quibus diceret: Nuntiabo nomen tuum fratribus meis (Ibid. XXI). Eliud, id est, Deus meus convenit Christo qui dicit: Deus meus ut quid dereliquisti me (Matth. XXVII)?

VERS. 15.-- Eleazar, id est, Deus meus adjutor. 69CIlle est qui ex persona hominis dixit: Deus meus sperabo in eum (Psal. XC). Mathan. Donans vel donatus. Ille est qui dedit bona hominibus, et de quo dicitur: Sic Deus dilexit mundum, ut Filium suum daret (Joan. III).

Per hos patres Christus in mundum, et omnium horum in se gerebat officium. Et dignum est ut per eamdem lineam sanctitatis ad eum ascendamus, per quam ad nos descendere est dignatus.

VERS. 16.-- Joseph. Apponens Christus qui Judaeis gentes apposuit, qui et pater Christi dicitur, quem de sua conjuge natum adoptive suscepit: sed et putative pater dicitur. Joseph Matthaeus dicit filium Jacob, et Jacob filium Mathan. Lucas Joseph filium Heli, et Heli filium Mathat, sed Mathan et 69DMathat de eadem uxore Hesta nomine singulos genuerunt. Mathan qui per Salomonem descendit, eam prius duxit, et relicto uno filio Jacob obiit, et postea Mathat qui per Mathan descendit de David, eamdem duxit et genuit Heli, sic Jacob et Heli fratres sunt uterini. Jacob autem uxorem Heli fratris sui sine liberis defuncti, ad suscitandum semen ejus, accipiens, genuit Joseph natura suum, sed secundum legem Heli filium. Quod dicitur filius nomine ejus vocari cui suscitatur, non est verum cum Booz eum quem genuit ex Ruth, non Elimelech cui suscitatur, sed Obeth vocavit.

Virum Mariae. Quid ad Christum generatio ex David deducta ad Joseph, cum Christus non ex semine 70AJoseph? Sed non est consuetudo Scripturarum, ut ordo mulierum in generationibus texatur; et ideo non per Mariam, sed per Joseph inducitur, cum de una Joseph et Maria tribu fuerint. Unde et eam quasi propinquam cogebatur accipere, ne tribus in aliam se confunderet: unde etiam simul tanquam de una stirpe profitentur in Bethlehem redire cum singulis in suam civitatem.

Exemplo Mariae liquet fidelibus conjugatis servato pari consensu continentiam posse permanere, conjugiumque vocari non permisto corporeo sexu, sed custodito mentis affectu. Unde et Joseph vir Mariae dicitur, quia conjugium verum est ubi conservatur amoris effectus, et in Maria fructus nuptiarum invenitur.

70BVERS. 17.-- Omnes itaque. Decurso ordine generationis tandem, in fine concludendo, evangelista recapitulat sub mystico numero.

VERS. 18.-- Christi autem generatio. Sine additamento, Jesu, ponit, ut eum intelligas de quo propheta: Unxit te Deus tuus. Jesus, quod substantialiter illi convenit, angelo reservat, ut qui post dicturus est conceptum de Spiritu sancto, nomen ei proprium ex salvationis officio sublimius declaret. Sic erat. Sicut dictum est, ut ex Joseph, origo Mariae, quae et de David claresceret: et Christus, non ex ejus semine, ut Deus et homo, per genealogiam homo, per hoc, de virgine de Spiritu sancto Deus ostenditur. Sic, inquam, erat ut praescripta est et mox dicetur, sicut postea signis et prodigiis manifestatur, 70Cut totus liber Deum et hominem natum demonstret: quod est credendum ut possit intelligi. Hic enim sensus deficit humanus, ubi non est natura, sed virtus: qui non credit non intelligit quod virgo peperit, quod verbum caro factum est.

Cum esset desponsata. (ORIG.) Ideo desponsata ut significaret Ecclesiam, quae virgo est et sponsa: et ut per Joseph, etc., usque ad desponsatio fiebat per aliquot dies ante assiduam cohabitationem, et interim erat uxor sub custodia viri.

Antequam convenirent. Non quod postea convenerint: ut si diceremus antequam poenituerit, morte praeventus est. Non quod postea convenerint; sed ostendit proximum tempus nuptiarum, in quo nuptiarum solemnia celebrantur.

70DInventa est in utero habens. Invenit Joseph in utero habere, sed non de Spiritu sancto sciebat esse: cum postea ut dubius deliberaret dimittere eam, apostolus autem hoc addit ne interim surreperet suspicio lectori. Quomodo hoc factum sit, et quo ordine, vel in qua civitate Christi conceptio sit celebrata, hoc praetermissum a Luca exponitur.

VERS. 19.-- Justus. Per fidem, qua credebat Christum de virgine nasciturum, et voluit se humiliare ante tantam gratiam.

Quod justus erat, hoc est testimonium castitatis Mariae, ut qui servat innocentem, justus dicatur: sed et pius dum nollet propalare, ex conscientia castitatis justus, ex timore pius. Sciebat illam esse 71Ainculpabilem: sed unde vel quid esset ignorabat: et ideo mediam elegit viam effugiendi, ut neque innocentem proderet, neque rei incognitae consentiendo se reum faceret coram Deo. Vera virtus est, cum nec pietas sine justitia, nec sine pietate justitia, quae separatae ab invicem dilabuntur.

Et nollet eam. Quam desponsaverat in conjugium ducere, ne videretur quod ignorabat celare. Vel traducere ad poenam in qua noverat non esse infamiam, quia sciebat se eam virginem accepisse, intactam servasse.

VERS. 20.-- Haec autem eo cogitante. Hic docemur diu deliberandum esse in incertis, ne peccetur temeritate levitatis. Ecce angelus Domini. Quia sic pie cogitat, consolari meretur, et consilium suum 71Bmeliori consilio mutatur. Fili David. Recognosce quod promissum est domui David, de qua tu es et Maria, et vide impletum in ea. Noli timere. Quamvis tantum sit quantum credis, et ne timeas pro reatu, sed amplectere charitatis intuitu. Conjugem. Conjux erat non concubitu, sed affectu, non conjunctione corporis, sed copulatione animorum.

Nota conjugem dici a prima fide desponsationis. Igitur dispensationis est, si quis permittitur aliam ducere post sacramentum sponsionis, et si nunquam debitum solvere possit.

Bene Joseph vir, et Maria conjux dicitur, cum in eo servatur affectus amoris, quod verum conjugium, et in ea sine coitu est fructus nuptiarum.

In ea natum est. De ea nasci, est in lucem produci. 71CIn ea vero nasci, est concipi, vel secundum praesentiam angeli quam habet ex Deo: cui futurum quasi praeteritum, natum dicitur. De Spiritu sancto est. Spiritus sanctus dicitur Deus, et donum Dei, et charitas, et sola charitas eum incarnari fecit.

VERS. 21.-- Pariet. Ne videretur Joseph non esse necessarius ei, subdit: Pariet. Et ita eris necessarius procurationi. Et vocabis nomen ejus Jesum. Idem dicit isti quod praedixerat Mariae ut promissum Salvatorem jam venisse tam viris quam feminis repraesentet ex voce. Ipse enim. Nomen interpretatur.

VERS. 22.-- Hoc autem, etc. Quod propinquo est desponsata: quod inventa est in utero habens: quod peperit virgo: quod Jesus vocatus est: quod salvat. Hoc enim ait Evangelista, quando jam omnia impleta 71Derant.

Ut adimpleretur. Isaiae prophetia, signum est praescientiae Dei, quia quod dicit certum est a Deo praesciri: et ideo ab homine debet timeri. Nullum autem signum quod designat, efficit: sed tantum signat quod ostendit, sic prophetia non necessitatem oerum facit quae praedicit, sed signum est praescientiae Dei.

(HIER.) Non est necessitas rerum ex eloquio prophetarum, sed sola exhibitio veritatis. Prophetia non fecit necessitatem eorum quae dicit, sed signum est praescientiae Dei.

Prophetia alia est ex praedestinatione Dei, quam necesse est omnibus modis evenire, ut sine nostro 72Aimpleatur arbitrio: ut haec de qua hic agitur. Alia est ex praescientia Dei cui nostrum admiscetur arbitrium. Alia est, quae comminatio dicitur, quae fit ob signum animadversionis divinae, ut fugiant a facie arcus electi, et juste pereant incauti, et non ex praescientia: quia longe aliter scitur, quam futurum comminetur.

VERS. 23.-- Ecce virgo. Admiratur propheta Isaias, et quasi omnes de somno suscitans, ait: Ecce virgo in utero habebit (Isa. VII). Spiritui sancto praesens erat quod in tempore nondum erat. Ecce virgo, etc. Dicunt Judaei quod non est hoc nomen integritatis, sed aetatis puella. Sed quomodo tunc est signum? Sensum Isaiae sequitur, non verba. Pro concipiet, ponit: In utero habebit: et vocabunt, pro vocabis, 72Bvel vocabitur.

Emmanuel. Proprium est Christi, ut Jesus, quia cum his quos salvat, semper adjuvando perseverat.

Tribus de causis de Veteri Testamento astruit ea quae ponit in Evangelio: pro testificatione, ut habeat testimonium a lege et prophetis: pro confirmatione fidei, quia facilius credimus cum videmus impleta quae sunt praedicta: pro conjunctione Novi et Veteris Testamenti.

VERS. 24.-- Exsurgens autem Joseph, etc. (BEDA.) Moraliter. Quisquis a Deo movetur, solvat moras, surgat a somno: faciat quod jubetur. Fecit sicut praecepit. Perfecta obedientia. Fecit non tantum quod praecepit angelus, sed etiam sicut praecepit. Exhibitione servitutis, et effectu amoris obediens recte dicitur 72Cfecisse sicut praecepit ei. Angelus Domini. Non relinquitur mentiendi locus, ubi officium angelorum celebratur. Et accepit conjugem, etc. Ad vitandam virginis infamiam, ad celandum Salvatoris adventum, ad necessarium pueri nascentis obsequium.

VERS. 25.-- Donec peperit. Non quod post, quod magis constat: cum multis modis cognovit Deum esse qui natus est.

Non quod postea cognoverit: sed ponitur donec, pro et, vel pro aeterno, ut: Donec ponam inimicos tuos scabellum pedum tuorum (Psal. CIX). Dicitur quod Joseph Mariam facie ad faciem videre non poterat, quam Spiritus sanctus a conceptione impleverat penitus. Et ideo non cognoscebat facie ad faciem quam desponsaverat, donec uterus evacuaretur: 72Dde quo hic non agitur. Primogenitum, etc. Primogenitum dicitur omne quod aperit vulvam, sive aliud sequatur sive non. Vel primogenitus inter omnes electos per gratiam. Proprie autem unigenitus Dei Patris vel Mariae dicitur.

CHAPTER ONE.

VERS. 1.-- The book of the generation. (HIER.) The Hebrews impose upon their volumes a name taken from their beginnings, as the book of Genesis, etc., as far as but this custom holds in many, chiefly in the prophets, as "the vision of Isaiah," where thou suppliest, "this is."

Of the son of David. The order is inverted, lest, Abraham being set first, the connected sequence of the generation should be broken. Of these 65Btwo he is specially called Son: because the one was first among the patriarchs, the other among the kings, to whom the promise concerning Christ was made, that he might disclose to the Jews that Christ was to come out of the law, in whom they should see the prophecies fulfilled.

Of the son of David the son of Abraham. He chose these two out of all, whose Son Christ is called, that to the Jews to whom he writes, who knew from the law that Christ was to come, it might appear that this is he who had long been promised, in whom all the prophecies should be seen to be fulfilled; for to these alone in the Old Testament is the promise of Abraham recorded as made, and in thy seed shall all the nations of the earth be blessed (Gen. XXII).

Abraham and David are two pillars standing in the law as in a porch before the door which is Christ: 65Cwhich a cord of twelve cubits encircles, that is, the faith of the apostles. He embraces these fathers in the genealogy, from the midst of whom Christ the door is opened to believers.

VERS. 2.-- Abraham begot Isaac: and Isaac begot Jacob. Fittingly let the beginning of the generation be from him to whom he was first promised, in whom is the blessing of all.

Matthew begins the generation from the beginning of the promise. And even to the end of the book this is pursued, that he who was born of this generation may be understood as God and man.

From the outset he begins from him to whom the promise of faith was first made, that in this father, to whom the promise was first made, they may learn who and what manner of men they ought to be who 65Dwish to be blessed in his seed. Matthew begins, or reckons, the generation by descending: because he shows the humanity of Christ, through which God descended to men. Luke, relating it by ascending, discloses the form of the sacrament. For beginning from the baptism he ascends as far as God, showing that the baptized ascend to this, that they may be sons of God.

Begot. Matthew sets down, begot: he who numbers only the sons of nature. Luke: Who was: he who inserts some who are sons according to the law or by adoption. Matthew sets down "genuit," and not "generavit," perhaps that he might be recognized, shown forth at the end, of whom it is said: This day have I begotten thee (Psal. II).

Judas and his brethren. Therefore Judas alone is named expressly, 66Athat thou mightest recognize him who had been promised out of his stock.

He mentions the brethren of Judas, the brethren of Isaac and Jacob being passed over: because those were driven off from the people of God as strangers: these were written as heirs in the book of life, and are as it were the twelve patriarchs, in whom the number of the apostles also is signified. But they are not expressed by name, lest the generation be extended beyond the number of the sacrament.

In the seed of the aforesaid fathers Christ entered into Egypt, and the enemy, foreboding this, oppressed the people, and slew not the women, but the males, that he might extinguish him.

From Judas is Christ, whom all the Gentiles received: at whose coming Herod, a foreigner, was already ruling.

66BVERS. 3.-- And Judas. Judas begot Phares and Zaram before he entered Egypt, into which both afterwards passed over with their father.

And Phares begot Esrom. In Egypt Phares begot Esrom, and Esrom Aram, and Aram Aminadab, and Aminadab Naasson: and then Moses led them out of Egypt. Naasson was leader under Moses in the tribe of Judah through the desert, in which he begot Salmon. This Salmon was prince in the tribe of Judah, who entered with Joshua into the land of promise. This Salmon in the land of promise begot Booz of Raab.

Christ is Phares, that is, division, because he will separate the sheep from the goats. He himself is Esrom, that is, an arrow or a court, because he pierces the hearts of his hearers. He has also 66Cthe breadth of charity, wherewith he loved even his enemies.

VERS. 4.-- And Aram. Aram is interpreted Christ the elect or the exalted: the Elect, of whom the prophet says: Behold my servant whom I have chosen (Ose. XI). And elsewhere: The Lord is high above all nations (Psal. CXII). He himself is Aminadab, that is, willing, who says: I will freely sacrifice to thee (Ibid. XXXIII). The same is Naasson, that is, augury, who knows things past, present and to come. Or Naasson, serpentine, because Christ after the manner of a serpent guarded against all snares.

VERS. 5.-- And Salmon begot Booz. Christ is Salmon, that is, perceiving, because he perceives all things. He himself took Raab, that is, the Church of the Gentiles. Raab, hunger, or blessedness, or onrush: because the Church of the Gentiles hungers and thirsts after justice, and, spread abroad through 66Dthe world, converts philosophers and kings by the onrush of doctrine. Raab displayed the scarlet in the window, that is, in the mouth she declares the passion of Christ by confession. Raab the harlot, who received the messengers of Joshua, whom Salmon, prince of the tribe of Judah, joined to himself, signifies the Church of the Gentiles, which is joined to the prince out of Judah, that is, to Christ. And she alone with her own is saved, while Jericho, that is, the present world, walled about with seven walls of vices, is beset for the space of seven days with the trumpets of preachings, until at the last trump all the walls fall down, and the last death is destroyed.

And Booz. Booz is Christ, of whom it is said: Behold the Lord God shall come with strength (Isa. XL). Note 67Athat none of the holy women is taken into the genealogy of Christ, but those whom Scripture reproves. For he ought to be born of sinners, who had come for sinners, that his own might learn that the sins of the fathers do not stand in their way. Whence the lamb to be immolated at the Passover was commanded to be taken from the goats and the sheep, that is, to be begotten from the just and from sinners

Of Ruth. A Moabitess, that he may show the prophecy of Isaiah fulfilled: Send forth the lamb, O Lord, the ruler of the earth (Isa. XVI). Through her the seed of Abraham is brought down to Christ. The Church of the Gentiles, signified by Ruth, before she came to Booz, that is, to Christ, was of her father the devil.

Ruth signifies the Church which is of Moab, that is, of the Gentiles, which [was] of her father the devil and of the rock of the desert 67Bbecause of idolatry, out of which God raised up sons of Abraham: forgetful of her former nation she came to Booz, who is called strong, that is, to Christ, who raises up the seed of his brother, that is, of Moses, who died without children, because the law brought nothing to perfection (Hebr. VII). Ruth, seeing or hastening; because the Church with a pure heart sees God, which hastens to the prize of the heavenly vision and determines to persevere in faith and love.

And Jesse. The father of David is more often named Isai, but he bore two names. Yet here he is called Jesse for this reason: that he might know that he had come; of whom Isaiah says: There shall come forth a rod out of the root of Jesse (Isa. XI), etc. This is the flower which Isaac smells, saying: Behold the smell of my son is as the smell of a full field (Gen. XXVII), because the Spirit filled him most abundantly.

67CKing. Not that he alone is king in this genealogy, but because he is the first king of Judah and the beginning of the kings, for whose sake the rest also are kept upon the throne of the kingdom: and through him the order of the generation runs down to Christ, who has the kingdom of David.

VERS. 6.-- And David the king. Mystically David, that is, Christ, who overcame Goliath, that is, the devil. He himself is strong of hand, or desirable in aspect. Of the same it is said: The Lord strong and mighty (Psal. XXIII), etc. And again: Beautiful in form above the sons of men (Ibid. XLIV).

Of Urias. Urias, my light, namely of God, that is, the devil, who said: I will be like the Most High (Isa. XIV): whose wedded wife, the Church, Christ loved from the upper chamber of the Father's majesty, and, when she was made fair, joined her to himself 67Din marriage. Urias, who is a figure of the devil, is interpreted my light is God, who still transforms himself into an angel of light, and makes himself the light of God.

VERS. 7.-- And Solomon. Christ is Solomon, that is, peaceable, because he himself is our peace, who made both one. He himself is Roboam, that is, the onrush of a people, who swiftly converts peoples to the faith. Roboam. Of him it is said: I will give to Roboam himself the tribe of Judah, that a lamp may remain to David all his days in Israel (III Reg. XI). [Vulg. 3 Reg. 11:36 reads filio autem ejus dabo tribum unam, ut remaneat lucerna David servo meo cunctis diebus coram me in Jerusalem, "to his son I will give one tribe, that a lamp may remain to David my servant all his days before me in Jerusalem"] Which cannot be taken according to the letter, because neither did Israel endure for all days, nor after Christ was there any leader of David's line in Israel. But Christ is an eternal lamp in the heavenly Israel, and therefore is kept always. But Christ is said 68Ato sit upon the throne of David, because David's temporal kingdom prefigured the eternal kingdom of Christ. Abias. The Lord is Father, that is, Christ, who says: I will be to him a Father, for as Lord he has done all things whatsoever he would (Psal. CXIII). Who is also Asa, that is, lifting up, because he himself took away the sins of the world.

VERS. 8.-- Josaphat, etc. Judging or the judgment of God, namely Christ, who judges the world in equity. And Joram begot. Christ is Joram, that is, exalted, who says: No man ascends into heaven but he who descended from heaven, the Son of man who is in heaven (Joan. III).

VERS. 9.-- And Ozias. Under Ozias, Joathan, Achaz and Ezechias, Isaiah prophesied concerning Christ. Because of the sins of the princes the mouths of the prophets were shut. 68BEzechias. The Lord is strong, he it is who says to the apostles: Fear not. To Ezechias, when he was without children, it was said: Set thy house in order, for thou shalt die and shalt not live (Isa. XXXVIII). Therefore he wept not for the sake of a longer life, since he knew that Solomon had for that very reason pleased God, because he had not asked for more years: but because he was in doubt whether the promise of God would be fulfilled, when he knew himself to be of the stock of David, through whom Christ must needs come: and he himself was without children, whence also he said (Ibid. XXXVIII): I shall not see the Lord God, likewise, I shall not look upon man any more, etc. On the contrary he believed, because he who had promised is faithful. But God changes the sentence which he had given, for the testing of faith, and grants space for begetting.

VERS. 10.-- Manasses. Forgetful. This is he who 68Csaid, as it were to a converted sinner: I will not remember all his iniquities (Ezech. XVIII). Amon, that is, faithful or a fosterer, signifying Christ, of whom it is said: The Lord is faithful in all his ways, who calls his sons together, as a hen her chicks (Psal. CXLIV). [Vulg. Ps. 144:13 reads Fidelis Dominus in omnibus verbis suis, "faithful in all his words"; in omnibus viis suis belongs to Ps. 144:17, Justus Dominus in omnibus viis suis, and the clause about the hen stands nowhere in the psalm (cf. Matt. XXIII)]

VERS. 11.-- Josias, that is, the salvation of the Lord or incense. He it is who says: Let my prayer be directed as incense (Psal. CXL), etc.

Josias, a just king, which his sons whom he begot were not; not in the transmigration, because he never went into transmigration: but according to God's predestination to the transmigration, he sets Jechonias and his brethren together, that of those whose iniquity is common the misery also may be alike. Jechonias, the preparation of the Lord, because the Lord prepared both the father and the son for the transmigration.

68D(ISID.) The truth of the history holds that there were two Jechoniases, so that the one, namely the father, stands at the end of the preceding fourteen, the other at the beginning of the following. But mystically, according to Augustine, one and the same is in the faith of the preceding and at the beginning of the following. But why has Joachim, the father of Jechonias, been passed over? Perhaps, that he might make Jechonias a type of Christ, he numbered him twice, and passed Joachim over lest the number should be increased.

All who are numbered from David to the transmigration of Babylon were kings.

Jechonias. The former Jechonias is called the resurrection of the Lord, the latter Jechonias the preparation of the Lord. Both 69Aagree with Christ, who is the resurrection and the life (Joan. XI).

VERS. 12.-- Salathiel. My petition is God, it agrees with Christ, who says: Holy Father, keep them whom thou hast given me, that they may be one as we also are (Joan. XVII).

VERS. 13.-- And Zorobabel begot Abiud. The master of Babylon, that is, of confusion. It agrees with him who called the world back from the error of idolatry to the way of truth. There were zealous men from among the Jews called Heriles on account of the nearness of Christ's kindred, and they were Nazaraeans, who, retaining the order of Christ's genealogy partly by memory, partly from the books of Days, partly from grandfathers and great-grandfathers, wrote it down in order. Abiud, that is, my father. This agrees with Christ, of whom it is said: He hath called upon me: thou art my father 69B(Psal. LXXXVIII), etc. [Vulg. Ps. 88:27 reads Ipse invocabit me, "He shall call upon me": the future, where Migne prints the perfect invocavit] Eliachim. The Lord raising up. He who says: Every one who sees the Son and believes in him shall have life everlasting, and I will raise him up (Joan. VI), etc.

VERS. 14.-- Azor. Helped. He it is who says: Behold God hath helped me, the Lord is the upholder of my soul (Psal. LIII). [Vulg. Ps. 53:6 reads Ecce enim Deus adjuvat me, "Behold, for God helpeth me": the present, where Migne prints the perfect adjuvit, answering to his own etymology of the name, "Helped"] Sadoch. Just, that is, Christ, of whom it is said: The Lord is just and hath loved justices (Ibid. X). Achim, that is, this my brother. Who else than he who willed to be made man, that he might have brethren, of whom he should say: I will declare thy name to my brethren (Ibid. XXI). Eliud, that is, my God, it agrees with Christ, who says: My God, why hast thou forsaken me (Matth. XXVII)?

VERS. 15.-- Eleazar, that is, my God is my helper. 69CHe it is who said in the person of man: My God, I will hope in him (Psal. XC). Mathan. Giving or given. He it is who gave good things to men, and of whom it is said: So God loved the world, that he gave his Son (Joan. III).

Through these fathers Christ [came] into the world, and bore in himself the office of them all. And it is fitting that we ascend to him by that same line of holiness by which he deigned to descend to us.

VERS. 16.-- Joseph. One who adds. Christ, who added the Gentiles to the Jews, who is also called the father of Christ, whom, born of his own wife, he received by adoption: but he is also called father by supposition. Joseph Matthew calls the son of Jacob, and Jacob the son of Mathan. Luke calls Joseph the son of Heli, and Heli the son of Mathat; but Mathan and 69DMathat begot one son each of the same wife, Hesta by name. Mathan, who descended through Solomon, married her first, and, leaving one son, Jacob, died, and afterwards Mathat, who descended from David through Mathan, married the same woman and begot Heli, thus Jacob and Heli are brothers by the same mother. But Jacob, taking the wife of Heli his brother, who had died without children, to raise up his seed, begot Joseph, his own by nature, but by the law the son of Heli. That the son is said to be called by the name of him for whom he is raised up, is not true, since Booz called him whom he begot of Ruth not Elimelech, for whom he was raised up, but Obeth.

The husband of Mary. What has the generation traced from David down to Joseph to do with Christ, since Christ is not of the seed 70Aof Joseph? But it is not the custom of the Scriptures that the order of women should be woven into the generations; and therefore it is brought in not through Mary, but through Joseph, since Joseph and Mary were of one tribe. Whence also he was compelled to take her as a kinswoman, lest one tribe should be confounded with another: whence also they make their declaration together, as of one stock, returning to Bethlehem when each went to his own city.

By the example of Mary it is clear to faithful married persons that, an equal consent being kept, continence can abide, and that it is called a marriage not by the mingling of bodily sex, but by the guarding of the mind's affection. Whence also Joseph is called the husband of Mary, because a marriage is true where the effect of love is preserved, and in Mary the fruit of the nuptials is found.

70BVERS. 17.-- All therefore. The order of the generation having at length been run through, the evangelist, concluding at the end, recapitulates under a mystical number.

VERS. 18.-- Now the generation of Christ. He sets it down without the addition, "Jesu," that thou mayest understand him of whom the prophet says: Thy God hath anointed thee. The name Jesus, which belongs to him substantially, he reserves for the angel, so that he who is afterwards to speak of the conception of the Holy Spirit may the more sublimely set forth his proper name from the office of salvation. So it was. As has been said, that from Joseph the origin of Mary, who was also of David, might be made clear: and Christ, not of his seed, is shown as God and man, as man through the genealogy, as God through this, that he was of a virgin by the Holy Spirit. So, I say, it was, as has been written above and will presently be said, as afterwards it is made manifest by signs and prodigies, 70Cso that the whole book may demonstrate that God and man was born: which must be believed that it may be understood. For here human sense fails, where there is not nature, but power: he who does not believe does not understand that a virgin brought forth, that the word was made flesh.

When she had been espoused. (ORIG.) She was espoused for this reason, that she might signify the Church, which is virgin and spouse: and that through Joseph, etc., as far as the espousal was made some days before the constant cohabitation, and in the meantime she was a wife under the guardianship of her husband.

Before they came together. Not that they came together afterwards: as if we should say, before he repented, he was overtaken by death. Not that they came together afterwards; but he shows the time next to the nuptials, in which the solemnities of the nuptials are celebrated.

70DShe was found with child. Joseph found that she was with child, but did not know that it was of the Holy Spirit: since afterwards, as one in doubt, he deliberated to put her away; but the apostle adds this, lest in the meantime a suspicion should creep upon the reader. How this was done, and in what order, or in what city the conception of Christ was accomplished, this, being here passed over, is set forth by Luke.

VERS. 19.-- Just. By the faith whereby he believed that Christ was to be born of a virgin, and he willed to humble himself before so great a grace.

That he was just, this is a testimony to the chastity of Mary, so that he who preserves the innocent may be called just: but merciful also in that he was unwilling to make it public, just from his knowledge of her chastity, merciful from fear. He knew that she was 71Ablameless: but whence or what the matter was he did not know: and therefore he chose a middle way of escape, that he might neither betray the innocent, nor by consenting to a thing unknown make himself guilty before God. It is true virtue when there is neither mercy without justice, nor justice without mercy, which, separated from one another, fall away.

And would not have her. Whom he had espoused, to lead into marriage, lest he should seem to conceal what he did not know. Or to expose her to a punishment in which he knew there was no infamy, because he knew that he had received her a virgin and had kept her untouched.

VERS. 20.-- But while he thought on these things. Here we are taught that in uncertain matters we must deliberate long, lest we sin by the rashness of levity. Behold the angel of the Lord. Because he thinks thus mercifully, he deserves to be consoled, and his own counsel 71Bis changed for a better counsel. Son of David. Acknowledge what was promised to the house of David, of which thou art, and Mary also, and see it fulfilled in her. Fear not. Although it be as great as thou believest, yet fear not on account of guilt, but embrace her with the regard of charity. Thy wife. She was a wife not by intercourse, but by affection, not by the joining of bodies, but by the coupling of souls.

Note that she is called wife from the first pledge of the espousal. It is therefore a matter of dispensation if any one is permitted to marry another after the sacrament of betrothal, and if he can never pay the debt.

Rightly is Joseph called husband, and Mary wife, since in him the affection of love is preserved, which is true marriage, and in her without intercourse there is the fruit of the nuptials.

It is born in her. To be born of her is to be brought forth into the light. 71CBut to be born in her is to be conceived; or according to the presence of the angel which he has from God: to whom the future is as the past, it is called born. It is of the Holy Spirit. The Holy Spirit is called God, and the gift of God, and charity; and charity alone made him to be incarnate.

VERS. 21.-- She shall bring forth. Lest Joseph should seem not to be necessary to her, he adds: She shall bring forth. And so thou shalt be necessary for her care. And thou shalt call his name Jesus. He says the same to this man which he had foretold to Mary, that from the utterance he may set before men as well as women that the promised Saviour has already come. For he. The name is interpreted.

VERS. 22.-- Now this, etc. That she was espoused to a kinsman: that she was found with child: that a virgin brought forth: that he was called Jesus: that he saves. For the Evangelist says this when all things had already been 71Dfulfilled.

That it might be fulfilled. The prophecy of Isaiah is a sign of the foreknowledge of God, because what it says is certainly foreknown by God: and therefore it ought to be feared by man. But no sign effects what it designates: but only signifies what it shows, so prophecy does not make a necessity of those things which it foretells, but is a sign of the foreknowledge of God.

(HIER.) There is no necessity of things from the utterance of the prophets, but only the showing forth of truth. Prophecy did not make a necessity of those things which it says, but is a sign of the foreknowledge of God.

One prophecy is from the predestination of God, which must in all ways come to pass, so that it is fulfilled without our 72Achoice: as is this of which we here treat. Another is from the foreknowledge of God, with which our choice is mingled. Another there is which is called a threatening, which is made for a sign of divine punishment, that the elect may flee from the face of the bow, and the incautious justly perish; and this not from foreknowledge: because a thing is known far otherwise than it is threatened as to come.

VERS. 23.-- Behold a virgin. The prophet Isaiah marvels, and as it were rousing all from sleep, says: Behold a virgin shall be with child (Isa. VII). To the Holy Spirit that was present which in time was not yet. Behold a virgin, etc. The Jews say that this is not a name of integrity, but of age, a girl. But how then is it a sign? He follows the sense of Isaiah, not the words. In place of "she shall conceive" he sets down: She shall be with child: and "they shall call" for "thou shalt call," 72Bor "he shall be called."

Emmanuel. It is proper to Christ, as is Jesus, because he abides always with those whom he saves, by helping them.

For three causes he supports from the Old Testament the things which he sets down in the Gospel: for testification, that he may have testimony from the law and the prophets: for the confirmation of faith, because we believe more easily when we see fulfilled the things which were foretold: for the joining of the New and the Old Testament.

VERS. 24.-- And Joseph rising up, etc. (BEDA.) Morally. Whosoever is moved by God, let him break off delays, let him rise from sleep: let him do what is commanded. He did as he had commanded. Perfect obedience. He did not only what the angel commanded, but also as he commanded. Obedient by the showing of service and by the effect of love, he is rightly said 72Cto have done as he commanded him. The angel of the Lord. No place is left for lying, where the office of angels is performed. And he took his wife, etc. To avoid the infamy of the virgin, to conceal the coming of the Saviour, for the necessary service of the child at his birth.

VERS. 25.-- Till she brought forth. Not that afterwards, which is the more certain: since in many ways he knew that he who was born was God.

Not that he knew her afterwards: but till is set for and, or for for ever, as: Till I make thy enemies the footstool of thy feet (Psal. CIX). It is said that Joseph could not see Mary face to face, whom the Holy Spirit had wholly filled from the conception. And therefore he did not know face to face her whom he had espoused, until the womb was emptied: 72Dof which there is no question here. The firstborn, etc. Everything is called firstborn which opens the womb, whether another follow or not. Or the firstborn among all the elect by grace. But properly he is called the only-begotten of God the Father, or of Mary.

CAPUT II.

VERS. 1.-- Cum ergo natus, etc. Quamvis Matthaeus nativitatem praetermittat, tamen adventum magorum referens, breviter eam commemorat, sic dicens: Cum ergo. (RAB.) Quatuor hic ponuntur a prophetis roborata, nativitas, nomen, locus et tempus, etc., usque ad de adventu magorum Lucas tacet, et Matthaeus exponit.

73ABethehem Judae. Ad differentiam illius quae est in Galilaea, in tribu Zabulon. Duae enim sunt Bethlehem: Bethlehem prius dicta est Ephratha, Bethlehem domus panis, quia ibi nasciturus erat Panis vivus qui de coelo descendit, qui ponitur in praesepio: ut irrationabiles pastos, faciat socios angelorum. Significat autem Ecclesiam, quae est domus in qua panis vivus comeditur.

In diebus. (HIER.) Hoc non ponit tantum pro tempore, sed ut videatur impletum, etc., usque ad Judaei locum insinuant: tempus adventus non cognoscunt.

(AUG.) Manifestatus est Jesus non doctis nec justis: praevalet namque, etc., usque ad ut nullus magnus superbiret, nullus infirmus desperaret.

73BVenerunt. Magi, non post annum: quia tunc non inveniretur in praesepio, sed in Aegypto, sed decima tertia die. Fuerunt autem de terra Persarum ubi et Saba fluvius a quo regio nominatur, juxta quam et Arabia est, ubi Magi fuerunt reges. Qui etsi tria munera obtulisse dicuntur, non ideo non plures quam tres fuisse probantur, sed ut per eos gentes quae ex tribus filiis Noe natae sunt, venturae ad fidem praefigurentur: vel tot fuerunt principes, qui multos duxerunt in suo comitatu. Ideo Magi apud Jerosolymam praecipue rogant: quia per Balaam de Israel nasciturum audierunt. Nova enim stella novum hominem indicabat. Stella, Christus quem sequi debemus, qui nisi in cordibus oriatur, rex Judaeorum in Oriente non quaeritur.

73CVERS. 2.-- Ubi est, qui natus est rex, etc. Confusio Judaeorum est, ubi Christi nativitatem a gentibus discunt, et si non credunt, inexcusabiles sunt. Vidimus enim stellam. Haec stella Dominicae nativitatis nuntia nunquam prius apparuit, sed eam tunc puer creavit, et Magis deputavit: quae mox peracto officio esse desiit. Christum nondum loquentem muta stella praedicat, postea loquentem apostoli nuntiaverunt Judaeis, tanquam ratione utentibus; substantia rationalis, id est, angelus ortum Christi nuntiavit. Magos vero primitias gentium nondum ratione utentium irrationalis, id est stella, perduxit. Pastoribus angeli, Magis stella, utrisque tamen loquitur lingua coelorum, quia jam cessabat lingua prophetarum. Utrum stella orta sit in oriente, an 73Dipsi ibi positi natam ad occidentem viderint, ambiguum est. Potuit enim nasci in oriente, et eos in Jerusalem perducere. Sed dum humanum quasi ex lege quaerunt auxilium, divino deseruntur. Per stellam intellexerunt eum in Judaea natum, et ideo Jerusalem veniunt, ubi locum discentes tunc aggressi iter primum habent stellam praeviam quam recognoscunt.

Allegorice. Stella est illuminatio fidei quae ad Christum ducit. Dum divertunt ad Judaeos, eam amittunt: quia dum a malis consilium quaerunt, veram illuminationem perdunt.

(LEO.) Sequuntur tres viri superni luminis ductum et praevii fulgoris indicium intenta contemplatione 74Acomitantes, ad agnitionem veritatis, etc., usque ad ut eos tantae visionis mysterium non lateret, et quod oculis ostendebatur insolitum, animis non esset obscurum.

VERS. 3.-- Audiens autem Herodes, etc. Rex dicitur, ut collatione ejus qui quaeritur, hic intelligatur extraneus, quia jam defecerat proprius.

Audiens autem Herodes. Alium regem Judaeorum quam se quaeri, volebat enim cum esset alienigena legitimus rex videri: et ideo codices quibus genealogia texitur cremavit.

Turbatus est, etc. Vel quia exitum regni sui timet, vel propter iram Romanorum si hoc pateretur, qui decreverant ne quis rex vel dominus sine eorum consilio diceretur. Nato rege coeli, rex terrae 74Bturbatus est, quia nimirum terrena altitudo confunditur cum coelestis celsitudo praedicatur.

Cum illo. Propter favorem: quia saepe populus plus eis injuste favet, quos crudeles sustinent. Unde Salomon: Rex injustus omnes ministros impios habet (Prov. XXIX).

VERS. 4.-- Et congregans omnes principes sacerdotum. Nota diligentiam inquirentis, ut si invenerit faciat quod post se velle ostendit: sin autem, excusatus sit Romanis. Explorabat etiam, si rex eorum tristes vel laetos faciat Judaeos. Ubi Christus nasceretur. A Judaeis quaerit locum, a Magis tempus, ut de utroque fiat certus.

VERS. 5.-- In Bethlehem Judae. Locum nativitatis exprimunt, quem testimonio Scripturae 74Cdidicerunt, ut ipsa eorum scientia illis fieret ad testimonium damnationis, nobis ad adjutorium fidei.

Ex hoc patet, quia non ex ignorantia, sed ex cordis duritia credere noluerunt.

VERS. 6.-- Et tu, Bethlehem. Hoc sic ponit, ut ab eis dictum est, quia etsi non verba, veritatem sensus quodammodo ponunt.

VERS. 7.-- Clam vocatis Magis. Remotis sacerdotibus et scribis; timebat enim ne Judaei quasi ex Dei oraculo promissum occultarent, si se velle eum perimere sentirent. Ideo etiam promisit se adorare, ne quis sentiret dolum ejus: sed sine suspicione ob gratiam favoris ei renuntiarent.

VERS. 8.-- Adorem eum. Herodes devotionem pro 74Dmittit, sed gladium acuit: malitiam cordis depingens colore humilitatis. Finxit se vultu et verbis adorare eum, quem invida mente cogitabat occidere. Cujus personam repraesentant hypocritae, qui ficte Deum quaerunt, nunquam merentur invenire.

VERS. 9.-- Et ecce stella quam viderant. Ingressi Judaeam deseruntur indicio stellae, ut cogantur ex lege inquirere. Vel divino deseruntur auxilio qui quaerunt humanum. Nec decebat signum infidelibus datum apparere domesticis legis et prophetarum; quia, si Moysen et prophetas non audiunt: neque si quis ex mortuis, etc. Non tenuit aethereas vias: sed multum domui vicina, aliter non discernerent domum.

75AMoraliter. Stella est bona exhortatio ducens ad Christum. Vel potius fides quae est mentis illuminatio, per quam ingrediens Christum videt qui est stella matutina: et eo viso gaudet gaudio et adorare non desinit.

VERS. 10.-- Gavisi sunt gaudio magno, etc. Gaudio gaudet, qui propter Deum (qui verum gaudium est) gaudet. Addit et magno, quo nihil est majus. Et valde gavisi, quia de magno potest alius plus, alius minus gaudere.

VERS. 11. Et intrantes domum, etc. Tres Magi uno itinere Deum veniunt adorare: quia in uno Christo (qui est via) inseparabilis Trinitas ab eis erat adoranda. Puerum cum Maria matre ejus, etc. Saepe puer nominatur, ut eum agnoscant, de quo 75Bdictum est: Puer natus est nobis.

Cur non et Joseph cum Maria inventus est a Magis? Ne aliqua inde malae suspicionis occasio daretur gentibus, quae primitias suas statim nato Salvatore ad eum adorandum miserunt.

Adoraverunt eum. Sic confitentes Deum, quem actum esse hominem intelligunt, et moriturum quod per myrrham ostendunt.

Et apertis, etc. Fidem suam mysticis protestantur muneribus: Munera clausa, fides est cordis; munera aperta, etc., usque ad vel per haec tria in eodem Christo intimantur, regia potestas, divina majestas, humana mortalitas.

VERS. 12.-- Et responso accepto. Sicut Moyses tacens 75Cclamabat, sic isti pio affectu interrogabant quid divina juberet voluntas. Haec responsio non fit per angelum, sed per ipsum Deum, quia nullus alius viam reversionis instituit, nisi ille qui dicit: Ego sum via, veritas, et vita. Non loquitur puer ad eos, ne divinitas ante tempus revelaretur, et vera humanitas habeatur. Unde et mox in Aegyptum mittitur: quia et fides nutrienda erat ad praemium, et vita doctrinae praeparanda est ad eundum. Per aliam viam, etc. In hoc forma datur credentibus, ut devoti ad Deum veniant: et quod jubeat intendant, scilicet, ne ad diabolum redeant: sed per semitas virtutum ad patriam veniant, et qui ceciderunt contemnendo resurgant obediendo.

(LEO.) Adorant in carne Verbum, in infantia sapientiam, 75Din infirmitate virtutem, et in hominis veritate Dominum majestatis, etc., usque ad quia quod erat in substantiis proprium, non erat in persona diversum.

VERS. 13.-- Accipe puerum, etc. (HILAR.) Cum desponsatam eam justo significabat, conjugem nuncupavit, sed post partum mater tantum Jesu ostenditur, ut quemadmodum justo Joseph deputaretur Mariae in virginitate conjugium, ita venerabilis ejus ostenderetur in Jesu matris virginitas.

VERS. 14, 15.-- Qui consurgens. Joseph figura praedicatorum, qui Christum, cum matre, id est, fidem Christi, et Ecclesiae tulerunt ad gentes, relicto Herode, id est Judaeorum infidelitate. Nocte. Quia 76Aocculte per fugam ad illustrandam Aegyptum descendit, relicta nocte Judaeis.

Nocte tulit in Aegyptum, quia nocte ignorantiae his a quibus ipse recessit reliquit incredulis. Quando redit, noctis non fit mentio, quia in fine mundi, Judaei fidem tanquam Christum ab Aegypto revertentem suscipientes illuminabuntur.

In Aegyptum, et erat ibi, etc. Omnigenum Deum monstrat venerantem: quia Christus a Judaeis non receptus, per praedicationem transit ad gentes. Fugit in Aegyptum sicut natus est, ut corruptam reparet naturam. Sic fugit, ut fugaces revocaret. Quod fugit, sacramenti fuit non timoris, ut exemplum fugiendi daret suis. Non fugit mortem qui mori venerat: nec insidias expavit qui venerat aperire versutias 76Bdiaboli. De hac fuga praedixit Isaias: Ecce Dominus ascendet super nubem levem, id est, carnem sine peccato: ingredieturque Aegyptum (Isa. XIX). Septem autem annis in Aegypto latuit.

Ex Aegypto vocavi. In Osaea juxta Hebraicam veritatem hoc invenitur: vel in libro Numerorum, ut quidam asserunt: quia totum dispositione Dei factum est.

VERS. 16.-- Tunc Herodes videns. Verisimile est, quod cum Herodi nihil renuntiatum esset a Magis, credidit illos fallaci stella deceptos esse, et ideo ad se non esse reversos: unde et tandiu quievit a pueri inquisitione. Deinde vulgatis his quae in templo facta dictave fuerant, se a Magis sensit illusum, et in mortem Christi properans pueros occidit.

76CVerisimile est per annum et quatuor dies a nativitate Christi Herodem in pueros desaevisse: quod forsitan ideo distulit, quia Romam profectus est, vel ibi accusatus, vel ut Romanos consuleret super his quae de Christo dicebantur. Ideo forsitan tandiu ab inquisitione pueri se continuit, ut sollicitius eum deprehenderet, neque elabi aliquo modo posset.

Occidit omnes pueros. Quam cito Christus apparuit mundo, incepit in eum pesecutio: quae figuravit persecutionem sanctorum: et dum infans quaeritur, infantes occiduntur; in quibus forma martyrii nascitur, ubi infantia Ecclesiae dedicatur.

Figurat mors parvulorum passionem omnium martyrum, qui parvuli, humiles et innocentes occisi sunt: qui non in Judaea tantum, sed ubique passi 76Dsunt ab impiis, quos significat Herodes. Quod bimi: quia doctrina et operatione perfecti. Illis occisis Christus evasit: quia corpora possunt perimi, divinitas non contingi.

Et in omnibus finibus ejus. Non est contentus vastatione Bethlehem, sed adjacentia loca vastavit; nec ullam misericordiam aetatis habuit a filio unius noctis usque ad filium duorum annorum, quin omnes occideret.

A bimatu. Ideo a bimatu, ut sicut praeoccupaverat Herodes fines civitatis, ita et praeoccuparet alium annum. Ideo etiam a bimatu: quia timebat, ne puer cui famulabantur sidera, paulo supra aetatem vel infra speciem sibi conformaret.

77AVERS. 17.-- Tunc adimpletum est. Adimpletum dicitur: quia antequam fieret legentibus erat semiplenum, quia quod in littera sonabat, aliquid futurum occulte promittebat.

(CHRYSOST.) Dicit quod stella per annum apparuit, sicut in destructione Romanorum signa apparuerunt in coelo, quando vox angelorum audita est: Transeamus ab his sedibus.

VERS. 18.-- Vox in Rama. Id est, in excelso longe lateque diffusa est.

Allegorice. Vel, vox Ecclesiae de nece membrorum gementis usque ad solium superni ascendit judicis. Vox in Rama. Propter uxorem Levitae fornicatione peremptam, secundum numerum tribuum in duodecim partes divisam, omnis Israel 77Btribum Benjamin praeter trecentos viros delevit: eorum qui superfuerunt ploratus et ululatus auditus est usque Rama, qui est locus juxta Gabaa duodecimo milliario a Bethlehem. Audita. Exaggerat, ut hyperbolice Rachel jam mortua, dolentis affectu filios jam flere dicatur. Unde per hoc de Chaldaeis historia texitur quod similis esset ab eis cladi priori populo Judaeorum futura vastatio. Sed verius revelante per evangelistam spiritu, prophetia de nece puerorum accipitur. Dicit ergo, quod tunc completum sit quod dictum est, quia etsi ad imminentem captivitatem sermo prophetiae respicit, tamen in eo sicut in umbra, futura veritas caedis infantium pronuntiatur.

Rachel. Quia sepulta est juxta Bethlehem, et ex 77Cterreno corporis hospitio matris nomen accepit. Vel quia multi de Benjamin et Joseph occisi sunt. De Rachel natus est Benjamin, ad cujus sortem non pertinet Bethlehem, sed ad tribum tantum Juda. Sed quia contiguae tribus sunt Juda et Benjamin in finibus Bethlehem, multi quoque de tribu Benjamin occisi sunt.

Nota quod melius per Rachel (quam si per Bethlehem plorasse diceretur) figura sacramenti innuitur, quia per eam in omnibus schema Ecclesiae commendatur, quae plorat non tam morte translatos, quam supplicio peremptos. Rachel. Id est Ecclesia, suos teneros agnos plorat peremptos: neque vult consolari in praesenti, quia non sunt: sed omnem spem et consolationem ad aeternam transmittit vitam.

Plorat Ecclesia, et non vult consolari hic: quia 77Dfilii sui non sunt de hoc mundo. Vel plorat: quia dum parit, justitiam habet unde dicitur: Vos plorabitis, etc., sed additur consolatio: Tristitia vestra vertetur in gaudium (Joan. XVI).

VERS. 19.-- Defuncto autem Herode, ecce, etc. Quia sopita Judaeorum perfidia, et Helia et Enoch praedicantibus, Judaea verbum Dei recipiet.

Defuncto rege illico complices ejus pereunt, sic cessante persecutione Ecclesiae ad coelestia invitatur.

VERS. 20.-- In terram. Non determinat in quam partem, ut dubitante Joseph angelus revertatur: et frequenti ejus allocutione Joseph certior reddatur. Quia non distinxit in quam partem, ideo Josephus intellexit Judaeam quae dignior pars regni erat. Defuncti. 78ATradunt quidam quia fecit Herodes super funus suum nobiliores Judaeorum occidere, ut sic cogeret Judaeos mortem suam flere.

VERS. 21.-- Qui consurgens. Allegorice. Joseph gerit figuram apostolorum, quorum officio Christus circumfertur. Apostoli primum venerunt in terram Israel: quia primum praedicaverunt Judaeis. Sed pro Herode, id est, manente haereditaria infidelitate, metuunt et recedunt admoniti gratiam gentibus praedicare, et ad eas Christum transferre.

Nominata terra Israel visum est Joseph non alicubi talem puerum debere habitare, nisi in Jerusalem ubi erat templum et celebratio prophetarum. Tamen angelus intelligi voluit Galilaeam, quam et Judaei incolebant, quod ostendit sic fuisse praedestinatum, 78Bquia Nazareus vocabitur.

VERS. 22.-- Timuit illo ire. Si timuit Judaeam pro Archelao haerede paternae crudelitatis, cur non et Galilaeam, ubi Herodes alius frater ejus regnabat? Quia Archelaus post patrem monarchiam totius regni obtinuit: sed insolescens a Tiberio Caesare dejectus est Lugdunum in exsilium, et tunc divisum est regnum in quatuor tetrarchias, quarum unam, id est Galilaeam, Herodes frater ejus obtinuit.

Quaeritur: quare non timuit Joseph ire in Galilaeam sicut in Judaeam, cum et ibi regnaret Archelaus? Sed melius potuit in Nazareth (quae remota erat) latere quam in Jerusalem, ubi erat caput regni et assiduus Archelaus.

Secessit in partes. Quod Christus illuc non transfertur 78Cubi regnabat Archelaus, significat, quia illis abscondetur Christus, in quibus regnabit Antichristus.

VERS. 23.-- Nazareth. Nazareth, germen novellum vel flos campi interpretatur, de cujus radice germinis sanctus sanctorum ad significandum aeternitatis suae substantiam ascendisse legitur. Sicut ergo eligit tempus quo nasceretur et locum, ita et civitatem in qua coalesceret: cujus nomen indicio est, quod ipsa sanctitas, in sanctitate nutritus, sanctus sanctorum recte dicitur: unde et discipuli ejus prius Nazaraei dicti sunt, sed postea a Christo Christiani vocati sunt.

Nazareth Galilaeae quo transfertur, partem ejusdem gentis quae fidem susceptura erat, significat: unde flos interpretatur: quia Ecclesia quo ardentius a terrenis ad coelestia transmigrat, eo magis virtutum 78Dflore et germine abundat.

Per prophetas. Prophetas pluraliter dicit, quia non habemus fixum de Scriptura exemplum: ostendit sensum non verba se sumpsisse. Vel eisdem verbis in Isaia juxta Hebraicam veritatem: Nazaraeus de radice ejus ascendet (Isa. XI).

CHAPTER II.

VERS. 1.-- When therefore he was born, etc. Although Matthew passes over the nativity, yet in relating the coming of the Magi he briefly commemorates it, saying thus: When therefore. (RAB.) Four things are here set down, confirmed by the prophets, the nativity, the name, the place and the time, etc., as far as of the coming of the Magi Luke is silent, and Matthew sets it forth.

73ABethlehem of Juda. To distinguish it from that which is in Galilee, in the tribe of Zabulon. For there are two Bethlehems: Bethlehem was formerly called Ephratha; Bethlehem is the house of bread, because there the living Bread was to be born, who came down from heaven, who is laid in a manger: that having fed the irrational he may make them fellows of the angels. But it signifies the Church, which is the house in which the living bread is eaten.

In the days. (HIER.) He sets this down not only for the time, but that it may be seen fulfilled, etc., as far as the Jews point out the place: the time of the coming they do not know.

(AUG.) Jesus was made manifest not to the learned nor to the just: for it prevails, etc., as far as that no great man might be proud, no weak man despair.

73BThey came. The Magi, not after a year: because then he would not be found in the manger, but in Egypt, but on the thirteenth day. But they were from the land of the Persians, where also is the river Saba, from which the region is named, near which is Arabia also, where the Magi were kings. Who, although they are said to have offered three gifts, are not on that account proved not to have been more than three; but that through them the Gentiles which were born of the three sons of Noe, and were to come to the faith, might be prefigured: or there were so many princes, who led many in their train. Therefore the Magi make their request chiefly at Jerusalem: because through Balaam they had heard that he was to be born of Israel. For a new star was showing forth a new man. The star is Christ, whom we ought to follow, who unless he arise in our hearts, the king of the Jews is not sought in the East.

73CVERS. 2.-- Where is he that is born king, etc. It is the confusion of the Jews, when they learn the nativity of Christ from the Gentiles, and if they do not believe, they are without excuse. For we have seen his star. This star, the herald of the Lord's nativity, never appeared before, but the child then created it and assigned it to the Magi: which soon, its office performed, ceased to be. A mute star proclaims Christ not yet speaking; afterwards, when he spoke, the apostles announced him to the Jews, as to those using reason; a rational substance, that is, an angel, announced the birth of Christ. But an irrational thing, that is, a star, led the Magi, the firstfruits of the Gentiles, not yet using reason. To the shepherds angels, to the Magi a star, yet to both the tongue of the heavens speaks, because the tongue of the prophets was already ceasing. Whether the star arose in the east, or whether 73Dthey, being placed there, saw it risen toward the west, is doubtful. For it could arise in the east, and lead them to Jerusalem. But while they seek human help, as it were from the law, they are forsaken by the divine. By the star they understood that he was born in Judaea, and therefore they come to Jerusalem, where, learning the place and then setting out upon their journey, they first have the star going before them, which they recognize.

Allegorically. The star is the illumination of faith which leads to Christ. While they turn aside to the Jews, they lose it: because while they seek counsel from the wicked, they lose the true illumination.

(LEO.) Three men follow the leading of the light from above, accompanying with intent contemplation the token of the brightness that went before them, 74Ato the acknowledgment of the truth, etc., as far as that the mystery of so great a vision might not lie hidden from them, and that what was shown to their eyes as unwonted might not be obscure to their minds.

VERS. 3.-- But Herod hearing, etc. He is called king, that by comparison with him who is sought, this one may be understood a stranger, because the proper one had already failed.

But Herod hearing. That another king of the Jews than himself was sought; for, being a foreigner, he wished to seem the lawful king: and therefore he burned the records in which the genealogy is woven.

He was troubled, etc. Either because he fears the end of his kingdom, or on account of the wrath of the Romans if he should allow this, who had decreed that no one should be called king or lord without their counsel. The king of heaven being born, the king of earth 74Bwas troubled, because assuredly earthly height is confounded when heavenly loftiness is proclaimed.

With him. Because of favour: because often the people the more unjustly favour those whom they endure as cruel. Whence Solomon: A king unjust has all his ministers wicked (Prov. XXIX). [Vulg. Prov. 29:12 reads Princeps qui libenter audit verba mendacii, omnes ministros habet impios, "A prince that gladly heareth lying words hath all his servants wicked"; Migne's Rex stands where the received text has Princeps, and injustus, set in roman between his italics, is his own supplied word]

VERS. 4.-- And assembling all the chief priests. Note the diligence of the inquirer, that if he should find him he might do what he afterwards showed himself to wish: but if not, that he might be excused to the Romans. He was also probing whether their king would make the Jews sad or glad. Where Christ should be born. From the Jews he asks the place, from the Magi the time, that he may be made certain of both.

VERS. 5.-- In Bethlehem of Juda. They express the place of the nativity, which by the testimony of Scripture 74Cthey had learned, so that their very knowledge might become for them a testimony of damnation, for us a help to faith.

From this it is clear that not from ignorance, but from hardness of heart, they refused to believe.

VERS. 6.-- And thou, Bethlehem. He sets this down as it was said by them, because although they do not set down the words, they do in some manner set down the truth of the sense.

VERS. 7.-- The Magi being called privately. The priests and scribes being removed; for he feared lest the Jews should hide the promised one as by an oracle of God, if they should perceive that he wished to destroy him. Therefore also he promised that he would adore him, lest any should perceive his guile: but that without suspicion, for the favour of his good will, they might bring him word.

VERS. 8.-- That I may adore him. Herod promises 74Ddevotion, but sharpens the sword: painting the malice of his heart with the colour of humility. He feigned by countenance and by words to adore him whom with envious mind he was planning to kill. Whose person the hypocrites represent, who seek God feignedly, and never deserve to find him.

VERS. 9.-- And behold the star which they had seen. Having entered Judaea they are forsaken by the token of the star, that they may be compelled to inquire from the law. Or they who seek human help are forsaken by the divine. Nor was it fitting that a sign given to unbelievers should appear to those of the household of the law and the prophets; because, if they hear not Moses and the prophets: neither if one from the dead, etc. It did not keep the paths of the upper air: but being very near the house, otherwise they would not have distinguished the house.

75AMorally. The star is a good exhortation leading to Christ. Or rather faith, which is the illumination of the mind, through which he who enters sees Christ, who is the morning star: and having seen him he rejoices with joy and does not cease to adore.

VERS. 10.-- They rejoiced with a great joy, etc. He rejoices with joy who rejoices for the sake of God (who is the true joy). He adds also "great," than which nothing is greater. And "they rejoiced exceedingly," because of a great thing one man can rejoice more, another less.

VERS. 11. And entering the house, etc. Three Magi come by one journey to adore God: because in the one Christ (who is the way) the inseparable Trinity was to be adored by them. The child with Mary his mother, etc. Often is he named a child, that they may recognize him of whom 75Bit was said: A child is born to us. [Vulg. Isa. 9:6 reads Parvulus enim natus est nobis, "For a little one is born to us"; Migne prints Puer, which is the very word the gloss is expounding]

Why was not Joseph also found with Mary by the Magi? Lest any occasion of evil suspicion should thence be given to the Gentiles, who sent their firstfruits to adore him as soon as the Saviour was born.

They adored him. Thus confessing him God, whom they understand to have been brought to be man, and about to die, which they show by the myrrh.

And opening, etc. They testify their faith by mystical gifts: the gifts closed are the faith of the heart; the gifts opened, etc., as far as or by these three there are intimated in the same Christ royal power, divine majesty, human mortality.

VERS. 12.-- And having received an answer. As Moses, being silent, 75Ccried out, so these with devout affection were asking what the divine will might command. This answer is not made through an angel, but through God himself, because no other appointed the way of return, save he who says: I am the way, the truth, and the life. The child does not speak to them, lest the divinity be revealed before the time, and that the true humanity may be held. Whence also he is soon sent into Egypt: because faith had to be nourished unto the reward, and the life of doctrine must be prepared for the going. By another way, etc. In this a pattern is given to believers, that they may come devoutly to God: and that they may attend to what he commands, namely, that they return not to the devil: but that they may come by the paths of the virtues to their country, and that they who fell by despising may rise again by obeying.

(LEO.) They adore in the flesh the Word, in infancy wisdom, 75Din weakness power, and in the truth of man the Lord of majesty, etc., as far as because what was proper in the substances was not diverse in the person.

VERS. 13.-- Take the child, etc. (HILAR.) When he was signifying that she was espoused to a just man, he called her wife; but after the birth she is shown only as the mother of Jesus, so that, as to the just Joseph the marriage with Mary was reckoned in virginity, so her venerable virginity as a mother might be shown in Jesus.

VERS. 14, 15.-- Who rising up. Joseph is a figure of the preachers, who bore Christ, with his mother, that is, the faith of Christ and of the Church, to the Gentiles, Herod being left behind, that is, the unbelief of the Jews. By night. Because 76Asecretly by flight he went down to enlighten Egypt, night being left to the Jews.

By night he bore him into Egypt, because in the night of ignorance he left them to those unbelieving ones from whom he himself withdrew. When he returns, no mention is made of night, because at the end of the world the Jews, receiving the faith as though Christ returning from Egypt, shall be enlightened.

Into Egypt, and he was there, etc. He shows [Egypt] venerating a God of every kind: because Christ, not received by the Jews, passes over by preaching to the Gentiles. He fled into Egypt as he was born, that he might repair corrupted nature. So he fled, that he might call back the fugitives. That he fled was a matter of sacrament, not of fear, that he might give his own an example of fleeing. He did not flee death, who had come to die: nor did he dread the snares, who had come to lay open the craft 76Bof the devil.
