Of this flight Isaiah foretold: Behold the Lord shall ascend upon a light cloud, that is, flesh without sin: and shall enter into Egypt (Isa. XIX). But for seven years he lay hid in Egypt.

Out of Egypt I have called. This is found in Osee according to the Hebrew verity: or in the book of Numbers, as some assert: because the whole was done by the disposition of God.

VERS. 16.-- Then Herod seeing. It is likely that, since nothing had been reported to Herod by the Magi, he believed that they had been deceived by a false star, and had therefore not returned to him: whence also he rested so long from the search for the child. Then, when the things which had been done or said in the temple were made public, he perceived that he had been mocked by the Magi, and hastening to the death of Christ he slew the children.

76CIt is likely that for a year and four days from the nativity of Christ Herod raged against the children: which perhaps he deferred for this reason, that he set out for Rome, or was accused there, or that he might consult the Romans concerning those things which were said about Christ. Perhaps therefore he held himself back so long from the search for the child, that he might apprehend him the more carefully, and that he might not be able to slip away in any manner.

He slew all the children. As soon as Christ appeared to the world, persecution began against him: which prefigured the persecution of the saints: and while the infant is sought, infants are slain; in whom the form of martyrdom is born, where the infancy of the Church is consecrated.

The death of the little ones figures the passion of all the martyrs, who were slain as little ones, humble and innocent: who suffered not in Judaea only, but everywhere 76Dat the hands of the wicked, whom Herod signifies. That they were two years old: because they were perfect in doctrine and in work. Those being slain, Christ escaped: because bodies can be destroyed, the divinity cannot be touched.

And in all the borders thereof. He was not content with the devastation of Bethlehem, but laid waste the neighbouring places; nor had he any mercy for age, from a son of one night to a son of two years, but that he slew them all.

From two years old. Therefore from two years old, that as Herod had taken in beforehand the borders of the city, so also he might take in beforehand another year. Therefore also from two years old: because he feared lest the child, whom the stars served, should fashion for himself an appearance a little above or below his age.

77AVERS. 17.-- Then was fulfilled. It is called fulfilled: because before it came to pass it was but half-full to those who read it, since what sounded in the letter secretly promised something to come.

(CHRYSOST.) He says that the star appeared for a year, as in the destruction of the Romans signs appeared in heaven, when the voice of the angels was heard: Let us pass over from these seats.

VERS. 18.-- A voice in Rama. That is, in a high place it was spread far and wide.

Allegorically. Or, the voice of the Church groaning over the slaughter of her members ascends to the throne of the judge on high. A voice in Rama. On account of the Levite's wife, destroyed by fornication and divided into twelve parts according to the number of the tribes, all Israel 77Bdestroyed the tribe of Benjamin save three hundred men: the weeping and wailing of those who survived was heard as far as Rama, which is a place near Gabaa, at the twelfth milestone from Bethlehem. Was heard. He heightens it, so that hyperbolically Rachel, already dead, is said with the feeling of one grieving to be already weeping for her sons. Whence by this the history concerning the Chaldeans is woven, that the devastation to come from them upon the people of the Jews would be like to the former disaster. But more truly, the Spirit revealing it through the evangelist, the prophecy is taken of the slaughter of the children. He says therefore that what was spoken was then completed, because although the word of the prophecy looks to the impending captivity, yet in it, as in a shadow, the future truth of the slaughter of the infants is proclaimed.

Rachel. Because she was buried near Bethlehem, and from 77Cthe earthly lodging of her body received the name of mother. Or because many of Benjamin and Joseph were slain. Of Rachel was Benjamin born, to whose lot Bethlehem does not belong, but only to the tribe of Judah. But because Judah and Benjamin are neighbouring tribes in the borders of Bethlehem, many also of the tribe of Benjamin were slain.

Note that the figure of the sacrament is better intimated through Rachel (than if Bethlehem were said to have wept), because through her in all things the pattern of the Church is commended, which weeps not so much for those carried off by death as for those destroyed by torment. Rachel. That is, the Church, weeps for her tender lambs destroyed: neither will she be comforted in the present, because they are not: but she sends forward all her hope and consolation to the eternal life.

The Church weeps, and will not be comforted here: because 77Dher sons are not of this world. Or she weeps: because while she brings forth, she has justice justitiam, whence it is said: You shall weep, etc., but a consolation is added: Your sorrow shall be turned into joy (Joan. XVI).

VERS. 19.-- But Herod being dead, behold, etc. Because, the perfidy of the Jews being lulled to sleep, and Elias and Enoch preaching, Judaea shall receive the word of God.

The king being dead, his accomplices straightway perish, so, the persecution of the Church ceasing, she is invited to heavenly things.

VERS. 20.-- Into the land. He does not determine into what part, so that, Joseph doubting, the angel may return: and that by his frequent address Joseph may be made the more certain. Because he did not distinguish into what part, therefore Josephus understood Judaea, which was the worthier part of the kingdom. They are dead. 78ASome relate that Herod caused the nobler of the Jews to be slain over his own funeral, that he might so compel the Jews to weep for his death.

VERS. 21.-- Who rising up. Allegorically. Joseph bears the figure of the apostles, by whose office Christ is carried about. The apostles came first into the land of Israel: because they preached first to the Jews. But because of Herod, that is, the hereditary unbelief remaining, they fear and withdraw, being admonished to preach grace to the Gentiles, and to carry Christ over to them.

The land of Israel being named, it seemed to Joseph that such a child ought to dwell nowhere but in Jerusalem, where the temple was and the celebration of the prophets. Yet the angel wished Galilee to be understood, which the Jews also inhabited, which shows that it had thus been predestined, 78Bbecause he shall be called a Nazarene.

VERS. 22.-- He feared to go thither. If he feared Judaea on account of Archelaus, the heir of his father's cruelty, why not Galilee also, where another Herod, his brother, was reigning? Because Archelaus after his father obtained the sole rule of the whole kingdom: but growing insolent he was cast down by Tiberius Caesar into exile at Lyons, and then the kingdom was divided into four tetrarchies, one of which, that is Galilee, Herod his brother obtained.

It is asked: why did Joseph not fear to go into Galilee as into Judaea, since Archelaus reigned there also? But he could better lie hid in Nazareth (which was remote) than in Jerusalem, where was the head of the kingdom and Archelaus constantly present.

He withdrew into the parts. That Christ is not carried over thither 78Cwhere Archelaus was reigning signifies that Christ shall be hidden from those in whom Antichrist shall reign.

VERS. 23.-- Nazareth. Nazareth is interpreted a new shoot or the flower of the field, from the root of which shoot the holy of holies is read to have ascended, to signify the substance of his eternity. As therefore he chose the time in which he should be born and the place, so also the city in which he should grow up: whose name is a token that he who is holiness itself, nurtured in holiness, is rightly called the holy of holies: whence also his disciples were first called Nazaraeans, but afterwards from Christ were called Christians.

Nazareth of Galilee, whither he is carried over, signifies that part of the same nation which was to receive the faith: whence it is interpreted a flower: because the more ardently the Church passes over from earthly things to heavenly, the more it abounds in the flower and shoot 78Dof the virtues.

By the prophets. He says prophets in the plural, because we have no fixed instance from Scripture: he shows that he took the sense, not the words. Or in the same words in Isaiah according to the Hebrew verity: A Nazarene shall ascend from his root (Isa. XI).

CAPUT III.

VERS. 1.-- In diebus autem. Praeterit multa, et transit ad praedicationem Joannis. In diebus. Non solum pueritiae annos, sed omnes usque ad praedicationem Joannis comprehendit, id est, cum fere esset triginta annorum. Lucas significantius tempora exprimit: Anno quintodecimo imperii Tiberii Caesaris. Praedicans. Manifestavit se qui prius siluerat, baptizando, viam 79Apraeparans Domino. In deserto Judaeae. Quia Judaei a Deo deserti erant, quibus necessaria est poenitentia, ut redeant.

VERS. 2.-- Poenitentiam. Hoc praeco nuntiat, quod post per seipsam Veritas; poenitentia praecedit, sequitur promissio regni: qui vere poenitet, in se praeteritos errores, deinde erigit animum ad coeleste regnum. Si gratis in baptismo fit remissio, cur praedicatur poenitentia? Sed triplex est modus poenitentiae. Primus, errores et omnia mala ante baptismum abrenuntiare et condemnare: quod necesse est ad Christum venturis. Secundus, post baptismum lapsos per poenitentiam revocari. Tertius, quo sancti quotidie a talibus purgantur peccatis sine quibus vita non agitur.

79BPoenitere est ante acta deflere, et deflenda non committere. Poenitentibus regnum coelorum adesse dicitur. Non terrena, ut in veteri lege: et hoc per obedientiam Christi. Et hoc regnum coelorum primus Joannes praedicavit: Poenitentiam agite. Prima virtus est, per poenitentiam perimere veterem hominem, et vitia odisse: quod qui non facit, non modo virtutes non comprehendit, sed nec etiam supplicia fugit. Poenitentiae virtus timore concipitur, qui est initium sapientiae. Poenitentia a puniendo, qua quisque punit quod illicite commisit.

Poenitentiam agere est dignos fructus poenitentiae facere. Regnum coelorum. Id est Christus, per quem sancti regnant in coelestibus. Vel Evangelium, quo invitat ad regnum.

79CAppropinquavit enim, etc. Nisi appropinquaret, nemo redire posset: quia infirmi et caeci via (quae est Christus) carebant: quam parans Joannes poenitenti anteponit.

VERS. 3.-- Vox clamantis. Joannes vox, Christus verbum, qui clamat in Joanne: Nullum verbum sine voce auditur, nec vox sine intelligentia verbi valet. Vox viam verbo praeparat, ut recipiatur. Unde: Paravi lucernam Christo meo (Psal. CXXXI).

In deserto. Forma poenitentibus: locus conversationis, non frequentia hominum, sed desertum. Vestitus non mollis ad fluxum, sed asper: victus tenuis et inusitatus.

Typice, desertum significat sanctorum vitam a mundi illecebris segregatam.

79DViam Domini. Via Domini ad cor dirigitur, cum sermo ejus diligenter auditur.

VERS. 4.-- Ipse autem Joannes. Qui poenitentiam praedicat, habitum poenitentiae praetendit. In eo vilitas vestis et cibi laudatur, quorum usus in divite arguitur.

Ipse autem Joannes in semetipso erudit poenitentes, et formam vitae exhibet in deserto habitans, de locustis vivens, pilos cameli vestiens.

Servus Dei non debet habere vestimentum ad decorem vel ad delectationem, sed tantum ad tegendam nuditatem. Inde Joannes pilis vestiebatur.

VERS. 5.-- Tunc exibat ad eum, etc. Quia nisi quis a malis exeat, non abluitur. Unde apte sequitur: Baptizabantur ab eo in Jordane: qui descensio interpretatur, 80Aquia de superbia veteris hominis ad humilitatem confessionis et emendationis descendebant. Jam tunc enim baptizandis exemplum dabatur confitendi peccata et promittendi meliora.

VERS. 6.-- In Jordane. Quia in eo figura baptismi praecessit, et ex nomine descensum de superbia exigit, quod congruit baptizatis. Confitentes. Confessio peccatorum, conscientiae est testimonium timentis Deum. Qui enim timet judicium Dei, peccata non erubescit confiteri. Perfectius timor solvit omnem pudorem. Confessio peccati pudorem habet, et ipsa erubescentia est gravis poena. Ideoque jubemur confiteri peccata, ut erubescentiam patiamur pro poena. Nam hoc ipsum pars est divini judicii.

VERS. 7.-- Multos Pharisaeorum, etc. Hi sunt ex 80Bhaereticis Judaeorum, et Pharisaei observationum et traditionum praeferunt justitiam. Unde et Pharisaei, id est, divisi a populo per privatam justitiam dicuntur. Sadducaei, id est justi: vindicant enim sibi quod non sunt: hi negant resurrectionem, dicentes animam interire cum corpore: quinque libros Moysi recipiunt, prophetas respuunt. Hos venientes ad baptismum Joannes increpat: quia maxime indigebant correctione et poenitentia.

Progenies viperarum, etc., id est, venenati venenatorum filii: quia bonis invident eosque persequuntur, proximos laedunt ut patres eorum.

Vipera patrem suum occidit, sic Judaei prophetas qui erant patres eorum.

VERS. 8.-- Facite ergo fructus dignos. (BEDA.) 80CQuasi diceret: Quare prius venena non deponitis, ut sic ad baptismum, etc., usque ad gratia est quae sentit, sentiens quaerit sanctitatem.

VERS. 9.-- Dico enim. A carne retrahit, ut cogitent opera praedestinationis Dei. Prima sunt rudimenta fidei, credere Deum posse quidquid voluerit, ut omnia qui de nihilo creavit, possit de lapidibus vel de pulvere filios Abrahae formare. De lapidibus. Demonstrative ad illos lapides quos Josue de Jordane transferre fecit, ad quos quasi digitum extendens ait.

Josue duodecim lapides de medio Jordanis in terram transportari fecit, et alios ex terra in eodem alveo restitui: per quos excaecatio Judaeorum et gentium ad lucem transformatio praesignatur.

80DVel gentiles lapides vocat: et est sensus: Ne gloriemini de semine carnis: quia hi tantum sunt filii Dei, qui per gratiam sunt illuminati et interius suscitati. Suscitare signanter dicit, quia Christus Saram, id est Ecclesiam, accepit, et suscitat per gratiam filios Abrahae defuncto sine liberis, ne tantus patriarcha privaretur promissis. In cujus rei praesagium olim Deus de Sara genuit filium.

VERS. 10.-- Jam enim securis. Ne autem viderentur immunes, si se a talibus sacramentis separarent, comminatur. Securis. Christus, qui ex manubrio constat et ferro, id est, humanitate qua tenetur, et divinitate quia incidit. Posita est: quia etsi per patientiam exspectat, videt tamen quid est facturus. 81AAd radicem. Id est, finem Judaici populi, ut auferat de terra viventium eos qui in Christo non credunt. Vel, securis, sententia judicii, vel praedicatio Evangelii. Ad radicem. Nota non ad ramos, sed ad radicem. Cum enim filii malorum tolluntur, quid aliud quam rami infructuosae arboris abscinduntur? Cum tota progenies simul cum parente tollitur, arbor radicitus abscinditur, ne quid remaneat unde iterum aliquid germinis oriatur. Radices sunt cogitationes, quibus plantati, vel sursum coelo sustolluntur, vel ad ima inferni mittuntur. Arbor humanum genus: hujus rami, alii sunt aridi, id est, pagani incendio apti. Alii virides, sed sine fructu: ut hypocritae, qui speciem sanctitatis praetendunt, sed intus vacui: alii fructuosi, sed venenosi, id est, haeretici: qui praedicando 81Bfructum pariunt, sed mortalem. Alii, id est, catholici, qui bonum fructum ferunt. Omnis ergo arbor, ut gentiles, hypocritae, qui nullum, et haeretici, qui malum.

VERS. 11.-- Ego quidem. Ne videatur sua auctoritate minari, incipit aperire quantae sit dignitatis: cujus praesentia comminatur, vel cujus beneficii persuadet: Ego quidem baptizo vos in aqua. Tantum corpora lavo, quia peccata solvere nequeo, ut sicut nascendo, praedicando, praecurro sic baptizando: ut poenitentes, quos ego hoc signaculo ab impoenitentibus discerno, ad baptismum Christi dirigam. Post me venturus est. Et si moneo dignos fructus poenitentiae facere, superbiam abjicere: non tamen possum a peccatis solvere. Fortior me est. Quia ego baptizo in 81Cpoenitentiam, ille in remissionem. Ego spiritum habeo, ille dat. Ego regnum coelorum praedico, ille dat.

Cujus non sum, etc. Alii scribunt: Cujus non sum dignus corrigiam calceamenti solvere. Potuit autem Joannes utrumque dicere, vel contextim vel diverso tempore: ut alius evangelista hoc, alius illud assumere. Omnes tamen verum narraverunt. Intendit autem in hoc ostendere excellentiam Christi et suam humilitatem.

Vel secundum allegoriam. Calceamentum est incarnationis mysterium; corrigia, mysterii ligatura. Non ergo valet Joannes corrigiam solvere, quia incarnationis mysterium non sufficit investigare. Vel, Non sum dignus solvere corrigiam calceamenti 81Dejus, id est, nomen sponsi mihi non usurpo, nec sponsus credi vel dici, sed amicus sponsi volo. Mos enim erat ut si quis eam quae sibi competeret accipere in uxorem nollet, ille ei calceamentum solveret qui ad hanc sponsus jure propinquitatis accederet. Joannes ergo, qui non sponsus, ait: Non sum dignus, etc. Ac si dicat. Ego redemptoris vestigia non valeo denudare: quia nomen sponsi non mihi usurpo.

Vel simpliciter se humiliat, vel Evangelii sacramenta non est dignus circumferre praedicando, quod non sibi deputatum, apostolis est commissum. Non sum dignus explicare naturam Divinitatis ad humanitatem. Vel, non sum dignus Evangelii praedicationem 82Aportare, vel nomen sponsi usurpare. Joannes ut purus homo spiritum dare non poterat, quo remittitur culpa. Christus vero dat, quia Deus: et baptizat in verbo, de quo dicitur: Et vos mundi estis propter verbum quod locutus sum vobis (Joan. XV); et alibi: Mundans eos lavacro aquae in verbo vitae sanctificatae (Ephes. V).

In Spiritu sancto. Vel spiritu sanctificationis, et igne, id est probatione tribulationis. Vel spiritu in praesenti abluit: post, si quae macula surrepit, igne purgatorio ad purum exuret: quod de levioribus credendum est peccatis. Unde in Levitico, super altare semper ignis missus de coelo ardebat, id est, ignis divini amoris, qui omnes in Christo (id est, qui super altare offeruntur), quasi rebaptizat et exurit 82Bcarnis eorum vitia.

VERS.12.-- Cujus ventilabrum. Ventilabrum in manu est examen judicii quo discernuntur leves et vacui a fructu boni operis. In manu, quia Pater non judicat quemquam, sed omne judicium dedit Filio. Permundabit, id est, quotidie a variis tentationibus mundat. Vel, Permundabit, dum ob manifesta peccata perversus de Ecclesia ejicitur, vel post mortem damnatur. Paleas autem. Paleae de origine unde et triticum oriuntur, id est de semine, zizania vero de diversa. Paleae ergo sunt qui fidei sacramentis imbuuntur, sed solidi non sunt: zizania vero qui et opere et professione secernuntur ab electis. De his dicitur: Qui non credit, jam judicatus est, et ideo non fit hic mentio de illis. Inexstinguibili. Quia non 82Cexstinguetur neque exstinguet cruciatos, sed aeternaliter puniet. Dicitur autem ad differentiam illius de quo dicitur: Igne nos examinasti (Psal. LXV), etc.

VERS. 13.-- Tunc venit. Quando Joannes praedicabat et baptizabat, scilicet quando Jesus triginta erat annorum, ostendens nullum debere sacerdotem vel praedicatorem fieri nisi utilis aetatis. Sicut Joseph tricenarius regnum Aegypti suscepit. David regnum ea aetate inchoavit: Ezechiel prophetiam promeruit. Venit Jesus his de causis ut baptisma Joannis comprobaret, et ut (quia homo erat) omnem impleret justitiam et legis humilitatem: et ut aquas sanctificans, per columbam in lavacro, adventum Spiritus sancti ostenderet. Venit enim non necessitate 82Dablutionis, sed ut nemo quantumlibet sanctus baptismi gratiam superfluam judicaret. A Galilaea in Jordanem. Galilaea, transmigratio: Jordanis, descensus. Qui ergo vult baptizari transmigret a vitiis, et descendens humilietur.

VERS. 15.-- Sine modo. Sine modo me a te baptizari, ut postea quod a me quaeris in spiritu baptizeris. Sicut enim decet dare exemplum implendae omnis justitiae, ut discant omnes neminem sine unda baptismi esse perfectum.

Tunc dimisit. Cum tali ordine cognovisset implendam justitiam. Dimisit, quia vere est humilitas, quam non deserit comes obedientia.

Spiritus in momento eum docuit, quod prius nescivit 83Aet ideo quod prius timuit humiliter, devotus implevit.

VERS. 16.-- Baptizatus autem. Christus aquas baptismi sanctificavit: quia cujus est creare, ejusdem est et sanctificare.

Sicut homo constat duobus, id est, corpore et anima, ita duobus renascitur: aqua quae purgat sordes et consepelit Christo: et igne spiritus quo conflagranti ad coelum rapimur. Sic Ecclesia post baptismum culmen virtutum quibusdam incrementis appetit, ut Christus baptizatus de aqua ascendit. Sic post baptismum ascendunt, qui ad virtutes proficiunt: et qui prius carnales et filii Adae, fiunt spirituales et filii Dei.

Aperti sunt, etc. Et hoc ad impletionem justitiae. 83BNon enim tunc primum sibi patuere coelestia vel Spiritus sanctus est datus: sed nobis per acceptum baptismum aditum coeli patere, et Spiritum sanctum dari monstravit. Quod enim coeli aperti, quod Spiritus sanctus venit, quod vox Patris insonuit, etc., haec mystica insunt nobis.

Patet renatis aditus in coelum per Christum, qui per Adam clausus fuit.

Sicut columbam. Verum corpus formatum ad horam habuit in specie columbae: quia aliter spiritus ab hominibus videri non posset sicut columba, non quia vera columba esset, sed quia spiritus in corporali specie, non tamen ipse corpus. Ita de hac figura sentitur sicut de aliis in quibus Deus apparuit: quae expleto officio resolvebantur. Bene spiritus in columba 83Cquae simplex et mansueta descendit, ut et suae naturae simplicitatem, et eum in quem descendebat, mitem misericordiae praeconem datoremque indicaret. Similiter omnes baptismo renati septem virtutibus in columba significatis debent repleri. Columba a malitia fellis est aliena, in quo prohibemur ab ira. Nullum ore vel unguibus laedit, in quo notatur innocentia. Nec minimas aviculas invadit, in quibus aliae aves se et pullos suos nutriunt: in quo prohibemur a rapina. Puro pascitur grano: in quo notatur abstinentia, alienos tanquam pullos suos fovet, ecce charitas. Gemitum dat pro cantu, ecce compunctio. Super aquas sedet ut accipitrem praevisa in aquis umbra declinet: ecce sollicitudo. In columba super Dominum spiritus apparuit: quia venit nos per mansuetudinem 83Dcolligere. Super discipulos in igne quos ad consumendam rubiginem peccati contra seipsos veniebat accendere. Adam peccando coelum amisit, Christus a Spiritu sancto per columbam glorificatus apparuit: flammam vibrantem quae viam prohibuit aqua baptismi exstinxit: quia aqua est contraria igni.

Complacui. Id est, bene placitum meum constitui, ut tu in quo nihil nisi bonum placeas, et per te alii non in se, sed in te, id est, tibi fide et claritate uniti placeant.

CHAPTER III.

VERS. 1.-- In those days. He passes over many things, and crosses to the preaching of John. In those days. He takes in not the years of boyhood only, but all of them up to the preaching of John, that is, when he was nearly thirty years old. Luke expresses the times more exactly: In the fifteenth year of the reign of Tiberius Caesar. Preaching. He who had before kept silence made himself known by baptizing, preparing the way 79Afor the Lord. In the desert of Judaea. Because the Jews had been deserted by God, and for them repentance is necessary, that they may return.

VERS. 2.-- Repentance. The herald announces this, which afterwards the Truth announces through himself; repentance goes before, the promise of the kingdom follows: he who truly repents, in himself his past errors, then lifts up his mind to the heavenly kingdom. If remission is made freely in baptism, why is repentance preached? But the manner of repentance is threefold. The first, to renounce and condemn errors and all evils before baptism: which is necessary for those who are coming to Christ. The second, that those who have fallen after baptism be called back through repentance. The third, whereby the saints are daily cleansed of such sins as life is not lived without.

79BTo repent is to weep for things done before, and not to commit what must be wept for. The kingdom of heaven is said to be at hand for the penitent. Not earthly things, as in the old law: and this through the obedience of Christ. And John first preached this kingdom of heaven: Do penance. The first virtue is, through repentance to slay the old man, and to hate vices: and he who does not do this not only fails to lay hold of the virtues, but does not even escape punishments. The virtue of repentance is conceived by fear, which is the beginning of wisdom. Repentance is from punishing, whereby each man punishes what he has unlawfully committed.

To do penance is to make fruits worthy of repentance. The kingdom of heaven. That is, Christ, through whom the saints reign in the heavenly places. Or the Gospel, by which he invites to the kingdom.

79CFor it hath drawn near, etc. Unless it had drawn near, no one could return: because the weak and the blind were without the way (which is Christ): and John, preparing that way, sets it before the penitent.

VERS. 3.-- The voice of one crying. John is the voice, Christ the word, who cries in John: No word is heard without a voice, nor is a voice of any avail without the understanding of the word. The voice prepares the way for the word, that it may be received. Whence: I have prepared a lamp for my Christ (Psal. CXXXI).

In the desert. A pattern for the penitent: the place of his converse is not the throng of men, but the desert. His clothing is not soft to the point of looseness, but rough: his food scanty and unaccustomed.

Typically, the desert signifies the life of the saints, set apart from the enticements of the world.

79DThe way of the Lord. The way of the Lord is made straight to the heart, when his word is diligently heard.

VERS. 4.-- And John himself. He who preaches repentance displays the habit of repentance. In him the meanness of clothing and of food is praised, the use of which in the rich man is called to account.

And John himself instructs the penitent in his own person, and sets forth a pattern of life by dwelling in the desert, living on locusts, clothing himself in camel's hair.

The servant of God ought not to have clothing for adornment or for delight, but only for covering nakedness. Hence John was clothed in hair.

VERS. 5.-- Then there went out to him, etc. Because unless a man go out from evils, he is not washed. Whence it fitly follows: They were baptized by him in the Jordan: which is interpreted a going down, 80Abecause they were going down from the pride of the old man to the humility of confession and amendment. For already then an example was given to those about to be baptized, of confessing sins and promising better things.

VERS. 6.-- In the Jordan. Because in it the figure of baptism went before, and from its name it requires a going down from pride, which is fitting for the baptized. Confessing. The confession of sins is the testimony of a conscience that fears God. For he who fears the judgment of God is not ashamed to confess his sins. More perfectly, fear looses every shame. The confession of sin has shame in it, and that blushing is itself a heavy punishment. And therefore we are bidden to confess our sins, that we may suffer the blushing for a punishment. For this very thing is part of the divine judgment.

VERS. 7.-- Many of the Pharisees, etc. These are of the 80Bheretics of the Jews, and the Pharisees set the righteousness of observances and traditions before all else. Whence also they are called Pharisees, that is, divided from the people by a private righteousness. The Sadducees, that is, the just: for they claim for themselves what they are not: these deny the resurrection, saying that the soul perishes with the body: they receive the five books of Moses, they reject the prophets. These, when they come to baptism, John rebukes: because they most of all had need of correction and repentance.

Brood of vipers, etc., that is, poisoned sons of the poisoned: because they envy the good and persecute them, they wound their neighbors as their fathers did.

The viper kills its own father, so the Jews killed the prophets who were their fathers.

VERS. 8.-- Bring forth therefore fruits worthy. (BEDA.) [Vulg. Mt 3:8 fructum dignum, singular; the plural fructus dignos is Lc 3:8's] 80CAs if he were saying: Why do you not first lay aside your poisons, that so to baptism, etc., as far as it is grace that feels, and feeling seeks holiness.

VERS. 9.-- For I say. He draws them back from the flesh, that they may consider the works of God's predestination. The first are the rudiments of faith, to believe that God can do whatever he will, so that he who created all things out of nothing can form sons of Abraham from stones or from dust. Of stones. Pointing to those stones which Josue caused to be carried over out of the Jordan, toward which he says this as though stretching out his finger.

Josue caused twelve stones to be carried from the midst of the Jordan onto dry land, and others to be set in their place out of the land in the same channel: by which the blinding of the Jews and the transforming of the Gentiles to the light are foresignified.

80DOr he calls the Gentiles stones: and the sense is: Do not glory in the seed of the flesh: because these only are the sons of God who have been enlightened by grace and inwardly raised up. He says "raise up" pointedly, because Christ received Sara, that is, the Church, and raises up sons of Abraham by grace, he having died without children, lest so great a patriarch should be deprived of the promises. In presage of this thing God once begot a son of Sara.

VERS. 10.-- For now the axe. But lest they should seem free of danger if they kept themselves apart from such sacraments, he threatens. The axe. Christ, who consists of haft and iron, that is, of the humanity by which he is held, and the divinity because he cuts. Is laid: because although he waits through patience, he yet sees what he is about to do. 81ATo the root. That is, the end of the Jewish people, that he may take away from the land of the living those who do not believe in Christ. Or, the axe is the sentence of judgment, or the preaching of the Gospel. To the root. Note, not to the branches, but to the root. For when the sons of the wicked are taken away, what else is cut off but the branches of an unfruitful tree? When the whole offspring is taken away together with the parent, the tree is cut off at the root, that nothing may remain from which some shoot might spring up again. The roots are thoughts, by which men, being planted, are either lifted up on high to heaven, or sent down to the depths of hell. The tree is the human race: of this tree some branches are dry, that is, the pagans, fit for the burning. Others are green, but without fruit: as the hypocrites, who make a show of holiness, but are empty within: others are fruitful, but poisonous, that is, the heretics: who by preaching 81Bbring forth fruit, but a deadly fruit. Others, that is, the catholics, who bear good fruit. Every tree therefore, as the Gentiles, the hypocrites, who bear none, and the heretics, who bear evil fruit.

VERS. 11.-- I indeed. Lest he should seem to threaten on his own authority, he begins to disclose of how great dignity he is: whose presence he holds out as a threat, or whose benefit he urges upon them: I indeed baptize you in water. I only wash bodies, because I cannot loose sins, so that as I run before in being born and in preaching, so I do in baptizing: that I may direct to the baptism of Christ the penitent, whom I mark off by this sign from the impenitent. He who is to come after me. And though I admonish you to bring forth fruits worthy of repentance, to cast away pride: yet I cannot loose from sins. He is mightier than I. Because I baptize unto 81Crepentance, he unto remission. I have the Spirit, he gives it. I preach the kingdom of heaven, he gives it.

Whose I am not, etc. Others write: Whose I am not worthy to loose the latchet of his shoe. Now John could have said both, either in one continuous speech or at different times: so that one evangelist took up this, another that. Yet all of them told the truth. His aim in this is to show the excellence of Christ and his own humility.

Or according to the allegory. The shoe is the mystery of the incarnation; the latchet, the tying of the mystery. Therefore John is not able to loose the latchet, because he does not suffice to search out the mystery of the incarnation. Or, I am not worthy to loose the latchet of his shoe, 81Dthat is, I do not usurp for myself the name of bridegroom, nor do I wish to be believed or called the bridegroom, but the friend of the bridegroom. For it was the custom that if any man refused to take to wife her who fell to him, the man who should come forward as bridegroom to her by right of kinship would loose his shoe. John therefore, who is not the bridegroom, says: I am not worthy, etc. As if he were to say: I cannot uncover the footsteps of the Redeemer: because I do not usurp for myself the name of bridegroom.

Or he simply humbles himself, or he is not worthy to carry about the sacraments of the Gospel by preaching, which was not assigned to him but committed to the apostles. I am not worthy to unfold the nature of the Divinity to the humanity. Or, I am not worthy to bear 82Athe preaching of the Gospel, or to usurp the name of bridegroom. John, as a mere man, could not give the Spirit, by whom guilt is remitted. But Christ gives it, because he is God: and he baptizes in the word, of which it is said: And you are clean by reason of the word which I have spoken to you (Joan. XV) [Vulg. Jo 15:3 Jam vos mundi estis propter sermonem quem locutus sum vobis]; and elsewhere: Cleansing them by the laver of water in the word of sanctified life (Ephes. V) [Vulg. Eph 5:26 ut illam sanctificaret, mundans lavacro aquae in verbo vitae, without eos and sanctificatae].

In the Holy Spirit. Either in the spirit of sanctification, and in fire, that is, in the proving of tribulation. Or in the spirit he washes in the present: afterwards, if any stain has crept in, he will burn it away to purity by the fire of purgatory: which is to be believed of the lighter sins. Whence in Leviticus, upon the altar a fire sent down from heaven burned continually, that is, the fire of divine love, which as it were rebaptizes all those in Christ (that is, those who are offered upon the altar) and burns away 82Bthe vices of their flesh.

VERS.12.-- Whose fan. The fan in his hand is the sifting of judgment by which are distinguished the light and those empty of the fruit of good work. In his hand, because the Father judges no man, but has given all judgment to the Son. He shall thoroughly cleanse, that is, he daily cleanses from divers temptations. Or, He shall thoroughly cleanse, when for manifest sins the perverse man is cast out of the Church, or after death is damned. But the chaff. The chaff springs from the same origin as the wheat, that is, from the seed, but the cockle from a different one. The chaff, therefore, are those who are steeped in the sacraments of the faith, but are not solid: the cockle are those who are severed from the elect both in work and in profession. Of these it is said: He that believeth not is already judged, and therefore no mention is made here of them. Unquenchable. Because it shall not 82Cbe quenched, neither shall it quench those it torments, but shall punish them eternally. And it is so called by way of distinction from that fire of which it is said: Thou hast tried us by fire (Psal. LXV), etc.

VERS. 13.-- Then he came. When John was preaching and baptizing, namely when Jesus was thirty years old, showing that no one ought to be made a priest or a preacher save at a serviceable age. As Joseph at thirty took up the rule of Egypt. David began his reign at that age: Ezekiel merited the gift of prophecy. Jesus came for these causes, that he might approve the baptism of John, and that (because he was man) he might fulfil all righteousness and the humility of the law: and that, sanctifying the waters, he might show through the dove the coming of the Holy Spirit in the laver. For he came not out of any need 82Dof washing, but that no one, however holy, should judge the grace of baptism superfluous. From Galilee to the Jordan. Galilee, a passing over: the Jordan, a going down. He therefore who wishes to be baptized, let him pass over from vices, and going down, let him be humbled.

VERS. 15.-- Suffer it now. Suffer me now to be baptized by thee, that afterwards thou mayest be baptized in the Spirit with that which thou seekest of me. For as it is fitting to give an example of fulfilling all righteousness, that all may learn that no one is perfect without the water of baptism.

Then he suffered him. When he had come to know that righteousness was to be fulfilled in such an order. He suffered him, because that is true humility which its companion obedience does not desert.

The Spirit taught him in a moment what he had not known before, 83Aand therefore what he had before humbly feared, he devoutly fulfilled.

VERS. 16.-- And being baptized. Christ sanctified the waters of baptism: because it belongs to him whose it is to create, to sanctify also.

As man consists of two things, that is, of body and of soul, so he is reborn of two: of water, which cleanses filth and buries him together with Christ: and of the fire of the Spirit, by whose blaze we are caught up to heaven. So the Church after baptism reaches after the summit of the virtues by certain increases, as Christ when baptized came up out of the water. So after baptism those go up who advance to the virtues: and those who before were carnal and sons of Adam become spiritual and sons of God.

They were opened, etc. And this too belongs to the fulfilling of righteousness. 83BFor not then for the first time did the heavenly things lie open to him, nor was the Holy Spirit then given: but he showed that to us, through the baptism we have received, the entrance of heaven lies open, and that the Holy Spirit is given. For that the heavens were opened, that the Holy Spirit came, that the voice of the Father sounded, etc., these mystical things are within us.

The entrance into heaven lies open to the reborn through Christ, which through Adam had been closed.

As a dove. He had a true body, formed for the hour, in the appearance of a dove: because otherwise the Spirit could not be seen by men as a dove, not because it was a true dove, but because the Spirit was in a bodily appearance, yet was not itself a body. So of this figure it is judged as of the others in which God appeared: which, their office fulfilled, were dissolved. Fitly the Spirit came down in a dove, 83Cwhich is simple and gentle, that he might point out both the simplicity of his own nature, and him upon whom he was descending, the meek herald and giver of mercy. In like manner all who are reborn by baptism ought to be filled with the seven virtues signified in the dove. The dove is a stranger to the malice of gall, in which we are forbidden anger. It wounds none with beak or claws, in which innocence is marked. Nor does it fall upon the smallest little birds, on which other birds feed themselves and their young: in which we are forbidden robbery. It feeds on clean grain: in which abstinence is marked, it cherishes the young of others as its own, behold charity. It gives a moan for a song, behold compunction. It sits above the waters, that by the shadow foreseen in the waters it may turn aside from the hawk: behold watchfulness. In a dove the Spirit appeared above the Lord: because he came to gather us through meekness. 83DAbove the disciples in fire, whom he was coming to kindle against their own selves for the consuming of the rust of sin. Adam by sinning lost heaven, Christ appeared glorified by the Holy Spirit through the dove: the quivering flame which barred the way, the water of baptism quenched: because water is contrary to fire.

I am well pleased. That is, I have established my good pleasure, that thou, in whom is nothing but good, mayest please, and that through thee others may please, not in themselves, but in thee, that is, united to thee by faith and brightness.

CAPUT IV.

VERS. 1.-- Tunc Jesus. Post baptismum, quia nulla est mora a tentatione: Ut mox doceat baptizatos 84Ade mundo exire, in quiete Deo vacare: sic filii Israel post transitum maris per desertum ascendunt, et manna comedunt donec veniant ad terram promissionis. Tunc Jesus ductus est, etc. (RAB.) Hic est ordo rectae conversationis, ut post acceptam Spiritus sancti gratiam contra diabolum arctius accingamur: ea via Samaritanus descendit, quia carne indutus easdem tentationes sustinuit.

Spiritui Sancto, qui quos replet ad pugnam mittit fortes. Sine hoc spiritu qui ad pugnam vadit, cito cadit. Ut tentaretur a diabolo. Non tentatur a diabolo, nisi quia ad desertum exierit, id est, bono studere coeperit.

VERS. 2.-- Et cum jejunasset. (BEDA.) Jejunat ut tentetur, tentatur quia jejunat, et exemplum jejunandi 84Bnobis dat, etc., usque ad et sic quandiu hic sumus, semper peccata ploremus: quia hoc numero praesens vita ostenditur.

(LEO.) Dum per varias actiones vitae hujus sollicitudo distenditur, necesse est de mundano pulvere, etc., usque ad ut in cujus sumus resurrectione conresuscitati, in ipsius inveniamur passione commortui.

Postea esuriit. Hoc est vere humanitatis: et per hoc est occasio tentandi. Latet potestas, patet infirmitas.

VERS. 3.-- Et accedens. Quia esuries est signum infirmitatis. Et sic nobis in abstinentia non deest tentatio. Per exteriorem infirmitatem Christum tentat in quo nullam legem peccati inveniebat. Si Filius 84CDei es. Aliud horum explorantis est, aliud tentantis, dum Deum confiteri videtur, et hujusmodi illudere conatur. Sic enim tentat ut exploret quod veretur. Sic explorat, ut tentando decipiat. Si Filius Dei es. Noverat Filium Dei venisse in mundum, seu per prophetas seu per angelos nuntiantes, seu per Joannem demonstrantem: sed quia humilia in eo videbat, quod de Deitate suspicatus est, ex superbia ei in dubium venit. Unde et callide exquirit: Nec indignum fuit Christum tentari qui venerat occidi, ut tentationes superando nobis potestatem daret superandi tentationes, sicut sua morte abstulit nostram mortalitatem.

(RAB.) Christus non nisi post baptismum se tentari permisit, insinuans ad se euntes graviores passuros, 84Detc., usque ad Christus vero sola suggestione tentatus fuit: quia delectatio peccati mentem ejus non momordit.

VERS. 4.-- Scriptum est. Non utitur potestate, sed Scripturarum auctoritate, docens nos magis doctrina quam miraculis pugnare. Non in solo pane vivit homo. Quasi: Persuasio tua tentatio est: quia agis de cibo corporis, et non de cibo mentis. Inferior pars hominis pane sustentatur, alia verbo Dei reficitur: quia vero agis de inferiori, patet quod tentator sis. Sed in omni verbo, etc. Omne verbum charitas, in qua complentur omnia quae procedunt de ore Dei, quia cujuscunque ministerio dicantur, non ejus sunt, sed Dei: sed si adversum quid inseritur, hoc non est 85ADei. De uno verbo, id est, sapientia Dei procedit quidquid loquuntur sancti qui sunt os ejus.

VERS. 5.-- Tunc assumpsit. Prima et ultima tentatio in deserto, media quoque historialiter, ultima fuit postquam regressus est Jerusalem. Quia Matthaeus non secundum ordinem historiae, sed secundum tentationem Adae prosequitur: Lucas vero ut res gesta est.

Quia ex responsione Christi victus et incertus remansit: post primam ad secundam accessit tentationem.

Quod permisit se assumi, non infirmitas fuit, sed patientia: in diabolo vero non virtus, sed superbia.

Nota haec omnia corporeis sensibus esse completa. Nec mirum si se permisit circumferri: si sic 85Baccipitur assumptio, qui permisit se crucifigi.

Supra pinnaculum. (RAB.) In Palaestina desuper plana erant tecta, et ibi erat sedes doctorum, unde, etc., usque ad ostendit ut cuicunque bona et alta imperanti obediamus, sed praecipitare volenti contraeamus.

Si filius. (BEDA.) In omnibus tentationibus hoc agit, ut intelligat, etc., usque ad in specie hominis diabolum apparuisse verisimile est.

Mitte. Haec vox convenit illi, qui omnes praecipitare satagit: in quo infirmus ostenditur, quod nulli nocere possit, nisi prius ille se deorsum miserit. Suggerebat enim, ut aliquo signo exploraret quantum apud Deum posset. Sed nemo debet tentare Deum, quando habet ex humana ratione quid faciat. 85CUnde quamvis omnia posset, ait tamen suis: Si persecuti vos fuerint in civitate ista, fugite in aliam. Et ipse idem fugit et abiit et latuit. Nunc autem poterat aliter de templo descendere, quam per jactantiam se praecipitare. Postquam deficit humana ratio, commendet se homo Deo, non tentando, sed devote confitendo.

Quia angelis suis mandavit de te. (BEDA.) Quod bene dicitur de corpore, diabolus male interpretatur de capite, etc., usque ad sed de auxilio angelorum quasi ad infirmum loquitur, de sua conculcatione tacet.

VERS. 8.-- Iterum assumpsit. Haec tentatio ordine praecessit, sed narratur praepostero, secundum quod 85Dfactum est Adae.

Ostendit, etc. Non quod visum ejus qui omnia videt ampliaverit, sed vanitatem mundanae pompae quam amabat, quasi speciosam, etc., usque ad Non quod eam concupiscentiae oculo intueretur sicut nos, sed sic haec ostensio fuit in mente, sicut medici vident morbos sine laesione.

VERS. 9.-- Haec omnia tibi dabo. Haec de arrogantia dicit: non quod totus mundus suus sit.

A principio quod Dei erat ut sibi usurparet diabolus laboravit: unde ut in eis coleretur, idola invenit. In his tribus notantur gula, avaritia, superbia.

(RAB.) Lucas avaritiam mediam ponit, ultimam superbiam, 86Aetc., usque ad in his tribus omnia genera tentationum comprehenduntur.

VERS. 10.-- Scriptum est. David Goliam tribus lapidibus de torrente prostravit, et Christus diabolum tribus testimoniis de lege. Dominum Deum tuum. Hoc vel ad diabolum dicitur: non quia ex devotione sit impleturus sed ut, econtra, quam ipse moliebatur scriptum ostendatur quod non ipse, sed Deus sit adorandus: vel, non diabolo, sed sibi secundum humanitatem et cuilibet homini hoc praeceptum esse insinuat. Similiter, et illud: Non tentabis Dominum Deum tuum, scilicet Filium et Patrem et Spiritum sanctum, qui est unus Deus. Deus adoratur, et specialiter ei propter se servitur.

Hic ostenditur, quod homo Christus, ut Deus et 86BDominus sit adorandus ex debito.

Servies. Graece λατρεύσεις : λατρεία enim servitus dicitur. Servitus communis Deo et homini et cuicunque, Graece δουλεία dicitur. Illa vero quae soli Deo debetur, latria dicitur: unde idololatria, quae quod soli Deo debet, idolis dat.

Nota diabolum in his vinci in quibus Adam vicit. Quem de gula tentavit, dum de ligno vetito gustare rogavit. De vana gloria, cum dixit (Gen. III): Eritis sicut dii. De avaritia, cum ait: Scientes bonum et malum.

VERS. 11.-- Accesserunt. Non quasi tunc primum adeuntes, sed agonem Domini procul aspicientes, ne videatur eorum praesidio eguisse vel vicisse. Sed ut ille victus abiit, parati ad obsequium venerunt. Sicut 86Cin hoc agone militia nostra praestruitur, ita in obsequium angelorum gloriosa remuneratio docetur.

VERS. 12.-- Cum autem audisset. (RAB.) Hic Matthaeus, sicut Marcus et Lucas, praetermittit primum adventum Domini in Galilaeam, postquam, etc., usque ad, Hoc fecit initium signorum Jesus. Et iterum: Nondum Joannes missus erat in carcerem.

Joannes, etc. Quod erat propter Evangelium. Jesus hoc idem perfectius praedicans ad tempus cedit et fugit, ut sit exemplum suis. In Galilaeam. Duae sunt Galilaeae: una dicitur Judaeorum, altera gentium propter inhabitationem gentium, quam partem dedit Salomon regi Tyri qui gentes ibi posuit.

86DVERS. 13.-- Nazareth. (RAB.) Unde Christus Nazaraeus dicitur, vicus est in Galilaea juxta montem Thabor, etc., usque ad quia in medio gentium ad dilatandum verbum Dei fortiter consistit, ut credentibus refugium periclitantibus ostendat fidei portum.

VERS. 15.-- Terra Zabulon. (ID.) Quod primo positum est praetermittit evangelista; scilicet: Primo tempore alleviata est terra Zabulon et terra Nephtalim (Isa. IX): sicut et quod in medio est, etc., usque ad sed pro figura eorum qui vocandi sunt de gentibus, ea quae gentium est hic memoratur.

VERS. 16.-- Populus. (AUG.) Populus iste qui prior ductus est in captivitatem, et in tenebris vitiorum erat, prior lucem praedicationis Christi vidit. Deinde 87Ain omnes Evangelium disseminatum est. Unde sequitur: Habitantibus in regione.

Umbrae mortis. Mors est in inferno. Umbra hujus mortis sunt peccata, quae nunquam sine morte. Qui ergo vult fugere mortem, vitet umbram.

VERS. 17.-- Exinde. Postquam baptizatus est. Baptismus idoneos facit ad praedicandum. Exinde, id est, tradito Joanne: quia lege desinente sequitur Evangelium, ut auroram sol. Humilis magister qui doctrinam discipuli non impedit, sed exspectat donec consummet cursum suum. In quo docet, ne quis ab inferiore persona sermonem contemnat. Unde Apostolus. Si cui revelatum fuerit, prior taceat.

Poenitentiam agite. Eadem quae praeco praedicat: 87But ostendatur non alius quam qui in voce clamaverat. Christus enim verbum quod clamat in Jordane organo et in omnibus qui ab initio aliquid divinitus dixerunt: et tamen iste solus vox, quia per eum praesens verbum ostenditur quod alii longe nuntiaverunt. Inter enim vocem et verbum nihil est medium, sed ut vox sonuit, mox verbum percipitur: sicut Joannes clausus destitit: Christus virtutibus claruit. Unde Joannes est figura prophetarum et legis, qui vere sunt vox clamantis sapientiae Dei, quorum Joannes est finis, quibus cessantibus solum verbum, quod in eis sonuerat, per se ostenditur.

(RAB.) Haec praedicatio Christi in Capharnaum coruscantibus miraculis incepta, dicitur, etc., usque ad Baptista incarcerato praedicare incepit.

87CVERS. 18.-- Qui vocatur Petrus. Vel quando primum Andraeas adduxit eum, hoc nomen inditum est ei, dum dicitur: Tu vocaberis Cephas (Joan. I), quod interpretatur Petrus: quanquam hic videtur de futuro promittere. Vel quando confessus est: Tu es Christus Filius Dei vivi, dictum est ei: Tu es Petrus et super hanc petram aedificabo Ecclesiam meam (Matth. XVI).

(BEDA.) Petrus, agnoscens: Andraeas, virilis: quod doctoribus convenit: quia hi soli, etc., usque ad quia vero primus homo inobediens exivit, secundus obediens ingressus est (RAB.) Matthaeus dicit post habitationem Capharnaum discipulos vocasse. Joannes dicit, etc., usque ad ante autem quam veniret Jesus in Cana Galilaeae, Andraeas Petrum adduxit, sed 87Dnondum discipuli.

Mittentes rete. Quia quos saepe terrenis lucris inhiare considerat, coelestibus desideriis advocat, et per eos retibus Evangelii, alios de profundo iniquitatis eripit, unde subdit: Venite post me, et faciam vos fieri piscatores hominum (Joan. I).

VERS. 19.-- Piscatores. Piscaturus per piscatoria vadit loca.

Mystice. Cum incarnatus carnem suscepit, divinitus vidit in mari mundi spirituales piscatores: et aliis quid essent ostendit, quos ab aeterno praevidit.

VERS. 20.-- Continuo. Perfecta obedientia est sua imperfecta relinquere. Relictis retibus: quia et 88Asi postea resumunt, non cupiditate et proprietatis amore. In his datur forma volentibus sequi. Mutatur intentio non piscatio, mutantur retia in doctrinam, cupiditas in amorem animarum: fit mare saeculum, navis Ecclesia, pisces boni et mali homines.

VERS. 21.-- Vidit alios duos. Ecce in his quatuor numerus evangelistarum praefiguratur, qui animas a mundo extrahunt de quatuor partibus mundi.

Bini et bini vocantur, quia charitas non nisi inter duos est. Vocatio dicitur: Vel secundum electionem gratiae. Unde dicitur: Quos vocavit (Rom. VIII), etc. Vel secundum illud: Multi sunt vocati (Matth. X).

(AUG.) Quaeritur quomodo Matthaeus et Marcus dicant binos vocasse Jesum: primo Petrum et Andraeam, etc., usque ad ideo autem piscatores illitteratos 88Bmisit praedicare, ut fides in virtute Dei, non eloquentia hominum putetur.

Zebedaei et Joannem. Zebedaeus fugitivus, id est, diabolus. Jacobus, supplantator qui adjuvatur a Joanne, id est, a gratia Dei. Et vocavit eos. Illi autem, etc. Vocavit et alios, sed sufficit nominare istos.

VERS. 23.-- Et circumibat Jesus totam Galilaeam. Coadjutoribus vocatis praedicationi insistit, docens impigrum esse debere doctorem. Docens in synagogis. Docens de moribus, vitae activae subditos. Praedicans de futuris vitae contemplativae perfectis. Docens naturales justitias, castitatem scilicet et humilitatem et similia quae homo naturaliter habet. Praedicans Evangelium regni. Ad Evangelium pertinet 88Cmysterium justitiae, bonum initium, beatitudinis promissio, peccatorum remissio, adoptio, resurrectio, coelestis haereditas, angelorum societas.

In synagogis. Nec otiosus doctor in vacuum discurrat, sed ubi sunt plures praedicet.

Evangelium. Quia justitia est molestia viventi in carne, labor justitiae non suscipitur, nisi merces operis exspectetur. Ideo Christus evangelizat beatitudinem, ut excitet ad boni operis patientiam.

Et sanans. Curare languorem et infirmitatem non fuit magnum, cum postea morituri. Sed ideo factum, ut sic erigerentur ad regnum. Moraliter instruens praedicatores etiam terrena subsidia subditis ministrare, et sic ad regnum trahere.

VERS. 24.-- Et abiit opinio. (BEDA.) Opera salutis 88Dsine fama boni odoris non satis relucent auditoribus, nec fama sine opere proficit: sine quibus, etc., usque ad curat corpora, ut paratius audiant invisibilia. Aliter parum erat mox morituris curam impendere.

VERS. 25.-- Secutae sunt. (AUG.) Quadripartitae turbae, scilicet. Alii, etc., usque ad alii, per invidiam volentes eum accusare et in aliquo capere.

CHAPTER IV.

VERS. 1.-- Then Jesus. After baptism, because there is no delay before temptation: that he may straightway teach the baptized 84Ato go out of the world, and in quiet to be free for God: so the sons of Israel after the passage of the sea go up through the desert, and eat manna until they come to the land of promise. Then Jesus was led, etc. (RAB.) This is the order of right converse, that after receiving the grace of the Holy Spirit we should gird ourselves the more closely against the devil: by that way the Samaritan came down, because clothed in flesh he bore the same temptations.

To the Spirit, the Holy, who sends strong to the battle those whom he fills. Without this Spirit, he who goes to the battle quickly falls. That he might be tempted by the devil. He is not tempted by the devil save because he has gone out to the desert, that is, has begun to be zealous for good.

VERS. 2.-- And when he had fasted. (BEDA.) He fasts that he may be tempted, he is tempted because he fasts, and he gives us an example of fasting, 84Betc., as far as and so, as long as we are here, let us always bewail our sins: because by this number the present life is shown.

(LEO.) While through the various actions of this life our solicitude is stretched out, it must needs be that from the dust of the world, etc., as far as that we, who have been raised together in his resurrection, may be found to have died together in his passion.

Afterwards he was hungry. This belongs to true humanity: and through this comes the occasion of tempting. The power lies hidden, the weakness lies open.

VERS. 3.-- And coming. Because hunger is a sign of weakness. And so temptation is not wanting to us in abstinence. Through the outward weakness he tempts Christ, in whom he found no law of sin. If thou be the Son 84Cof God. It is one thing to be a man exploring these matters, another to be a man tempting, while he seems to confess God and endeavors to mock at one of this kind. For so he tempts, that he may explore what he fears. So he explores, that by tempting he may deceive. If thou be the Son of God. He had known that the Son of God had come into the world, whether by the prophets, or by the angels announcing it, or by John pointing him out: but because he saw humble things in him, what he had suspected concerning the Godhead came into doubt with him out of pride. Whence also he cunningly makes inquiry: Nor was it unworthy that Christ, who had come to be slain, should be tempted, that by overcoming temptations he might give us the power of overcoming temptations, as by his own death he took away our mortality.

(RAB.) Christ did not suffer himself to be tempted except after baptism, hinting that those who come to him will suffer heavier things, 84Detc., as far as but Christ was tempted by suggestion only: because the delight of sin did not bite his mind.

VERS. 4.-- It is written. He does not use his power, but the authority of the Scriptures, teaching us to fight rather with doctrine than with miracles. Not in bread alone doth man live. As if to say: Thy persuasion is a temptation: because thou treatest of the food of the body, and not of the food of the mind. The lower part of man is sustained by bread, the other is refreshed by the word of God: but because thou treatest of the lower part, it is plain that thou art a tempter. But in every word, etc. Every word is charity, in which are fulfilled all the things that proceed out of the mouth of God, because by whosesoever ministry they are spoken, they are not his but God's: but if anything contrary is inserted, this is not 85Aof God. Out of the one word, that is, the wisdom of God, proceeds whatever the saints speak, who are his mouth.

VERS. 5.-- Then he took him. The first and the last temptation were in the desert; the middle one, moreover, was historically the last, after he had returned to Jerusalem. Because Matthew follows not the order of the history, but the order of Adam's temptation: whereas Luke follows the order in which the thing was done.

Because he was left conquered and uncertain by Christ's answer: after the first he went on to the second temptation.

That he permitted himself to be taken up was not weakness, but patience: whereas in the devil it was not power, but pride.

Note that all these things were accomplished by the bodily senses. Nor is it a wonder if he permitted himself to be carried about: if the taking up is so 85Bunderstood, he who permitted himself to be crucified.

Upon the pinnacle. (RAB.) In Palestine the roofs were flat on top, and there was the seat of the teachers, whence, etc., as far as he shows that we should obey whoever commands good and lofty things, but resist him who wills to cast us down.

If thou be the Son. (BEDA.) In all the temptations he does this, that he may understand, etc., as far as it is likely that the devil appeared in the shape of a man.

Cast thyself. This voice befits him who busies himself to cast all men down: in which he is shown to be weak, because he can hurt no one, unless that man first cast himself down. For he was suggesting that by some sign he should explore how much he could avail with God. But no one ought to tempt God, when he has from human reason what he may do. 85CWhence though he could do all things, he yet says to his own: If they persecute you in this city, flee into another. [Vulg. Mt 10:23 Cum autem persequentur vos in civitate ista, fugite in aliam] And he himself likewise fled and departed and hid himself. Now he could have come down from the temple otherwise than by casting himself down out of boastfulness. When human reason fails, let a man commend himself to God, not by tempting, but by devoutly confessing.

For he hath given his angels charge over thee. (BEDA.) What is rightly said of the body, the devil wrongly interprets of the head, etc., as far as but he speaks of the help of the angels as though to a weak man, and is silent about his own being trodden underfoot.

VERS. 8.-- Again he took him. This temptation went before in order, but is related out of its place, according to what 85Dwas done in Adam's case.

He showed him, etc. Not that he enlarged the sight of him who sees all things, but the vanity of worldly pomp, which he loved, as though it were beautiful, etc., as far as Not that he beheld it with the eye of concupiscence as we do, but this showing was in the mind, as physicians see diseases without being harmed by them.

VERS. 9.-- All these will I give thee. This he says out of arrogance: not that the whole world is his.

From the beginning the devil labored to usurp for himself what was God's: whence, that he might be worshipped in them, he invented idols. In these three are marked gluttony, avarice, pride.

(RAB.) Luke sets avarice in the middle, and pride last, 86Aetc., as far as in these three all the kinds of temptations are comprehended.

VERS. 10.-- It is written. David laid Goliath low with three stones from the torrent, and Christ laid the devil low with three testimonies from the law. The Lord thy God. This is either said to the devil: not because he was going to fulfil it out of devotion, but that, on the contrary to what he himself was attempting, it might be shown by what is written that not he, but God is to be adored: or he hints that this precept was given not to the devil, but to himself according to his humanity, and to any man whatsoever. Likewise also that other word: Thou shalt not tempt the Lord thy God, namely the Son and the Father and the Holy Spirit, who is one God. God is adored, and is served especially for his own sake.

Here it is shown that the man Christ, as God and 86BLord, is to be adored by debt.

Thou shalt serve. In Greek λατρεύσεις : for λατρεία is called service. Service common to God and to man and to whomsoever is called in Greek δουλεία. But that which is owed to God alone is called latria: whence idolatry, which gives to idols what it owes to God alone.

Note that the devil is conquered in those things in which he conquered Adam. Him he tempted by gluttony, when he asked him to taste of the forbidden tree. By vainglory, when he said (Gen. III): You shall be as gods. By avarice, when he said: Knowing good and evil.

VERS. 11.-- They came. Not as though then coming to him for the first time, but beholding the Lord's contest from afar, lest he should seem to have needed their protection or to have conquered by it. But when the other went away conquered, they came ready for his service. As 86Cin this contest our warfare is laid out beforehand, so in the service of the angels a glorious recompense is taught.

VERS. 12.-- And when he had heard. (RAB.) Here Matthew, like Mark and Luke, passes over the Lord's first coming into Galilee, after which, etc., as far as, This beginning of miracles did Jesus. And again: John was not yet cast into prison. [Vulg. Jo 3:24 Nondum enim missus fuerat Joannes in carcerem]

John, etc. Which was for the Gospel's sake. Jesus, preaching this same thing more perfectly, gives place for a time and flees, that he may be an example to his own. Into Galilee. There are two Galilees: one is called the Galilee of the Jews, the other of the Gentiles, because of the dwelling of Gentiles there, which portion Solomon gave to the king of Tyre, who settled Gentiles there.

86DVERS. 13.-- Nazareth. (RAB.) From which Christ is called a Nazarene, is a village in Galilee beside mount Thabor, etc., as far as because in the midst of the Gentiles it stands firm for the spreading abroad of the word of God, that it may show to believers in peril the harbor of faith.

VERS. 15.-- The land of Zabulon. (ID.) The evangelist passes over what stands first; namely: In the first time the land of Zabulon and the land of Nephtalim was lightened (Isa. IX): as also what stands in the middle, etc., as far as but for a figure of those who are to be called from among the Gentiles, that which is of the Gentiles is here mentioned.

VERS. 16.-- The people. (AUG.) That people which was first led into captivity, and was in the darkness of vices, first saw the light of Christ's preaching. Thereafter 87Athe Gospel was scattered abroad among all. Whence it follows: To them that dwell in the region. [Vulg. Mt 4:16 sedentibus in regione, "to them that sit"]

Of the shadow of death. Death is in hell. The shadow of this death are sins, which are never without death. He therefore who wishes to flee death, let him avoid the shadow.

VERS. 17.-- From that time. After he was baptized. Baptism makes men fit for preaching. From that time, that is, John having been delivered up: because when the law ceases the Gospel follows, as the sun follows the dawn. A humble master, who does not hinder the teaching of his disciple, but waits until he complete his course. In which he teaches that no one should despise a word from a lesser person. Whence the Apostle. If anything be revealed to another, let the first hold his peace. [Vulg. 1Cor 14:30 Quod si alii revelatum fuerit sedenti, prior taceat]

Do penance. The same things which the herald preaches: 87Bthat it may be shown that he is no other than he who had cried in the voice. For Christ is the Word which cries in the Jordan by his instrument, and in all who from the beginning spoke anything by divine inspiration: and yet this man alone is the voice, because through him is shown the Word present, which the others announced from afar. For between the voice and the word there is nothing between, but as soon as the voice has sounded, straightway the word is perceived: as John, shut up, ceased: Christ shone forth in mighty works. Whence John is the figure of the prophets and of the law, who are truly the voice of the crying wisdom of God, of whom John is the end, and when they cease the Word alone, which had sounded in them, is shown by itself.

(RAB.) This preaching of Christ, begun in Capharnaum with miracles flashing forth, is said, etc., as far as he began to preach when the Baptist was imprisoned.

87CVERS. 18.-- Who is called Peter. Either when Andrew first brought him to him, this name was put upon him, when it is said: Thou shalt be called Cephas (Joan. I), which is interpreted Peter: although here he seems to promise it for the future. Or when he confessed: Thou art Christ the Son of the living God, it was said to him: Thou art Peter, and upon this rock I will build my Church (Matth. XVI).

(BEDA.) Peter, acknowledging: Andrew, manly: which befits teachers: because these alone, etc., as far as but because the first man went out disobedient, the second entered in obedient (RAB.) Matthew says that he called the disciples after his dwelling at Capharnaum. John says, etc., as far as but before Jesus came into Cana of Galilee, Andrew brought Peter, yet 87Dthey were not disciples.

Casting a net. Because those whom he sees often gaping after earthly gains he calls to heavenly desires, and through them, by the nets of the Gospel, he snatches others out of the deep of iniquity, whence he adds: Come ye after me, and I will make you to be fishers of men (Joan. I).

VERS. 19.-- Fishers. He who is about to fish goes through the places of fishing.

Mystically. When being incarnate he took flesh, he saw by his divinity in the sea of the world spiritual fishers: and to others he showed what they were, whom he had foreseen from eternity.

VERS. 20.-- Immediately. Perfect obedience is to leave one's own things unfinished. Their nets being left: because even 88Aif afterwards they take them up again, it is not out of covetousness and the love of ownership. In these a pattern is given to those who wish to follow. The intention is changed, not the fishing, the nets are changed into doctrine, covetousness into the love of souls: the sea becomes the world, the ship the Church, the fishes good and evil men.

VERS. 21.-- He saw two others. Behold, in these four the number of the evangelists is prefigured, who draw souls out of the world from the four quarters of the world.

They are called two and two, because charity is not save between two. A calling is spoken of: either according to the election of grace. Whence it is said: Whom he called (Rom. VIII), etc. Or according to that word: Many are called (Matth. X).

(AUG.) It is asked how Matthew and Mark say that Jesus called them two by two: first Peter and Andrew, etc., as far as but he sent unlettered fishermen 88Bto preach for this reason, that faith might be reckoned to stand in the power of God, not in the eloquence of men.

Of Zebedee, and John. Zebedee, a fugitive, that is, the devil. James, a supplanter, who is helped by John, that is, by the grace of God. And he called them. But they, etc. He called others also, but it suffices to name these.

VERS. 23.-- And Jesus went about all Galilee. His fellow workers being called, he presses on with preaching, teaching that a teacher ought to be untiring. Teaching in the synagogues. Teaching about morals, those subject to the active life. Preaching about things to come, those perfect in the contemplative life. Teaching the natural righteousnesses, namely chastity and humility and the like, which man has by nature. Preaching the Gospel of the kingdom. To the Gospel belongs 88Cthe mystery of righteousness, a good beginning, the promise of beatitude, the remission of sins, adoption, resurrection, the heavenly inheritance, the fellowship of the angels.

In the synagogues. Nor let an idle teacher run about to no purpose, but let him preach where there are many.

The Gospel. Because righteousness is a trouble to one living in the flesh, the labor of righteousness is not undertaken unless the wage of the work be looked for. Therefore Christ preaches beatitude for good news, that he may stir men up to patience in good work.

And healing. To cure sickness and infirmity was no great thing, since they were to die afterwards. But it was done for this reason, that they might so be raised up to the kingdom. Morally instructing preachers to minister earthly supports also to their subjects, and so to draw them to the kingdom.

VERS. 24.-- And his fame went abroad. (BEDA.) The works of salvation 88Ddo not shine out enough to hearers without the fame of a good odor, nor does fame profit without the work: without which, etc., as far as he cures bodies, that they may the more readily hear invisible things. Otherwise it was a small thing to spend care upon men soon to die.

VERS. 25.-- They followed. (AUG.) Fourfold crowds, namely. Some, etc., as far as others, wishing out of envy to accuse him and to catch him in something.

CAPUT V.

VERS. 1.-- Videns autem Jesus. (AUG.) Huic rei consentit Lucas dicens: Factum est autem in illis diebus, exiit in montem orare (Luc. VI), etc. Sed Lucas dicit, etc., usque ad non enim omnes omnia possunt: 89Aquod verius putatur quamvis idem utriusque simul possit videri factus.

Ascendit. Docens suos ascendere nec in turba remanere: altiorem docturus justitiam, quam illam quae est Scribarum et Pharisaeorum. Ascendit ipse mons in montem, ut altiora virtutum culmina doceret, et Ecclesiam supra quam sedet praedicans praeceptum Domini sublimius erigendam ostenderet quam eadem doctrina usque ad finem saeculi plenius erudiret. Apostolos praeeminentius abducit, ut a monte prius suscipiant montes pacem populo. Sedisset. Sessio humanitatis per quam cognoscitur. Unde: Et mundus eum non cognovit, ante incarnationem. Accesserunt. Ut vicinius audiant, qui ad implendum animo properant.

89BVERS. 2.-- Et aperiens. Apertio oris profunditatem significat sacramenti. Quasi abyssum, quasi thesaurum aperuit, quasi fluvium paradisi. In hoc monte Novum Testamentum in cordibus filiorum scribit, incipiens a beatitudine qui in monte Sinai Vetus Testamentum in lapidibus servis dedit incipiens a terrore.

(AUG.) Sermonem quem locutus est Dominus noster in monte, sicut in Evangelio, etc., usque ad, Omnis qui audit verba mea haec et faciat, assimilabo eum viro sapienti (Matth. VII).

VERS. 3.-- Beati. (GREG. NYSS.) Beatitudo, ut mea fert opinio, comprehensio est omnium earum rerum quae, etc., usque ad miseria enim est in acerbis et tristibus nostraque voluntate non occidentibus casibus 89Cet calamitatibus aerumna.

Beati pauperes. Sententiarum numerus diligenter est attendendus. Incipit enim beatitudo ab humilitate.

(HIER.) Beati pauperes spiritu. Quia non necessitas sed fides, et devotio paupertatis beatos facit, etc., usque ad sicut initium sapientiae timor Domini, sic paupertas est principium beatitudinis.

(CHRYSOST.) Pauperes spiritu sunt humiles et contriti corde. Spiritum autem hic animam et voluntatem dixit. Quoniam autem multi sunt humiles non spontanei, sed astricti necessitate, primos vocat beatos.

VERS. 4.-- Beati mites. Mites sunt qui malis moribus dominantur: perfecta virtus est morum possessio.

89D(THEOPH.) Mites sunt, non qui prorsus non irascuntur, etc., usque ad sic doctrina viri per patientiam dignoscitur.

Possidebunt terram. (AUG.) Illam terram ego credo de qua in Psalmo dicitur: Spes mea es tu, portio mea in terra viventium (Psal. CLXI).

VERS. 5.-- Beati qui lugent. Pro suis vel aliorum peccatis, qui est ab irriguo inferiori, sed qui fit ab irriguo superiori est ex desiderio coelestis patriae. Ille lavat praesentes sordes, hic accendit acrius aeternorum amatores. Qui autem peccata deposuit, mores mansuetudine correxit, flevit etiam, jam potest esurire et sitire justitiam, quod prius non poterat. Haec 90Avirtus sine spiritu fortitudinis non impletur: quia per eum esuriendo ad satietatem quandoque veniemus. Beati qui lugent. Restat post praedicta ruinarum colluvionem luctu purgare. Luctus pro peccatis vel pro desiderio coelesti consolationem meretur et non alius.

Hi scientiae spiritu illustrantur, ut sciant quibus malis involvantur.

(HILAR.) Lugentibus non orbitates, aut contumelias, aut damna moerentibus, sed peccata vetera flentibus, et criminum quibus obsordescimus conscientia aerumnosis, haec sedula in coelo consolatio praeparatur.

VERS. 6.-- Beati qui esuriunt. Amatores verbi boni, quibus non satis est quod justi sunt, sed semper sitiunt opus justitiae.

90BJustitia est sua cuique tribuere, sibi, et proximis, et Deo. Haec justitia non plene implebitur, donec Deus sit omnia in omnibus: ideo hic possumus esurire, non saturari. Justitiae lumen est misericordia, misericordiae virtus justitia; haec misericordia eget spiritu consilii, sine quo nemo circumspecte miseretur. Initium est sui misereri, finis est pro alio mori. Ordo enim est miserendi ut unusquisque incipiat a se. Finem Christus insinuat, pro peccatoribus mortuus ex misericordia.

VERS. 7.-- Beati misericordes. Misericordia nascitur de praecedentibus, quia si praecesserit vera humilitas et animus mansuescat, et suos et aliorum casus fleat, et justitiam esuriat, post nascitur vera misericordia. Tunc enim miserias alienas faciet suas 90Cet pro viribus juvabit: et si juvandi facultas deest, compassio non deerit: Estote misericordes sicut et Pater vester misericors est (Luc. VI), id est, ad hoc misereamini ad quod et Deus, ut bonitas redundet in omnibus: misericordia sine justitia remissio et tepiditas; justitia sine misericordia severitas et austeritas dicitur.

VERS. 9.-- Beati pacifici. (RAB.) Pacifici sunt qui omnes motus animi componunt et rationi subjiciunt, quia his nihil repugnat qui similes patri, etc., usque ad cum autem Deus erit omnia in omnibus, tunc beatitudo adoptionis dabitur.

VERS. 10.-- Beati qui persecutionem. In praedictis septem est perfectionis ostensio et probatio. Qui praedicta habent, pati possunt persecutionem: et 90Drecte tenet praedicta quae accepit qui pro eis paratus est mori.

VERS. 11.-- Beati estis cum maledixerint. Hucusque generaliter sententias posuit quasi ad absentes, cum et ad praesentes pertinerent, nunc ad eos verba convertit (quamvis et hoc aliis conveniat), praedicens quanta pro nomine ejus essent passuri: quia dignitatem gradus eorum eis intimare volebat dicens: Vos estis sal terrae, vos estis lux mundi; ut ex tristibus humilitatem, ex laetis acciperent consolationem.

Maledixerint. Antiqua translatio habet: Cum oderint vos homines, et dixerint omne malum, et expulerint. Idem tamen sensus in nostra, cum maledieta ex odio cordis fiant. Qui gloriam coeli optat, non 91Atimet opprobrium in terra; quantum quisque de laude hominum laetatur, tantum de opprobrio tristatur. Quem laus extollit, vituperatio deprimit: ubi quis quaerit laudari tantum apud Deum, non timet confundi in conspectu hominum. Mentientes. Hoc addit ne glorietur de quo vere mala dicuntur. Etiam addit: Propter me, quia aliquando falsa dicuntur, quod accedit temeritate hominum, non tamen propter Christum hoc patiuntur, quem non sequuntur.

VERS. 12.-- Quoniam merces vestra copiosa est in coelis. Non in volubilibus, cum non in rebus volubilibus merces aeterna esse debeat, sed in spiritualibus firmamentis, ubi habitat sempiterna justitia. Jam ergo sentiunt hanc mercedem qui spiritualibus bonis gaudent. Sed perficietur cum mortale hoc induet 91Bimmortalitatem. Sic enim persecuti, etc. Temperat exemplo rigorem passionis. Tribus modis fit persecutio: odio cordis, dictis, factis.

VERS. 13.-- Vos estis sal terrae. Hic jam ostenditur apostolorum officium, scilicet quod sunt sal terrae et lux mundi. Hoc specialiter ad apostolos, qui praedictis virtutibus, paupertate scilicet et aliis, debent ornari, ut sint sal optimum condientes alios doctrina, et vitae suae exemplo. Sal, salus a corruptione. Unde propheta: Posui te in lucem gentium, ut sis salus mea usque ad extrema terrae.

Quod si sal, etc. Id est, si vos, per quos alii condiendi sunt, adversis vel prosperis cesseritis: per quos a vobis error auferetur, cum vos Deus tollere 91Caliis elegerit? Ad nihilum valet: quia (ut alius evangelista ait) nec terrae utilis est quam suo injectu germinare prohibet, nec sterquilinio quod non fecundare sinit. Sic qui retro vadit, nec ipse fructum fert, nec alios valet excolere, sed ab Ecclesia ejicitur, et in haec verba ridetur: Hic homo coepit aedificare, et non potuit consummare. Si sal evanuerit (Luc. IV). Id est, si timore vel cupidine doctor saporem sapientiae omiserit, per quem fatuitas ejus emendabitur? Hoc solis apostolis convenit: Quod sequitur, omnibus magistris: Ad nihilum valet ultra, etc. In fine de omnibus concludit sic: Nisi abundaverit justitia, etc.

Et conculcetur ab hominibus. Sanctus et verus minister Christi, etsi a malis ridetur, conculcari non 91Dpotest, quia mente fixus in coelo manet.

VERS. 14.-- Vos estis lux mundi. Prius sal quam lux, quia prius vita quam doctrina: vita ducit ad scientiam veritatis. Qui Deum timet, scientia non caret.

Lux mundi. Alia causa est quare non debent deficere: quia illuminati a Christo verbo et exemplo: quocunque illati mundanis lucem fidei et scientiae debent ministrare. Et sicut (quia sunt sal) debent corruptionem pellere: sic et tenebras (quia lux sunt) fugare.

Non potest civitas, etc. Quia apostoli fundamenta sunt, apte civitas dicuntur. Supra montem. Locus montem justitiae innuit, qui excelsus est, quo propalamini 92Ane vos abscondatis. Non enim facultas subterfugiendi vel clam aliquid faciendi; ideo subditos misericorditer intus consilio regite, et extra contra adversarios pro justitia pugnando defendite: quod pertinet ad spiritum consilii et fortitudinis.

VERS. 15.-- Neque accendunt lucernam. Domesticis exemplis docet, ne celent lucem verbi sibi commissam. Non accendunt, id est, non ideo data est doctrina praedicationis ut celent eam et subjiciant servituti corporis, dum aliqua timent. Sed corpus suum ministerio Dei subjiciant, ut per vocem et caetera corporis officia lux excellentior caeteris appareat. Unde dicitur (I Cor.
