IX): Castigo corpus meum, etc. Vel, Christus lucernam accendit, dum testam humanae naturae flamma suae divinitatis implevit: 92Bquam nec credentibus abscondit, nec modio, id est mensura legis, vel terminis unius gentis inclusit. Sed super candelabrum, id est, Ecclesiam fidem suae incarnationis affixit, ubi omnes possint intueri, qui in domo, id est, Ecclesia sunt vel mundo. Unde subdit: Sic luceat, etc. Candelabrum. Sicut lucerna Christus, candelabrum crux est, super quam erat levandus. Candelabrum Ecclesia, quae verbum vitae praesentis bajulat.

VERS. 16.-- Sic luceat, etc. Et haec omnia ita agite, ut non finem boni operis in laudibus hominum constituatis. Ut videant, etc. Opera requiro, ut videantur, et sic doctrina confirmetur: et hoc non ad gloriam vestram.

VERS. 17.-- Nolite putare. Postquam hortatus est 92Comnia pati pro veritate, et non celare, et gloriam Dei facere, incipit docere quid docturi sint. Quasi diceret: Quid est quod vetas abscondi, pro quo jubes omnia pati? an est aliquid contra legem? Non veni solvere legem, sed adimplere, vel faciendo quod dicit, vel supplendo quod deerat et imperfectum erat.

VERS. 18.-- Iota. (BEDA.) Hic ostenditur, quod quae minima putantur in lege, sacramentis spiritualibus plena sunt, et omnia, etc., usque ad quia iota decem significat, id est decalogum, cujus apex est Evangelii perfectio.

Omnia fiant. Spiritualiter impleantur, vel imperfecta perficiantur, et significantia, id est impletione, consummentur.

92DVERS. 19.-- Qui ergo solverit, etc. Minima sunt crux Christi et passio, quam qui erubescit minimus est; qui confitetur magnus. Vel Pharisaeos notat, qui mandata Dei dimiserunt pro suis traditionibus, quae nihil valent, dum vel minimum, quod in lege est, praetereunt. Moraliter autem solvit qui non implet, sed tenet sicut infirmis data sunt. Vel qui non intelligit spiritualiter. Solvere, non est agere, quod recte intelligit, vel non intelligere quod non depravat, aut minuere integritatem superadditionis Christi.

Minimus vocabitur, etc. In regno coelorum minimus vocabitur qui erit in eo indignus, in quo nullus minimus sed omnes magni.

93AQui autem fecerit. Qui non solverit, et sic docet, non minimus, non tamen magnus: sed facit quod docet. Soli enim factores legis justi sunt apud Deum (Rom. II).

VERS. 20.-- Dico autem vobis. Cum ait, dico, magnam vim et profundam intelligentiam cogitandi incussit: ostendit quomodo superaddit. Et non quae prius mandaverat, absolvit. Plus etiam est diligere inimicos quam non odisse: non cupere, quam non adulterari: non irasci, quam non occidere.

Nisi, etc. Id est, non solum minima legis praecepta impleveritis, sed et ea quae addo: quia dum fiunt addita ad perfectionem, etiam haec plenius aguntur quae praemissa sunt ad inchoationem: 93Bquia qui non videt mulierem ad concupiscendum eam, nec fornicatur: nec occidit qui non irascitur.

Justitia vestra. Justitiam Pharisaeorum vocat omnia illa ad cultum Dei actualiter praecepta: seu figurarum mystica tantum ad litteram instituta.

VERS. 21.-- Audistis, etc. Exponit quae est justitia Pharisaeorum, quam qui adimplet non statim magnus, sed aliquem gradum conscendit.

VERS. 22.-- Ego autem, etc. Perfectionem quam venit implere incipit explanare, et quomodo justitia eorum debeat superabundare.

Omnis qui irascitur. Quia nesciebant homicidium esse nisi peremptionem: aperit omnem motum animi ad nocendum, in homicidium computari. Mandatum Christi non est contrarium legi, sed latius 93Clegem in se continens. Qui non irascitur, non occidit. Sed non econtra, licentia irascendi causa homicidii. Tolle iram, et homicidium non fit.

VERS. 23.-- Si ergo offers munus tuum. Explanat ampliorem justitiam ostendendo quod si non licet fratri irasci, multo minus in animo retinere quod in odium quaerat converti. Si quidquam mali contra proximum in animo habemus, Deo placite offerre non possumus. Si ergo offers. Secundum litteram hoc absurdum est. Est ergo in interiori templo altare, id est fides, cui quodlibet munus, id est, psalmus, hymnus, oratio, et hujusmodi, nisi sit innixum non est Deo placiturum. Et ibi, etc., id est, cum in corde (quod est templum interius) tale munus oblaturus es, si in mentem 93Dvenerit quod frater tuus habet aliquid adversum te, quem tu laesisti. Si enim ipse te laesit, non ipse, sed tu habes adversus eum: nec tunc oportet te veniam petere, sed dare sicut vis tibi dimitti.

VERS. 24.-- Relinque ibi, etc. Non ait refer: quia quod recte offertur immutari non licet. Sed relinque, donec placabilius offeras: non enim munus repellit, sed charitatem proximi quaerit, sine qua nemo ad charitatem Dei pervenit. Unde: Prius charitatem proximo exsolvat qui munus suum Deo placere optat. Vade. Non pedibus, sed animo humiliter ei prosternas in conspectu illius cui oblaturus es. Vel petendo veniam si praesens est. Offeres. Hoc ex spiritu pietatis quo mitis fit, et divinum verbum 94Apie intelligit, intelligendo obedit, et ad implendum cor erigit.

VERS. 25.-- Esto consentiens. Sicut jam praecepit fratri reconciliari et post jubet inimicos diligi, ita hic dum currimus in via hujus saeculi, ad pacem et concordiam hortatur, sicut dicitur: Si fieri potest quod ex vobis est cum omnibus hominibus pacem habentes (Rom. XII). Et pro consentiens ex Graeco habetur benignus vel benevolus. Cito, ne differas in crastinum: Dum es in via, dum adhuc licet operari.

Si fratrem laesisti, reconciliare ei. Si adversarius exstiterit, esto benevolus, ut fiat amicus.

In via cum eo. Sermo divinus, nobiscum est in Christi via. Adversarius nobiscum in via saeculi. 94BSermo divinus nobis via est qua currimus. Unde: Viam mandatorum tuorum cucurri, cum dilatasti cor meum (Psal. CXVIII).

Ne forte tradat. Sic temperavit, ut poenitentiae locum non afferret: putaretur enim, quod si obiisset adversarius et non posset ei in via esse benevolus, necessario judici traderetur. Sed et locum poenitentiae reliquit, et impoenitentem judici tradidit, dicens: Judex tradat te ministro. Tradat, id est, causa sit cur tradaris in manus Christi, cui Pater omne judicium dedit. Christus ministro, id est angelo, qui colliget zizania.

VERS. 26.-- Novissimum quadrantem. Id est, minuta peccata, quia nihil erit impunitum. Vel per quadrantem novissimum, significantur terrena peccata, 94Cquia prima pars mundi coelum; secunda, aer; tertia aqua; quarta, novissima terra. Ergo donec reddas novissimum quadrantem, id est, donec luas peccata quae de terra contraxisti.

VERS. 27.-- Audistis. (RAB.) Adhuc qui dixit: Non veni solvere legem, sed adimplere (Marc. XI). Addit: quantum debeat abundare, etc., usque ad quando anima relicta superiori lege, turpi voluntate inferiorum prostituta corrumpitur.

Viderit mulierem. (BEDA.) Magna superadditio. Visus ponitur pro omni motu qui est, etc., usque ad quia non secundum quod quisque potuit et non licuit, sed secundum quod voluit et proposuit ex conscientia accusatur vel defenditur.

Ad concupiscendum. Ut transeat in affectum cordis, 94Dut facere disponat, ubi non deest voluntas, sed occasio: quod passio dicitur, quae est mors in domo. Propassio est animi subitus affectus vel irae, vel amoris. Passio est animi forma vel deliberatio ex consensu. Unde dicitur aliquis iracundus vel amator.

(ISID.) Si contemplatio vertitur in taedium vel in arrogantiam, dimitte eam, et transi ad activam: quia melius est per activam salvari, quam per contemplativam perire.

VERS. 29.-- Erue eum. Ad litteram nullum membrum erui praecipitur, sed interior visus qui dexter, et exterior qui sinister dicitur, per studium pii laboris si nocet est revocandus vel removendus: et 95Aetiam longe faciendus, ut scilicet nec in memoria habeatur. Eruitur oculus cum frangitur malus usus. Projicitur, cum ex toto annihilatur, nec intrare permittitur.

VERS. 30.-- Dextera manus tua. Prospera operatio, vel aliquis adjutor strenuus intra vitae negotia: qui si scandalum est in lege Dei, melius est illo praeciso salvari, quam cum illo damnari. Abscinde eam, etc. Si quis hoc praedicat de membris, affectu pietatis non debet audiri. Sed ut improbitas morum et pravitas actionum inde resecetur, ut quidquid in oculo mentis de via justitiae et morum probitate nos subvertit, et quidquid contra operationem virtutum extra pulsat, procul pellatur. In gehennam. Nomen gehennae in veteribus non invenitur: sed prius a 95BDomino ponitur. Cujus nominis occasio est. Idolum Baal fuit juxta Jerusalem ad radicem montis Moria ubi Siloe fuit. Haec vallis irrigua fuit et nemorosa et plena deliciis, et locus in ea idolis consecratus: populus autem Israel adeo insanivit, ut relicto templo ibi immolarent, et filios suos incenderent daemoniis. Et dicebatur locus iste Gehennon, id est, vallis Ennon. Comminatur autem Deus se impleturum locum illum cadaveribus, ut vocetur polyandrum, id est, tumulus mortuorum: hujus ergo loci nomine futura supplicia designantur.

VERS. 31.-- Det illi. (AUG.) Scripsit Moyses in Deuteronomio, quod si uxor non placet viro propter aliquam foeditatem, dimittat eam. Quod Christus exponit, etc., usque ad sicut diligit inimicum, non in 95Choc quod inimicus et malus, sed quod homo ut secum sit salvus.

(CHRYSOST.) Permisit Moyses dari libellum repudii, ne propter odium funderetur sanguis. Permisit fieri mala, etc., usque ad quia malas hominum voluntates ad plenum prohibere non possumus.

VERS. 32.-- Causa fornicationis. Quia in hoc fides castitatis et connubii rumpitur: et ille non facit eam moechari, sed ipsa causa est reatus sui. Si autem pro fornicatione dimittit, a fornicatione debet esse immunis, nec aliam ducere licet dum illa vivit. Fornicationis causa. Feminae, quae fornicata est, vel viri ne fornicetur, scilicet si uxor cogat ad idololatriam, vel ad avaritiam, vel ad alias illicitas concupiscentias. Non enim fornicatio est tantum stupri, 95Dsed generaliter quae a lege Dei aberrare facit. Et qui, etc. (CHRYSOST.) Non dicas quoniam vir suus eam dimisit, quia etiam postquam dimissa est, remanet dimittentis uxor.

VERS. 33.-- Reddes autem. Si jurare contigerit: per Creatorem jurabis: non per creaturam. Hoc enim per quod jurat quilibet veneratur: hoc amat, hoc timet. Ideo lex praecipit ut non juretur nisi per Deum.

Ego autem, etc. Docet nil tam vile in creaturis quod quis pejerare debeat, dum a summo usque ad imum, juramentum vetat, id est, a coelo usque ad capillum. In Deum pejerare convincitur, cujus thronus est coelum, et cujus est quidquid est.

96APer coelum. Illi vel coelum plus aliquid suspicabantur, vel per illud jurantes non putabant se juramento teneri: ideo sententiam sic temperat, ut et coelum creatura Dei credatur, non plus aliquid: et quia thronus ejus est perjurium timeatur. Sic et de terra, quia scabellum pedum ejus. Qui ergo per creaturam jurat, illi debet juramentum qui eam creavit et regit, nec alii recte putatur reddi.

Non juremus per creaturas ut veneremur eas ultra quam debemus per eas, ut viles existimemus, ut scilicet cum per eas juremus nihil jurare putemus.

Neque per terram. Alii per salutem Pharaonis, qui Dei judicio positus est in infimis. Non est potestas nisi a Deo (Rom. XIII): et in hoc Deum juras.

96BNeque, etc. Quod non est tuum opus, sed Dei: et ita in minimis Deum jurando perjurium incurrere potes.

Qui per caput jurat, illi debet cujus figura et potestas in eo manet.

VERS. 36.-- Quia non potes, etc. Haec praedicta cum sint ardua et difficilia, nemo sine spiritu fortitudinis servat. Compellit ad perfectionem, dum tollit jurandi occasionem.

VERS. 37.-- Est, est; non, non. Ideo bis dicit, ut quod ore dicis, operibus probes. Quod verbis negas, factis non comprobes vel confirmes.

Non penitus jurare prohibuit, sed occasione perjurii. Quod perfectius est docuit ostendens quod ulterius est a malo esse. Apostolus jurat, ut fidem 96Cpersuadeat: Ecclesia etiam pro foedere pacis ac fidei suos jurare concedit. Sed Christus quod perfectius est docuit, quod infirmitatis indulsit, quod superstitiosum resecavit.

Veritas Evangelii juramentum non recipit, sed alterius infirmitas id cogit, dum veritati non credit.

A malo est. Non quidem malum, quia etsi non bonum, tamen necessarium, sed a malo est, non tuo sed illius a quo cogeris jurare, id est, ab infirmitate illius quae malum est.

VERS. 38.-- Audistis. (AUG.) Lex modum ultionis instituit: oculum pro oculo. Quod si dici potest, etc., usque ad sed pro dilectione justitiae, justum pro injusto, quod est bonum pro malo, quod etiam judex facit.

96DQuod dicit, oculum pro oculo, dentem pro dente: non alterum vult tollere, sed utrumque conservare, dum minatur talionem. Quam etiam cum modo facit, ut injuriam vindicta non transeat: et haec est pacis inchoatio, et quasi justitiae incoeptio.

VERS. 39.-- Non resistere. Justitia legis rudes instruit in initio justitiae, id est, non plus quam est illatum reddere. Justitia Evangelii (quae ducit homines ad regnum) est non resistere malo: Non resistere: ut ipse idem: Discite a me, quia mitis sum, et humilis corde (Matth. XI). Et David: Si reddidi retribuentibus mihi mala (Psal. VII). Jerem. Et maxillam dedi percutienti (Thren. III). Ecce idem Dominus legis et Evangelii.

97APraebe illi. Non tantum non repercutias, sed si vult alteram ferire, patienter feras: hoc de injuria corporis. De necessariis autem si perdideris unum, sine tolli et reliquum. Quid ergo de superfluis?

Alteram. (CHRYSOST.) Primum indigna res est ut nomo fidelis stet in judicio ante conspectum judicis infidelis. Nam et si infidelis, etc., usque ad Si semel in judicium ingressus fueris, non jam desideras ut veritas causae appareat, sed ut quocunque modo victor exsistas.

VERS. 40.-- Et ei qui, etc. Consilium est antequam contendas. Licet quidem infirmis sua repetere, sed non contendere. Lucas econverso: Qui aufert vestem, etiam tunicam noli prohibere. Quod non de his solis, sed de omnibus temporalibus faciendum est. Tunicam 97Btuam. Vel, tunica est anima, quam si quis perdere noluerit, dimittat pallium, id est, corpus ad martyrium. Spiritualiter, doctor tunicam tollit, cum interiora vitia facit confiteri, ut est invidia, superbia, et hujusmodi. Ille addit: Et pallium; qui et exteriora, ut furtum et fornicationem, non erubescit confiteri.

VERS. 41.-- Mille passus. Hoc est iter sabbati, quo designatur perfectio spiritualis: quia non licet in sabbato servile opus facere. Alia duo. Vadit et alia duo qui dum corpore tantum in servitutem redigitur, spiritu et anima, id est ratione et voluntate, redigi est paratus.

VERS. 42.-- Da ei. Quae honeste et juste possunt dari. Si deest facultas, da affectum verbo vel obsequio. 97CDa ei. Ita, scilicet, ut nec tibi noceat, nec alii: pensanda est enim justitia. Ita enim omni petenti dabis, et si non id quod petit, sed melius cum injuste petentem correxeris. Et volenti. Duo genera praestandi complectitur. Vel enim benevole damus, vel reddituro commodamus. Et quandoque qui donat, non vult commodare, quasi nihil mercedis in eo sit cum rem acceptam debitor persolvit. Sed vere ille qui etiam hoc jubet, retribuit. Dicit ergo: Ne avertaris, id est, nec alienes voluntatem tuam. Hilarem enim datorem diligit Deus (II Cor. IX), sic et spiritualis doctor verbi divini foenus auditoribus debet accommodare, ut boni operis retributionem ab illis possit accipere, et a Deo mercedem sperare.

VERS. 43.-- Audistis. Qui legem implere venit, in 97Dfine ad charitatem plenam perducit, quae est impletio legis.

Odio habebis. Forsitan intelligendum est juxta hoc quod supra: Oculum pro oculo, etc. Vel subtrahendo officia humanitatis.

VERS. 44.-- Diligite. Contra Ecclesiam pugnatur tribus modis: odio, verbis, cruciatu corporis. Ecclesia econtra diligit, benefacit, orat. Hoc est novum mandatum, hoc de filiis irae facit filios Dei; unde sequitur: Ut sitis fiili. Adoptio filiorum sola charitate acquiritur.

Benefacite. Ministrando cibum mentis vel corporis. Quod facit etiam qui dicit eum hujusmodi nec cibum sumere. Omnia enim ea spectant, ut fratres fiant. 98ASingulis singula opponuntur, ut omnes partes malitiae bonitate vincantur. Videntur contra esse imprecationes prophetarum quae non dicuntur voto optantium, sed spiritu praevidentium.

Orate. (AUG.) Non ait hoc pro fratribus in quibus sunt aliquando peccata persecutione inimicorum graviora, etc., usque ad cum etsi peccasse fateantur, et fecisse mala vehementer eos poeniteat, tamen hujusmodi affectiones mentis habent ut se non humilient ad petendam veniam.

Movere autem potest, quod Joannes in Epistola de dilectione Dei et proximi tantum monere videtur, ut fratres diligamus, cum Dominus dicat et pro inimicis orandum. Sed fratres intelliguntur non solum qui jam credunt, sed qui credituri sunt, pro quibus 98Borandum est, ut fraterno foedere nobis socientur.

VERS. 45.-- Filii. Non degeneres. Quasi diceret: Accepta potestate operibus agite ut sitis filii renascendo per charitatem. Patris. Jam dicitur pater. Videte ut sitis filii renati ex Deo qui est charitas. Qui solem. Hunc visibilem. Nam inquiunt in libro Salomonis, Sol justitiae non illuxit nobis (Sap. V). Et de pluvia doctrinae propheta dicit: Super unam civitatem pluit, et non super alteram. Solem suum (Amos. IV), etc. Quasi dicat: Quem fecit, cujus proprie sunt omnia quae de nihilo creavit. Quid ergo nos faciemus de his quae de munere ejus accepimus?

VERS. 46.-- Publicani. Exactores Romanorum, quos Judaei sic vocant, quia de republica curam gerebant. Dicuntur et nomen traxisse a Publio Romano 98Crege, qui prius eos ordinavit.

VERS. 47.-- Et si salutaveritis. Si pro his tantum oraveritis, qui aliqua affinitate vobis sint conjuncti, quid amplius habet beneficium vestrum quam infidelium? Oportet fratres diligere, et humiliter salutare: sed parum est.

VERS. 48.-- Perfecti. In charitate Dei et proximi. Cumulus perfectionis diligere etiam inimicos, et orare pro illis, sicut et Christus fecit.

CHAPTER V.

VERS. 1.-- And Jesus seeing. (AUG.) With this Luke agrees, saying: And it came to pass in those days, he went out into a mountain to pray (Luc. VI), etc. But Luke says, etc., as far as for not all men can do all things: 89Awhich is thought the truer, although the same [sermon] of both may be seen to have been delivered at once.

He went up. Teaching his own to go up and not to remain in the crowd: being about to teach a higher righteousness than that which is of the Scribes and the Pharisees. He himself, a mountain, went up into a mountain, that he might teach the higher summits of the virtues, and might show that the Church, above which he sits preaching, is to be raised more loftily by the Lord's precept, which the same teaching would more fully instruct unto the end of the world. He leads the apostles apart more eminently, that from the mountain the mountains may first receive peace for the people. He was set down. The sitting is of the humanity, by which he is known. Whence: And the world knew him not, before the incarnation. They came. That they may hear the nearer, who hasten in mind to fulfil.

89BVERS. 2.-- And opening. The opening of the mouth signifies the depth of the sacrament. He opened as it were an abyss, as it were a treasure, as it were a river of paradise. On this mountain he writes the New Testament in the hearts of sons, beginning from beatitude, who on mount Sinai gave the Old Testament on stones to servants, beginning from terror.

(AUG.) The sermon which our Lord spoke on the mountain, as in the Gospel, etc., as far as, Everyone who hears these my words and shall do them, I will liken him to a wise man (Matth. VII). [Vulg. Mt 7:24 Omnis ergo qui audit verba mea haec, et facit ea, assimilabitur viro sapienti]

VERS. 3.-- Blessed. (GREG. NYSS.) Beatitude, as my opinion carries it, is the comprehension of all those things which, etc., as far as for misery is a wretchedness in bitter and sorrowful chances 89Cand calamities that do not fall out by our own will.

Blessed are the poor. The number of the sentences is to be diligently attended to. For beatitude begins from humility.

(HIER.) Blessed are the poor in spirit. Because it is not necessity but faith, and the devotion of poverty, that makes men blessed, etc., as far as as the fear of the Lord is the beginning of wisdom, so poverty is the beginning of beatitude.

(CHRYSOST.) The poor in spirit are the humble and contrite of heart. And by spirit he here means the soul and the will. But because many are humble not of their own accord, but constrained by necessity, he calls the first sort blessed.

VERS. 4.-- Blessed are the meek. The meek are those who master evil habits: perfect virtue is the possession of one's habits.

89D(THEOPH.) The meek are not those who are not angry at all, etc., as far as so the doctrine of a man is discerned through patience.

They shall possess the land. (AUG.) That land I believe to be the one of which it is said in the Psalm: Thou art my hope, my portion in the land of the living (Psal. CLXI).

VERS. 5.-- Blessed are they that mourn. For their own sins or for others', which mourning is from the lower watering, but that which comes from the upper watering is out of desire for the heavenly country. The one washes present filth, the other kindles more sharply the lovers of eternal things. But he who has laid down his sins, has corrected his habits by meekness, and has wept besides, can now hunger and thirst after righteousness, which before he could not. This 90Avirtue is not fulfilled without the spirit of fortitude: because through him, by hungering, we shall one day come to fulness. Blessed are they that mourn. It remains, after the things aforesaid, to purge by mourning the foulness of our ruins. Mourning for sins or out of heavenly desire earns consolation, and no other does.

These are enlightened by the spirit of knowledge, that they may know in what evils they are wrapped.

(HILAR.) For those that mourn, not for those grieving over bereavements, or insults, or losses, but for those weeping over their old sins, and made wretched by the consciousness of the crimes with which we are befouled, this assiduous consolation is prepared in heaven.

VERS. 6.-- Blessed are they that hunger. Lovers of the good word, to whom it is not enough that they are just, but they always thirst for the work of righteousness.

90BRighteousness is to render to each his own, to oneself, and to one's neighbors, and to God. This righteousness will not be fully fulfilled until God be all in all: therefore here we can hunger, not be filled. The light of righteousness is mercy, the strength of mercy is righteousness; this mercy has need of the spirit of counsel, without which no one is merciful with circumspection. The beginning is to have mercy on oneself, the end is to die for another. For the order of showing mercy is that each man begin from himself. Christ hints at the end, having died out of mercy for sinners.

VERS. 7.-- Blessed are the merciful. Mercy is born of the things that go before, because if true humility has gone before, and the mind grows meek, and weeps for its own falls and others', and hungers after righteousness, afterwards true mercy is born. For then it will make others' miseries its own 90Cand will help according to its powers: and if the means of helping is wanting, compassion will not be wanting: Be ye merciful as your Father also is merciful (Luc. VI), that is, let us have mercy unto that end unto which God also has mercy, that goodness may overflow upon all: mercy without righteousness is called remission and lukewarmness; righteousness without mercy is called severity and harshness.

VERS. 9.-- Blessed are the peacemakers. (RAB.) The peacemakers are those who compose all the motions of the mind and subject them to reason, because nothing resists those who are like the Father, etc., as far as but when God shall be all in all, then the beatitude of adoption will be given.

VERS. 10.-- Blessed are they that suffer persecution. In the seven aforesaid there is the showing and the proving of perfection. Those who have the aforesaid things can suffer persecution: and 90Dhe rightly holds the aforesaid things which he has received who is ready to die for them.

VERS. 11.-- Blessed are you when they shall revile you. Thus far he set down his sentences generally, as though to those absent, although they belonged to those present also, now he turns his words to them (although this belongs to others also), foretelling how great things they should suffer for his name: because he wished to make known to them the dignity of their rank, saying: You are the salt of the earth, you are the light of the world; that from the sad things they might take humility, from the glad things consolation.

Shall revile. The ancient translation has: When men shall hate you, and shall say all that is evil, and shall cast you out. Yet the sense is the same in ours, since revilings come from hatred of the heart. He who chooses the glory of heaven does not 91Afear reproach on earth; as much as each man rejoices at the praise of men, so much does he grieve at reproach. Him whom praise lifts up, blame casts down: where a man seeks to be praised only before God, he does not fear to be confounded in the sight of men. Lying. He adds this, lest he glory of whom evil things are truly said. He adds also: For my sake, because sometimes false things are said, which comes about through the rashness of men, yet they do not suffer this for Christ's sake, whom they do not follow.

VERS. 12.-- For your reward is very great in heaven. Not in rolling things, since an eternal reward ought not to be in rolling things, but in spiritual firmaments, where everlasting righteousness dwells. Already therefore they feel this reward who rejoice in spiritual goods. But it will be made perfect when this mortal shall put on 91Bimmortality. For so they persecuted, etc. He tempers the rigor of the passion by an example. Persecution is made in three ways: by hatred of the heart, by words, by deeds.

VERS. 13.-- You are the salt of the earth. Here now is shown the office of the apostles, namely that they are the salt of the earth and the light of the world. This belongs especially to the apostles, who ought to be adorned with the aforesaid virtues, namely poverty and the rest, that they may be the best salt, seasoning others by doctrine and by the example of their own life. Salt, health from corruption. Whence the prophet: I have set thee for a light of the Gentiles, that thou mayest be my salvation unto the ends of the earth. [Vulg. Is 49:6 dedi te in lucem gentium, ut sis salus mea usque ad extremum terrae; Act 13:47 has Posui te in lucem gentium, ut sis in salutem]

But if the salt, etc. That is, if you, by whom others are to be seasoned, give way before adverse or prosperous things: by whom shall error be taken away from you, when God has chosen 91Cto raise you up for others? It is good for nothing: because (as another evangelist says) it is useful neither to the earth, which by being cast upon it it hinders from sprouting, nor to the dunghill, which it does not suffer to grow fruitful. So he who goes backward neither bears fruit himself, nor is able to till others, but is cast out of the Church, and is laughed at in these words: This man began to build, and was not able to finish. If the salt shall lose its savor (Luc. IV). That is, if through fear or through greed the teacher has let go the savor of wisdom, by whom shall his foolishness be amended? This befits the apostles alone: what follows befits all masters: It is good for nothing any more, etc. At the end he concludes thus concerning all: Unless your righteousness abound, etc.

And be trodden upon by men. The holy and true minister of Christ, even if he is laughed at by the wicked, cannot 91Dbe trodden upon, because fixed in mind he remains in heaven.

VERS. 14.-- You are the light of the world. Salt before light, because life before doctrine: life leads to the knowledge of truth. He who fears God does not lack knowledge.

The light of the world. There is another cause why they ought not to fail: because they are enlightened by Christ in word and in example: wherever they are carried among worldly men they ought to minister the light of faith and of knowledge. And as (because they are salt) they ought to drive away corruption: so also they ought to put darkness (because they are light) to flight.

A city cannot, etc. Because the apostles are foundations, they are fitly called a city. Upon a mountain. The place hints at the mountain of righteousness, which is lofty, whereby you are made public 92Aso that you may not hide yourselves. For there is no power of slipping away or of doing anything in secret; therefore rule your subjects mercifully within by counsel, and without against adversaries defend them by fighting for righteousness: which belongs to the spirit of counsel and of fortitude.

VERS. 15.-- Neither do they light a lamp. By household examples he teaches them not to hide the light of the word committed to them. They do not light, that is, the doctrine of preaching was not given for this, that they should hide it and subject it to the servitude of the body, while they fear certain things. But let them subject their body to the ministry of God, that through the voice and the other offices of the body the light may appear more excellent than the rest. Whence it is said (I Cor. IX): I chastise my body, etc. Or, Christ lit a lamp, when he filled the earthen shell of human nature with the flame of his divinity: 92Bwhich he neither hid from believers, nor shut up under a bushel, that is, the measure of the law, or within the bounds of a single nation. But upon the candlestick, that is, the Church, he fastened the faith of his incarnation, where all can behold it who are in the house, that is, in the Church, or in the world. Whence he adds: So let your light shine, etc. A candlestick. As Christ is the lamp, the candlestick is the cross, upon which he was to be lifted up. The candlestick is the Church, which bears the word of the present life.

VERS. 16.-- So let your light shine, etc. And do all these things in such a way that you do not set the end of your good work in the praises of men. That they may see, etc. I require works, that they may be seen, and that so the doctrine may be confirmed: and this not to your own glory.

VERS. 17.-- Do not think. After he had exhorted them 92Cto suffer all things for the truth, and not to hide it, and to work the glory of God, he begins to teach what they are to teach. As if he were saying: What is this that thou forbiddest to be hidden, for which thou biddest all things to be suffered? is there anything against the law? I am not come to destroy the law, but to fulfil, [Vulg. Mt 5:17 non veni solvere, sed adimplere, without legem in the second clause] either by doing what it says, or by supplying what was lacking and imperfect.

VERS. 18.-- One jot. (BEDA.) Here it is shown that the things which are thought least in the law are full of spiritual sacraments, and all things, etc., as far as because the jot signifies ten, that is, the decalogue, whose apex is the perfection of the Gospel.

All things be fulfilled. Let them be spiritually fulfilled, or let the imperfect be perfected, and let the things that signify be consummated, that is, by the fulfilment.

92DVERS. 19.-- He therefore that shall break, etc. The least things are the cross of Christ and his passion, and he who is ashamed of them is least; he who confesses them is great. Or he marks the Pharisees, who let go the commandments of God for their own traditions, which are worth nothing, while they pass over even the least thing that is in the law. But morally he breaks it who does not fulfil it, but holds it as these things were given to the weak. Or he who does not understand it spiritually. To break, is not to do what one rightly understands, or not to understand what one does not distort, or to lessen the wholeness of Christ's superadding.

He shall be called the least, etc. In the kingdom of heaven he shall be called least who will be unworthy in it, in which there is none least but all are great.

93ABut he that shall do. He who shall not break it, and so teaches, is not least, yet not great: but he does what he teaches. For only the doers of the law are just before God (Rom. II). [Vulg. Rom 2:13 Non enim auditores legis justi sunt apud Deum, sed factores legis justificabuntur]

VERS. 20.-- But I say to you. [Vulg. Mt 5:20 Dico enim vobis] When he says, I say, he struck in a great force and a deep understanding for thinking: he shows how he superadds. And he does not undo the things he had commanded before. It is even more to love enemies than not to hate them: not to covet, than not to commit adultery: not to be angry, than not to kill.

Unless, etc. That is, you shall fulfil not only the least precepts of the law, but also those which I add: because while the things added are done unto perfection, these too are more fully performed which were set down before unto a beginning: 93Bbecause he who does not look upon a woman to lust after her, neither does he commit fornication: nor does he kill who is not angry.

Your righteousness. He calls the righteousness of the Pharisees all those things actually commanded for the worship of God: or the mystical things of the figures, established only according to the letter.

VERS. 21.-- You have heard, etc. He sets forth what the righteousness of the Pharisees is, and he who fulfils it is not at once great, but has climbed some step.

VERS. 22.-- But I, etc. He begins to unfold the perfection which he came to fulfil, and how their righteousness ought to abound the more.

Everyone who is angry. Because they did not know that anything was murder except a killing: he discloses that every motion of the mind toward doing harm is reckoned as murder. The commandment of Christ is not contrary to the law, but more broadly 93Ccontains the law in itself. He who is not angry does not kill. But not conversely, the leave to be angry is the cause of murder. Take away anger, and murder is not committed.

VERS. 23.-- If therefore thou offer thy gift. He unfolds the fuller righteousness by showing that if it is not lawful to be angry with a brother, much less is it lawful to keep in the mind that which seeks to be turned into hatred. If we have anything of evil in the mind against a neighbor, we cannot offer to God in a way that pleases him. If therefore thou offer. According to the letter this is absurd. There is therefore in the inner temple an altar, that is, faith, upon which any gift whatsoever, that is, a psalm, a hymn, a prayer, and the like, unless it rest, will not be pleasing to God. And there, etc., that is, when in the heart (which is the inner temple) thou art about to offer such a gift, if it should come into thy mind 93Dthat thy brother has something against thee, whom thou hast wronged. For if he has wronged thee, it is not he, but thou who hast something against him: nor is it needful for thee then to seek pardon, but to give it, as thou wishest to be forgiven thyself.

VERS. 24.-- Leave there, etc. He does not say, Carry it back: because what is rightly offered may not be changed. But leave it, until thou mayest offer it more acceptably: for he does not drive back the gift, but seeks charity toward one's neighbor, without which no one comes to the charity of God. Whence: Let him first pay charity to his neighbor who desires his gift to please God. Go. Not with the feet, but in mind mayest thou humbly prostrate thyself before him in the sight of him to whom thou art about to offer. Or by seeking pardon if he is present. Thou shalt offer. This is from the spirit of piety, whereby a man becomes meek, and understands the divine word 94Apiously, and by understanding obeys, and lifts up his heart to fulfil it.

VERS. 25.-- Be at agreement. As he has already commanded a man to be reconciled to his brother, and afterwards bids that enemies be loved, so here, while we run in the way of this world, he exhorts to peace and concord, as it is said: If it be possible, as much as is in you, having peace with all men (Rom. XII). And in place of "consenting" the Greek has "kindly" or "well-disposed." Quickly, lest thou put it off till the morrow: While thou art in the way, while it is still lawful to work.

If thou hast wronged a brother, be reconciled to him. If an adversary has arisen, be well-disposed, that he may become a friend.

In the way with him. The divine word is with us in the way of Christ. The adversary is with us in the way of the world. 94BThe divine word is to us the way in which we run. Whence: I have run the way of thy commandments, when thou didst enlarge my heart (Psal. CXVIII).

Lest perhaps he deliver thee up. He so tempered it, that it should not bring in a place of repentance: for it might be thought that if the adversary had died and one could not be well-disposed to him in the way, he would necessarily be delivered up to the judge. But he both left a place of repentance, and delivered the impenitent up to the judge, saying: Let the judge deliver thee to the officer. Let him deliver thee up, that is, let him be the cause why thou art delivered into the hands of Christ, to whom the Father has given all judgment. Christ to the officer, that is, to the angel, who shall gather the cockle.

VERS. 26.-- The last farthing. That is, small sins, because nothing will be unpunished. Or by the last farthing are signified earthly sins, 94Cbecause the first part of the world is heaven; the second, air; the third water; the fourth, and last, earth. Therefore until thou repay the last farthing, that is, until thou pay for the sins which thou hast contracted from the earth.

VERS. 27.-- You have heard. (RAB.) Still he who said: I am not come to destroy the law, but to fulfil (Marc. XI). He adds: how much it ought to abound, etc., as far as when the soul, having left the higher law, is corrupted, prostituted by a base will for lower things.

Shall look upon a woman. (BEDA.) A great superadding. Sight is put for every motion which is, etc., as far as because a man is accused or defended out of his conscience not according to what he was able to do and was not permitted, but according to what he willed and purposed.

To lust after her. That it may pass over into the affection of the heart, 94Dso that he sets himself to do it, where the will is not wanting, but the occasion: which is called passion, which is death in the house. Pro-passion is a sudden affection of the mind, either of anger or of love. Passion is a form or deliberation of the mind out of consent. Whence a man is called wrathful or a lover.

(ISID.) If contemplation turns into weariness or into arrogance, let it go, and pass over to the active life: because it is better to be saved through the active than to perish through the contemplative.

VERS. 29.-- Pluck it out. According to the letter no member is commanded to be plucked out, but the inner sight, which is called the right, and the outer, which is called the left, if it hurts, is to be called back or removed by the zeal of dutiful labor: and 95Aeven to be put far away, so that it may not so much as be held in memory. The eye is plucked out when an evil habit is broken. It is cast away when it is wholly brought to nothing, nor is it suffered to enter.

VERS. 30.-- Thy right hand. Prosperous working, or some vigorous helper within the business of life: and if he is a scandal in the law of God, it is better to be saved with him cut off than to be damned along with him. Cut it off, etc. If anyone preaches this of the bodily members, he ought not to be listened to out of any affection of dutifulness. But that from thence the wickedness of habits and the crookedness of actions may be cut away, so that whatever in the eye of the mind overturns us from the way of righteousness and from uprightness of habits, and whatever beats without against the working of the virtues, may be driven far off. Into hell. The name of gehenna is not found among the ancients: but is first set down by the 95BLord. The occasion of that name is this. The idol of Baal was beside Jerusalem at the foot of mount Moria, where Siloe was. This valley was well-watered and wooded and full of delights, and a place in it was consecrated to idols: but the people of Israel went so far in madness that, leaving the temple, they sacrificed there, and burned their own sons to demons. And that place was called Gehennon, that is, the valley of Ennon. And God threatens that he will fill that place with corpses, so that it may be called polyandrum, that is, a burial-mound of the dead: by the name of this place therefore the punishments to come are designated.

VERS. 31.-- Let him give her. (AUG.) [Vulg. Mt 5:31 det ei libellum repudii] Moses wrote in Deuteronomy that if a wife does not please her husband on account of some foulness, he should put her away. Which Christ expounds, etc., as far as as he loves an enemy, not in 95Cthis, that he is an enemy and evil, but that he is a man, so that he may be saved along with him.

(CHRYSOST.) Moses permitted a bill of divorce to be given, lest for hatred's sake blood should be shed. He permitted evils to be done, etc., as far as because we cannot forbid to the full the evil wills of men.

VERS. 32.-- By reason of fornication. Because in this the faith of chastity and of wedlock is broken: and he does not make her commit adultery, but she herself is the cause of her own guilt. But if he puts her away for fornication, he ought to be free from fornication himself, nor is it lawful to marry another while she lives. By reason of fornication. Of the woman, who has committed fornication, or of the man, lest he commit fornication, namely if the wife compels him to idolatry, or to avarice, or to other unlawful lusts. For fornication is not only of defilement, 95Dbut generally that which makes a man stray from the law of God. And he that, etc. (CHRYSOST.) Do not say that her husband has put her away, because even after she is put away, she remains the wife of him who put her away.

VERS. 33.-- But thou shalt render. If it should happen that thou swear: thou shalt swear by the Creator: not by a creature. For each man venerates that by which he swears: this he loves, this he fears. Therefore the law commands that no oath be sworn save by God.

But I, etc. He teaches that there is nothing so cheap among creatures that a man ought to forswear himself by it, since he forbids the oath from the highest to the lowest, that is, from heaven to a hair. He is convicted of forswearing himself against God, whose throne is heaven, and whose is whatever is.

96ABy heaven. They either supposed heaven to be something more, or, swearing by it, did not think themselves held by an oath: therefore he so tempers the sentence, that heaven may be believed to be a creature of God, and not something more: and that, because it is his throne, perjury may be feared. So also concerning the earth, because it is the footstool of his feet. He therefore who swears by a creature owes the oath to him who created and rules it, nor is it rightly thought to be paid to another.

Let us not swear by creatures so as to venerate them beyond what we ought by them, so as to esteem them cheap, namely so that when we swear by them we should think we swear by nothing.

Nor by the earth. Others swear by the safety of Pharao, who by the judgment of God is set among the lowest. There is no power but from God (Rom. XIII): and in this thou swearest by God.

96BNor, etc. Because it is not thy work, but God's: and so in the least things, by swearing by God, thou canst incur perjury.

He who swears by his head owes it to him whose image and power abides in it.

VERS. 36.-- For thou canst not, etc. Since these things aforesaid are hard and difficult, no one keeps them without the spirit of fortitude. He drives men to perfection, while he takes away the occasion of swearing.

VERS. 37.-- Yea, yea; no, no. He says it twice for this reason, that what thou sayest with the mouth thou mayest prove by works. What thou deniest in words, thou mayest not approve or confirm by deeds.

He did not altogether forbid swearing, but the occasion of perjury. He taught what is more perfect, showing that what goes beyond is of evil. The Apostle swears, that he may persuade 96Cto faith: the Church too, for the sake of a covenant of peace and of faith, grants her own to swear. But Christ taught what is more perfect, indulged what belonged to weakness, cut away what was superstitious.

The truth of the Gospel does not admit an oath, but another's weakness compels it, so long as he does not believe the truth.

It is of evil. Not indeed evil, because although not good, yet necessary, but it is of evil, not of thine, but of his by whom thou art compelled to swear, that is, of that weakness of his which is an evil.

VERS. 38.-- You have heard. (AUG.) The law appointed a measure of vengeance: an eye for an eye. But if it can be said, etc., as far as but out of love of justice, the just for the unjust, which is good for evil, which the judge also does.

96DAs to what he says, an eye for an eye, a tooth for a tooth: he does not wish to take away the one, but to preserve both, while he threatens retaliation. Which he also does with measure, that the vengeance may not exceed the injury: and this is the beginning of peace, and as it were the commencement of justice.

VERS. 39.-- Not to resist. The justice of the law instructs the untaught in the beginning of justice, that is, to repay no more than was inflicted. The justice of the Gospel (which leads men to the kingdom) is not to resist evil: Not to resist: as he himself likewise: Learn of me, because I am meek and humble of heart (Matth. XI). And David: If I have repaid them that repaid me evils (Psal. VII). Jerem. And I have given my cheek to him that struck me (Thren. III) [Vulg. Lam 3:30 Dabit percutienti se maxillam, "He shall give his cheek to him that striketh him"]. Behold, the same Lord of the law and of the Gospel.

97AOffer to him. Not only shouldst thou not strike back, but if he wishes to strike the other cheek, bear it patiently: this concerning injury to the body. But as to necessary things, if thou hast lost one, allow the remainder also to be taken away. What then of things superfluous?

The other. (CHRYSOST.) First, it is an unworthy thing that a faithful man nomo should stand in judgment before the sight of an unbelieving judge. For even if he be an unbeliever, etc., as far as If once thou hast entered into judgment, thou dost no longer desire that the truth of the cause should appear, but that in whatever way thou mayest come off victor.

VERS. 40.-- And to him who, etc. It is counsel, before thou contend. It is indeed lawful for the weak to demand back their own, but not to contend. Luke conversely: He that taketh away thy garment, forbid not thy coat also. Which is to be done not concerning these things alone, but concerning all temporal things. Thy 97Bcoat. Or, the coat is the soul, which if a man be unwilling to lose, let him give up his cloak, that is, his body to martyrdom. Spiritually, the teacher takes away the coat when he makes a man confess his inward vices, such as envy, pride, and the like. He adds: And the cloak; he who does not blush to confess the outward ones also, such as theft and fornication.

VERS. 41.-- A thousand paces. This is a sabbath day's journey, by which spiritual perfection is signified: because it is not lawful to do servile work on the sabbath. Other two. He goes other two also who, while he is reduced to servitude in body only, is ready to be reduced in spirit and soul, that is, in reason and in will.

VERS. 42.-- Give to him. Such things as can be given honourably and justly. If the means be wanting, give the affection by a word or by a service. 97CGive to him. So, namely, that it hurt neither thee nor another: for justice is to be weighed. For thus wilt thou give to everyone that asketh, and if not that which he asks, yet something better, when thou shalt have corrected him that asks unjustly. And to him that would borrow. He embraces two kinds of bestowing. For either we give of good will, or we lend to one who will repay. And sometimes he who gives is unwilling to lend, as though there were no reward in it when the debtor pays back the thing received. But truly he who commands even this, repays. He says therefore: Turn not away, that is, nor alienate thy will. For God loveth a cheerful giver (II Cor. IX), and so the spiritual teacher ought to put out to his hearers the interest of the divine word, that he may be able to receive from them the recompense of a good work, and to hope for a reward from God.

VERS. 43.-- You have heard. He who came to fulfil the law leads at the 97Dend to full charity, which is the fulfilling of the law.

Thou shalt hate. Perhaps it is to be understood according to what is above: An eye for an eye, etc. Or by withdrawing the offices of humanity.

VERS. 44.-- Love. The Church is fought against in three ways: by hatred, by words, by the torment of the body. The Church on the contrary loves, does good, prays. This is the new commandment, this makes, out of sons of wrath, sons of God; whence there follows: That you may be sons. The adoption of sons is acquired by charity alone.

Do good. By ministering food to the mind or to the body. Which he also does who says that such a one is not even to take food. For all these things look to this, that they may become brethren. 98ASingle things are set against single, that all the parts of malice may be overcome by goodness. The imprecations of the prophets seem to be against this, which are not uttered by the wish of those desiring, but by the spirit of those foreseeing.

Pray. (AUG.) He does not say this for brethren, in whom there are sometimes sins graver than the persecution of enemies, etc., as far as although they confess that they have sinned, and vehemently repent of having done evil, yet they have such dispositions of mind that they do not humble themselves to ask pardon.

But it may move one, that John in his Epistle seems to admonish, concerning the love of God and of the neighbour, only that we should love the brethren, whereas the Lord says that prayer is to be made even for enemies. But by brethren are understood not only those who already believe, but those who are to believe, for whom 98Bprayer must be made, that they may be joined to us by a brotherly covenant.

VERS. 45.-- Sons. Not degenerate. As if he should say: Having received the power, act by works that you may be sons by being born again through charity. Of the Father. He is already called Father. See that you be sons born again of God, who is charity. Who maketh his sun to rise. This visible one. For they say in the book of Solomon, The sun of justice hath not shined upon us (Sap. V) [Vulg. Sap 5:6 justitiae lumen non luxit nobis, "the light of justice hath not shined upon us"]. And of the rain of doctrine the prophet says: Upon one city it rained, and not upon another. His sun (Amos. IV) [Vulg. Am 4:7 et plui super unam civitatem, et super alteram civitatem non plui, "and I rained upon one city, and upon another city I rained not"], etc. As if he should say: Whom he made, whose properly are all the things which he created out of nothing. What then shall we do concerning those things which we have received of his gift?

VERS. 46.-- The publicans. The exactors of the Romans, whom the Jews so call, because they bore the charge of the commonwealth. They are also said to have drawn their name from Publius, a Roman 98Cking, who first appointed them.

VERS. 47.-- And if you salute. If you shall have prayed for those only who are joined to you by some kinship, what more has your benefit than that of unbelievers? Brethren must be loved, and humbly saluted: but it is little.

VERS. 48.-- Perfect. In the love of God and of the neighbour. The crown of perfection is to love even enemies, and to pray for them, as Christ also did.

CAPUT VI.

VERS. 1.-- Attendite, etc. Qui hucusque praecepit de misericordia, nunc praecipit de cordis munditia qua Deus videtur. Si enim non simplici intentione sed pro hominum laude, non probantur a Deo opera 98Dmisericordiae. Laus hominum non debet appeti, sed subsequi, ut exemplo alios invitet. Hactenus de justitia Evangelii, quae est super justitiam Scribarum et Pharisaeorum. Nunc qua munditia cordis eadem justitia debeat impleri: quod in eleemosyna servatur et oratione. Non enim monet hic vel orare vel eleemosynam facere: sed quo animo debemus facere. Agitur enim hic de mundando corde. Apud Patrem vestrum. Cujus gloriam filii quaerere debent, et omnia bona ad hoc ut placeant ei, referre.

VERS. 2.-- Cum ergo, etc. A toto in partes, ut exponat quae bona non sint pro hominum laude facienda. Tuba canere. Forsitan convocandi gratia hoc fecerunt: ut omnes irent quasi ad spectaculum. 99AAnte te. Ante se tuba canit qui ante alios vult laudari: ut appareat major illis. Receperunt. A Deo cordis inspectore fallaciae supplicium de quibus dicitur: Recedite a me, operarii dolosi: nomen enim meum habuistis: sed opera mea non fecistis.

VERS. 3.-- Nesciat. Sinistra, vitia: dextera, virtutes. Quod ergo agit virtus, nesciat elatio, vel vana gloria, seu aliquod vitium: sed lux rectae operationis fuget tenebras peccati.

VERS. 4.-- In abscondito. In abscondito eleemosyna fit, quae nullis miscetur tenebris: lux intus est, tenebrae extra. Videt in abscondito. Videre Dei est approbare. Illa autem tantum approbat quae sunt abscondita, quae extra sunt non placent ei neque approbat.

99BVERS. 5.-- Et cum oratis. Ad superiora conjungitur haec sententia. Supra dixerat: Attendite ne justitiam vestram faciatis coram hominibus; hic subdit: Et cum oratis. Non prohibet orare, sed in orando hypocritis similes esse. In angulis. Quasi absconsores precum, et tamen in plateis ut videantur, et per hoc amplior gloria. Allegorice. In angulo orat qui in abscondito se orare simulat: et tamen in platea cum famam captat. Anguli platearum sunt ubi via per transitum viae ducitur, et quadrivium reddit. Non enim nefas est videri, sed appetere videri. Receperunt. In his non solum vera merces prava intentione evacuatur: sed vitium simulationis et irrisionis contra Deum augetur.

VERS. 6.-- Tu autem. Hoc simpliciter intellectum, 99Cdocet vanam gloriam mundi fugiendam. Sed magis videtur praecipi, ut, inclusa pectoris cogitatione labiisque compressis, oret Deum. Cubiculum autem est cordis secretum. Ostium, carnalis sensus per quem haec exteriora improbe se ingerunt, et turbae phantasmatum orantibus obstrepunt. Parum est ergo cubiculum intrare, si importunis cogitationibus ostium patet. Sed resistendum est carnali sensui, ut oratio spiritualis ex intimis cordis dirigatur ad Deum. Clauso ostio. Sensibus clausis, ne turbae phantasmatum exteriorum occurrant. Vel ostium vocat amorem vel timorem hujus saeculi. Sicut enim omne bonum ex timore et amore est Dei, sic omne malum ex amore et timore saeculi. His clausis, id est ablatis, ora.

99D(CHRYSOST.) Quid ergo, inquis? in Ecclesia non oportet orare? Et valde quidem, sed cum hac voluntate ut illum unum intuearis, qui habet donandi quod petieris potestatem. Ubique enim Deus, quo aliquid facias intuitu, spectat.

Patrem. Quem intus habes per fidem; unde: Apud me oratio Deo vitae meae (Psal. XLI).

VERS. 7.-- Orantes autem. Sicut hypocritarum est ostendi et placere hominibus, ita gentilium est multiloquium, a quibus et coepit. Hi enim exercendae linguae magis operam dant, quam menti mundandae: ut hoc nugatorii officii genus, etiam ad Deum prece flectendum transferant, putantes quod sicut orator judicem, ita et ipsi Deum flectere queant.

100A(CHRYSOST.) Si paucis orandum est, cur parabolam proposuit Lucas ad montem semper esse orandum, etc., usque ad Si quidem et Christus et Paulus jussit breves, sed crebras fieri orationes ex paucis intervallis.

(CASS.) Utilius censent (Patres) breves quidem orationes, sed creberrimas, etc., usque ad succincta brevitate vitemus.

Putant enim. Putantes quod persuasibilibus verbis Deus mulceatur. Vel si absens est vocetur: et quod nescit, verborum ambagibus doceatur.

VERS. 8.-- Nolite ergo assimilari eis. Non enim apud Deum agendum est verbis, sed rebus, intentione cordis, affectu simplici. Quae cum dicimus, non eum docemus, sed nos tempore orandi recordamur. 100BScit enim pater. Cur ergo scienti loquimur? Non ut nostrae faciamus indicium voluntatis, sed ut excitemus piae devotionis obsequium.

VERS. 9. Pater noster. (BEDA.) Inter caetera salutaria monita et divina, quibus consulit credentibus, formam orandi proposuit, etc., usque ad et hi sunt veri adoratores, qui Filii per gratiam adorant Patrem in spiritu et veritate.

Nulla oratio magis spiritualis est quam illa quae ex ore Filii, qui est Veritas, processit. Pater noster. Cui non timore, sed amore haereditatis servimus. Dum Pater dicitur, de adoptione cogitemus, ut Patri pium affectum rependamus. Dum dicitur noster, fraternitatis admonetur, cum sit communis adoptio omnibus. Nemo dicat meus, quod proprie filio convenit, 100Ccui pater est per naturam. Unde alibi. Vado ad Patrem meum (Joan. VII), specialiter per naturam: et vestrum, conjunctum per gratiam. Pater noster dicitur, quod commune omnibus, nemo dicat meus: quod solum Christo convenit, qui filius est per naturam; aliis conceditur communis gratia adoptionis.

(RAB.) Monetur nobilis et dives non superbire, etc., usque ad Quid negabit filiis, qui jam dedit quod pater est?

(AUG.) Nusquam invenitur praeceptum populo Israel, ut diceret: Pater noster, aut oraret, etc., usque ad si ab ejus mandatis non aberrarent. Sicut est illud: Filios genui et exaltavi (Isa. I), etc

In coelis. Spiritualibus, ut ad spiritualitatem in qua 100Dhabitat, festinare discamus, et coelestia ab eo quaeramus.

Sanctificetur nomen tuum. Videnda est harum distinctio septem petitionum. Nam cum vita nostra temporaliter agitur speraturque aeterna, priora sunt, etc., usque ad liberatio enim facit liberos, id est, filios, ut dicamus spiritu adoptionis, Abba Pater.

Amen, significat in omnibus his petitionibus indubitanter a Domino tribui quod petitur, si ultimae conditionis pactum servare non negligimus. Unde subdit: Si dimiseritis, etc. Sanctificetur, etc. Hic spiritum sapientiae oramus, ne a sanctificatione nominis primus in aliquo discrepemus: sed patris nomen in filiis moribus et vita ostendatur.

101ANomen patris in nobis est, qui filii dicimur: quod petimus sanctificari. Non ut ipse sanctior sit, sed ut in nobis operetur augmentum suae sanctificationis: nam sanctificatio filiorum nomini ejus attribuitur. Ita dicitur sanctificari sicut clarificari, et magnificari in nobis: Sanctificetur, id est, ita sicut est sanctum, habeatur hominibus: et ita innotescat illis Deus, ut non putent aliquid sanctius.

VERS.10.-- Adveniat regnum tuum. Hic spiritus intelligentiae petitur, quo mundatur cor ut Deus videatur. Duritia et potestas diaboli excluditur, et in futuro perfectius, cum evacuaverit omnem principatum et potestatem, et tradetur regnum Deo Patri. Congrue sequitur, ut post adoptionem filiorum regnum petamus quod filiis debetur.

101BRegnum Dei semper est: sed veniat, id est, manifestetur hominibus a quibus ut lux caecis non videtur, quod nullus ignorare poterit, cum Filius judicare venerit. In quo regnabunt et sancti, ut dicitur, Percipite regnum quod vobis paratum (Matth. XXV), etc.

Spiritu intelligentiae regnum futurum ingredientibus declaratur, et munditia cordis attribuitur, per quam Deus ibidem regnans sicut est videbitur, ubi spiritus sapientiae complebitur septima sanctificatio nominis ejus. In perfectione senarii adventus regni poscitur, ut curramus omnes in virum perfectum: deinde in eo quiescentibus sanctificetur ipse in nobis dum nos in illo sanctificamur. Ejus requiem jure poscimus in nobis sanctificari, ut perenniter in eo sanctificati requiescamus, in qua sanctificatione pax 101Cbeatitudinis firmatur et plena sapientia donatur.

Fiat voluntas tua. Hic petitur spiritus consilii, per quem voluntas Dei inquiritur, ut a nobis impleatur.

Voluntas Dei in terris sicut in coelo spiritu consilii regitur. Nobis etiam opus est spiritu consilii: ut voluntas Dei dirigatur in nobis, ut misericordes in re esse possimus. Voluntas Dei semper fit: sed oramus ut libertas nostri arbitrii societur per gratiam suae voluntati, quae est sanctificatio nostra et vita aeterna. Voluntas Dei est praeceptorum ejus custodia.

Sicut in coelo. Sicut in angelis, ita in hominibus. Vel sicut justis, ita in peccatoribus conversis. Vel 101Dsicut in Christo, ita in Ecclesia. Vel sicut mente servio legi Dei, ita et in carne.

Secundum spiritum coelum, secundum carnem terra sumus. Oramus ergo ut sicut in coelo, id est in spiritu, ita in terra fiat voluntas: ut caro et spiritus sit homo spiritualis de quo dicitur: Cor meum et caro mea exsultaverunt in Deum vivum. Nec tantum oratur ut bona velimus, sed fiat voluntas in nobis, ut vivamus tibi, non nobis. Haec tria perfectius habentur in futuro, sed hic interim ex parte oramus, ut postea perfectius habeantur. Quatuor sequentia praesentis vitae sunt praesidia.

VERS. 11.-- Panem. Esuries justitiae ut ad saturitatem pertingat, fulcitur spiritu fortitudinis. Ideo 102Aoramus: Panem nostrum quotidianum da nobis: sine quo fame moreremur.

Panis, corpus Christi est, vel verbum Dei, vel ipse Deus, quo quotidie egemus, vel qui est super omnes substantias. Hic est panis vivus qui de coelo descendit (Joan. VI), cujus naturae sublimitas super omnes substantias est.

Nostrum. Quia Christus noster est factus, et Deus Pater cum Christo omnia nobis donavit. Ideo oramus ut quod nostrum est per donum, quotidie detur per effectum.

Supersubstantialem. Vel quotidianum. Quia qui edunt me adhuc esurient (Eccl. XLIV). Hic quotidianus, in patria aeternus. Hic panis assidue per fidem et charitatem interius reficere debet: quia sine eo 102Bnemo vivere potest. Vel panis dicuntur spiritualia praecepta, quae semper sunt exercenda vel conservanda.

VERS. 12.-- Et dimitte, etc. Post cibum petit veniam peccatorum, ut qui a Deo pascitur, in Deo vivat, et ad vitam aeternam perveniat.

Cum dicimus: Dimitte nobis debita nostra: spiritum scientiae rogamus, quo delicta intelligimus, quae sine spiritu scientiae non intelliguntur. Scientia quippe ad usum temporalium pertinet, quae virtus est, in vitandis malis et petendis bonis, ut nostra et aliorum contagia ploremus, bona cupiamus.

Sicut. Ecce qua cautione constringimur, in qua etiam nos adinvicem ad misericordiam inflectit, et peccantibus in se veniam promittit, et nobis et per 102Cnos aliis veniam promittit.

Culpae veniam non debemus negare; unde: Cum oratis, remittite si quid habetis adversus aliquem: aliter enim Pater vester non dimittet vobis peccata vestra.

Pecuniam alicui repetere conceditur, sed debita peccatorum petenti veniam nunquam juste negamus.

VERS. 13.-- Et ne nos inducas. Sed spiritu scientiae illustra, ut sciamus quibus malis involvimur, et ut lacrymae prosint rogamus: Dimitte nobis debita nostra. Et ne nos, etc. Non quod ipse inducat, sed quia deserit et ire permittit. Non vero precatur non tentari, sed nec ad modicum vinci. Tentatio enim est necessaria ad coronam. Et ne nos, etc., deserens 102Din tentatione sinas vinci: etsi sinas tentari, sine qua nullus probatus est.

Sed libera nos a malo. Haec una petitio, tantum continet quantum omnes superiores.

Ut spiritu timoris Dei omnia noxia intus et exterius pellantur: et sub paupertate beate vivamus. Sed libera nos. In illam quae nondum est ne sinas induci, et ab ea in qua sumus ducti, libera: quo facto nihil est metuendum, nihil restat orandum.

A malo, omni, visibili et invisibili. Haec liberatio quotidie necessaria est, dum sine malo animae et corporis non vivitur, sed in fine complebitur, quando immortales et impassibiles erimus. De imis ad summa gradatim sic ascenditur. A malo per spiritum timoris 103Aliberamur: inde liberati pauperes spiritu et rebus esse cupimus, dum dicimus, Ne nos inducas in tentationem. Spiritum pietatis rogamus, ut homo interior mansuescat et mitis fiat, ne tentatione moveatur.

VERS. 14.-- Si enim dimiseritis. Formam imposuit qualiter velimus judicari. Unde indulgentibus coram Deo fiducia respondet impetrandi: exigentes vero, poena remordet ultionis vel damnationis.

VERS. 15.-- Si autem non dimiseritis. Nulla excusatio relinquitur, quia unusquisque secundum hanc sententiam in futuro judicabitur. Ex hoc patet, quod unusquisque in sua infidelitate condemnatur.

VERS. 16.-- Cum jejunatis. Postquam docuit quomodo 103Borandum sit, docet de jejunio quod iterum pertinet ad munditiam cordis, ne non sit simplex ad Deum, dum ad exteriora rapitur. Nolite fieri. Non prohibet virtutem, sed fictam virtutis speciem.

Bonum est jejunio pro peccatis tristes esse ad poenitentiam, et humiliare animam nostram; unde: Cor contritum et humiliatum, Deus, non despicies (Psal. L). Sed tristitiam quae pro laude est, prohibet. Unde non ait simpliciter: Nolite jejunare, sed addit: sicut hypocritae, tristes.

Exterminant. Id est, extra terminos humanae conditionis obducunt. Sicut de nitore vestium jactantia est: sic de nimio squalore et macie. Demoliri est quasi dissipare: et venustatem vultus quasi ad nihilum redigere. Facies suas. Luctuosis sordibus in 103Cquibus etiam jactantia est: et magis sub specie religionis decipiunt.

Nota non debere oves pelles suas deponere, sed vestiri, si aliquando lupi eis se contegunt.

VERS. 17.-- Unge caput tuum. Spirituali laetitia mentem refice diebus abstinentiae: ut dilectionis odor fragret in operibus misericordiae. Conscientiam lava lacrymis: ut sit munda coram Deo. Non enim sufficit opus misericordiae sine munda conscientia: nec hoc sine illo. Palaestinis mos erat in festis caput ungere: ergo caput unge, id est, festivum et hilarem te exhibe: et mentem spirituali oleo refice: et de jejunio non quaeras laudem exterius. Sed quia sic a saeculo averteris, coram Deo gaudeas: et sic faciem, id est cor, mundabis a sordibus: quibus remotis, 103DDeum videre licebit. Unge caput tuum. Ecce nondum omnia in Novo Testamento ad litteram. Ridiculi enim essent in jejunio oleo delibuti: sed de spiritu amoris ejus, cujus passionibus debemus participare nos macerandum, mens debet inungi et non oleo adulationis: aliter vana sunt jejunia.

Christus factus est unguentum nobis: quod effusum per passionem mortis Ecclesiam suam suo odore implevit. Ideo non alterius causa jejunare debemus: sed ejus perfusi spiritu per abstinentiam macerantes carnem ejus passioni participemus. Illo ergo ungamur: ut opera misericordiae cumulentur: et charitas interius diffundatur: quia sine illo calix nostri jejunii non inebriatur affectu pietatis.

104ADeo jejunat qui pro amore ejus se macerat et quod sibi subtrahit alteri largitur.

VERS. 18.-- Et Pater tuus. Pater per fidem intus est: et remunerat in abscondito facta. Ibi ergo jejunandum quo ipse videat. Et necesse est ut jejunans sic jejunet, ut ei placeat quem in pectore portat.

VERS. 19.-- Nolite thesaurizare. Ad munditiam cordis terrena erogari praecipit.

VERS 20.-- Thesaurizare autem. Quasi: Cum jejunatis aut aliquid boni operis agitis: ibi recondite ubi corruptio non possit accedere.

In coelo. Id est, omnem spem in coelestibus ponite: fide gradientes, charitatem amplectantes. In coelo, id est, in spirituali firmamento: quod non transit: 104BCoelum et terra transibunt: verba autem mea non transibunt. Si ergo cor est in coelo, mundum est, quia munda sunt coelestia. Si autem in terra volutatur, quomodo mundum est? id est, si quid agit ut terrena consequatur, quomodo mundum est? Si etenim illa in genere suo munda tamen sordescit res dignior dum inferiori miscetur: ut aurum argento, argentum plumbo.

VERS. 21.-- Ubi enim est thesaurus. Si in terra, cor est deorsum; si in coelestibus, cor est in Christo fixum.

VERS. 22.-- Lucerna. Prosequitur munditiam cordis. Sicut oculus membra ad operandum dirigit, sic intentio et lux fidei omnes virtutes. Lucerna est lumen in testa, sic charitas in fide. Cum fides cessabit, 104Csola charitas lux erit. De exteriori, docet officium interioris hominis, intentione mentis et luce fidei omnes virtutes illustrantur, ut reddant lucidum corpus.

Item ad munditiam cordis per se hoc capitulum est, vel gratia priorum operum quae palam apparent.

Si fuerit oculus, etc. Si pura intentione, quae potes bona agis, opera sunt lucis, etiamsi non ita hominibus videatur. Si perversa intentio est, pravum est quidquid agitur, etsi videatur rectum.

Oculus unus et simplex est, scilicet charitas, quae modo lucet in fide sicut in testa.

VERS. 23.-- Si autem oculus. Si intentio non est ex fide, totus homo interior tenebrosus est. Quia 104Dquod non est ex fide, peccatum est. Tenebrosum erit. Quandoquidem intentio sequens opus informat. Ergo si bona quae agis, quia mala intentione fiunt, tenebrae sunt, quid ea quae per se mala sunt, et mala intentione fiunt? Si ergo lumen. Intentio qua facimus quodcunque, quae in nobis est nota, lumen dicitur; exitus vero rei incertus est, ut cum quid alicui do vel ago nescio utrum cedat ei bono an malo: et ideo tenebras vocat. Dicit ergo: Si intentio qua facis (quae tibi nota est) appetitu temporalium sordidatur: quanto magis ipsum factum cujus dubius est exitus? quia etsi bene cedat alii, quod non bona intentione facis, nihil tibi proderit: quia quomodo feceris tibi imputatur, non quomodo illi evenerit.

105AVERS. 24.-- Nemo potest, etc. Quasi: non poteritis ad oculum hominum bona agere, et ex his Deo placere. Non simul in coelo et in terra thesaurizare, nec et vitiis et virtutibus deservire. Quidquid facis, vel ex amore Dei, vel servitute diaboli.

Cum regnum Dei et diaboli dissideant inter se, in utroque nemo potest simul militare: unde qui baptizantur, diabolo abrenuntiant et operibus ejus.

Aut enim unum odio. Nihil est medium. Quidquid enim facis, aut ex amore Dei, vel ex servitute diaboli.

(AUG.) Diabolus enim semper est odio habendus: Deus vero, etc., usque ad sed contemnunt, victi concupiscentia sua et persuasione diabolica.

Aut unum sustinebit. (ID.) Non quidem diligit: 105Bnemo enim diabolum diligit, sed sua cupiditate implicatus, etc., usque ad etsi non amet illum cujus ancillam diligit.

Alterum contemnet. Non quidem odio habebit: nullius enim conscientia Deum potest odisse, quod retractavit idem Augustinus, dicens: Non video fuisse dicendum. Multi enim sunt de quibus scriptum est: Superbia eorum qui te oderunt (Psal. III), etc.

Et mammonae. Mammona, Syra lingua, divitiae, quibus servire Deum negare est. Non dicit habere: quod fieri licet ut bene dispenset, sed servire, quod est avari. Dicitur hoc nomen esse daemonis qui praeest divitiis, non quod in ejus ditione sunt: sed quod his utitur ad decipiendum, irretiendo laqueis divitiarum.

105CVERS. 25.-- Ideo dico vobis, etc. Quia non potestis Deo servire et mammonae. Ne sitis solliciti, quod est servire divitiis. Contemptum saeculi et fiduciam futurorum toto sermone praecepit, dum et mala sustinere et non ulcisci, et inimicos diligere jussit, et humanam gloriam fugere. Nunc vult confirmare per fidem in spe aeternorum, quia plures sunt quos amor praesentium et desperatio futurorum torpentes facit.

In sudore vultus paratur panis, sed labor est exercendus, sollicitudo tollenda est, quae mentem perturbat: dum timet ne aut possessa dispereant aut eleborata non contingant.

Animae vestrae. Id est animalitati cui haec necessaria sunt. Totam spem Deo committite, quia nihil, 105Dnisi quod decrevit ille, potestis acquirere vel retinere.

Anima hic ponitur pro vita, quae cibo sustentatur, ut ibi: Qui amat animam suam, perdet eam (Joan. XII).

Animalitas cibo alitur: et ponitur hic pro vita: vivit cum pecoribus. Quam qui perdit propter Christum, inveniet eam spiritualem in vitam aeternam.

Nonne anima? etc. Non vita propter escam, nec corpus propter vestimentum, sed econtra. Ideo non sit sollicitus animus, ut percipiat quod propter se creatum est.

Quasi dicat: Qui dedit majora, id est vitam et corpus, dabit et minora, id est victum et vestes. 106AIn his promissis veritatis nemo dubitet: Sit homo quod esse debet, mox adduntur ei omnia propter quem sunt facta.

VERS. 26.-- Respicite. Hoc ad escam. Sancti merito comparantur avibus, quia coelum petunt, et quidam ita remoti sunt a mundo, ut jam in terris nil agant, sed sola contemplatione jam in coelo degunt. De quibus dicitur: Qui sunt hi, qui ut nubes volant? (Isa. LX), etc.

Haec exempla non prohibent providentiam ne laboret, sed sollicitudinem, ut tota fiducia vestra sit in Deo, cum et aves sine cura vivant.

Et Pater vester. Exaggerat fiduciam filiorum et confirmat: quia si bonitas Patris etiam ad illa se extendit, quanto magis ad filios adoptionis?

106BVERS. 27.-- Quis autem vestrum, etc. Illi autem curam regendi corporis relinquite, qui illud ad hanc mensuram fecit pervenire.

VERS. 28.-- Et de vestimentis. Non quod laborare et providere hoc non deceat, sed omnis sollicitudo abjicienda est, et spes in Deo habenda est. Considerate lilia agri. Exhortationem de indumento satis congruo confirmat exemplo.

VERS. 29.-- Quoniam nec Salomon, etc. Quae purpura regum, quae pictura textricum potest floribus comparari? Ipse color dicitur operimentum florum, sicut dicimus: operuit istum rubor.

VERS. 30.-- Si enim fenum agri. Si ista quae ad usum sunt temporis tantum, et cito decidua igne cremabuntur, Deus tam venuste adornat.

106CCras pro futuro ponitur in Scripturis, dicente Jacob: Exaudiet me cras justitia mea (Gen. XXX).

In clibanum mittitur. Alii libri habent: in ignem vel in acervum qui habet speciem clibani. Quanto magis vos, scilicet vestiet; et ideo omnis fiducia in eo habenda; quod si nec de his fidentes sumus, quomodo invisibilia speramus? Permodica fides est, quae nec de minimis certa est. Ideo et de aeternis ingerit fiduciam, et condemnat infidelitatem. Modicae fidei. Quicunque in aliqua promissione dubitat, modicus est fidei, et quanto plus dubitat, tanto minus invenit ea pro quibus sollicitus fuit.

VERS. 31.-- Nolite ergo solliciti, etc. Plenius inculcat 106Det revolvit, quod a principio sermonis docuit, ut absque sollicitudine vivamus. Nolite, etc. Hic videntur argui hi qui, spreto victu vel vestitu communi, lautiora sibi vel austeriora prae his cum quibus vivunt, alimenta quaerunt vel indumenta. Solliciti esse. Sollicitudo suffocat verbum Dei, ne fructum referat, et facit similem gentili, id est infidelem.

VERS. 32.-- Haec enim omnia. Quid ergo amplius habet a gentili: cujus infidelitas animum sollicitat, et curis vitae hujus fatigat? Gentes. Quibus de futuris cura non est, sed filiorum est quaerere regnum. Scit enim. Provisa sunt haec a Deo, qui novit nobis haec in via sua esse necessaria. Pater vester. Sic filios de 107Abonitate et providentia patris securos reddit, absque cura scilicet.

VERS. 33.-- Quaerite ergo. Hic aperte ostendit ista non esse petenda tanquam bona nostra, etsi necessaria. Regnum autem Dei petendum est, et in eo finis noster est ponendus, propter quem omnia faciamus: verbi gratia: Manducemus ut evangelizemus, non evangelizamus ut manducemus. Cum dicit primum, significat hoc posterius, non tempore, sed dignitate. Illud enim petendum est, hoc propter illud necessarium: ergo primum quaerite regnum Dei, id est, praeponite omnibus rebus caeteris.

Et haec omnia. Quia omnia sunt filiorum: et ideo haec omnia adjicientur, etiam non quaerentibus, quibus si subtrahuntur, ad probationem est; si 107Bdantur, ad gratiarum actionem; quia omnia cooperantur in bonum.

VERS. 34.-- Solliciti. Timore, moerore, et anxietate curarum, quae mentem perturbant.

In crastinum. (AUG.) In futurum, de praesentibus concedit: unde Apostolus: Nocte ac die operabamur ne quem gravaremus (I Thes. II). De futuris quae divina procurat ordinatio, non convenit, etc., usque ad quia ille qui hoc praecepit cui ministrabant angeli, propter hoc exemplum dandum loculos habuit: unde necessaria usibus praeberet, quorum custos erat Judas.

Dies. Non quem fecit Dominus, sed cui maledixit Job et Jeremias, scilicet quem diabolus invenit. Sufficit. Et quia cura praesenti mentes obruitur, non debet germinari malitia Dei.

CHAPTER VI.

VERS. 1.-- Take heed, etc. He who hitherto has given precept concerning mercy, now gives precept concerning cleanness of heart, by which God is seen. For if not with a simple intention but for the praise of men, the works 98Dof mercy are not approved by God. The praise of men ought not to be sought, but to follow, that by example it may invite others. Thus far concerning the justice of the Gospel, which is above the justice of the Scribes and Pharisees. Now with what cleanness of heart that same justice ought to be fulfilled: which is observed in almsgiving and in prayer. For he does not here admonish either to pray or to give alms: but with what mind we ought to do it. For the matter here is of cleansing the heart. With your Father. Whose glory sons ought to seek, and to refer all good things to this, that they may please him.

VERS. 2.-- When therefore, etc. From the whole to the parts, that he may set forth what good things are not to be done for the praise of men. To sound a trumpet. Perhaps they did this for the sake of calling men together: that all might go as if to a spectacle. 99ABefore thee. He sounds a trumpet before himself who wishes to be praised before others: that he may appear greater than they. They have received. From God, the inspector of the heart, the punishment of their deceit, of whom it is said: Depart from me, you crafty workers: for you have had my name: but you have not done my works.

VERS. 3.-- Let it not know. The left hand, vices: the right hand, virtues. What therefore virtue does, let not elation know, nor vainglory, nor any vice: but let the light of right working put to flight the darkness of sin.

VERS. 4.-- In secret. That alms is done in secret which is mingled with no darkness: the light is within, the darkness without. He seeth in secret. To see, in God, is to approve. But he approves only those things which are hidden, the things which are without do not please him, neither does he approve them.

99BVERS. 5.-- And when you pray. This sentence is joined to what goes before. He had said above: Take heed that you do not your justice before men; here he adds: And when you pray. He does not forbid to pray, but to be like the hypocrites in praying. In the corners. As it were hiders of prayers, and yet in the streets that they may be seen, and by this a fuller glory. Allegorically. He prays in a corner who feigns to pray in secret: and yet in the street, when he catches at fame. The corners of the streets are where a way is led through by the crossing of a way, and makes a crossroads. For it is not wrong to be seen, but to desire to be seen. They have received. In these men not only is the true reward emptied out by a depraved intention: but the vice of dissimulation and of mockery against God is increased.

VERS. 6.-- But thou. This, simply understood, 99Cteaches that the vainglory of the world is to be fled. But it seems rather to be enjoined that, the thought of the breast being enclosed and the lips pressed together, one should pray to God. Now the chamber is the secret place of the heart. The door, the carnal sense through which these outward things shamelessly thrust themselves in, and the crowds of phantasms make a din against those who pray. It is little therefore to enter the chamber, if the door stands open to importunate thoughts. But the carnal sense must be resisted, that spiritual prayer may be directed to God out of the inmost parts of the heart. The door being shut. The senses being shut, lest the crowds of outward phantasms should meet him. Or he calls the door the love or the fear of this world.
