VERS. 25.-- Et cum ejecta esset. Id est, cum reprobati essent irrisores, intravit in electorum mentes. Et tenuit manum. Nisi prius manus Judaeorum, etc., usque ad secreta potest deleri poenitentia.

VERS. 27.-- Transeunte. Id est, de domo principis in tuam domum. Domus principis Synagoga est, sua domus coelestis Jerusalem. Duo caeci, etc. Duo caeci, duo populi inscii, etc., usque ad idem factum, sed aliud simile.

VERS. 32.-- Hominem. Significat gentilem populum: vel omne genus hominum, quo ducto per praedicationem 117Cad Christum, et ablata idololatria, confessus est Christum. Κωφὸς Graece usitatius, sed tamen indifferenter, et mutus et surdus dicunt.

VERS. 34.-- Pharisaei autem. In Pharisaeis infidelitas Judaeorum signatur.

CHAPTER IX.

VERS. 1.-- Into the city. Lest thou think this to be Nazareth, etc., as far as Capharnaum, as it were the metropolis.

VERS. 2.-- They offered. They are the teachers, who lift men up and offer them to Christ by admonishing and by interceding. Faith. How much a man's own faith avails with God, with whom another's so availed that he healed the man within and without. Be of good heart, son, they are forgiven thee. Luke says: Man, etc., as far as taking away the cause of the disease, he afterwards heals the body.

VERS. 5.-- Which is. Note that infirmities sometimes 115Ccome about on account of sins, etc., as far as whence: The Lord shall crush them with a double crushing. (BED.) Which is easier, [Vulg. Jer 17:18 duplici contritione contere eos, an imperative with no subject: "crush thou them"] but since you do not believe this spiritual thing, let it be proved by a visible sign, which is agreed to be of no less power, so that in the Son of man you may recognize the hidden power of majesty, which can forgive sins as God.

VERS. 6.-- Then said he to the man sick of the palsy. Behold, by the remission of the penalty understand the forgiveness of the fault. Arise. To arise is to draw the soul away from carnal desires. To take up the bed is to lift the flesh from earthly desires to the will of the spirit. To go home is to return to paradise, or to the inward keeping of oneself, lest one sin again. Take up thy bed and go into thy house. That what was the witness of infirmity 115Dmay be the proof of health.

VERS. 7.-- And he arose. Great is the power, where without delay healing accompanies the command. Whence deservedly those who were present, leaving their blasphemies and amazed, are turned to the praise of so great majesty.

VERS. 9.-- He saw a man. Here it seems more probable that he is recording things passed over, since he chose the twelve before the sermon. In the custom-house. Τέλος in Greek, in Latin a toll. Matthew in Hebrew is in Latin "given", who received so great a gift of grace: Levi, "added" or "taken up", because the divine election took him up and added him to the number of the apostles. Matthew. The other Evangelists, out of modesty, etc., as far as the aforesaid discourse was. Rising up. (BED.) Luke says more fully: 116Aleaving all things, he followed him as much in body as in mind. Led not by levity, but drawn by the truth of the works and by the divine power working within, a perfect obedience and a perfect renunciation, he left his gains also, eager to follow Christ.

VERS. 10.-- In the house. He does not say whose, but Luke expresses it, etc., as far as is shown by the conversion of the penitent. Behold many publicans. A good omen: already by his example many are converted. Already he draws many after him, being about to be an Apostle and teacher of the Gentiles. (HIER.) With publicans and sinners. Tertullian says these were heathen, etc., as far as he commanded: Go ye not into the way of the Gentiles. Sinners coming sat down. Penitents, by Matthew's example, hoping for pardon: whence Mark says here: that many followed 116Bhim (Marc. II).

VERS. 11.-- Why with publicans. Every question, etc., as far as and all that he did serves for salvation.

VERS. 12.-- But Jesus hearing it, said. He both strengthens further in faith the penitents he has received, and provokes the proud to the grace of humility and of mercy. They that are in health need not a physician, but they that are ill. Christ is the physician, etc., as far as they receive the word of God, they cannot hunger.

VERS. 13.-- And going, say ye. [Vulg. Mt 9:13 discite, "learn"] To those swelling with a false righteousness he gives counsel of correction, etc., as far as by the sheep, and the drachma, and the two sons. For I am not come to call the just. Were not Nathanael, and Peter, and Andrew, etc., as far as they no longer seem just to themselves.

116CVERS. 14.-- But thy disciples do not fast. John, Mark and Luke, etc., as far as thou mayest understand the grace and the power.

VERS. 15.-- Can they. As if he should say: You are about to do so, that mourning, etc., as far as but from all earthly things. To mourn as long as the bridegroom is with them. By this word he expresses that fast, etc., as far as afterwards they shall fast, they shall not mourn. And then they shall fast. Grief for the bridegroom's absence was wanting neither in the fathers before the incarnation, nor after the passion, save for so long as he lived in the flesh with the disciples. They shall fast. From the love of the world, not only from foods. For since the spiritual fast is a part of the new life, it does not yet befit those who in other respects are given over to the old life.

VERS. 16.-- And no man. As if he should say: The carnal, 116Dnot yet made firm by faith in the passion and the resurrection, etc., as far as it does not suit that very oldness. For it taketh away. That is, the perfection of the new life, which is one, is divided, and from the old is taken away the faith which it has, to which the perfection of the new was owed.

VERS. 17.-- Neither do they put. He compares those same disciples to old bottles, etc., as far as we are inwardly renewed in newness of understanding.

VERS. 18.-- Behold a certain ruler came up. Mark and Luke call him a ruler of the synagogue, etc., as far as he began to hope for a remedy and to seek it.

VERS. 20.-- A woman who. The salvation prepared for the Jews the Gentile world snatched beforehand by a sure hope. Twelve years. For twelve years she had an issue, as that daughter also had grown 117Ato twelve: because from the time when the Synagogue began to serve God, the Gentile world began to be defiled with idolatry and sin. She came near. She believed, she spoke, because by these three, by faith and by work, all salvation is gained.

VERS. 21.-- If I shall touch. The hem, not the garment. Because she did not see the Lord in the flesh, but received the word of the incarnation through the apostles. Blessed is he who touches by faith even the outermost part of the word.

VERS. 22.-- Be of good heart, daughter. She who asked did indeed believe, but he impresses upon her strength and perseverance in faith.

VERS. 23.-- He had seen. The minstrels are those singing a mournful song. These are the masters soothing their subjects by their own grief, and the crowd of the Jews kicking against the doctrine of Christ. And the crowd. Because the Synagogue the joy of the Lord's indwelling, etc., 117Bas far as it was obedient in the receiving.

VERS. 24.-- She is not. To you she is dead. But to God, who can raise her up, she sleeps, as much in soul as in body.

VERS. 25.-- And when the crowd was put forth. That is, when the mockers had been rejected, he entered into the minds of the elect. And he took her hand. Unless first the hand of the Jews, etc., as far as can be blotted out by secret penance.

VERS. 27.-- As he passed. That is, from the ruler's house into thy house. The ruler's house is the Synagogue, his own house is the heavenly Jerusalem. Two blind men, etc. Two blind men, two ignorant peoples, etc., as far as the same deed, but another like it.

VERS. 32.-- A man. He signifies the Gentile people: or the whole race of men, which, being led by preaching 117Cto Christ, and idolatry being taken away, confessed Christ. Κωφὸς is the more usual Greek word, but yet they say indifferently both dumb and deaf.

VERS. 34.-- But the Pharisees. In the Pharisees the unbelief of the Jews is signified.

CAPUT X.

VERS. 1.-- Et convocatis. A curatione socrus Petri, etc., usque ad curatum centurionis servum. Duodecim. Hi sunt operarii qui mittendi erant, et quadrati orbis partes ad fidem Trinitatis vocaturi. Quorum praedicatio ut pondus haberet auctoritatis, datur eis potestas curandae omnis infirmitatis.

VERS. 2.-- Petrus et Andreas. Apostolorum paria jugaque sociat, etc., usque ad Christus in quem credebat.

117DVERS. 3.-- Thomas. Abyssus: quia altitudinem divinae virtutis in resurrectione certa fide penetravit. Vel, Thomas geminus, quod est Graece didymus, propter dubium cor in credendo.

VERS. 4.-- Et Judas Scariothis. Magnum in nobis exemplum doctrinae, cum qui omnia noverat non dubitaret proditorem in discipulum eligere, atque apostolum ordinare. Habet enim Christus conscium munditiae testem, cum suum non dubitet eligere proditorem.

VERS. 5.-- In viam. Post resurrectionem dicit: Docete omnes gentes. Sed hoc ante: quia prius oportet Judaeis praedicari, ne causam habeant non recipiendi, quia ad gentes apostolos misit.

118AVERS. 7.-- Appropinquabit regnum coelorum. Per fidem prope est regnum. Sicut enim sit peccans terra, ita justus sit coelum, in quo Deus praesidet; unde dicitur: Regnum Dei intra vos est (Luc. XVII), etc. Ecce quid debeant praedicare: Appropinquabit regnum coelorum.

VERS. 8.-- Infirmos curate. Ecclesia infirmos statu boni operis confortat, mortuos in peccatis, vel sine fide, suscitat; leprosos erroribus aspersos mundat, daemones per exorcismos fugat. Gratis accepistis. Ne Judas, qui loculos habebat, de praedicta potestate pecuniam congregare vellet, neguitiam ejus supprimit Dominus, dicens: Gratis accepistis, etc. Damnat etiam hic perfidiam Simoniacae haereseos. Dona spiritualia pretio vilescunt.

118BVERS. 9.-- Aurum neque argentum. Qui haec haberent, viderentur non causa salutis praedicare, sed lucri. Neque pecuniam in zonis vestris. Duo sunt genera necessariorum: unum quo emuntur necessaria, aliud ipsa necessaria. Per pecuniam in zonis, illud unde emuntur: per peram quod emitur, quod ibi reponitur. De hoc in passione: Quando misi vos sine sacculo et pera, nunquid defuit vobis aliquid? At illi dixerunt: Non.

VERS. 10.-- Neque calceamenta. Et Plato dicit duas corporum summitates non esse velandas, nec assuetam fieri debere mollitiem capitis et pedum: Cum autem haec habuerint firmitatem, caetera robustiora sunt. Neque virgam. Matthaeus et Lucas dicunt neque virgam; Marcus non nisi virgam: quo 118Cnomine, etc., usque ad iter praedicationis pergere debeat, ostendatur. Dignus est. Unde ergo necessaria? Tantum accipite, quantum ad necessitatem sufficit, ut inde securi aeternis melius vacetis.

VERS. 11.-- Interrogate quis. Testimonio vicinorum eligendus est hospes, ne hujus infamia praedicatio laedatur. Dignus sit. Ut sciat se magis accipere gratiam quam dare. Ibi manete. (AMBR.) Dat constantiae generale mandatum ut hospitalis necessitudinis jura custodiant: ideo domus eligenda, ut mutandi hospitii necessitudinisque violandae causa non suppetat.

VERS. 14.-- Excutite pulverem de pedibus. Per excussionem pulveris, intelligitur amissio itineris. Excutite pulverem, ad contestationem terreni laboris 118Dquem pro eis inaniter susceperant, etc., usque ad per ipsum Salvatorem narrantur abluti.

VERS. 16.-- Ecce ego mitto. Illis sua poena servabitur in futurum, etc., usque ad mala inferre non debet, sed tolerare. Sicut serpentes. Serpentis astutia est, etc., usque ad veterem exuit hominem.

VERS. 18.-- In testimonium. Ut inde perversi pereant, etc., usque ad testimonium ipsorum innocentiae.

VERS. 19.-- Dabitur enim vobis in illa hora quid loquamini. Voluntatem vestram pro Christo offerte; Christus vero loquetur pro se.

VERS. 21.-- Tradet autem frater, etc. Minorem dolorem ingerunt mala quae ab extraneis: majorem 119Aquae ab illis patimur, de quorum mentibus praesumebamus: quia cum damno corporis, mala nos cruciant amissae charitatis.

VERS. 22.-- Qui autem, etc. Docet virtutem longanimitatis et patientiae, etc., usque ad hoc ipsum incipimus possidere quod sumus.

VERS. 23.-- Cum autem. Hoc ad illud tempus pertinet, quo dictum est.: In via gentium ne abieritis, quod persecutiones non debeant timere, sed declinare: ut tribulationis occasio sit Evangelii seminarium.

VERS. 24.-- Non est discipulus, etc. Ne indignemini tolerare quod tolero qui Dominus sum, faciens quod volo, et magister docens quod utile scio.

VERS. 25.-- Si patrem familias. Ostendit calumnias 119Bsuas, ut suo exemplo ferant injurias. Unde dixit: Sufficit diei malitia sua. Beelzebub. Beel ipse est Bel, Zebub, musca. etc., usque ad vir muscarum, sive habens muscas.

VERS. 26.-- Ne ergo timueritis eos. Eorum saevitiam vel blasphemias, quia veniet dies cum Deus judicabit occulta hominum, ubi et nequitia discooperietur, et vestra intentio, nunc soli Deo cognita, ab omnibus scietur.

VERS. 27.-- Quod dico. Hoc est, in quo volo vos constantes esse, etc., usque ad calcato carnis domicilio praedicate.

VERS. 29.-- Super. Loquitur juxta morem Palestinae, etc., usque ad in toto mundo dicite. Nonne duo passeres? Lucas: Nonne quinque passeres veneunt 119Cdipondio (Luc. XII)? Nec multum distat, ut duo asse et quinque dipondio. Dipondio enim est ex duobus assibus. Quod autem in numeris est unus, hoc in pondere as: et quod duo, hoc dipondius. Et unus ex illis. Quomodo ergo Apostolus: Nunquid de bobus cura est Deo (Luc. IX)? Sed aliud est cura, aliud scientia.

VERS. 30.-- Numerati. Hoc est, scientia Dei, etc., usque ad parva otiosaque dicta eum lateant.

VERS. 32.-- Qui confitebitur. Vel confitetur quis Jesum ea fide, quae per dilectionem operatur: negat qui praeceptis Dei non obedit.

VERS. 33.-- Qui autem. Negatur a Filio apud Patrem, cui non manifestatur in divinitatis potentia 119DFilius et Pater. Confitetur a Filio apud Patrem, qui per Filium habet accessum ad Patrem.

VERS. 34.-- Pacem. Missum est bellum bonum, ut rumperetur pax mala.

VERS. 35.-- Veni enim. Hic Lucas ait: Erunt quinque in una domo divisi, tres in duo et duo in tres dividentur. Quomodo ergo, etc., usque ad circumcisionem caeterasque carnales observantias. Separare. Id est, privatos carnis affectus exstinguere, quibus ita invicem diliguntur, ut Deo minus serviant. Vel penitus avertantur, non quod non sint honorandi pater et mater, sed post Deum; hic est ordo charitatis.

VERS. 37.-- Qui amat patrem. Post Deum amandi 120Asunt, sed si utrumque non potest servari, odium in suos, pietas est in Deum.

VERS. 38.-- Et qui non accipit, etc. Crux duobus modis portatur, etc., usque ad amor Christi ostendatur. Et sequitur me. Aliquis enim carnem macerat pro inani gloria, vel proximo carnaliter compatitur ut culpae faveat: Hic crucem ferre videtur, sed Dominum non sequitur.

VERS. 39.-- Qui invenit, etc. Nomine animae vita praesens designatur.

VERS. 40.-- Qui recipit, etc. Cum ad praedicationem mittit, etc., usque ad debere stipendia accipere.

VERS. 42.-- Et quicumque. Qui periculum non suscipientium testimonio excussi pulveris denuntiavit, meritum 120Brecipientium ultra speratum commendat. Nota Deum magis ad pium affectum respicere quam ad quantitatem rei exhibitae. Uni ex minimis. Minimi sunt qui nihil penitus habent in hoc mundo, et judices erunt cum Christo.

CHAPTER X.

VERS. 1.-- And having called together. From the healing of Peter's mother-in-law, etc., as far as the centurion's servant healed. Twelve. These are the labourers who were to be sent, and who were to call the quarters of the four-square world to faith in the Trinity. That their preaching might have the weight of authority, power is given them to heal every infirmity.

VERS. 2.-- Peter and Andrew. He joins the apostles in pairs and yokes, etc., as far as Christ, in whom he believed.

117DVERS. 3.-- Thomas. The abyss: because by a sure faith he penetrated the depth of the divine power in the resurrection. Or, Thomas a twin, which in Greek is didymus, because of a doubting heart in believing.

VERS. 4.-- And Judas Scariothis. A great example of teaching for us, since he who knew all things did not hesitate to choose a betrayer for a disciple and to ordain him an apostle. For Christ has a witness conscious of his own cleanness, since he does not hesitate to choose his own betrayer.

VERS. 5.-- Into the way. After the resurrection he says: Teach ye all nations. But this beforehand: because the Jews must first be preached to, lest they have a cause for not receiving him, in that he sent the apostles to the Gentiles.

118AVERS. 7.-- The kingdom of heaven shall draw near. [Vulg. Mt 10:7 appropinquavit, "hath drawn near"] Through faith the kingdom is near. For as one who sins is earth, so let the just man be heaven, in which God presides; whence it is said: The kingdom of God is within you (Luc. XVII), etc. Behold what they ought to preach: The kingdom of heaven shall draw near.

VERS. 8.-- Heal the sick. The Church strengthens the sick by the standing of good work, raises the dead in sins, or without faith; cleanses the lepers sprinkled with errors, puts demons to flight by exorcisms. Freely have you received. Lest Judas, who had the purse, should wish to gather money out of the aforesaid power, the Lord suppresses his wickedness, saying: Freely have you received, etc. Here also he condemns the perfidy of the Simoniac heresy. Spiritual gifts are cheapened by a price.

118BVERS. 9.-- Gold nor silver. Those who had these would seem to preach not for the sake of salvation, but of gain. Nor money in your purses. There are two kinds of necessary things: one whereby necessaries are bought, the other the necessaries themselves. By the money in the purses, that whereby they are bought: by the scrip, what is bought, what is stored there. Of this in the passion: When I sent you without purse and scrip, did you want anything? But they said: No. [Vulg. Lc 22:35 At illi dixerunt : Nihil, "Nothing"]

VERS. 10.-- Nor shoes. Plato too says that the two extremities of the body are not to be covered, and that softness of head and of feet ought not to be made habitual: But when these have firmness, the rest are stronger. Nor a staff. Matthew and Luke say nor a staff; Mark, nothing but a staff: by which 118Cname, etc., as far as it may be shown by what way he ought to go upon the journey of preaching. He is worthy. Whence then necessaries? Take only so much as suffices for need, that thence being free from care you may better have leisure for eternal things.

VERS. 11.-- Inquire who. By the testimony of the neighbours the host is to be chosen, lest the preaching be injured by his ill fame. Is worthy. That he may know that he rather receives a favour than gives one. There abide. (AMBR.) He gives a general command of constancy, that they should keep the rights of the bond of hospitality: therefore a house is to be so chosen that no occasion be at hand for changing the lodging and for violating the bond.

VERS. 14.-- Shake off the dust from your feet. By the shaking off of the dust is understood the loss of the journey. Shake off the dust, for a testimony of the earthly labour 118Dwhich they had undertaken for them in vain, etc., as far as they are said to have been washed by the Saviour himself.

VERS. 16.-- Behold I send you. Their punishment shall be kept for them in the time to come, etc., as far as he ought not to inflict evils, but to bear them. As serpents. The serpent's craft is, etc., as far as he puts off the old man.

VERS. 18.-- For a testimony. That thence the perverse may perish, etc., as far as the testimony of their innocence.

VERS. 19.-- For it shall be given you in that hour what to speak. Offer your will for Christ; but Christ will speak for himself.

VERS. 21.-- And the brother shall deliver up, etc. Less grief do those evils bring which are from strangers: greater 119Athose which we suffer from those upon whose minds we had counted: because, along with the harm of the body, the evils of a charity lost torment us.

VERS. 22.-- But he that, etc. He teaches the virtue of long-suffering and of patience, etc., as far as we begin to possess this very thing which we are.

VERS. 23.-- And when. This belongs to that time of which it was said: Go ye not into the way of the Gentiles, namely that they ought not to fear persecutions, but to shun them: so that the occasion of tribulation may be a seed-plot of the Gospel.

VERS. 24.-- The disciple is not, etc. Do not disdain to bear what I bear, who am the Lord, doing what I will, and the master teaching what I know to be useful.

VERS. 25.-- If the master of the house. He shows his own calumnies, 119Bthat by his example they may bear injuries. Whence he said: Sufficient for the day is the evil thereof. Beelzebub. Beel is Bel himself, Zebub, a fly, etc., as far as the man of flies, or having flies.

VERS. 26.-- Therefore fear them not. Their savagery or their blasphemies, because the day will come when God shall judge the hidden things of men, where both their wickedness shall be uncovered, and your intention, now known to God alone, shall be known by all.

VERS. 27.-- What I tell you. That is, in what I wish you to be constant, etc., as far as the dwelling-place of the flesh being trodden underfoot, preach ye.

VERS. 29.-- Upon. He speaks according to the custom of Palestine, etc., as far as tell it in the whole world. Are not two sparrows? Luke: Are not five sparrows sold for 119Ctwo farthings (Luc. XII)? Nor is there much difference, seeing that two go for an as, and five for a dipondius. For a dipondius is out of two asses. And what one is in numbers, that the as is in weight: and what two is, that is the dipondius. And one of them. How then does the Apostle say: Doth God take care for oxen (Luc. IX)? But care is one thing, knowledge another.

VERS. 30.-- Numbered. That is, by the knowledge of God, etc., as far as small and idle words should lie hidden from him.

VERS. 32.-- Whosoever shall confess. Or a man confesses Jesus with that faith which works by love: he denies who does not obey the precepts of God.

VERS. 33.-- But he that. He is denied by the Son before the Father, to whom there is not manifested, in the power of the divinity, 119Dthe Son and the Father. He is confessed by the Son before the Father, who through the Son has access to the Father.

VERS. 34.-- Peace. A good war was sent, that an evil peace might be broken.

VERS. 35.-- For I came. Here Luke says: There shall be five in one house divided, three against two and two against three shall be divided. How then, etc., as far as circumcision and the other carnal observances. To set apart. That is, to extinguish the private affections of the flesh, by which men so love one another that they serve God the less. Or that they be utterly turned away, not that father and mother are not to be honoured, but after God; this is the order of charity.

VERS. 37.-- He that loveth father. After God they 120Aare to be loved, but if both cannot be kept, hatred toward one's own is piety toward God.

VERS. 38.-- And he that taketh not, etc. The cross is borne in two ways, etc., as far as the love of Christ may be shown. And followeth me. For one man mortifies the flesh for empty glory, or has a carnal compassion for his neighbour so as to favour his fault: he seems to bear the cross, but does not follow the Lord.

VERS. 39.-- He that findeth, etc. By the name of the soul the present life is designated.

VERS. 40.-- He that receiveth, etc. When he sends them to preach, etc., as far as that they ought to receive their wages.

VERS. 42.-- And whosoever. He who by the testimony of the shaken-off dust denounced the peril of those who do not receive them, 120Bcommends beyond what was hoped the desert of those who do receive them. Note that God looks rather to the devout affection than to the quantity of the thing bestowed. To one of these least ones. The least are those who have absolutely nothing in this world, and will be judges with Christ.

CAPUT XI.

VERS. 2.-- Joannes autem. Et dum Christus praedicaret, etc., usque ad quis ordinem rei gestae teneat, non apparet.

VERS. 3.-- Tu es. Non ait qui venisti, quasi dicat: Cum ad inferna descensurus sum: debeo te nuntiare inferis, qui nuntiavi superis. An conveniat Filio Dei mori, an alium ad haec sacramenta missurus 120Ces?

VERS. 6.-- Qui non fuerit. Qui de divinitatis potentia non dubitaverit, hoc non contra Joannem dixisse putandus est quem mox adeo commendat.

VERS. 7.-- De Joanne. Praecursor Christi de Christo certificare discipulos voluit, quia sensit eos dubitare. De praecursore, Christus turbas dubitantes ne dubitent docuit, mollem eum levemque putantes.

VERS. 8.-- Ecce qui mollibus, etc. Qui ponunt pulvinaria sub cubito, qui vitam peccantium palpant, non pungunt.

Rigida vita et praedicatio debet declinare mollium palatia, quae frequentant mollibus indutis adulantes.

(CLEM. ALEX.) Beatus Joannes, despectis quoque ovium pilis, qui delicias redoleant, pilos camelorum, 120Detc., usque ad ab omni inani studio, vanaque boni specie, sordidoque ac illiberali decore, se removit.

VERS. 9.-- Plusquam propheta. (BEDA.) Quia angelus non natura sed officio.

VERS. 11.-- Major Joanne. In hoc non praefertur patriarchis, et cunctis hominibus, sed aequalis caeteris sanctis ostenditur. Qui autem. Id est, omnis sanctus qui jam est cum Deo, major est eo qui adhuc in mundo. Vel ego quem minorem facit multorum opinio, in Ecclesia sanctorum illi praelatus sum. Vel novissimus angelus coeli melior est quovis homine. In regno. Regnum coelorum duobus modis accipitur: aut supernum, in quo nondum sumus: 121Aaut praesens Ecclesia, in qua adhuc vivimus, et jam quodammodo regnamus.

VERS. 12.-- A diebus Joannis. Apostolos Dominus ad oves quae perierunt domus Israel ire jusserat, ut ad regnum reducerentur. Sed haec praedicatio ad profectum publicanorum et peccatorum versa est qui crediderunt. Caeterum Joanni a plebe non creditur, Christi opera non auctorisantur, quia crux est scandalo. Jam prophetia cessat, jam lex expletur; Christus aliis praedicatur, et ab aliis recipitur: sui respuunt, inimici accipiunt. Itaque regnum coelorum vim patitur, quia gloria Israel patribus debita, a prophetis nuntiata, a Christo oblata, fide gentium occupatur et rapitur. Vel a diebus Joannis, qui praesentiam praedicavit, qua peccator reviviscit: 121Bet non ante, quia lex poenam intulit et non per poenitentiam vivificavit. Patria coelestis vim patitur dum peccator ad eam quasi in alienum locum intrat per poenitentiam. Grandi violentia est in terra nasci et coelum capere, et habere per virtutem quod non possumus per naturam.

VERS. 13.-- Omnes enim. Non quod post Joannem non fuerint aliqui, ut Agabus, sed quia quidquid prophetaverunt, de Domino vaticinati sunt. Omnes enim. Quia quidquid illi dixerunt, de venturo locuti sunt, hic vero venientem ostendit.

VERS. 14.-- Et si vultis recipere. Ut minus excusabiles sitis, quia eum non recepistis. Ipse est Elias in virtute, non in persona, par in vitae severitate, mentis rigore praecedens Christi primum adventum, 121Cut ille secundum.

VERS. 16.-- Cui autem, etc. Laudato Joanne transit ad increpandum illos, qui nec praedicatione Joannis moti sunt: vel ipsius Christi praedicationem despiciunt. Joannes talis ac tantus ut dixi, et adeo dignus, ut ei debeat credi: sed vos cui estis assimilandi, qui noluistis credere ei? Similis est pueris. Pueri doctores sunt humiles spiritu. Horum synagoga, vel ipsa Jerusalem, ubi jura praeceptorum Dei recondebantur. Coaequales contribules quos solebant exprobrare, quod ad laudes Dei non assurrexerunt corde devoto, membrorum agilitate quod notat saltus. Nec per lamenta prophetarum pro excidiis jam factis vel faciendis ad poenitentiam conversi sunt.

121DVERS. 17.-- Cecinimus vobis. Ut David: Exsultate Deo adjutori nostro, jubilate Deo Jacob (Psal. LXXX), et hujusmodi. Lamentavimus. Ut: Convertimini ad me in toto corde vestro in jejunio et fletu et planctu (Joel. II), et hujusmodi

VERS. 18.-- Venit enim. Sicut tunc ita et nunc utramque viam salutis respuitis. Lamentavimus ad Joannem, cecinimus ad Christum. Haec est summa. Generatio ista nec plorat cum Joanne plorante, neque gaudet cum Christo gaudente. Nec mirum si duplicem viam salutis contemnunt, cum et jejunium et saturitatem petitur spernunt; si jejunium placet, cur Joannes displicet? si saturitas, cur Filius hominis?

122AVERS. 19.-- Et justificata est sapientia, etc. Ostendit filios sapientiae intelligere, nec in abstinendo, nec in manducando esse justitiam: sed in aequanimitate tolerandi inopiam, et temperantiam non se corrumpendi per abundantiam. Non est regnum Dei esca et potus: quorum non usus sed concupiscentia reprehenditur, in quibusdam libris habetur: Justificata est sapientia ab operibus suis, quia sapientia non quaerit vocis testimonium, sed operum.

VERS. 20.-- Tunc. Qui hactenus Judaeos communiter increpavit, nunc nominatim civitates exprimit, quae frequenter audita doctrina et visis tot signis credere noluerunt, quibus non optat mala, sed praedicit ventura

122BVERS. 21.-- Vae tibi, etc. Supra ubi legitur, quia circumibat Jesus civitates et omnes vicos, curans omnem languorem: intelligendum est inter alias etiam in Corozaim et Bethsaida Dominum fecisse virtutes. Corozaim interpretatur mysterium meum; Bethsaida domus fructuum vel venatorum. Si in Tyro, etc. Hoc impletum modo videmus, quia Corozaim et Bethsaida praesente Domino credere noluerunt. Tyrus et Sidon postea praedicantibus Apostolis crediderunt: adeo devoti, ut Paulum Tyro abeuntem omnes cum uxoribus et liberis usque ad naves prosequerentur. Cur ergo non credituris praedicavit, credituros evitavit: ipse scit cujus viae sunt misericordia et veritas. Proposuit tamen fines Judaeae non excedere, ne justam Pharisaeis et sacerdotibus 122Cpersecutionis occasionem daret. Et erant tunc qui in Corozaim essent vocandi: nec tamen de contemptu gravius puniendi.

Tyrus, angustia. Sidon, venatio.

VERS. 23.-- Et tu, Capharnaum. Non solum Corozaim et Bethsaidae vae erit, sed et tibi Capharnaum. Nunquid usque in coelum exaltaberis? Nonne tu es quae fama hospitii mei, et signorum in te factorum famosa efficeris, et contra praedicationem meam superbissime restitisti? Capharnaum interpretatur villa pulcherrima, in qua condemnatur perfida Jerusalem, cui dicitur: Justificata est Sodoma soror tua ex te, id est, ex tui comparatione cum illa, quae non est exemplo Loth correcta, tu Filii Dei verbis et miraculis semper exstitisti ingrata.

122DVERS. 25.-- Respodens Jesus. Respondens tacitae objectionis quasi quaereretur, quae ratio quod Judaicus populus Dei cultor, Dei Filium in carne venientem tam obstinatus rejecit, quem gentilitas tam prona recepit? Domine coeli et terrae. Qui facis coelos, et relinquis in terrenitate quos vis, vel ad litteram. Parvulis. Sapientibus et prudentibus non opponit insipientes et hebetes, sed parvulos, ut probet se tumorem damnare, non acumen.

VERS. 26.-- Ita pater. Blandientis affectu loquitur ut inceptum in apostolis beneficium compleatur. Hic exemplum humilitatis accipimus, ne temere discutere praesumamus superna consilia de vocatione horum, et repulsione illorum; cum enim utrumque 123Aattulisset, non mox rationem reddidit, sed sic Deo placitum dixit, ostendens quia injustum esse non potest quod justo placet. In omnibus ergo quae exterius disponuntur, apertae causa rationis est, occultae justitia voluntatis. Quoniam sic fuit placitum ante te. Ne quis iterum quaerat cur humiles eligat, sapientes reprobet.

VERS. 27.-- Omnia mihi, etc. Tradentem Patrem, accipientem Filium, mystice intellige. Alioquin, si juxta fragilitatem nostram accipis, cum coeperit habere qui accepit, incipiet non habere qui dedit per omnia coomnipotentem nota, per tradita, Filium, cui per naturam omnia pro unius substantiae jure sunt propria, non data per gratiam. Tradita autem omnia non coelum et terra et caetera quae fecit: sed hi qui 123Bper Filium accessum habent ad Patrem, et ante rebelles, Dominum postea sentire coeperunt.

Et nemo novit Filium, etc. Seipsum revelare. Et nemo novit Patrem nisi Filius, et cui Patrem voluit revelare. Ad utrumque enim illud subditum refertur. Verbo enim suo Pater se declarat. Et Verbum seipsum declarat, quia mentis nostrae lumen est.

Nisi Filius, et cui Filius voluerit, etc. Mutua cognitio, quae est aequalitate naturae. Illa autem tantilla quae in nobis est, dignatione revelantis est.

VERS. 29.-- Tollite jugum, etc. Jugum Christi, Evangelium est, quod Judaeos et gentes in una fide conjungit. Tollite super vos. Id est, in honore habete: non subtus, ut contemnatis. Qui laboratis, difficultate legis, et onerati peccatis, scilicet, reficiam, 123Csi jugum tollitis, id est, praecepta mea. Et quid jugo ejus suavius? quid onere levius? probabilem fieri, abstinere a malo, bonum velle, amare omnes, aeterna sequi, praesentibus non capi, nolle alii facere quod pati non vis.

Discite a me. Docente verbo et exemplo, ut sitis humiles mentibus, mites moribus. Humiles, ut neminem contemnant; mites, ut nullum laedant. Corde, ut idem extra in opere sit et in corde, ne lateat serpens in specie columbae.

VERS. 30.-- Jugum enim meum. Quomodo Evangelium lege levius est, cum ibi opera, hic et concupiscentia damnatur? Sed in lege multa jubentur, quae Apostolus impleri non posse dicit: hic voluntas quaeritur quae in arbitrio nostro est, et quae sola sufficere 123Dpotest et praemium habere. Item quomodo jugum Christi suave, cum supra dicitur: Arcta via est quae ducit ad vitam. Sed angusta, quia non nisi angusto initio incipitur, processu vero temporis ineffabili dilectionis dulcedine dilatatur.

Onus meum. Quia illud momentaneum nostrae tribulationis aeternum pondus gloriae operatur in coelis (I Cor. IV).

CHAPTER XI.

VERS. 2.-- Now John. And while Christ was preaching, etc., as far as it does not appear who keeps the order of the thing done.

VERS. 3.-- Art thou he. He does not say who hast come, as if he should say: Since I am about to descend to the lower regions: I ought to announce thee to those below, who have announced thee to those above. Is it fitting for the Son of God to die, or art thou about to send another for these sacraments 120C?

VERS. 6.-- He that shall not be scandalized. He who shall not have doubted of the power of the divinity, he is not to be thought to have said this against John, whom he presently commends so greatly.

VERS. 7.-- Concerning John. The forerunner of Christ wished to make his disciples certain concerning Christ, because he perceived that they doubted. Concerning the forerunner, Christ taught the doubting crowds not to doubt, since they thought him soft and light.

VERS. 8.-- Behold they who are clothed in soft garments, etc. Who put pillows under the elbow, who soothe the life of sinners, and do not prick them.

A rigorous life and preaching ought to shun the palaces of the soft, which those frequent who flatter men clothed in soft garments.

(CLEM. ALEX.) Blessed John, despising even the hairs of sheep, which savour of delicacies, [chose] the hairs of camels, 120Detc., as far as he removed himself from all empty zeal, and from the vain appearance of good, and from a sordid and illiberal elegance.

VERS. 9.-- More than a prophet. (BEDA.) Because he is an angel not by nature but by office.

VERS. 11.-- Greater than John. In this he is not preferred to the patriarchs and to all men, but is shown equal to the other saints. But he that. That is, every saint who is now with God is greater than he who is still in the world. Or I, whom the opinion of many makes less, am set before him in the Church of the saints. Or the last angel of heaven is better than any man whatever. In the kingdom. The kingdom of heaven is taken in two ways: either the one above, in which we are not yet: 121Aor the present Church, in which we still live, and already in a manner reign.

VERS. 12.-- From the days of John. The Lord had bidden the apostles go to the sheep which had perished of the house of Israel, that they might be brought back to the kingdom. But this preaching turned to the profit of publicans and sinners, who believed. For the rest, John is not believed by the people, Christ's works are given no authority, because the cross is for a scandal. Now prophecy ceases, now the law is fulfilled; Christ is preached to some, and received by others: his own reject him, his enemies accept him. And so the kingdom of heaven suffers violence, because the glory of Israel, owed to the fathers, announced by the prophets, offered by Christ, is seized and carried off by the faith of the Gentiles. Or, from the days of John, who preached his presence, by which the sinner comes to life again: 121Band not before, because the law brought in punishment and did not give life through penance. The heavenly fatherland suffers violence while the sinner enters into it, as into a place not his own, through penance. It is by a great violence to be born on earth and to lay hold of heaven, and to have through virtue what we cannot have through nature.

VERS. 13.-- For all. Not that after John there were not some, as Agabus, but because whatever they prophesied, they foretold of the Lord. For all. Because whatever they said, they spoke of one to come, but he pointed out the one who was coming.

VERS. 14.-- And if you will receive it. That you may be the less excusable, because you did not receive him. He is Elias in power, not in person, equal in severity of life, going before by rigour of mind Christ's first coming, 121Cas that other the second.

VERS. 16.-- But to whom, etc. John being praised, he passes to rebuking those who were not moved by John's preaching: or who despise the preaching of Christ himself. John is such and so great as I have said, and so worthy that he ought to be believed: but to whom are you to be likened, who would not believe him? He is like to children. The children are teachers humble in spirit. Their synagogue, or Jerusalem itself, where the ordinances of God's precepts were laid up. Their equals, their fellow-tribesmen, whom they used to reproach, because they did not rise up to the praises of God with a devout heart, with the nimbleness of limbs which the dance marks. Nor by the lamentations of the prophets for the destructions already wrought or yet to be wrought were they turned to penance.

121DVERS. 17.-- We have piped to you. As David: Rejoice in God our helper, sing aloud to the God of Jacob (Psal. LXXX), and the like. We have lamented. As: Be converted to me with your whole heart, in fasting and in weeping and in mourning (Joel. II), and the like

VERS. 18.-- For John came. As then, so now also you reject both ways of salvation. We lamented to John, we piped to Christ. This is the sum. This generation neither weeps with John weeping, nor rejoices with Christ rejoicing. Nor is it a wonder if they despise the twofold way of salvation, when they spurn both fasting and the fullness that is sought; if fasting pleases, why does John displease? if fullness, why the Son of man?

122AVERS. 19.-- And wisdom is justified, etc. He shows that the sons of wisdom understand that righteousness is neither in abstaining nor in eating: but in an evenness of mind in bearing want, and a temperance in not corrupting oneself through abundance. The kingdom of God is not meat and drink: of which not the use but the concupiscence is blamed, in certain books it stands: Wisdom is justified by her works, because wisdom does not seek the testimony of the voice, but of works.

VERS. 20.-- Then. He who hitherto rebuked the Jews in general, now names the cities by name, which, having often heard his doctrine and seen so many signs, would not believe, to whom he does not wish evils, but foretells the things that are to come

122BVERS. 21.-- Woe to thee, etc. Above, where it is read that Jesus went about the cities and all the towns, healing every disease: it is to be understood that among the others the Lord wrought miracles also in Corozain and Bethsaida. Corozain is interpreted my mystery; Bethsaida the house of fruits or of hunters. If in Tyre, etc. We now see this fulfilled, because Corozain and Bethsaida would not believe when the Lord was present. Tyre and Sidon afterwards believed when the Apostles preached: so devoted that all, with their wives and children, followed Paul as far as the ships when he departed from Tyre. Why then he preached to those who would not believe, and avoided those who would believe: he himself knows, whose ways are mercy and truth. Yet he purposed not to go beyond the borders of Judaea, lest he should give the Pharisees and the priests a just 122Coccasion of persecution. And there were then those in Corozain who were to be called: nor yet were they to be punished more heavily for their contempt.

Tyre, narrowness. Sidon, hunting.

VERS. 23.-- And thou, Capharnaum. Not to Corozain and Bethsaida only shall there be woe, but to thee also, Capharnaum. Shalt thou be exalted up to heaven? Art not thou she who art made famous by the report of my lodging and of the signs wrought in thee, and hast most proudly withstood my preaching? Capharnaum is interpreted a most beautiful village, in which perfidious Jerusalem is condemned, to which it is said: Sodom thy sister is justified by thee, that is, by the comparison of thee with her, who was not corrected by the example of Lot, thou, to the words and miracles of the Son of God, hast always proved ungrateful.

122DVERS. 25.-- Jesus answering. Answering of a tacit objection, as though it were asked what the reason was that the Jewish people, a worshipper of God, so obstinately rejected the Son of God coming in the flesh, whom the Gentile world so readily received? Lord of heaven and earth. Thou who makest the heavens, and leavest in earthliness whom thou wilt, or according to the letter. To little ones. To the wise and prudent he does not oppose the foolish and the dull, but little ones, that he may prove that he condemns swelling, not sharpness.

VERS. 26.-- Yea, Father. He speaks with the affection of one caressing, that the benefit begun in the apostles may be completed. Here we take an example of humility, that we may not rashly presume to discuss the counsels above concerning the calling of these and the rejection of those; for when he had brought forward both, 123Ahe did not at once render a reason, but said that it so pleased God, showing that what pleases the Just One cannot be unjust. In all things therefore which are ordered outwardly, there is the cause of an open reason, the justice of a hidden will. Because so it hath seemed good before thee. Lest anyone should again ask why he chooses the humble and rejects the wise.

VERS. 27.-- All things are delivered to me, etc. Understand mystically the Father delivering, the Son receiving. Otherwise, if thou takest it according to our frailty, when he who has received has begun to have, he who gave will begin not to have, by "all things" note the co-omnipotent, by "delivered", the Son, to whom by nature all things are his own by the right of one substance, not given through grace. But all things delivered are not heaven and earth and the rest which he made: but those who 123Bthrough the Son have access to the Father, and who, rebels before, afterwards began to perceive the Lord.

And no one knoweth the Son, etc. To reveal himself. And no one knoweth the Father but the Son, and he to whom he willed to reveal the Father. For that which is subjoined refers to both. For by his Word the Father declares himself. And the Word declares itself, because it is the light of our mind.

But the Son, and he to whom the Son will, etc. A mutual knowledge, which is by equality of nature. But that little which is in us is of the condescension of him who reveals.

VERS. 29.-- Take up my yoke, etc. The yoke of Christ is the Gospel, which joins Jews and Gentiles in one faith. Take it upon you. That is, hold it in honour: not underneath, that you should despise it. You who labour, by the difficulty of the law, and are burdened with sins, namely, I will refresh you, 123Cif you take up the yoke, that is, my precepts. And what is sweeter than his yoke? what lighter than his burden? to become approved, to abstain from evil, to will the good, to love all men, to follow after eternal things, not to be taken by things present, to be unwilling to do to another what thou art unwilling to suffer.

Learn of me. He teaching by word and by example, that you may be humble in mind, meek in manners. Humble, that they may despise no one; meek, that they may injure none. In heart, that the same be outwardly in work as in the heart, lest the serpent lie hidden in the appearance of a dove.

VERS. 30.-- For my yoke. How is the Gospel lighter than the law, when there works are condemned, here concupiscence also? But in the law many things are commanded which the Apostle says cannot be fulfilled: here the will is required, which is in our own choice, and which alone can suffice 123Dand have a reward. Again, how is the yoke of Christ sweet, when it is said above: Narrow is the way that leadeth to life. But it is narrow, because it is begun only with a narrow beginning, but in the course of time is widened with the unspeakable sweetness of love.

My burden. Because that which is momentary of our tribulation worketh an eternal weight of glory in the heavens (I Cor. IV). [Vulg. 2 Cor 4:17 operatur in nobis, "worketh in us"]

CAPUT XII.

VERS. 1.-- In illo tempore. Narrata praedicatione et miraculis unius anni ante quaestionem Joannis factis, transit ad illa quae facta sunt alio anno, scilicet, post mortem Joannis, quando jam in omnibus Christo contradicitur. Esurientes, etc. Quia, ut alius 124Aait evangelista, nec locum vescendi habebant pro turbis: propter nimiam importunitatem eorum. Alii Evangelistae addunt: manibus confricasse (Luc. VI). Unde et Petro dicitur: Macta et manduca (Act. X).

VERS. 2.-- Non licet. Secundum praeceptum legis. Sed quia alia scriptura dicit: Misericordiam malo quam sacrificium (Ose. VI), melius fuit hominem de famis periculo liberare, quam sacrificium Deo offerre, quia hostia placens est salus hominum. De vastatione frugum non arguunt, quia licet per legem inde edere.

VERS. 3.-- At ille dixit, etc. In hoc facto nihil aliud instruimur, nisi ut sabbatum non ad litteram, sed spiritualiter intelligamus. Non legistis. Si David et Abimelech non reprehenditur, quorum uterque 124Bmandatum legis probabili necessitate transgressus est, et hic similis causa famis est, cur non idem probatis in apostolis?

VERS. 4.-- Panes propositionis. Quod significavit sacerdotalem cibum transiturum in usum populorum. Vel quia omnes sacerdotes debemus imitari. Vel quod omnes sacerdotes sumus.

VERS. 5.-- Quia sabbatis sacerdotes. Hic sine necessitate ostendit sabbatum violari ab ipsis qui calumniantur. Unum exemplum datum est regiae potestatis de David: aliud sacerdotalis, de his qui per ministerium templi sabbatum violant, ut multo minus ad ipsum pertineat crimen spicarum sabbato vulsarum, qui verus rex et sacerdos est et dominus sabbati.

124CVERS. 6.-- Dico autem vobis. Quasi dicat: Si figurale templum potuit defendere sacerdotes sibi servientes, cur spirituale non potest defendere discipulos sibi credentes?

VERS. 7.-- Si autem. Et vos ostenderet omnia futuri temporis speciem continere, adjecit: Si autem sciretis, id est, quia Deus per gratiam disposuit liberare populum, et non per exteriores observantias, non reprehenderetis meos, si vetera omittunt.

VERS. 8.-- Dominus est enim. Si David pastus et sacerdotes templi ministerio sabbatum violantes sine crimine sunt, quanto magis Filius hominis, qui verus est rex et sacerdos et dominus sabbati?

VERS. 10.-- Et ecce homo. Iste homo dicitur 124Dcaementarius fuisse, quia manus cui spiritualis aedificii cura commissa fuit, arida erat usque ad tempus Christi. Ecce homo manum, etc. Significat humanum genus in foecunditate boni operis arefactum pro manu ad pomum extensa, quam sanavit manus innocens in cruce extensa. Bene manus in Synagoga erat arida, quia ubi majus donum scientiae, ibi transgressor majori subjacet culpae.

Interrogabant. Quia discipulos de violatione sabbati probabili exemplo excusaverat, ipsum volunt calumniari, ut si non curet, crudelitatis, vel imbecillitatis: vel si curet, transgressionis arguant.

Sabbatis, etc. Praecipue sabbatis docet et operatur Jesus, non solum propter speciale sabbatum, 125Ased et propter celebriorem populi conventum, quaerens salutem omnium.

VERS. 11.-- Quis erit. Competenti exemplo solvit quaestionem, ut eos ostendat sabbatum violare in opere cupiditatis, qui eum violare arguunt in opere charitatis. Arguit eos qui legem male interpretantur, qui dicunt in sabbato a bonis operibus feriandum. Sed a malis tantum feriandum est; unde dicitur: Omne opus servile non facietis in eo (Levit. XXIII). Id est, peccatum. Sic et in aeterna requie a malis tantum feriabuntur, non a bonis.

VERS. 13.-- Extende. Quia nil plus valet curationi eleemosynarum largitate, unde dicitur: Non sit manus tua ad accipendum porrecta, sed ad dandum collecta (Eccl. IV). Quia frustra pro peccatis rogaturus 125Bmanus ad Deum expandit, qui has ad pauperes pro posse non extendit.

Sicut altera. Homo ante adventum Christi dexteram habuit languidam, quia ab eleemosynis torpebat: sinistram sanam quia suae utilitati intendebat: sed veniente Christo dextera sanatur ut sinistra: quia quod congregaverat avide, modo distribuit charitate.

VERS. 15.-- Recessit inde. Nota exemplum: Si vos persecuti sunt in una civitate, fugite in aliam (Matth. X). Secuti sunt. Docti visis virtutibus persequuntur, indocti opinione ejus ducti amando sequuntur, unde et curari merentur.

VERS. 16.-- Et praecepit. Non timore, ne proditus occideretur, sed parcendo Judaeis, ne invidia trucidarentur 125Cin ejus innocentia et mansuetudine. Hic mystice nos instruit, ne cum aliquid magnifacimus, laudem foris quaeramus.

VERS. 18.-- Animae meae. Per animam omnis affectus demonstratur.

VERS. 20.-- Arundinem. Judaeos, omni vento cedentes, qui ab invicem discrepando dissipati sunt, non mox damnabit, sed supportabit. Arundinem quassatam. Qui peccanti non porrigit manum, nec portat onus fratris, quassatum calamum confringit. Et qui fidei modicam scintillam contemnit in parvulis, linum fumigans exstinguit: quod Christus non fecit, qui potius venit quaerere quod perierat; patientiam namque servavit, donec judicium quod in eo agebatur, ad victoriam perveniret, et expulso 125Dmundi principe ad regnum rediret, in dextera Patris sedens, donec ponat inimicos sub pedibus. Linum fumigans. Gentes, quas naturalis legis ardore fere exstincto fumus erroris amarissimus, et odoris noxius involvit: quas non exstinxit, sed de parva scintilla in magnum ardorem ignis Dei suscitavit. Donec. Tandiu praedicatio ejus nullis insidiis superata mundo lucet, donec ponat in terra judicium, et impleatur: Fiat voluntas tua, sicut in coelo et in terra.

VERS. 22.-- Et curavit. Tria signa simul facta sunt quae et quotidie in conversione hominum fiunt, ut expulso daemone lumen fidei videant et Deum laudent.

126AVERS. 24.-- In Beelzebub. Beel, id est, Baal: muscarum, ob sordes immolatitii sanguinis: deus est Accaron, quo nomine vocant principem daemoniorum.

VERS. 25.-- Omne regnum. Necessaria complexione eos arguit. Vel enim Christus virtute Dei daemones ejicit, vel in principe daemoniorum. Si virtute Dei, frustra calumniantur. Si in principe daemoniorum, regnum ejus divisum est, nec stabit: et ideo a regno ejus recedant: quod innuit eos sibi elegisse, dum in se non credunt.

VERS. 26.-- Et omnis civitas. Eadem ratio est civitatis et domus, quae regni. Civitas Jerusalem semper gloriosa, postquam in Christum et in Apostolos exarsit, facta divisione, excidio cecidit.

126BVERS. 27.-- Et si ego in Beelzebub. Si expulsio daemonum in filiis vestris Deo non daemonibus deputatur, quare in me idem opus eamdem non habet causam?

Ipsi, etc. Contemptibilia mundi in quibus sancta simplicitas meae virtutis apparet. Vel ita potius: Filii, id est, Apostoli qui bene sibi conscii erant, nihil malae artis ab eo didicisse: ideo ipsi testes mei judicii, judicantes duodecim tribus Israel.

VERS. 28.-- Si autem ego. Illa pars: Si ego in Beelzebub ejicio daemones, ad plura inconvenientia perducta est, modo agitur de alia parte complexionis. Si autem ego in spiritu Dei. Hic Lucas ait: in digito (Luc. XI). Digitus dicitur Spiritus sanctus pro partitione donorum. Regnum Dei, vel seipsum dicit. 126CUnde alibi: Medius inter vos stetit, quem vos nescitis (Luc. XI, Joan. I). Vel, regnum quod Joannes et ipse Jesus praedicavit. Poenitentiam agite: appropinquabit enim regnum coelorum (Matth. XXI). Est et tertium regnum Scripturae sanctae, quod aufertur a Judaeis et datur genti facienti fructum ejus. Vel regnum, id est, omnes mundi partes, Apostolis et successoribus eorum ad convertendum distribuit.

VERS. 29.-- Aut quomodo potest. Ostendit ergo Dominus per manifestam parabolam, quod non concordat in fallaci operatione cum daemonibus sicut calumniabantur, sed virtute divinitatis homines a daemonio liberaret; ideoque grande scelus committunt, qui quod Dei esse cognoverant, diaboli esse clamabant. Neque enim possunt esse concordes: 126Dquorum opera diversa sunt. Unde addit: Qui non est mecum, etc.

Domum illius. Vel domum, id est, omnes mundi partes Apostolis et eorum successoribus ad convertendum distribuit.

VERS. 30.-- Qui non est mecum, etc. Id est, qui dissimilia meis facit opera, contrarius mihi est: ille trahit ad vitia, ego ad virtutes.

VERS. 31.-- Omne peccatum et blasphemia. Quod humana fragilitate contrahitur, remissibile est, si congregatio non deseratur. Si vero diabolica obstinatione de dispersione ad congregationem nunquam reditur, peccatum insolubile judicatur.

(HIER.) Omne peccatum. Non omnia peccata omnibus 127Aremittuntur, sed his, etc., usque ad omnes veniam consecuturos post universale judicium, transactis licet multis saeculis, asserens.

Spiritus autem. Qui manifeste opera Dei intelligit, cum de virtute negare non possit, si stimulatus: invidia calumniatur, et dicit esse Beelzebub, isti non dimittetur. Non quod et ei negetur remissio si poeniteret: sed quia hic blasphemus, exigentibus meritis suis malis, sicut nunquam ad remissionem, ita nec ad poenitentiam perventurus sit. Sicut Joannes evangelista de quibusdam blasphemiae suae merito excaecatis ait: Propterea non poterant credere (Joan. X). Quod iterum dixit Isaias: Excaecavit oculos eorum (Isa. VI). Qui Spiritum sanctum non esse, vel minorem Patre credunt, et dicunt: quia non invidia 127Bdiabolica, sed humana ignorantia hoc faciunt, irremissibili crimine non tenentur. Sed qui in similitudine diaboli contra conscientiam suam majestatem Dei oppugnant, non habent remissionem in aeternum, sicut Marcus aperte declarat: qui posito hoc testimonio Domini subjunxit: Quoniam dicebant, spiritum immundum habet (Matth. III).

VERS. 32.-- Et quicunque. (AMBR.) Etiam qui scandalizatus infirmitate carnis meae, purum hominem vel voratorem suspicatus fuerit, remissibile erit.

Qui autem. Gratiam sancti Spiritus qua poenitentia inspiratur et ad congregationem reditur, non cognoscens impoenitenti corde permanserit: cum hoc jam non sit humanum, sed diabolicum, irremissibile 127Cerit. Neque in hoc saeculo. Quaedam enim culpae in hoc saeculo laxantur: quaedam vero (parvae scilicet) etiam in futuro. Quae quidem post mortem gravant, sed et dimittuntur, si homo bonis actibus in vita, ut ibi dimittantur, promeruit. Neque in futuro. Quia sunt quaedam levia, quae si in hac vita nondum sunt remissa, post mortem gravant, sed et remittuntur, si digni sunt.

VERS. 33. Aut facite. Nihil enim medium quin boni auctoris bona tantum sint opera, et mali mala. Coarctat eos hinc inde complexione, et utroque cornu premit. Si malus est diabolus, non facit bona; si bona sunt, non sunt diaboli.

Aut facite, etc. Mystice arbor bona, Christus; fructus, praedicatio Evangelii; curationes, redemptio. 127DArbor mala diabolus et sui, id est, Scribae et Pharisaei: et caeteri mali fructus, invidia, detractio, blasphemia, haeresis et hujusmodi.

VERS. 34.-- Progenies viperarum, etc. Ostendit eos malam arborem: et fructus afferre blasphemiae. Ex abundantia. Homo saepe fallitur, qui pensat cor ex ore, et opere. Sed Deus opus et verba ex corde quod novit judicat. Illi ergo ex abundantia cordis os loquitur qui scit ex qua intentione exit.

VERS. 35.-- Bonus homo. Thesaurus cordis intentio est, ex qua Deus judicat opera, ut et aliquando minora majorem, majora minorem habeant mercedem. Hoc in loco, vel Judaeos ostendit de quali thesauro proferant blasphemiam. Vel cum superioribus 128Ahaeret, quod sicut bonus homo non potest proferre mala, vel malus bona, sic nec Christus mala, nec diabolus bona opera potest facere.

VERS. 36.-- Verbum otiosum. Si de otioso verbo reddet homo rationem in die judicii, quanto magis vos qui opera sancti Spiritus dicitis esse Beelzebub.

(GREG.) Otiosum verbum est, quod aut utilitate rectitudinis, aut ratione justae necessitatis caret, quod scilicet, etc., usque ad hic non otiosi verbi sed criminosi tenebitur reus.

VERS. 37. Ex verbis. Mors et vita in manibus linguae.

VERS. 38.-- Tunc responderunt. Cum Scribae et Pharisaei verba nullatenus possent improbare, in operibus quaerunt tentare, quaerentes signa videre, 128Bquasi non fuerint signa quae viderant.

VERS. 39.-- Nisi signum Jonae. Ostendit Judaeos ad instar Ninivitarum criminosos, et nisi poeniteant subversioni proximos. Sed sicut illis et denuntiatur supplicium, et demonstratur remedium: ita Judaei non debent desperare veniam, si saltem postquam Christus resurrexit a mortuis, commoniti egerint poenitentiam: quia mors Christi reconciliatio est mundi.

VERS. 40.-- Sicut enim fuit Jonas. Jonas, id est, dolens, significat illum qui dolores nostros portavit. Vel columba: Piscis qui voravit in pelago, mors quam passus est in mundo. Sicut ejectus est in aridam, sic Christus surrexit in gloriam.

Tribus diebus. More antiquorum, qui diem cum 128Cnocte praecedente computabant. Partem diei qua sepultus est cum praeterita nocte, pro nocte et die accipe, hoc est pro toto die per synecdochen. Sabbati noctem et diem integre. Noctem Dominicam cum eadem die illucescente, partem pro toto, et ita habes tres dies et tres noctes. Primi dies propter futurum hominis lapsum a luce in noctem. Isti propter hominis reparationem a tenebris ad lucem computantur.

Mystice. Tres dies, tria tempora, ante legem, sub lege, sub gratia. Tres noctes, tres mortui quos suscitavit Dominus.

VERS. 41.-- Viri Ninivitae. Et est sensus: Jonas paucis diebus et sine signis praedicavit genti incredulae, et profecit: ego longo tempore, et populo 128DDei, et compluribus signis, in vanum praedico. Eo magis indigni sunt venia, qui ex lege sunt infideles, quo plus est fidei in his, qui legem nescierunt. Ninive, speciosa: signat gentilitatem quam Christus amavit foedam, ut faceret pulchram. In Ninivitis et regina Austri, fides Ecclesiae signatur, quae non minus per poenitentiam peractae insipientiae quam per industriam discendae sapientiae Deo conciliatur. Duae enim partes sunt Ecclesiae, quae peccare nescit, et quae peccare desistit, poenitentia enim peccatum abolet, sapientia cavet.

VERS. 43.-- Cum autem, etc. Quasi dicat: Vos non estis similes Ninivitis vel reginae Austri: quia me praedicante poenitentiam non poenituistis, et me 129Adocente sapientiam non didicistis. Sed estis similes homini a quo diabolus exclusus iterum ingreditur.

Distat inter opera mea qui polluta mundo, et Satanae, qui mundata gravius contaminat.

Ambulat per loca. Explorat corda fidelium a mollitie fluxae cogitationis purgata, si quos gressus ibi figere possit quod, quia nequit, ad humida redit.

VERS. 44.-- Revertar in domum. Sub umbra dormit, in secreto calami, et locis humentibus. Umbra tenebrosa, conscientia: calamus foris nitidus, intus vacuus, simulator: locus humidus, lasciva mollisque mens.

VERS. 45.-- Septem alios spiritus. Septem vitia, septem virtutibus spiritualibus contraria. Nequiores. 129BQuia non modo habet vitia, sed et bona simulat. Tanto nequiores, quanto callidiores. Septem, quia tot erant gratiae cum Christo destinatae.

Septem vitia, septem daemonia septem spiritibus contraria spiritui sapientiae et intellectus, etc.

Sic erit, etc. Quod de una sola re dixit, hoc in hac tota gente fit. Immundus Spiritus a Judaeis exivit, quando legem acceperunt. Ambulavit ad gentes, quae cum postea Domino crederent, non invento loco in eis dixit: Revertar in Judaeos vacantes praesidio Dei et angelorum, increpationibus et correptionibus mundatos, ornatos superfluis observationibus. Adduntur septem, quia multo majore numero daemonum possidentur blasphemantes Jesum quam in Aegypto possessi fuerunt. Contra septem spiritus 129Cvirtutum est numerus vitiorum.

VERS. 46.-- Ecce mater ejus. (RAB.) Mystice. Mater et fratres populus est Judaeorum qui Jesu intus docente non possunt intrare, quia spiritualiter ejus verba, etc., usque ad Fratres Domini sunt consobrini ejus, filii Mariae materterae ejus: quae dicitur mater Jacobi minoris et Joseph, et Judae et Simonis.

VERS. 47.-- Dixit autem ei. Insidiose, utrum spirituali operi carnem praeferat: et ideo se nosse dissimulat matrem et proximos per cognitionem, sed per conjunctionem spiritus esse sibi proximos dicit.

VERS. 48.-- Quae est mater mea? Non recusat obsequia matris, sed praefert ministeria Patris: fratres 129Dnon contemnit, sed opus spirituale praefert cognationi, ut et nos carni spiritum praeferamus. Unde: Qui amat patrem aut matrem plus quam me, non est me dignus (Matth. X). Ecce mater mea, etc. Hi sunt mater mea, qui me in credentium cordibus generant. Hi sunt fratres, qui faciunt opera Patris mei. Fratres et soror credendo, mater praedicando.

CHAPTER XII.

VERS. 1.-- At that time. The preaching and the miracles of one year, done before John's question, having been related, he passes over to those things which were done in another year, namely, after John's death, when now in all things Christ is contradicted. Being hungry, etc. Because, as another 124Aevangelist says, they had not even room to eat, for the crowds: on account of their exceeding importunity. The other Evangelists add: that they rubbed them with their hands (Luc. VI). Whence also it is said to Peter: Slaughter and eat (Act. X) [Vulg. Act 10:13 occide, et manduca, "kill, and eat"].

VERS. 2.-- It is not lawful. According to the precept of the law. But because another scripture says: I choose mercy rather than sacrifice (Ose. VI) [Vulg. Os 6:6 misericordiam volui, et non sacrificium, "I have willed mercy, and not sacrifice"; the comparison is Migne's, and the gloss argues from it], it was better to free a man from the peril of famine than to offer sacrifice to God, since the victim that pleases is the salvation of men. Of the wasting of the crops they bring no charge, because by the law it is lawful to eat thereof.

VERS. 3.-- But he said, etc. In this deed we are taught nothing else, save that we should understand the sabbath not according to the letter, but spiritually. Have ye not read. If David and Abimelech are not reproved, each of whom 124Btransgressed the commandment of the law under a probable necessity, and here the cause of hunger is like theirs, why do you not approve the same in the apostles?

VERS. 4.-- The loaves of proposition. Which signified that the priestly food would pass over into the use of the peoples. Or because we ought all to imitate priests. Or because we are all priests.

VERS. 5.-- That on the sabbaths the priests. Here he shows that the sabbath is violated without necessity by those very men who bring the charge. One example is given of the royal power, from David: another of the priestly, from those who by the ministry of the temple violate the sabbath, so that much less does the crime of the ears of corn plucked on the sabbath pertain to him who is the true king and priest and lord of the sabbath.

124CVERS. 6.-- But I say to you. As if he should say: If the figurative temple could defend the priests that served it, why cannot the spiritual defend the disciples that believe in it?

VERS. 7.-- But if. And that he might show you that all things contain the figure of the time to come, he added: But if you knew, that is, that God has appointed to free the people by grace, and not by outward observances, you would not reprove mine, if they let the old things go.

VERS. 8.-- For he is Lord. If David when he was fed, and the priests who violate the sabbath by the ministry of the temple, are without crime, how much more the Son of man, who is the true king and priest and lord of the sabbath?

VERS. 10.-- And behold a man. This man is said 124Dto have been a mason, because the hand to which the care of the spiritual building had been committed was withered until the time of Christ. Behold a man having a hand, etc. It signifies the human race dried up in the fruitfulness of good work, in requital for the hand stretched out to the apple, which hand the innocent hand stretched out upon the cross healed. Well was the hand withered in the Synagogue, because where the gift of knowledge is greater, there the transgressor lies under a greater fault.

They asked. Because he had excused the disciples concerning the violation of the sabbath by a probable example, they wish to accuse him himself, so that, if he should not heal, they may charge him with cruelty or with weakness: or if he should heal, with transgression.

On the sabbaths, etc. Chiefly on the sabbaths does Jesus teach and work, not only on account of the special sabbath, 125Abut also on account of the more crowded gathering of the people, seeking the salvation of all.

VERS. 11.-- What man shall there be. By a fitting example he resolves the question, that he may show that they violate the sabbath in a work of covetousness who charge him with violating it in a work of charity. He reproves those who interpret the law ill, who say that on the sabbath one must keep holiday from good works. But one must keep holiday from evil ones only; whence it is said: Ye shall not do any servile work therein (Levit. XXIII). That is, sin. So also in the eternal rest they shall keep holiday from evil things only, not from good.

VERS. 13.-- Stretch forth. Because nothing avails more for healing than largeness in almsgiving, whence it is said: Let not thy hand be stretched out to receive, but closed to give (Eccl. IV) [Vulg. Sir 4:36 joins the two members with et, not sed, so that the single negative governs both]. For in vain does he spread out 125Bhis hands to God when about to entreat for his sins, who does not stretch them out to the poor according to his power.

As the other. Man before the coming of Christ had his right hand feeble, because he was sluggish toward almsgiving: his left hand sound, because he was intent upon his own advantage: but at Christ's coming the right hand is healed like the left: because what he had gathered greedily, he now distributes in charity.

VERS. 15.-- He departed thence. Mark the example: If they have persecuted you in one city, flee into another (Matth. X) [Vulg. Mt 10:23 Cum autem persequentur vos in civitate ista, "But when they shall persecute you in this city"]. They followed. The learned, when they have seen his miracles, persecute; the unlearned, led by his repute, follow him in love, whence also they deserve to be healed.

VERS. 16.-- And he charged. Not out of fear, lest being betrayed he should be killed, but sparing the Jews, lest out of envy they should be slaughtered 125Cin the face of his innocence and meekness. Here he instructs us mystically, that when we make much of anything, we should not seek praise abroad.

VERS. 18.-- My soul. By the soul every affection is shown.

VERS. 20.-- The reed. The Jews, yielding to every wind, who by disagreeing among themselves have been scattered, he will not straightway condemn, but will bear with them. The bruised reed. He who does not stretch out a hand to one that sins, nor bear his brother's burden, breaks the bruised reed. And he who despises the small spark of faith in the little ones, quenches the smoking flax: which Christ did not do, who came rather to seek that which had perished; for he kept patience, until the judgment that was being carried on in him should come through to victory, and, the prince of the world being cast out, 125Dhe should return to his kingdom, sitting at the right hand of the Father, until he put his enemies under his feet. Smoking flax. The Gentiles, whom, the ardour of the natural law being well-nigh quenched, the most bitter smoke of error, and hurtful of odour, wrapped about: whom he did not quench, but from a small spark he roused up into a great burning of the fire of God. Until. So long does his preaching, overcome by no snares, shine to the world, until he set judgment in the earth, and this be fulfilled: Thy will be done, as in heaven and in earth.

VERS. 22.-- And he healed. Three signs were wrought together, which are also wrought daily in the conversion of men, that, the demon being cast out, they may see the light of faith and praise God.

126AVERS. 24.-- In Beelzebub. Beel, that is, Baal: of flies, on account of the filth of the sacrificial blood: he is the god of Accaron, by which name they call the prince of the demons.

VERS. 25.-- Every kingdom. He convicts them by a necessary dilemma. For either Christ casts out demons by the power of God, or in the prince of the demons. If by the power of God, they accuse him in vain. If in the prince of the demons, his kingdom is divided, and shall not stand: and therefore let them withdraw from his kingdom: which he hints that they have chosen for themselves, while they do not believe in him.

VERS. 26.-- And every city. The same reason holds of a city and of a house as of a kingdom. The city Jerusalem, ever glorious, after it blazed out against Christ and against the Apostles, division being made, fell in destruction.

126BVERS. 27.-- And if I in Beelzebub. If the casting out of demons in your sons is ascribed to God and not to demons, why in me does the same work not have the same cause?

They themselves, etc. The contemptible things of the world, in which the holy simplicity of my power appears. Or rather thus: The sons, that is, the Apostles, who were well aware in themselves that they had learned nothing of evil art from him: therefore they themselves are the witnesses of my judgment, judging the twelve tribes of Israel.

VERS. 28.-- But if I. That member: If I cast out demons in Beelzebub, has been carried through to many absurdities; now the other member of the dilemma is handled. But if I in the spirit of God. Here Luke says: in the finger (Luc. XI). The Holy Spirit is called a finger, on account of the parcelling out of gifts. By the kingdom of God, either he means himself. 126CWhence elsewhere: There hath stood among you one whom you know not (Luc. XI, Joan. I) [Vulg. Jo 1:26 medius autem vestrum stetit, "there hath stood in the midst of you"]. Or the kingdom which John and Jesus himself preached. Do penance: for the kingdom of heaven shall draw near (Matth. XXI). There is also a third kingdom, that of Holy Scripture, which is taken away from the Jews and given to a nation bringing forth the fruit thereof. Or the kingdom, that is, all the parts of the world, which he distributed to the Apostles and their successors to be converted.

VERS. 29.-- Or how can. The Lord therefore shows by a plain parable that he is not in concord with the demons in a deceitful working, as they falsely charged, but that by the power of his divinity he was freeing men from the demon; and therefore they commit a great crime who cried that what they had recognized to be of God was of the devil. For neither can they be in concord: 126Dthey whose works are diverse. Whence he adds: He that is not with me, etc.

His house. Or the house, that is, all the parts of the world, which he distributed to the Apostles and their successors to be converted.

VERS. 30.-- He that is not with me, etc. That is, he who does works unlike mine is contrary to me: he draws to vices, I to virtues.

VERS. 31.-- Every sin and blasphemy. What is contracted by human frailty is remissible, if the congregation be not forsaken. But if by diabolical obstinacy there is never a return from dispersion to the congregation, the sin is judged indissoluble.

(HIER.) Every sin. Not all sins are remitted to all 127A, but to these, etc., as far as asserting that all shall obtain pardon after the universal judgment, though many ages have passed.

But the Spirit. He who manifestly understands the works of God, since he cannot deny their power, if goaded: out of envy he accuses, and says that it is Beelzebub, to him it shall not be forgiven. Not that remission would be denied even to him if he should repent: but because this blasphemer, his own evil merits requiring it, as he will never come to remission, so neither will he come to repentance. As John the evangelist says of certain men blinded by the desert of their blasphemy: Therefore they could not believe (Joan. X). Which Isaias said again: He hath blinded their eyes (Isa. VI). They who believe that the Holy Spirit is not, or is less than the Father, and say so: because they do this not out of diabolical envy, 127Bbut out of human ignorance, they are not held by an irremissible crime. But they who, in the likeness of the devil, assail the majesty of God against their own conscience, have no remission for ever, as Mark plainly declares: who, this testimony of the Lord being set down, subjoined: Because they said, he hath an unclean spirit (Matth. III).

VERS. 32.-- And whosoever. (AMBR.) Even he who, being scandalized at the infirmity of my flesh, shall have suspected me a mere man or a glutton, it shall be remissible.

But he that. He who, not acknowledging the grace of the Holy Spirit by which repentance is inspired and return is made to the congregation, shall have remained with an impenitent heart: since this now is not human but diabolical, it will be irremissible 127C. Neither in this world. For certain faults are loosed in this world: but certain (the small ones, namely) even in the world to come. These indeed weigh heavy after death, but they are also forgiven, if a man by good acts in this life has deserved that they be forgiven there. Neither in the world to come. Because there are certain light ones, which, if in this life they are not yet remitted, weigh heavy after death, but they are also remitted, if they are worthy.

VERS. 33. Either make. For there is nothing between, but that the works of a good author are good only, and those of an evil author evil. He hems them in on this side and on that with a dilemma, and presses them with both horns. If the devil is evil, he does not do good things; if they are good, they are not the devil's.

Either make, etc. Mystically the good tree is Christ; the fruit, the preaching of the Gospel; the healings, redemption. 127DThe evil tree is the devil and his own, that is, the Scribes and Pharisees: and the other evil fruits are envy, detraction, blasphemy, heresy and the like.

VERS. 34.-- Offspring of vipers, etc. He shows that they are an evil tree: and that they bring forth the fruits of blasphemy. Out of the abundance. A man is often deceived who weighs the heart by the mouth and by the deed. But God judges the deed and the words out of the heart, which he knows. To him therefore the mouth speaks out of the abundance of the heart, who knows out of what intention it comes forth.

VERS. 35.-- A good man. The treasure of the heart is the intention, out of which God judges works, so that sometimes the lesser works have a greater reward and the greater a lesser. In this place he either shows out of what sort of treasure the Jews bring forth blasphemy. Or it hangs together with the things above 128A, that as a good man cannot bring forth evil things, nor an evil man good, so neither can Christ do evil works, nor the devil good ones.

VERS. 36.-- An idle word. If a man shall render an account for an idle word in the day of judgment, how much more you who say that the works of the Holy Spirit are Beelzebub.

(GREG.) An idle word is that which lacks either the usefulness of rectitude or the ground of a just necessity, that namely, etc., as far as he shall here be held guilty not of an idle word but of a criminal one.

VERS. 37. By thy words. Death and life are in the hands of the tongue.

VERS. 38.-- Then answered. Since the Scribes and Pharisees could in no way disprove his words, they seek to tempt him in his works, seeking to see signs, 128Bas though the things they had seen were not signs.

VERS. 39.-- But the sign of Jonas. He shows that the Jews are criminal after the likeness of the Ninivites, and, unless they repent, near to overthrow. But as to those men both the punishment is announced and the remedy is shown: so the Jews ought not to despair of pardon, if at least, after Christ has risen from the dead, they be admonished and do penance: because the death of Christ is the reconciliation of the world.

VERS. 40.-- For as Jonas was. Jonas, that is, one grieving, signifies him who bore our griefs. Or a dove: the fish that devoured him in the deep is the death which he suffered in the world. As he was cast out upon the dry land, so Christ rose again into glory.

Three days. After the manner of the ancients, who reckoned the day together with 128Cthe night preceding. Take the part of the day on which he was buried, together with the night gone before, for a night and a day, that is, for a whole day, by synecdoche. The night and the day of the sabbath entire. The Lord's night with the same day dawning, the part for the whole, and so thou hast three days and three nights. The first days are reckoned on account of man's coming fall from light into night. These on account of man's restoration, from darkness to light.

Mystically. Three days, three times, before the law, under the law, under grace. Three nights, the three dead whom the Lord raised up.

VERS. 41.-- The men of Ninive. And the sense is: Jonas in a few days and without signs preached to an unbelieving nation, and prevailed: I over a long time, and to the people 128Dof God, and with very many signs, preach in vain. They are so much the more unworthy of pardon who are unfaithful though they have the law, by how much there is more faith in those who have not known the law. Ninive, comely: it signifies the Gentile world, which Christ loved when it was foul, that he might make it fair. In the Ninivites and the queen of the South the faith of the Church is signified, which is reconciled to God no less by repentance for folly committed than by diligence in learning wisdom. For there are two parts of the Church, that which knows not how to sin, and that which ceases from sinning, for repentance abolishes sin, wisdom guards against it.

VERS. 43.-- But when, etc. As if he should say: You are not like the Ninivites or the queen of the South: because when I preached repentance you did not repent, and when I 129Ataught wisdom you did not learn. But you are like the man from whom the devil, being shut out, enters in again.

There is a difference between my works, who cleanse the things that are polluted, and Satan's, who more grievously defiles the things that have been cleansed.

He walketh through places. He explores the hearts of the faithful, cleansed from the softness of fluid thought, whether he can fix any steps there; and because he cannot, he returns to the damp places.
