VERS. 44.-- I will return into my house. He sleeps under the shadow, in the covert of the reed, and in damp places. The dark shadow is the conscience: the reed, bright without and empty within, is the dissembler: the damp place is the wanton and soft mind.

VERS. 45.-- Seven other spirits. The seven vices, contrary to the seven spiritual virtues. More wicked. 129BBecause he not only has vices, but also counterfeits good things. The more wicked, by so much the more crafty. Seven, because so many were the graces appointed with Christ.

Seven vices, seven demons contrary to the seven spirits, to the spirit of wisdom and of understanding, etc.

So shall it be, etc. What he said of one single case, this comes to pass in this whole nation. The unclean Spirit went out from the Jews when they received the law. He walked to the Gentiles, and when these afterwards believed the Lord, finding no place in them he said: I will return to the Jews, who stand empty of the protection of God and of the angels, cleansed by rebukes and chastisements, adorned with superfluous observances. Seven are added, because they that blaspheme Jesus are possessed by a far greater number of demons than they were possessed by in Egypt. Against the seven spirits 129Cof the virtues is the number of the vices.

VERS. 46.-- Behold his mother. (RAB.) Mystically. The mother and the brethren are the people of the Jews, who, while Jesus teaches within, cannot enter in, because his words spiritually, etc., as far as The brethren of the Lord are his cousins, the sons of Mary his mother's sister: who is called the mother of James the less and of Joseph, and of Jude and of Simon.

VERS. 47.-- And one said to him. Insidiously, to try whether he would prefer the flesh to the spiritual work: and therefore he feigns not to know his mother and his kinsfolk by acquaintance, but says that they are near to him by the joining of the spirit.

VERS. 48.-- Who is my mother? He does not refuse the dutiful service of his mother, but prefers the ministries of the Father: he does not 129Ddespise the brethren, but prefers the spiritual work to kinship, that we too may prefer the spirit to the flesh. Whence: He that loveth father or mother more than me, is not worthy of me (Matth. X). Behold my mother, etc. These are my mother, who beget me in the hearts of them that believe. These are the brethren, who do the works of my Father. Brethren and sister by believing, a mother by preaching.

CAPUT XIII.

VERS. 1.-- In illo die. Per hoc indicat hoc gestum consequenter, nisi forte dies ponatur pro tempore, more Scripturae. Marcus eumdem tenet ordinem. Lucas in aliud transit, sed nec repugnat huic. (RAB.) Non solum facta et dicta Domini, sed et 130Aitinera, etc., usque ad ad quae Judaei non intraverunt, foris in littera fixi.

VERS. 2.-- Turba stabat. Hi sunt qui nuper audire venerunt, qui et a fluctibus maris secreti sunt, necdum coelestibus mysteriis quae desiderant imbuti cum Christo ascendunt.

VERS. 3.-- Locutus est multa. Quia turba non unius est sententiae sed diversarum voluntatum, loquitur eis multis parabolis, ut juxta varias voluptates, diversas recipiant disciplinas. Qui seminat. Ipse Christus, qui verbum veritatis quod apud Patrem vidit, hominibus per se vel per suos seminavit. Qui discipulis sacramenta loquebatur, exiit seminare Dei verbum in turbas.

VERS. 4.-- Secus viam. Via est, mens malarum 130Bcogitationum sedulo meatu trita et arefacta. Ubi dupliciter perit semen, quia et transitu cogitationum conculcatur, ut Lucas ait, et a volucribus rapitur. Daemones dicuntur volucres, seu quia coelestis spiritualisque naturae, seu quia per aera volitant. Et venerunt. Secundum Lucam ab hominibus conculcatum est, quia prava persuasione impiorum sublatum est. Et comederunt. Quia a mentibus hominum illud surripuerunt.

VERS. 5.-- In petrosa. Petrosa cito quaerunt germinare, sed non figitur radix, quia non est alta terra, id est, quia parum inest desiderii salutaris. In hac expositione Domini discimus, quia res non semper in eadem significatione ponuntur. Nam petra hic pro duritia, terra pro lenitate mentis, sol pro fervore 130Cpersecutionis. Cum sol alibi in bono: Fulgebunt justi sicut sol (Matth. XIII), et petra in fundamento, pro fortitudine fidei, et terra pro terrenis cogitationibus. Altitudinem terrae. Altitudo terrae est probitas animi disciplinis coelestibus eruditi, et in tentationibus probati.

VERS. 8.-- Scaagesimum. Quia in numero senario mundi ornatus expletus est, per hunc bona operatio signatur.

VERS. 9.-- Qui habet aures audiendi. Quoties haec admonitio in Evangelio vel in Apocalypsi Joannis interponitur, mysticum quod dicitur insinuatur.

VERS. 10.-- Et accedentes discipuli, etc. Quomodo accedunt discipuli cum Dominus in navi sederet? Sed forte intelligendum, quod dudum cum ipso navem 130Dconscenderint: et ibi stantes quaerunt.

VERS. 11.-- Vobis datum est. Nota non solum verba Domini, sed et facta, parabolas esse: id est, signa mysticarum rerum, quae non intelligebant multi qui foris sunt.

VERS. 12.-- Qui enim habet. Id est, apostolis habentibus fidem, si quid minus virtutum habent, conceditur Judaeis, qui non credunt in Filium Dei, etiam si quid boni per naturam habent, tollitur. Nam nihil possunt sapienter intelligere qui caput non habent sapientiae. Auferetur ab eo. Nulla verae sapientiae dulcedine gaudebit.

VERS. 13.-- Ideo in parabolis. Dicitur quod ideo excaecati sunt, et sententiae Domini per parabolas 131Aeis occultatae sunt, ut post salubrius converterentur. Nam quia obscure dicta non intellexerunt, ideo non crediderunt in eum, et sic crucifixerunt, et sic post resurrectionem miraculis territi, majoris criminis reatu compuncti sunt. Unde accepta indulgentia ampliori flagrant dilectione. Sed Joannes hunc locum ita dicit: Propterea non poterant credere, quia iterum dixit Isaias: Excaevavit oculos eorum, ut non videant (Joan. XI). Ubi aperte dicit non ideo factam excaecationem, ut ea commoniti, et dolentes se non intelligere, converterentur aliquando: non enim hoc possent nisi crederent, sed potius ideo excaecati, ut non crederent, quod aliis peccatis meruerunt. Videntes non vident. Sicut avarus multas habet opes et nullas, quia inutiles sunt ei.

131BVERS. 14.-- Auditu audietis. Haec de turbis prophetata sunt, quia stant in littore, et Domini non merentur audire sermonem.

VERS. 15.-- Oculos suos. Id est, ipsi sibi causa fuerunt ut Deus eis oculos clauderet.

Ne quando. Id est, ideo, quia oculos et aures aperire noluerunt, non meruerunt aliquando converti: quod tamen misericorditer factum est eis.

VERS. 16.-- Beati. Vere beati, quia hoc etiam praesentialiter videre et audire meruerunt, quod nullus priorum quantivis meriti meruit, quamvis multum desideraverit.

VERS. 17.-- Et justi. Lucas justos reges dicit, quia motus suos bene regunt

VERS. 18.-- Vos ergo audite. Primam parabolam 131Cexponit, ut idem attendant in caeteris. Exponit non secundum singula verba, sed summam sententiae.

(AUG. sup. Gen.) Proinde quod narravit evangelista (etiam factum est) Dominum scilicet talia fuisse locutum. Ipsius autem, etc., usque ad illud quippe scripsit narrator rerum praeteritarum, hoc praenuntiator tantummodo futurorum.

VERS. 20.-- Super petrosa. Super petram et in spinis seminantur, qui bene accipiunt: sed vel adversa terrendo, vel prospera blandiendo, retardant eos ne perficiant. Divitiis jungitur sollicitudo custodiae, quae affligit, et voluptas, quae effluere facit.

VERS. 22.-- Fallacia divitiarum. (CHRYSOST.) Suffocantur spinis quae audivimus, non quidem ipsarum spinarum culpa, sed ejus qui illas, etc., usque ad 131Dduo quippe vitia inter se opposita in divitiis sunt, unum quod incendit sollicitudinem, et offuscat curis: alterum quod molliores deliciis reddit.

VERS. 23.-- Aliud quidem centesimum. (RAB.) Tres differentiae sunt in bona terra, sicut fuere in mala. Triginta enim refertur ad nuptias. Sexaginta ad, etc., usque ad tricesimum conjugatorum, quia haec est aetas praeliantium, et his est major conflictus ne libidine superentur.

VERS. 24.-- Aliam parabolam. Quasi dives paterfamilias invitatos diversis reficit cibis, ut unusquisque secundum naturam stomachi sui varia alimenta susciperet. Unde et dixit aliam parabolam et non alteram, ut plures sequi intelligantur. Haec parabola 132Aproponitur, dimissis turbis, domi rogantibus discipulis exponitur. Et docet hic bonam voluntatem, et cautelam, et patientiam, et discretionem, et longanimitatem, et justitiam.

VERS. 25.-- Homines. Qui aliis ad tutelam et custodiam deputati sunt praelati in Ecclesia: vel cum dormitionem mortis accipient apostoli.

VERS. 28.-- Vis, imus. Cum spiritualis homo audit hoc esse a diabolo qui nihil valet contra voluntatem Dei, potest ei voluntas suboriri, ut tales auferat de mundo si facultatem habeat. Sed consulit justitiam Dei, an facere debeat, et an Deus velit esse hoc officium hominum. Sed Veritas respondet: Non nosse hominem in hac vita qualis futurus sit qui modo errat, et quid bonis conferat ejus error: et 132Bideo eos non esse tollendos, ne forte interficiantur, qui forte futuri sunt: vel ne bonis obsit, quibus prosunt, etc. Tunc vero opportune fieri, cum jam non est commutandae vitae tempus et proficiendi aliis: et tunc non ab hominibus, sed ab angelis dicit fieri.

VERS. 29.-- Et ait: Non, etc. Ecce patientia. Non enim audito dolore exarsit in iram, nec mox ulcisci voluit, ita et nos laesi patienter feramus.

Ne forte. Hic datur locus poenitentiae et monemur non cito amputare: quia qui errat hodie, cras forte defendet veritatem. Si ergo modo avelleretur, triticum quod futurum erat eradicaretur. Vel necesse est ut mali permisti sint bonis per quos exerceantur. Vel quorum comparatione nitantur ad melius, quibus 132Csublatis altitudo charitatis marcescit quod est eradicari.

Ibi patienter tolerandi sunt mali, ubi aliqui inveniuntur quibus adjuventur boni.

(AUG.) Multitudo non est excommunicanda, nec princeps populi.

VERS. 30.-- Sinite. Hoc videtur contrarium illi apostolico: Auferte malum ex vobis (I Cor. V). Sed sicut inter lolium et triticum quamdiu herba est, parum distat: ita monet Dominus ne de ambiguo judicemus, quod Dominus in die judicii non suspiciose, sed manifeste damnabit.

In patiendo justitiam meditemur: in judicando justitiam non negligamus.

Et in tempore. Qui longanimiter tulit per justitiam, 132Ddigna retribuit: discretae enim sunt virtutes, sed non contrariae. Et alligate. In hac vita non est alligatio, alioquin non tot ab errore resipiscerent.

VERS. 31.-- Simile est regnum, etc. Regnum coelorum, praedicatio est Evangelii, de qua alibi: Auferetur a vobis regnum Dei, et dabitur genti facienti fructum ejus (Matth. XXI). Vel notitia Scripturarum. Grano sinapis. Grano sinapis comparatur pro fervore fidei, quia dicitur venena expellere, id est, omnia dogmata pravitatis. Potest in grano sinapis humilitas Dominicae incarnationis intelligi. Quo accepto homo misit in hortum suum, quia corpus crucifixi Salvatoris accipiens Joseph in horto sepelivit. Expandit ramos in quibus volucres coeli requiescent, 133Aquia praedicatores dispersit in mundum, in quorum dictis et consolationibus fideles ab hujus vitae fatigatione respirarent.

VERS. 32.-- Cum autem creverit. Toto mundo vel in animo singulorum: quia nemo repente perfectus. Crevit et factum est in arborem, quia surrexit et ascendit in coelum. Vel creverit, id est, mentibus hominum quantae sit virtutis innotuerit. Caetera apparent olera, quae cito crescunt et corruunt: non vivida, sed marcida: illa non olus, sed arbor fit, ubi folia cum fructu, et in ejus diversis sententiis spirituales quiescunt.

Oleribus et fit arbor. Id est, dogmatibus philosophorum, quae cito crescunt et corruunt, nil vitale promittentia.

133BVERS. 33.-- In farinae satis. Ut spiritus, anima, et corpus in unum redacta non discrepent inter se. Vel tres animae virtutes in unum rediguntur, ut in ratione possideamus prudentiam, in ira odium vitiorum, in cupiditate desiderium virtutum. Vel, mulier, Ecclesia, fidem hominum farinae tribus satis miscet, credulitati Patris et Filii et Spiritus sancti. Cumque in unum fuerit fermentatum, non nos ad triplicem Deum, sed unius deitatis notitiam perducit, quia non est diversa natura in singulis.

Donec fermentatum. Tandiu charitas in mente recondita crescere debet, donec mentem totam in sui perfectionem commutet, ut nihil praeter Deum diligat vel recolat, quod hic incipitur, in alia vita perficitur.

VERS. 34.-- In parabolis ad turbas. Quia quidquid 133Cibi dicitur de exercitu Israel, et omnia signa occulta sunt sacramenta.

VERS. 44.-- Thesauro abscondito in agro. Thesaurus potest accipi verbum Dei, qui in carne Christi absconditur. Vel Scriptura, in qua reposita est notitia Salvatoris. Vel, duo Testamenta in Ecclesia, pro quorum intelligentia omnibus renuntiatur ut in otio discantur.

Terrenis regnum coelorum similatur, ut per cognita surgat ad incognita animus, et diligat.

Abscondit et prae gaudio illius. Ut servetur a malignis spiritibus et laudibus hominum. Sic sit opus in publico, ut intentio maneat occulta.

VERS. 45.-- Negotiatori. Beatitudinem qua exuti sumus culpa primi hominis, debemus omnes, intelligendo 133Drecte et operando bene, negotiari. Bonas margaritas. Bonae margaritae, lex et prophetae. Una pretiosa, scientia Salvatoris, pro qua homo, ut Paulus, omnibus mysteriis legis et observationibus renuntiavit.

VERS. 46.-- Inventa autem. Id est, Christo, qui praeest omnibus hominibus, vel spirituali praecepto dilectionis. Vel intellecto verbo, quod erat apud Deum penetrata carnis testudine. Vel coelestis vitae dulcedine, pro qua omnia vilescunt.

VERS. 47.-- Sagenae missae. Impletur Jeremiae vaticinium: Ecce ego mitto ad vos piscatores multos (Jer. XVI). Postquam audierunt Petrus et Andreas, Jacobus et Joannes: Sequimini me, faciam vos piscatores hominum: contexuerunt sibi sagenam ex Veteri 134Aet Novo Testamento, et in mare saeculi miserunt, quae usque hodie trahitur, et in fluctibus tenditur: capiens quidquid incidit, bonos et malos, quos ad littus trahit, id est, ad finem mundi, ut tunc fiat divisio. Unde sequitur: Sic erit in consummatione saeculi, etc. Sagenae comparatur Ecclesia vel praedicatio evangelica, quae a fluctibus saeculi ad, coelum trahit.

VERS. 48.-- Et secus littus. Littus, finis saeculi, in quo boni ad coelum, mali ad tenebras mittentur: in praesenti enim erant permisti.

VERS. 52.-- Ideo omnis Scriba doctus, etc. Quia intelligitis mysteria quae vobis revelo: ideo faciam vos praedicatores, et eritis mihi similes qui de thesauro scientiae meae, nova et vetera profero, ut vos 134Bsimiliter faciatis.

Apostoli sunt scribae et notarii Christi, verba ejus signantes in tabulis cordis, et pollent opibus patrisfamilias, ejicientes de thesauro doctrinarum suarum nova et vetera, quia quidquid in Evangelio praedicatur, legis et prophetarum vocibus comprobant.

VERS. 54.-- Ut mirarentur. Magna caecitas, quem verbis et factis Christum cognoscunt, ob generis notitiam contemnunt.

VERS. 55.-- Fabri filius. Non sine re voluit dici fabri filius, non illius quem putant, sed fabricatoris omnium. Sed hujus sacramenti Judaei ignari divinae virtutis opera carnalis prosapiae contemplatione contemnunt. Non enim considerant praesentia ejus opera, sed fragilem recordantur infantiam.

134CVERS. 57.-- Propheta. Christus dicitur propheta. Unde Moses dicit: Prophetam suscitabit vobis Dominus de semine vestro (Deut. XVIII). In patria. Id est, in populo Judaeorum: majora autem facit in gentibus.

VERS. 58.-- Et non fecit. Fecit virtutes aliquas, sed non multas, ne incredulos condemnaret. Vel despectus pauca facit, ne penitus excusabiles sint, majora autem in gentibus facit.

CHAPTER XIII.

VERS. 1.-- On that day. By this he indicates that this was done next in order, unless perhaps the day be put for the time, after the manner of Scripture. Mark keeps the same order. Luke passes to another matter, yet neither does he contradict this. (RAB.) Not only the deeds and the sayings of the Lord, but his 130Ajourneys also, etc., as far as to which the Jews did not enter in, fixed outside in the letter.

VERS. 2.-- The crowd stood. These are they who have lately come to hear, who are also set apart from the waves of the sea, and do not yet ascend with Christ, steeped in the heavenly mysteries which they desire.

VERS. 3.-- He spoke many things. Because the crowd is not of one mind but of diverse wills, he speaks to them in many parables, that according to their various pleasures they may receive diverse instructions. He that soweth. Christ himself, who sowed among men, by himself or by his own, the word of truth which he had seen with the Father. He who was speaking sacraments to the disciples went out to sow the word of God among the crowds.

VERS. 4.-- By the way side. The way is a mind trodden and dried up by the 130Bbusy passage of evil thoughts. There the seed perishes in two ways, because it is both trampled by the passing of thoughts, as Luke says, and snatched away by the birds. The demons are called birds, either because they are of a heavenly and spiritual nature, or because they fly through the air. And they came. According to Luke it was trodden down by men, because it was taken away by the crooked persuasion of the wicked. And they ate it up. Because they stole it away from the minds of men.

VERS. 5.-- Upon stony ground. Stony places seek to sprout quickly, but the root is not fixed, because the earth is not deep, that is, because there is little of saving desire within. In this exposition of the Lord we learn that things are not always set down in the same signification. For the rock here stands for hardness, the earth for softness of mind, the sun for the heat 130Cof persecution. Whereas elsewhere the sun is in a good sense: The just shall shine as the sun (Matth. XIII), and the rock in a foundation, for the strength of faith, and the earth for earthly thoughts. The depth of earth. The depth of earth is the uprightness of a mind schooled in heavenly disciplines and proved in temptations.

VERS. 8.-- Sixtyfold. Because in the number six the adornment of the world was completed, by this good working is signified.

VERS. 9.-- He that hath ears to hear. As often as this admonition is interposed in the Gospel or in the Apocalypse of John, it is intimated that what is said is mystical.

VERS. 10.-- And the disciples coming, etc. How do the disciples come to him, when the Lord was sitting in the boat? But perhaps it is to be understood that they had some while before gone up into the boat 130Dwith him: and standing there they ask.

VERS. 11.-- To you it is given. Mark that not only the words of the Lord, but his deeds also, are parables: that is, signs of mystical things, which many that are without did not understand.

VERS. 12.-- For he that hath. That is, to the apostles, who have faith, if they have anything less of the virtues, it is granted; from the Jews, who do not believe in the Son of God, even if they have anything of good by nature, it is taken away. For they can understand nothing wisely who have not the head of wisdom. It shall be taken away from him. He shall rejoice in no sweetness of true wisdom.

VERS. 13.-- Therefore in parables. It is said that they were blinded for this reason, and that the Lord's sentences were hidden from them by parables 131A, that afterwards they might be more wholesomely converted. For because they did not understand the things obscurely spoken, therefore they did not believe in him, and so they crucified him, and so, after the resurrection, being terrified by miracles, they were pricked with the guilt of a greater crime. Whence, having received pardon, they burn with a fuller love. But John states this passage thus: Therefore they could not believe, because Isaias said again: He hath blinded Excaevavit their eyes, that they should not see (Joan. XI). Where he plainly says that the blinding was not brought about that, admonished by it and grieving that they did not understand, they might at some time be converted: for they could not do this unless they believed; but rather that they were blinded for this reason, that they should not believe, which they deserved by their other sins. Seeing they see not. As a covetous man has many riches and none, because they are useless to him.

131BVERS. 14.-- With hearing you shall hear. These things were prophesied of the crowds, because they stand on the shore, and do not deserve to hear the discourse of the Lord.

VERS. 15.-- Their eyes. That is, they themselves were the cause to themselves that God should close their eyes.

Lest at any time. That is, therefore, because they would not open their eyes and ears, they did not deserve to be converted at any time: which nevertheless was done mercifully for them.

VERS. 16.-- Blessed. Truly blessed, because they deserved to see and hear even in the present that which none of the former men, of however great merit, deserved, however much he desired it.

VERS. 17.-- And the just. Luke calls the just kings, because they rule their own motions well

VERS. 18.-- Hear you therefore. He expounds the first parable 131C, that they may attend to the same in the rest. He expounds it not according to the several words, but the sum of the meaning.

(AUG. sup. Gen.) Accordingly, what the evangelist related (was also done), namely that the Lord had spoken such things. But of his own, etc., as far as for that a narrator of things past wrote, this only a foreteller of things to come.

VERS. 20.-- Upon stony ground. They are sown upon the rock and among thorns who receive well: but either adversity by terrifying, or prosperity by flattering, holds them back from bringing to perfection. To riches is joined the anxiety of keeping them, which afflicts, and pleasure, which makes a man run to waste.

VERS. 22.-- The deceitfulness of riches. (CHRYSOST.) The things we have heard are choked by thorns, not indeed by the fault of the thorns themselves, but of him who them, etc., as far as 131Dfor there are in riches two vices opposed to one another, one which kindles anxiety and darkens with cares: the other which makes men softer with delights.

VERS. 23.-- Some a hundredfold. (RAB.) There are three differences in the good earth, as there were in the bad. For thirty is referred to marriage. Sixty to, etc., as far as the thirtyfold of the married, because this is the age of them that do battle, and to these the conflict is greater, lest they be overcome by lust.

VERS. 24.-- Another parable. As a rich householder refreshes those he has invited with diverse foods, that each one might take various nourishments according to the nature of his own stomach. Whence he also said another parable and not the other, that more may be understood to follow. This parable 132Ais set forth, and, the crowds being dismissed, it is expounded at home to the disciples who ask. And here he teaches good will, and caution, and patience, and discretion, and long-suffering, and justice.

VERS. 25.-- Men. Those who are appointed over others in the Church for their protection and keeping: or when the apostles shall receive the sleep of death.

VERS. 28.-- Wilt thou that we go. When a spiritual man hears that this is from the devil, who avails nothing against the will of God, a will may arise in him to take such men out of the world, if he should have the power. But he consults the justice of God, whether he ought to do it, and whether God wills this to be the office of men. But the Truth answers: that a man does not know in this life what he will be who now errs, and what his error confers upon the good: and 132Bthat they are therefore not to be taken away, lest perhaps they be slain who perhaps are yet to be [good]: or lest it hurt the good, to whom they are profitable, etc. But that this is then done seasonably, when there is no longer a time for changing one's life and for profiting others: and then, he says, it is done not by men, but by angels.

VERS. 29.-- And he said: No, etc. Behold patience. For he did not blaze out into anger when the grievance was heard, nor did he wish to avenge himself at once; so let us also, when injured, bear it patiently.

Lest perhaps. Here place is given to repentance, and we are admonished not to cut off quickly: because he who errs today will perhaps tomorrow defend the truth. If therefore he were plucked up now, the wheat that was to be would be rooted out. Or it is necessary that the evil be mingled with the good, by whom they may be exercised. Or by comparison with whom they may strive toward what is better; and when these are taken away, 132Cthe height of charity withers, which is to be rooted out.

There the evil are to be borne patiently, where some are found by whom the good are helped.

(AUG.) A multitude is not to be excommunicated, nor the prince of the people.

VERS. 30.-- Suffer. This seems contrary to that word of the Apostle: Put away the evil one from among you (I Cor. V). But as between the cockle and the wheat, so long as it is in the blade, there is little difference: so the Lord admonishes us not to judge concerning a doubtful thing, which the Lord in the day of judgment will condemn not by suspicion, but manifestly.

In suffering let us meditate justice: in judging let us not neglect justice.

And in the time. He who has borne long-sufferingly, by justice 132Drepays what is deserved: for the virtues are distinct, but not contrary. And bind ye. In this life there is no binding, otherwise so many would not come to their senses out of error.

VERS. 31.-- The kingdom is like, etc. The kingdom of heaven is the preaching of the Gospel, concerning which elsewhere: The kingdom of God shall be taken away from you, and shall be given to a nation bringing forth the fruit thereof (Matth. XXI). Or the knowledge of the Scriptures. To a grain of mustard seed. It is compared to a grain of mustard seed for the fervour of faith, because it is said to drive out poisons, that is, all the doctrines of depravity. In the grain of mustard seed the humility of the Lord's incarnation may be understood. Which being received, a man put it in his garden, because Joseph, receiving the body of the crucified Saviour, buried it in a garden. It spread out branches in which the birds of the air shall rest, 133Abecause he scattered preachers throughout the world, in whose words and consolations the faithful might take breath from the weariness of this life.

VERS. 32.-- But when it is grown up. In the whole world, or in the mind of each: because no one is perfect on a sudden. It grew and became a tree, because he rose again and ascended into heaven. Or it shall have grown, that is, it shall have become known to the minds of men how great its power is. The rest appear as herbs, which grow quickly and fall: not living, but withered: that becomes not an herb, but a tree, where there are leaves with fruit, and in its diverse meanings spiritual men rest.

Than herbs, and becometh a tree. That is, than the doctrines of the philosophers, which grow quickly and fall, promising nothing that has life.

133BVERS. 33.-- In three measures of meal. That spirit, soul and body, brought into one, may not disagree among themselves. Or the three powers of the soul are brought into one, that in reason we may possess prudence, in anger the hatred of vices, in desire the longing for virtues. Or, the woman, the Church, mixes the faith of men in three measures of meal, in the belief of the Father and of the Son and of the Holy Spirit. And when it has been leavened into one, it leads us not to a threefold God, but to the knowledge of one deity, because the nature is not diverse in the several persons.

Until it was leavened. So long ought charity, hidden in the mind, to grow, until it change the whole mind into its own perfection, so that it love or think upon nothing beside God, which is begun here, and perfected in the other life.

VERS. 34.-- In parables to the crowds. Because whatsoever 133Cis there said of the army of Israel, and all the signs, are hidden sacraments.

VERS. 44.-- To a treasure hidden in a field. By the treasure may be understood the word of God, which is hidden in the flesh of Christ. Or the Scripture, in which the knowledge of the Saviour is laid up. Or the two Testaments in the Church, for the understanding of which all things are renounced, that they may be learned in leisure.

The kingdom of heaven is likened to earthly things, that through things known the mind may rise to things unknown, and love them.

He hideth it, and for joy thereof. That it may be kept safe from malignant spirits and from the praises of men. Let the work be so in public, that the intention remain hidden.

VERS. 45.-- To a merchant. The blessedness of which we were stripped by the fault of the first man, we ought all to trade with, by understanding 133Drightly and working well. Good pearls. The good pearls are the law and the prophets. The one precious pearl is the knowledge of the Saviour, for which a man, like Paul, renounced all the mysteries and observances of the law.

VERS. 46.-- But having found. That is, Christ, who is over all men, or the spiritual precept of love. Or the Word understood, which was with God, the shell of the flesh being pierced through. Or the sweetness of the heavenly life, in comparison with which all things grow cheap.

VERS. 47.-- To a net cast. The prophecy of Jeremias is fulfilled: Behold I send to you many fishers (Jer. XVI) [Vulg. Jr 16:16 Ecce ego mittam piscatores multos, "Behold I will send many fishers"; the verb is future, and there is no "to you"]. After Peter and Andrew, James and John had heard: Follow me, I will make you fishers of men: [Vulg. Mt 4:19 Venite post me, et faciam vos fieri piscatores hominum, "Come ye after me, and I will make you to be fishers of men"] they wove themselves a net out of the Old 134Aand the New Testament, and cast it into the sea of the world; which is drawn even to this day, and is stretched among the waves: taking whatsoever falls in, good and bad, whom it draws to the shore, that is, to the end of the world, that then the division may be made. Whence follows: So shall it be at the consummation of the world, etc. To a net is compared the Church, or the preaching of the Gospel, which draws from the waves of the world to heaven.

VERS. 48.-- And by the shore. The shore is the end of the world, in which the good shall be sent to heaven, the bad to darkness: for in the present they were mingled together.

VERS. 52.-- Therefore every Scribe instructed, etc. Because you understand the mysteries which I reveal to you: therefore I will make you preachers, and you shall be like me, who out of the treasure of my knowledge bring forth new things and old, that you 134Bmay do likewise.

The apostles are the scribes and notaries of Christ, marking his words upon the tablets of the heart, and they are strong in the riches of a householder, bringing forth out of the treasure of their doctrines new things and old, because whatsoever is preached in the Gospel they confirm by the voices of the law and of the prophets.

VERS. 54.-- So that they wondered. A great blindness, him whom by words and deeds they recognize to be Christ, they despise on account of their acquaintance with his family.

VERS. 55.-- The carpenter's son. Not without cause did he will to be called the carpenter's son, not of him whom they suppose, but of the maker of all things. But the Jews, ignorant of this sacrament, despise the works of the divine power by looking upon his fleshly lineage. For they do not consider his present works, but remember his frail infancy.

134CVERS. 57.-- A prophet. Christ is called a prophet. Whence Moses says: The Lord shall raise up to you a prophet out of your seed (Deut. XVIII) [Vulg. Dt 18:15 Prophetam de gente tua et de fratribus tuis, sicut me, suscitabit tibi Dominus Deus tuus, "A prophet of thy nation and of thy brethren, like unto me, shall the Lord thy God raise up unto thee"]. In his own country. That is, among the people of the Jews: but he does greater things among the Gentiles.

VERS. 58.-- And he did not. He did some miracles, but not many, lest he should condemn the unbelieving. Or, being despised, he does few, lest they be wholly excusable, but he does greater things among the Gentiles.

CAPUT XIV.

VERS. 1.-- In illo tempore audivit Herodes. Lucas vero ita: Audivit autem Herodes tetrarcha (Luc. IX), etc. Quod ergo ait; Hic est Joannes (Joan. IV). Vel confirmatus quorumdam sententia post haesitationem dixit, vel adhuc dubius: haec enim verba ad utrumque se habent.

134DVERS. 2.-- Et ait. Nota invidiam Judaeorum. Hic alienigena dicit resurrexisse Joannem, qui nullum signum fecit. Illi Jesum tot virtutibus approbatum non resurrexisse contendunt. Et ideo. Senserunt enim quod majoris virtutis sunt futuri sancti a mortuis resurgentes, quam fragili carne gravati.

VERS. 3.-- Herodes enim. Quia dixit de opinione resurrectionis cum nihil de morte dixisset, revertitur et narrat qualiter et cur obierit. Herodes iste et Philippus fratres fuerunt filii Herodis, sub quo Dominus fugit in Aegyptum. Herodiadem uxorem. Herodias filia Arethae regis, quam ipse pater tulit Philippo, et in dolorem ejus dedit Herodi inimico illius.

VERS. 4.-- Non licet. Herodes forsitan legem Judaeorum 135Atenebat, et ideo Joannes eum ab adulterio prohibebat.

VERS. 5.-- Timuit populum. Populum timebat, sed amor superabat, pro quo et Deum postponebat. Timor Dei corrigit, timor hominum differt, sed voluntatem non aufert: unde et avidiores reddit ad crimen quos aliquando suspendit a crimine.

VERS. 7.-- Cum juramento. Ad hoc forte juravit ut occasionem pararet, qua videretur facere invitus quod faciebat spontaneus. Non enim hoc implesset de aliquo amato, quod implevit de propheta: quod et ante fecisset, si excusabiliter posset.

(BEDA.) Si quid nos forte incaute jurasse contigerit, etc., usque ad nec aliquid culpae se tali perjurio contraxisse doluit.

135B(ISID.) Non est conservandum sacramentum quo malum incaute promittitur, velut si, etc., usque ad tolerabilius enim est non implere sacramentum, quam manere in stupro.

VERS. 9.-- Constristatus. Ut discumbentibus videbatur. Talis quippe erat tristitia Herodis, qualis poenitentia videlicet Pharaonis.

VERS. 10.-- Et decollavit. Minutus est Joannes capite, exaltatus est in cruce Christus: quia illius fama decrevit, istius crevit; unde: Illum oportet crescere, me autem minui (Joan. III). In truncato capite Joannis, ostenduntur Judaei Christum (qui est caput omnium prophetarum) perdidisse.

Moraliter. Mors Joannis finem significat legis, qua finita merito migratur ad Christum, qui est finis 135Clegis ad justitiam omni credenti.

VERS. 13.-- Quod cum audisset Jesus. Postquam apud Judaeos lex et propheta caput perdit et vocem: Jesus transit ad desertum Ecclesiae locum, quae ante non habebat virum. Secessit. Non fugit timore, sed secessit parcens inimicis ne homicidium homicidio jungerent, dans exemplum vitandae temeritatis. Vel secessit, ut probaret fidem credentium; unde: Et cum audissent turbae, etc. Pedestres. Proprio labore, ut ardorem mentis ostenderent. Relinquunt civitates, id est, pristinas conversationes. Habent voluntatem, sed non vires perveniendi. Ideo Jesus obviam exit, et misertus curat, ut plena fides statim praemium habeat. Ecce quantum delectatur devotione credentium.

VERS. 15.-- Vespere autem facto, etc. Recedit 135DJesus de Judaea, vadit in desertum, turbae sequuntur, quibus obviat et curat. Hoc non mane vel meridie, sed vespere quando sol justitiae occubuit et finis mundi appropinquavit.

Discipuli ejus. Carnales, et de futuro miraculo refectionis ignari, quibus dictum erat: In viam gentium ne abieritis (Matth. X).

Mystice. Fastidiunt Judaei gentes recipi et murmurant: quorum voce utitur Isaias (Isa. XL): Ecce gentes quasi stilla situlae. Iterum: Omnes gentes quasi non sint, sed sunt coram eo. Sed Dominus per apostolos reficere decrevit; unde subdit: Jesus autem, etc.

Dimitte turbas. Diu est quod idola sequitur, ideoque non est a nobis pascenda pane verbi Dei.

136AVERS. 16.-- Date illis. Provocat ad dandum, ut illis se non habere testantibus notior sit magnitudo signi, ubi etiam innuit, quod per eos sunt pascenda jejuna corda.

VERS. 17.-- Quinque panes et duos pisces. Per quinque panes et duos pisces, totum Vetus Testamentum significatur. Per quinque panes, quinque libri Moysi, qui bene hordeacei, qui cibus est jumentorum, rudibus enim aspera et grossa danda. Animalis enim homo non percipit ea quae sunt spiritus Dei (I Cor. I). Intus tamen latet medulla suavissimi sensus. Per duos pisces, prophetae et psalmi: unde et post resurrectionem ait: Quae scripta sunt in lege et prophetis plurimis de me (Luc. XXIV).

VERS. 19.-- Acceptis quinque, etc. Accepit quinque 136Bpanes, non novos creavit, quia veniens in carne non alia quam praedicta erant praedicavit, et opere implevit. Benedixit. Cum aperiens sensum, ut intelligerent Scripturas, dono internae suavitatis multiplicavit. Fregit cum mysteria legis in medium protulit.

VERS. 20.-- Reliquias. Secretiora quae a rudibus capi nequeunt, non sunt negligenter habenda, sed a duodecim apostolis qui per cophinos duodecim significantur, et ab eorum successoribus diligenter inquirenda. Cophinis servilia opera geruntur. Et infirma mundi elegit Deus, ut confundat fortia (I Cor. I).

VERS. 21.-- Exceptis mulieribus et parvulis. Sexus fragilis et minor aetas sunt numero indigni. Significat infirmos in fide nondum idoneos pugnae.

VERS. 22. Et statim compulit, etc. (RAB.) Qui, 136Cut ait Joannes, venturi erant ut facerent eum regem (Joan. VI), qui miraculum panum viderant, etc., usque ad pro quibus ne nos emolliant, oremus.

VERS. 23.-- Ascendit. Pavit turbam ut Deus, ascendit orare ut homo. Non quod omnia non habens in potestate, sed ut formam hominis impleret. In hoc officium pietatis est, sequitur insigne potestatis miraculum. Vespere autem. Merito dicitur haec tempestas esse in vespere, quia dum Ecclesia in tribulatione est, dicitur esse quasi in nocte, et sol justitiae se abscondisse videtur.

VERS. 24.-- Navicula autem. Navis in medio mari, solus Jesus in terra: quia aliquando ita est Ecclesia non modo afflicta, sed et foedata ut prorsus videatur derelicta a Deo: sed Deus eam non 136Drelinquit. Unde Marcus ait: Vidit eos laborantes remigando (Marc. VI). Etsi non praesens, et si differt, tamen videt et corroborat.

VERS. 25.-- Quarta autem. Tota nocte periclitantur, quia cum se homo considerat, nihil invenit nisi tenebras angustiarum et aestus hostium.

Venit ad eos. Marcus ait: Volens praeterire eos, pro quibus tamen liberandis venit. Quod fit ut ad horam turbati, sed mox liberati amplius stupeant, et gratias agant, quod idem saepe fit in passionibus sanctorum.

VERS. 26.-- Et videntes, etc. Quia saepe fit ut cum aliqua subito diutinae tribulationis oritur sedatio, non statim credatur vera liberatio. Ideoque permanente 137Apriori formidine intensa, ad liberationem dirigitur oratio.

VERS. 27.-- Ego sum, nolite timere. Non supponit quis, quia ex voce sibi nota poterant eum cognoscere. Vel quia ipse est qui dixit: Qui est misit me ad vos (Exod. III).

VERS. 28.-- Domine, si tu es. Si tu es ille liberator, jube me venire ad tuam imitationem superando pressuras.

VERS. 29.-- Et descendens. De humano desperans adjutorio, mente calcat pressuras, ut Jesu sit conformis. Ambulabat, etc. Ecce homo super aquas ambulat. Ne ergo dubites Christum verum corpus esse, quia super aquas ambulavit.

Super mare ambulare fecit, ut ostendat divinam 137Bpotestatem. Mergi facit, ne infirmitatem obliviscatur, ne putet se aequalem Deo, et ne superbiat.

VERS. 30.-- Et cum coepisset, etc. Paululum relinquitur tentationi, ut augeatur fides; ut enim credidit se ejus imperio posse fluctibus sustentari, sic dum mergitur, posse ejus virtute salvari.

VERS. 32.-- Cessavit ventus. In quocunque Deus per gratiam adest, mox universa bella quiescunt.

VERS. 33.-- Et adoraverunt, etc. Viso signo Filium Dei confitentur: cur ergo Arius creaturam esse perducat?

VERS. 34.-- In terram Genesar. Allegorice. Genesar interpretatur hortus principum. Per typum ergo apostolorum et navis, intelligendum est Ecclesiam de persecutionibus liberatam a Domino duci 137Cad littus, id est quietem, ubi paradisi restituetur haereditas et primae stolae jucunditas.

VERS. 36.-- Fimbriam vestimenti, etc. Id est, minimum mandatum, vel caro qua ad verbum Dei venimus, quem qui credendo tangit, ab omni morbo impietatis sanatur.

CHAPTER XIV.

VERS. 1.-- At that time Herod heard. But Luke thus: And Herod the tetrarch heard (Luc. IX), etc. What therefore he says: This is John (Joan. IV). Either he said it after his hesitation, being confirmed by the opinion of certain men, or being still in doubt: for these words admit of either.

134DVERS. 2.-- And he said. Mark the envy of the Jews. Here a foreigner says that John has risen again, who wrought no sign. They contend that Jesus, approved by so many miracles, has not risen again. And therefore. For they perceived that the saints, rising again from the dead, are to be of greater power than when weighed down with frail flesh.

VERS. 3.-- For Herod. Because he spoke of the opinion of the resurrection when he had said nothing of the death, he turns back and relates how and why he died. This Herod and Philip were brothers, sons of that Herod under whom the Lord fled into Egypt. Herodias his brother's wife. Herodias was the daughter of king Aretas, whom her own father took away from Philip, and, to Philip's grief, gave to Herod his enemy.

VERS. 4.-- It is not lawful. Herod perhaps held the law of the Jews 135A, and therefore John forbade him the adultery.

VERS. 5.-- He feared the people. He feared the people, but love prevailed, for which he even set God behind. The fear of God corrects; the fear of men puts off, but does not take away the will: whence it also makes the more eager for the crime those whom for a time it holds back from the crime.

VERS. 7.-- With an oath. Perhaps he swore to this end, that he might prepare an occasion by which he should seem to do unwillingly what he was doing of his own accord. For he would not have fulfilled this concerning anyone he loved, which he fulfilled concerning the prophet: which he would have done even before, if he could have done it excusably.

(BEDA.) If it should happen that we have perhaps sworn something incautiously, etc., as far as nor did he grieve that he had contracted any fault to himself by such perjury.

135B(ISID.) An oath is not to be kept whereby an evil is incautiously promised, as if, etc., as far as for it is more tolerable not to fulfil an oath than to remain in defilement.

VERS. 9.-- Being made sorrowful Constristatus. As it seemed to those that sat at meat. For such was the sorrow of Herod as was, namely, the repentance of Pharaoh.

VERS. 10.-- And he beheaded. John was made less by the head, Christ was lifted up on the cross: because the fame of the one decreased, that of the other increased; whence: He must increase, but I must decrease (Joan. III). In the severed head of John it is shown that the Jews had lost Christ (who is the head of all the prophets).

Morally. The death of John signifies the end of the law, which being ended, one deservedly passes over to Christ, who is the end 135Cof the law unto justice to everyone that believeth.

VERS. 13.-- And when Jesus had heard it. After among the Jews the law and the prophet lose their head and their voice: Jesus passes over to the desert place of the Church, which before had no husband. He withdrew. He did not flee out of fear, but he withdrew, sparing his enemies, lest they should join murder to murder, giving an example of rashness to be shunned. Or he withdrew, that he might prove the faith of them that believe; whence: And when the crowds had heard, etc. On foot. By their own labour, that they might show the ardour of their mind. They leave the cities, that is, their former ways of life. They have the will, but not the strength to arrive. Therefore Jesus goes out to meet them, and having pity heals them, that a full faith may have its reward at once. Behold how greatly he is delighted with the devotion of them that believe.

VERS. 15.-- And when it was evening, etc. Jesus withdraws 135Dfrom Judea, goes into the desert, the crowds follow, whom he meets and heals. This not in the morning or at midday, but in the evening, when the sun of justice had set and the end of the world had drawn near.

His disciples. Carnal, and ignorant of the coming miracle of the refreshment, to whom it had been said: Go ye not into the way of the Gentiles (Matth. X).

Mystically. The Jews disdain that the Gentiles should be received, and they murmur: whose voice Isaias uses (Isa. XL): Behold the nations are as a drop of a bucket. Again: All nations are as though they were not, but they are before him. [Vulg. Is 40:17 sic sunt coram eo, "so are they before him"] But the Lord determined to refresh them by the apostles; whence he subjoins: But Jesus, etc.

Send away the crowds. It is long that she has followed idols, and therefore she is not to be fed by us with the bread of the word of God.

136AVERS. 16.-- Give ye them. He provokes them to give, that, when they testify that they have nothing, the greatness of the sign may be the better known; wherein he also hints that by them the hungry hearts are to be fed.

VERS. 17.-- Five loaves and two fishes. By the five loaves and the two fishes the whole Old Testament is signified. By the five loaves, the five books of Moses, which are well of barley, which is the food of beasts of burden, for to the untaught things rough and coarse must be given. For the sensual man perceiveth not the things that are of the spirit of God (I Cor. I) [Vulg. 1 Cor 2:14 Animalis autem homo, "But the sensual man"]. Within, nevertheless, lies hidden the marrow of a most sweet meaning. By the two fishes, the prophets and the psalms: whence also after the resurrection he says: The things that are written in the law and in very many prophets concerning me (Luc. XXIV) [Vulg. Lc 24:44 in lege Moysi, et prophetis, et Psalmis de me, "in the law of Moses, and the prophets, and the psalms concerning me"; the gloss's own point is the psalms].

VERS. 19.-- Having taken the five, etc. He took five 136Bloaves, he did not create new ones, because coming in the flesh he preached no other things than those which had been foretold, and fulfilled them in deed. He blessed. When, opening their understanding that they might understand the Scriptures, he multiplied them by the gift of an inward sweetness. He broke, when he brought forth the mysteries of the law into the midst.

VERS. 20.-- The fragments. The more secret things, which cannot be grasped by the untaught, are not to be held negligently, but are to be diligently sought out by the twelve apostles, who are signified by the twelve baskets, and by their successors. With baskets servile works are carried on. And the weak things of the world hath God chosen, that he may confound the strong (I Cor. I).

VERS. 21.-- Besides women and children. The frail sex and the lesser age are unworthy of the number. It signifies those weak in faith, not yet fit for battle.

VERS. 22. And immediately he compelled, etc. (RAB.) Who, 136Cas John says, were about to come to make him king (Joan. VI), who had seen the miracle of the loaves, etc., as far as let us pray for them, lest they soften us.

VERS. 23.-- He went up. He fed the crowd as God, he went up to pray as man. Not as one not having all things in his power, but that he might fulfil the form of a man. In this there is a duty of piety; there follows a notable miracle of power. And when it was evening. Rightly is this storm said to be in the evening, because while the Church is in tribulation, she is said to be as it were in the night, and the sun of justice seems to have hidden himself.

VERS. 24.-- But the boat. The ship in the midst of the sea, Jesus alone on the land: because sometimes the Church is so, not only afflicted but also defiled, that she seems utterly forsaken by God: but God does not 136Dforsake her. Whence Mark says: He saw them labouring in rowing (Marc. VI) [Vulg. Mc 6:48 videns eos laborantes in remigando, "seeing them labouring in rowing"]. Though not present, and though he defers, yet he sees and strengthens.

VERS. 25.-- And in the fourth. [Vulg. Mt 14:25 Quarta enim vigilia noctis, "For in the fourth watch of the night"] They are in peril the whole night, because when a man considers himself, he finds nothing but the darkness of straits and the surgings of enemies.

He came to them. Mark says: Willing to pass by them, for whose deliverance nevertheless he came. Which happens so that, being troubled for an hour but soon delivered, they may wonder the more, and give thanks; which same thing often happens in the passions of the saints.

VERS. 26.-- And seeing him, etc. Because it often happens that when some sudden abatement of a long tribulation arises, it is not straightway believed to be a true deliverance. And therefore, the former dread remaining intense 137A, prayer is directed toward deliverance.

VERS. 27.-- It is I, fear ye not. He does not add who, because from the voice known to them they could recognize him. Or because he himself is he who said: He who is hath sent me to you (Exod. III).

VERS. 28.-- Lord, if it be thou. If thou art that deliverer, bid me come to the imitation of thee by overcoming pressures.

VERS. 29.-- And going down. Despairing of human help, he treads down pressures with his mind, that he may be conformed to Jesus. He walked, etc. Behold, a man walks upon the waters. Doubt not therefore that Christ is a true body, because he walked upon the waters.

He made him walk upon the sea, that he might show the divine 137Bpower. He makes him sink, lest he forget his infirmity, lest he think himself equal to God, and lest he grow proud.

VERS. 30.-- And when he had begun, etc. He is left a little to temptation, that faith may be increased; for as he believed that by his command he could be upheld upon the waves, so, while he sinks, that he could be saved by his power.

VERS. 32.-- The wind ceased. In whomsoever God is present by grace, straightway all wars grow quiet.

VERS. 33.-- And they adored him, etc. The sign being seen, they confess him the Son of God: why then should Arius bring it about that he is a creature?

VERS. 34.-- Into the land of Genesar. Allegorically. Genesar is interpreted the garden of princes. By the type therefore of the apostles and of the ship, it is to be understood that the Church, delivered from persecutions, is led by the Lord 137Cto the shore, that is, to rest, where the inheritance of paradise and the gladness of the first robe shall be restored.

VERS. 36.-- The hem of his garment, etc. That is, the least commandment, or the flesh by which we come to the word of God, and he who touches him by believing is healed of every disease of impiety.

CAPUT XV.

VERS. 1.-- Tunc accesserunt. Homines Genesar, minus docti, credunt, sed qui sapientes videntur ad pugnam veniunt.

(CHRYSOST.) Tunc, inquit, quando scilicet innumera signa operatus est, quando, etc., usque ad ut magnam eorum malitiam ostenderet.

VERS. 2.-- Non enim lavant. Intus nihil arguunt, 137Dqui intus livore polluti sunt. In vanum quidem sua baptismata servant, qui suorum cordium et corporum sordes abluere negligunt.

VERS. 3.-- Quare et vos. Si vos mandata Dei contemnitis propter traditiones hominum, quare arguitis discipulos, qui mandata hominum dimittunt, ut scita Dei custodiant?

VERS. 4.-- Honora patrem. (HIER.) Honor in Scripturis, non tantum in salutationibus et officiis deferendis, quantum, etc., usque ad ut filii honorarent etiam in vitae necessariis ministrandis parentes suos.

VERS. 5.-- Vos autem dicitis, etc. Vestra autem traditio contraria est. Hoc filios dicere parentibus 138Acompellitis: quod Deo oblaturus eram in tuos consumo cibos, tibique prodest, o pater, ut timeat consecrata comedere. Vel quod Deo offero, tibi ipsi prodest. Quicunque dixerit. Scilicet, vita aeterna dignus erit. Vel dixerit, id est, dicere proderit. Proderit. Quasi dicat: Non, imo sacrilegus esses, si acciperes, quia Deo devovi, Vel tibi proderit: si Deo dedero.

VERS. 7.-- Bene prophetavit, etc. In spiritu simulationem Judaeorum, quod in dolo repugnarent Evangelio.

VERS. 8.-- Labiis me honorat. Magister, scimus quia verax es (Marc. XII). Vel exteriorem munditiam commendando, interior et quae vera est, in eis non est.

138BVERS. 11.-- Non quod intrat. Nihil est extra hominem quod interius inquinet, nec idolothitum in hoc quod Dei creatura est; uno sermone omnis superstitio observationum eliditur, cum in discernendis cibis religio putetur.

VERS. 12.-- Tunc accedentes. Pharisaeorum magna est religio in cibis, qui audito verbo hoc scandalizati sunt, id est, indignati sunt. Scandalizati sunt. Σκάνδαλον Graece, nos offendiculum vel ruinam vel impactionem pedis possumus dicere. Unde: Qui scandalizaverit aliquem, id est, occasionem ruinae dederit.

VERS. 14.-- Sinite, etc. Permittite suo arbitrio, quia irrevocabiles sunt. Unde Paulus: Haereticum hominem post primam et secundam correctionem devita 138C(Tit. III).

VERS. 15.-- Edissere. Quod aperte est dictum, putant apostoli per parabolam dictum. Vitiosus est auditor, qui vel obscura manifeste, vel manifeste dicta obscure vult intelligere.

VERS. 19.-- De corde, etc. Non enim a diabolo immittuntur, sed ex propria voluntate nascuntur. Diabolus enim incentor est non auctor, quia nec interiora nisi per habitus et gestus novit.

VERS. 20.-- Non lotis. Spiritualia dicta prophetarum, de cordis et corporis castigatione, ut haec: Lavamini, mundi estote (Isa. I). Pharisaei carnaliter de solo corpore accipiebant, quod vanum est fieri.

VERS. 21.-- Et egressus, etc. Relictis calumniatoribus 138Dintra fines Tyri et Sidonis, ut filiam liberaret, et per fidem gentilis feminae perfidiam Scribarum argueret. Relictis calumniatoribus significat quod post resurrectionem relicturus esset perfidiam Judaeorum, per praedicationem iturus ad gentes.

VERS. 22.-- Clamavit, etc. Per fidem clamat, unde, novit vocare filium David, Christi incarnationem confitens. Rogat pro filia, id est, pro populo suo nondum credente, ut ipsa fraude diaboli absolvantur. Vel, filia est anima vel conscientia cujuslibet intra Ecclesiam diabolo mancipata, pro qua mater Ecclesia rogat, vel ipse homo pro foedata conscientia.

VERS. 23.-- Qui non respondit. Non de superbia, sed ne sit contrarius sententiae suae: In viam gentium 139Ane abieritis (Matth. X). Unde vitat calumniam Judaeorum, gentium autem salus, passionis et resurrectionis tempori servatur.

VERS. 24.-- Non sum missus. Quasi dicat: Prius oportet me sacramentum mei adventus complere in Judaeis, et sic adducere alias oves, et hoc de signo petitionem hujus differendo.

VERS. 26.-- Panem filiorum. Verbum salutis, quod prius debetur Judaeis, non est bonum dare canibus vel gentibus, canina rabie latrantibus semper.

VERS. 27.-- Etiam, Domine. Sub persona mulieris, mira fides Ecclesiae, et patientia, et humilitas praedicatur. Fides, quia credit posse sanari filiam. Patientia, quia toties despecta in precibus perstat. 139BHumilitas, quia se non canibus, sed catellis comparat.

Nam et catelli. Mensa, est sacra Scriptura; micae puerorum, sunt interna mysteria quibus humiles reficiuntur sub mensa: quia Scripturae humiliter subditi, catelli gentes humiles. Parvuli edunt non panes, non crustas, id est, non litterae superficiem, sed medullam.

VERS. 28.-- Magna est fides. Sic superius de centurione: Non inveni tantam fidem in Israel (Matth. VIII), qui similiter (ut haec Chananaea) fidem gentium praesignabat. Magna est fides gentium, quae in auditu auris obediunt: et in Deo salutem sibi suisque impetrat. Quod puerum centurionis et filiam Chananeae non veniens ad eos sanat, significat gentes, 139Cad quas non venit per praesentiam, salvandas per verbum suum.

Et sanata est. Ubi datur exemplum catechizandi et baptizandi infantes, qui nec sapere, nec facere aliquid possunt, sed per fidem et confessionem parentum liberantur a diabolo.

VERS. 29.-- Et cum transisset. Cum in filia Chananitidis salutem gentium praesignasset, revertitur in Judaeam, quia cum plenitudo gentium intraverit, tunc omnis Israel salvus fiet. In montem. Dominus in monte, verbum in alto sedet: quia in sua majestate non posset intelligi.

VERS. 30.-- Caecos. Caeci sunt qui non intelligunt, etiamsi obediant jubentibus. Surdi, qui non obediunt, etiamsi intelligant.

139DVERS. 32.-- Jesus autem. Convocat discipulos, ut doceat magistros cum minoribus communicare consilia sua. Vel ut ex locutione intelligant signi magnitudinem. Misereor turbae. Turba triduo sustinet Dominum, quia fideles poenitentes se ad Dominum in opere et sermone et cogitatione convertunt, qui in praesentis vitae via deficerent, si in sua conscientia sine doctrinae pabulo dimitterentur. In superiori signo propinqui memorantur, et vicini quinque sensuum et eorum non recordatur Dominus, sed discipuli in vespera jam inclinato sole. Hic Dominus se misereri dicit, et quare, quia triduo jam perseverant mecum. In toto saeculo tertium tempus est, quo gratia datur, primum ante legem, secundum sub lege, 140Atertium sub gratia, quartum est in futuro: ubi dies erit sine nocte, ad quod tendens reficitur in via.

VERS. 33.-- In deserto panes. Panes praedicatores, desertum sese, id est, infecundos dicunt. Quasi dicat: Unde nobis tanta facundia ut possimus tam avidis auditoribus sufficere?

VERS. 36.-- Et accipiens. Ut ostendat se congaudere de salute hominum et nos ad agendas gratias informat etiam de temporalibus. Septem panes. Septiformi Spiritu pascit, adjuncta apostolica auctoritate, quasi paucorum piscium sapore qui nos reficiunt exemplo suae vitae vel mortis. Septem panes, Scriptura Novi Testamenti in quo gratia Spiritus sancti et revelatur et datur. Nec sunt hordeacei, 140But supra, quia hic non (ut in lege) vitale alimentum animae figuris, quasi tenacissima palea tegitur. Hic non duos pisces (ut in lege duo ungebantur, rex et sacerdos), sed pauci, id est, sancti Novi Testamenti, qui de fluctibus saeculi sunt erepti, et sustinent turbulentum mare, et exemplo suo nos reficiunt, ne in via deficiamus.

(BEDA.) Ibi supra fenum, ut desideria carnis comprimantur, hic super terram ubi et ipse mundus, etc., usque ad utraque refectio in monte celebrata est, quia utriusque Testamenti Scriptura altitudinem coelestium praeceptorum mandat et praemiorum: utraque altitudinem Christi praedicat.

Et quod superfuit. Altiora mysteria quae non capit communis turba, apostoli sustollunt et implent 140Cseptem sportas, id est, perfectorum corda septiformi Spiritus gratia ad intelligendum illustrata. Septem sportas. Sportae junco et palmarum foliis solent contexi, et significant sanctos, qui radicem cordis in ipso fonte vitae collocant, ne arescant, ut juncus in aqua: et palmam aeternae retributionis in corde retinent.

VERS. 39.-- Et dimissa turba. Hic docet quod praedicatores ministrato turbae verbo, ipsi intra cubiculum cordis virtutum debent refici pomis. In fines. Magedan quae alio nomine dicitur Dalmanuta, quae est regio circa Gerasam. Et interpretatur poma vel nuntia, et significat hortum de quo dicitur: Hortus conclusus, fons signatus, ubi crescunt poma virtutum. Et ibi nuntiatur nomen Domini.

CHAPTER XV.

VERS. 1.-- Then they drew near. The men of Genesar, being less learned, believe, but those who seem wise come for a fight.

(CHRYSOST.) Then, he says, that is, when he had wrought countless signs, when, etc., as far as that he might show how great their malice was.

VERS. 2.-- For they wash not. Within they reprove nothing, 137Dwho within are polluted with spite. In vain indeed do they keep their washings, who neglect to wash away the filth of their own hearts and bodies.

VERS. 3.-- Why do you also transgress. If you despise the commandments of God for the sake of the traditions of men, why do you reprove the disciples, who let go the commandments of men that they may keep the decrees of God?

VERS. 4.-- Honour father. (HIER.) Honour, in the Scriptures, is not so much in salutations and in the paying of courtesies, as, etc., as far as that sons should honour their parents also in the ministering of the necessaries of life.

VERS. 5.-- But you say, etc. But your tradition is contrary. You compel sons to say this to their parents: 138AWhat I was to have offered to God I spend upon food for thee, and it profits thee, O father, that he should be afraid to eat what has been consecrated. Or: what I offer to God profits thee thyself. Whosoever shall say. That is, he will be worthy of eternal life. Or shall say, that is, to say it will profit. It shall profit. As if he said: No, nay, thou wouldst be sacrilegious if thou didst take it, because I have vowed it to God, Or it shall profit thee: if I shall have given it to God.

VERS. 7.-- Well hath he prophesied, etc. In the spirit, the dissembling of the Jews, that in guile they would fight against the Gospel.

VERS. 8.-- He honoureth me with the lips. Master, we know that thou art true (Marc. XII). Or, by commending outward cleanness — the inward, and that which is true, is not in them.

138BVERS. 11.-- Not that which entereth in. There is nothing outside a man that defiles him within, nor meat offered to idols in this, that it is God's creature; by one word every superstition of observances is struck down, when religion is thought to lie in distinguishing foods.

VERS. 12.-- Then the disciples, drawing near. Great is the Pharisees' religion in the matter of foods, who on hearing this word were scandalized, that is, were indignant. They were scandalized. Σκάνδαλον in Greek, we can say a stumbling-block, or a ruin, or the striking of the foot. Whence: He that shall scandalize anyone, that is, shall have given occasion of ruin.

VERS. 14.-- Let them alone, etc. Leave them to their own judgment, because they are past recall. Whence Paul: A man that is a heretic, after the first and second correction, avoid 138C(Tit. III). [Vulg. Tit 3:10 post unam et secundam correptionem, "after one and a second rebuke"]

VERS. 15.-- Expound. What was said plainly, the apostles think was said in a parable. He is a faulty hearer who wishes to understand either obscure things plainly, or things plainly said obscurely.

VERS. 19.-- Out of the heart, etc. For they are not sent in by the devil, but are born of a man's own will. For the devil is an inciter, not an author, since neither does he know inward things save through outward bearing and gesture.

VERS. 20.-- With unwashen hands. The spiritual sayings of the prophets, concerning the chastening of the heart and of the body, such as these: Be washed, be clean (Isa. I). The Pharisees took them carnally, of the body alone, which it is vain to do.

VERS. 21.-- And going out, etc. The slanderers being left behind, 138Dwithin the borders of Tyre and Sidon, that he might set the daughter free, and by the faith of a Gentile woman convict the perfidy of the Scribes. "The slanderers being left behind" signifies that after the resurrection he would leave behind the perfidy of the Jews, and would go by his preaching to the Gentiles.

VERS. 22.-- She cried out, etc. By faith she cries out, whence, she knows how to call him Son of David, confessing the incarnation of Christ. She prays for her daughter, that is, for her own people not yet believing, that they may be loosed from that very deceit of the devil. Or, the daughter is the soul or the conscience of anyone within the Church that has been made over to the devil, for which mother Church prays, or the man himself for his defiled conscience.

VERS. 23.-- Who answered not. Not out of pride, but lest he should be contrary to his own saying: Into the way of the Gentiles 139Ago ye not (Matth. X). Whence he avoids the slander of the Jews, but the salvation of the Gentiles is reserved for the time of the passion and the resurrection.

VERS. 24.-- I was not sent. As if he said: First I must fulfil the sacrament of my coming among the Jews, and so bring in other sheep, and this by deferring, in the matter of the sign, the petition of this woman.

VERS. 26.-- The children's bread. The word of salvation, which is owed first to the Jews, is not good to give to dogs, or to the Gentiles, that always bark with a dog's madness.

VERS. 27.-- Yea, Lord. Under the person of the woman, the marvellous faith of the Church, and her patience, and her humility are proclaimed. Faith, because she believes that her daughter can be healed. Patience, because so often despised she persists in her prayers. 139BHumility, because she compares herself not to dogs, but to whelps.

For even the whelps. The table is Holy Scripture; the children's crumbs are the inward mysteries with which the humble are refreshed under the table: because, being humbly subject to the Scriptures, the whelps are the humble Gentiles. The little ones eat not loaves, not crusts, that is, not the surface of the letter, but the marrow.

VERS. 28.-- Great is the faith. So above, of the centurion: I have not found so great faith in Israel (Matth. VIII), who likewise (as this Canaanite woman does) prefigured the faith of the Gentiles. Great is the faith of the Gentiles, who at the hearing of the ear obey: and in God she obtains salvation for herself and for her own. That he heals the centurion's servant and the Canaanite woman's daughter without coming to them signifies that the Gentiles, 139Cto whom he did not come by his presence, were to be saved by his word.

And she was healed. Here an example is given of catechizing and baptizing infants, who can neither understand nor do anything, but are set free from the devil through the faith and confession of their parents.

VERS. 29.-- And when he had passed over. When in the daughter of the Canaanite woman he had prefigured the salvation of the Gentiles, he returns into Judaea, because when the fulness of the Gentiles shall have come in, then all Israel shall be saved. Into a mountain. The Lord on the mountain, the Word, sits on high: because in his own majesty he could not be understood.

VERS. 30.-- The blind. The blind are those who do not understand, even if they obey those who command. The deaf, those who do not obey, even if they understand.

139DVERS. 32.-- But Jesus. He calls the disciples together, that he may teach masters to share their counsels with their inferiors. Or that from his speech they may understand the greatness of the sign. I have compassion on the crowd. The crowd endures with the Lord for three days, because faithful penitents turn themselves to the Lord in deed and in word and in thought, who would faint on the way of the present life if they were left in their own conscience without the food of doctrine. In the earlier sign the kinsfolk are called to mind, and the neighbours of the five senses, and the Lord does not remember them, but the disciples do, at evening, the sun being now declined. Here the Lord says that he has compassion, and why: because they have now continued with me three days. In the whole world the third is the time in which grace is given: the first before the law, the second under the law, 140Athe third under grace, the fourth is in the world to come, where there will be day without night, toward which, as he presses on, a man is refreshed on the way.

VERS. 33.-- Loaves in the desert. The loaves are the preachers, the desert, themselves, that is, they call themselves unfruitful. As if he said: Whence have we such eloquence that we can suffice for hearers so greedy?

VERS. 36.-- And taking. That he may show that he rejoices together with us over the salvation of men, and he forms us to give thanks even for temporal things. Seven loaves. He feeds with the sevenfold Spirit, with apostolic authority joined to it, as with the savour of a few fishes, who refresh us by the example of their life or of their death. Seven loaves: the Scripture of the New Testament, in which the grace of the Holy Spirit is both revealed and given. Nor are they of barley, 140Bas above, because here the life-giving food of the soul is not (as in the law) covered over with figures, like a most clinging husk. Here there are not two fishes (as in the law two were anointed, the king and the priest), but a few, that is, the saints of the New Testament, who have been snatched from the waves of the world, and endure the troubled sea, and refresh us by their example, lest we faint on the way.

(BEDA.) There upon the grass, that the desires of the flesh may be pressed down, here upon the ground, where the world itself also, etc., as far as both refreshments were celebrated on a mountain, because the Scripture of both Testaments enjoins the height of the heavenly precepts and of the rewards: both proclaim the height of Christ.

And that which was left over. The higher mysteries which the common crowd does not grasp, the apostles take up and fill 140Cseven baskets, that is, the hearts of the perfect, enlightened for understanding by the sevenfold grace of the Spirit. Seven baskets. Baskets are wont to be woven of rush and of palm leaves, and they signify the saints, who set the root of the heart in the very fountain of life, lest they wither, as the rush in the water: and they keep the palm of the eternal reward in their heart.

VERS. 39.-- And having sent away the crowd. Here he teaches that preachers, when the word has been ministered to the crowd, ought themselves to be refreshed within the chamber of the heart with the fruits of the virtues. Into the coasts. Magedan, which by another name is called Dalmanutha, which is the region about Gerasa. And it is interpreted fruits or tidings, and it signifies the garden of which it is said: A garden enclosed, a fountain sealed, where the fruits of the virtues grow. And there the name of the Lord is announced.

CAPUT XVI.

  • 140D

VERS. 1.-- Et accesserunt, etc. Ut signum de coelo, etc. Ut qui pavit multos paucis: manna plueret quo omnes reficeret. Unde Joannes post edulium panum: Quod signum facis, ut videamus et credamus tibi? quid operaris? Patres nostri manducarunt manna in deserto (Joan. VI). Petebant tentando ut in morem Eliae ignem de sublimi mitteret, vel in similitudinem Samuelis aestivo tempore mugire tonitrua faceret, quasi non possent et illa calumniari, et dicere ex occultis et variis aeris passionibus accidisse.

VERS. 2.-- Facto vespere. Increpat eos, quod ex ordine elementorum discernant dies, sed signa temporum 141Aignorant, cum omnis lex et prophetae, adventus sui indicia ex operum quae gereret admiratione significent.

VERS. 3.-- Rutilat enim. Patiuntur apostoli post resurrectionem, per quos me judicare in futuro scire potestis, quia cum non parco meis bonis quin patiantur, non parcam aliis in futuro.

VERS. 4.-- Signa. Ad tempus gratiae pertinentia prophetis praedicta, non possunt vobis fidem facere de meo adventu. Signa autem. Signa temporum dixit de adventu suo vel passione cui simile est roseum coelum vespere. Et item de tribulatione ante adventum futurum, cui simile est roseum coelum cum tristitia. Non dabitur ei. Sed datur fidelibus de coelo quando videntibus apostolis in coelum ascendit, 141Bet Spiritum sanctum misit.

VERS. 5.-- Obliti sunt panes accipere. Quomodo panes non habent qui modo impletis sportis navem ascenderunt et navigantes audiunt ut caveant a fermento Pharisaeorum. Sed tamen ait Scriptura quod obliti sunt tollere panes secum unius veri et interni panis (qui cum eis erat) dulcedine capti, parum se curare judicantes de cura corporis et necessariis, cum et ipsa naturalis refectio a mente excesserat.

VERS. 6.-- A fermento. Fermentum est perversa doctrina, quae vel modica totam massam corrumpit, et in suum saporem trahit.

VERS. 8.-- Quid cogitatis. Quid cogitatis me dixisse de terrenis panibus de quibus non est vobis dubitandum, cum de tam paucis tantas feci abundare 141Creliquias?

VERS. 11.-- Cavete a fermento Pharisaeorum. A superstitiosa doctrina, quae more fermenti veri panis simulat se substantiam habere. Per hoc innuit, quid significant quinque panes vel septem. Si enim fermentum dicit haeretica dogmata, panes sunt vera doctrina.

VERS. 13.-- In partes Caesareae Philippi. Iste locus est, ubi Jordanus oritur ad radicem Libani, ex Jor et Dan duobus fontibus. Homines esse. Homines sunt qui de Filio hominis loquuntur: dii enim qui Deitatem intelligunt. Filium hominis? Dominus de se humilitatem humanitatis profitetur, discipuli Deitatem intelligunt.

VERS. 14.-- Alii Joannem Baptistam. Sic poterant 141Derrare in Elia vel in caeteris aliis, sicut Herodes erravit in Joanne cum ait: Joannes quem ego decollavi surrexit a mortuis: et ideo virtutes operantur in eo (Marc. VI). Ideo vult exponi errores eorum, ut eis repulsis discipuli veritatem proferant, non ex vulgi opinione, sed ex Domini revelatione.

VERS. 17.-- Respondens Simon Petrus. Ideo unus respondens, et uni respondetur pro omnibus ut unitas in omnibus observetur. Dei vivi. Ad distinctionem falsorum deorum, quos gentes vel de mortuis hominibus, vel de sensibili materia sibi instituerunt.

Barjona. Bene filius columbae, quia Dominum pia simplicitate sequebatur, vel quia plenus spirituali gratia erat. Filius Spiritus sancti dicitur, 142Aqui ostensus est in columba ab eo quem Filium Dei vivi vocaverat, et filio columbae pater revelat, quia una est operatio Patris et Filii cum sancto Spiritu. Revelat, inquam, mysterium fidei, quia caro et sanguis non revelare potuit, id est, homines carnali sapientia inflati, qui non columbae filii, et ideo a sapientia Spiritus alieni; de quibus supra: Alii Joannem Baptistam, alii autem Etiam, etc.

VERS. 18.-- Tu es. Non dico vocaberis, sed tu es Petrus ob fortitudinem fidei et confessionis constantiam. Petrus. A me petra, ita tamen ut mihi retineam dignitatem fundamenti: tu super me ordinabis lapides mundos, et abjicies leprosos.

Sicut lumen et caetera nomina, ut: Vos estis sal terrae, a Domino sortiti sunt, apostoli, sic Simoni a 142Bse nomen Petri dedit.

Et portae. Portae inferi sunt peccata, minae, blandimenta, haereses, quibus infirmi ruunt in mortem, qui non vere supra petram, sed super arenam domum professionis suae credendi sunt aedificavisse, id est, non simplici et vera intentione Christum sequi, sed ob terrenam quamlibet causam habitum praetendisse. Qui enim in intimo cordis amore fidem Christi percipit, quidquid ingruit extra, facile vincit.

VERS. 19.-- Et quodcunque ligaveris. Id est, quem in peccatis persistentem aeternis poenis adjudicaveris: vel quem humilem et vere poenitentem absolveris, sic erit et in coelis.

Et quodcunque ligaveris super terram. (HIER.) Habent 142Cquidem eamdem judiciariam potestatem alii apostoli, quibus post resurrectionem ait: Accipite Spiritum sanctum (Joan. XX), etc., usque ad unde in Levitico ostendere se sacerdotibus jubentur leprosi, quos illi non faciunt leprosos, sed discernunt qui mundi vel immundi sunt, ita et hic.

VERS. 20.-- Ut nemini, etc. Supra adventum suum praedicare praecipit, hic ne dicant eum esse Jesum. Sed aliud est praedicare Christum, quod est commune nomen dignitatis: aliud Jesum, quod est proprium Salvatoris. Quasi dicat: Non prodest publice praedicari majestatem populis: qui visuri sunt me multa pati, quod tunc demum licebit, cum resurrectio potentiam Deitatis ostenderit. Jesus Christus. Salvator, ne scandalizarentur in eo quem paulo 142Dpost visuri sunt conspui et crucifigi.

VERS. 21.-- Exinde, etc. Ecce causa prohibitae praedicationis, quasi dicat: Tunc me praedicate cum ista passus fuero, quia non ante prodest.

VERS. 22.-- Absit. Non vult Petrus destrui confessionem suam: nec putat Filium Dei posse occidi.

VERS. 23.-- Vade. Supra: omnia illa gloriosa in futurum promissa sunt, non in praesenti data. Aedificabo Ecclesiam meam, et tibi dabo claves, etc. Quae si statim dedisset, nunquam postea locum error invenisset in eo. Quia non. Non placet tibi, ut per mortem meam mundum redimam, quae est voluntas Patris: sed carnaliter haec sapis.

143AVERS. 24.-- Si quis vult. Praedicta apostolis passione et resurrectione, hortatur ad sequendum tam eos quam turbas. Abneget semetipsum. Quod per vetustatem est, ut capiat quod ultra se est. Remotis vitiis, virtus sequatur: tollat crucem suam, et sequatur me. Quod erat per vetustatem, abnegaverat ille qui dicebat: Vivo autem ego, jam non ego. Quod vero ultra se erat, coeperat: vivit vero in me Christus (Gal. II). Tollat crucem. Scilicet: Crux tollitur vel cum per abstinentiam corpus, vel cum per compassionem proximi animus affligitur. Crucem abstinentiae habuit Paulus, dum dicebat (II Cor. XI): Castigo corpus meum et in servitutem redigo. Compassionis quoque cum ait: Quis infirmatur, et ego non infirmor?

143BVERS. 26.-- Quid enim prodest. Ecclesia habet tempus persecutionis: habet et tempus pacis. Haec tempora designat in praeceptis. Nam tempore persecutionis ponenda est anima, et tempore pacis frangenda sunt terrena desideria.

Aut quam, etc. Nec lucra, nec timor, nec aliquid aliud retrahat a salute: quia quam commutationem dabit homo, etc. Calicem salutaris accipiam, et nomen Domini invocabo (Psal. CXV). Vel nihil terrenum pro anima perdita poterit homo dare.

VERS. 27.-- Filius enim. Nec durum videatur hoc pati: Quia non sunt condignae passiones hujus temporis ad futuram gloriam, quae revelabitur in nobis (Rom. VIII).

VERS. 28.-- Sunt quidam. Quia rudes erant apostoli, etiam in praesenti vita promittitur eis aliqua 143Cconsolatio, ut certius credant de futuro. In regno. In regno beatitudinis aeternae, vel ostendendo suam potestatem in praesenti Ecclesia.

CHAPTER XVI.

  • 140D

VERS. 1.-- And they came, etc. That he would show a sign from heaven, etc. That he who fed many with few things should rain down manna wherewith to refresh all. Whence John, after the eating of the loaves: What sign dost thou do, that we may see and believe thee? what dost thou work? Our fathers did eat manna in the desert (Joan. VI). They asked by way of tempting, that after the manner of Elias he should send fire from on high, or in the likeness of Samuel should make the thunders roar in the summer season, as though they could not have slandered those things too, and said that they had come to pass from the hidden and various affections of the air.

VERS. 2.-- When it is evening. He rebukes them, because from the order of the elements they distinguish the days, but the signs of the times 141Athey do not know, since the whole law and the prophets signify the tokens of his coming out of the wonder of the works that he was to do.

VERS. 3.-- For it is red. The apostles suffer after the resurrection, by whom you can know that I shall judge in time to come, because when I do not spare my own good ones but that they suffer, I shall not spare others in time to come.

VERS. 4.-- Signs. Things belonging to the time of grace, foretold by the prophets, cannot make you believe concerning my coming. But the signs. He spoke of the signs of the times concerning his own coming or his passion, to which a rosy sky at evening is like. And likewise concerning the tribulation before the coming that is to be, to which a rosy sky with gloom is like. It shall not be given him. But it is given to the faithful from heaven, when, the apostles looking on, he ascended into heaven, 141Band sent the Holy Spirit.

VERS. 5.-- They forgot to take bread. How do they not have loaves, who but now, the baskets being filled, went up into the boat, and as they sail hear that they are to beware of the leaven of the Pharisees.
