But nevertheless the Scripture says that they forgot to take loaves with them, being caught by the sweetness of the one true and inward bread (who was with them), judging that they cared little about the care of the body and about necessaries, since even natural refreshment itself had passed from their mind.

VERS. 6.-- Of the leaven. Leaven is perverse doctrine, which even in small quantity corrupts the whole mass, and draws it into its own savour.

VERS. 8.-- Why do you think. Why do you think that I spoke of earthly loaves, about which you need not be in doubt, since out of so few I made so many 141Cleavings abound?

VERS. 11.-- Beware of the leaven of the Pharisees. Of superstitious doctrine, which after the manner of leaven feigns that it has the substance of true bread. By this he hints what the five loaves or the seven signify. For if by leaven he means heretical teachings, the loaves are true doctrine.

VERS. 13.-- Into the parts of Caesarea Philippi. This is the place where the Jordan rises at the foot of Lebanon, out of Jor and Dan, two springs. That men say he is. They are men who speak of the Son of man: for they are gods who understand the Godhead. The Son of man? The Lord professes of himself the lowliness of his humanity, while the disciples understand his Godhead.

VERS. 14.-- Some, John the Baptist. So they could 141Derr about Elias or about the rest of the others, as Herod erred about John when he said: John, whom I beheaded, is risen from the dead: and therefore mighty works are wrought in him (Marc. VI) [Vulg. Mc 6:16 Quem ego decollavi Joannem, hic a mortuis resurrexit, "John, whom I beheaded, he is risen again from the dead", and the clause about the mighty works belongs to Mc 6:14, et propterea virtutes operantur in illo]. Therefore he wills that their errors be set forth, so that, these being driven off, the disciples may bring forth the truth, not out of the opinion of the common people, but out of the Lord's revelation.

VERS. 17.-- Simon Peter answering. Therefore one answers, and to one answer is made on behalf of all, that unity may be kept in all. Of the living God. For a distinction from the false gods, which the Gentiles set up for themselves either out of dead men or out of sensible matter.

Barjona. Well is he the son of the dove, because he followed the Lord with a devout simplicity, or because he was full of spiritual grace. He is called the son of the Holy Spirit, 142Awho was shown in the dove, by him whom he had called the Son of the living God, and to the son of the dove the Father reveals, because the operation of the Father and of the Son with the Holy Spirit is one. He reveals, I say, the mystery of faith, because flesh and blood could not reveal it, that is, men puffed up with carnal wisdom, who are not sons of the dove, and therefore strangers to the wisdom of the Spirit; of whom above: Others John the Baptist, and others Elias, etc.

VERS. 18.-- Thou art. I do not say, thou shalt be called, but thou art Peter, for the strength of thy faith and the constancy of thy confession. Peter. From me, the Rock, yet so that I keep for myself the dignity of the foundation: thou shalt set clean stones upon me, and shalt cast away the leprous.

Just as light and the other names, as: You are the salt of the earth, were allotted to the apostles by the Lord, so to Simon he gave from 142Bhimself the name of Peter.

And the gates. The gates of hell are sins, threats, blandishments, heresies, by which the weak rush into death, who are to be believed to have built the house of their profession not truly upon the rock, but upon the sand, that is, not to follow Christ with a simple and true intention, but to have put forward the habit for some earthly cause. For he who receives the faith of Christ in the inmost love of the heart easily overcomes whatever assails him from without.

VERS. 19.-- And whatsoever thou shalt bind. That is, whomsoever thou shalt have adjudged to eternal punishments as persisting in his sins, or whomsoever thou shalt have absolved as humble and truly penitent, so shall it be also in heaven.

And whatsoever thou shalt bind upon earth. (HIER.) The other apostles have 142Cindeed the same judicial power, to whom after the resurrection he says: Receive ye the Holy Spirit (Joan. XX), etc., as far as whence in Leviticus the lepers are bidden to show themselves to the priests, whom the priests do not make lepers, but discern who are clean or unclean, so also here.

VERS. 20.-- That they should tell no man, etc. Above he commands them to preach his coming, here that they should not say that he is Jesus. But it is one thing to preach Christ, which is a common name of dignity: another to preach Jesus, which is the Saviour's own name. As if he said: It does not profit that my majesty be publicly preached to the peoples, who are to see me suffer many things, which will be lawful only then, when the resurrection shall have shown the power of the Godhead. Jesus Christ. The Saviour: lest they should be scandalized in him whom a little 142Dafter they are to see spat upon and crucified.

VERS. 21.-- From that time, etc. Behold the reason why the preaching was forbidden, as if he said: Preach me then, when I shall have suffered these things, because before that it does not profit.

VERS. 22.-- Far be it. Peter does not wish his own confession to be destroyed, nor does he think that the Son of God can be slain.

VERS. 23.-- Go. Above: all those glorious things were promised for the future, not given in the present. I will build my Church, and to thee I will give the keys, etc. Which if he had given at once, error would never afterwards have found place in him. Because thou savourest not. It does not please thee that I should redeem the world by my death, which is the will of the Father: but thou savourest these things carnally.

143AVERS. 24.-- If any man will. The passion and the resurrection having been foretold to the apostles, he exhorts both them and the crowds to follow. Let him deny himself. That which is by oldness, that he may take hold of that which is beyond himself. Vices being removed, let virtue follow: let him take up his cross, and follow me. That which was by oldness, he had denied who said: But I myself live, now not I. [Vulg. Gal 2:20 Vivo autem, jam non ego, without the emphatic ego Migne adds after Vivo autem] But that which was beyond himself he had begun: yet Christ liveth in me (Gal. II). Let him take up his cross. That is: the cross is taken up either when the body is afflicted through abstinence, or when the mind is afflicted through compassion for one's neighbour. Paul had the cross of abstinence when he said (II Cor. XI): I chastise my body and bring it into subjection. And the cross of compassion too, when he says: Who is weak, and I am not weak?

143BVERS. 26.-- For what doth it profit. The Church has a time of persecution: it has also a time of peace. These times he designates in his precepts. For in the time of persecution the soul must be laid down, and in the time of peace earthly desires must be broken.

Or what, etc. Let neither gains, nor fear, nor anything else draw a man back from salvation: because what exchange shall a man give, etc. I will take the chalice of salvation, and I will call upon the name of the Lord (Psal. CXV). Or: a man will be able to give nothing earthly for a soul that is lost.

VERS. 27.-- For the Son. Nor let it seem hard to suffer this: Because the sufferings of this time are not worthy to be compared with the glory to come, that shall be revealed in us (Rom. VIII).

VERS. 28.-- There are some. Because the apostles were untaught, there is promised them even in the present life some 143Cconsolation, that they may believe the more surely concerning the life to come. In the kingdom. In the kingdom of eternal blessedness, or by showing his power in the Church that now is.

CAPUT XVII.

VERS. 1.-- Et post dies, etc. Idem Marcus dicit, sed Lucas octo. Hi ergo ponunt medios, Lucas addit, etc., usque ad et in futuro omni modo separabuntur, cum abscondet eos in abscondito faciei suae a conturbatione hominum.

Petrum. Nomina istorum conveniunt. Quisque debet esse Petrus, id est firmus in fide. Inde Jacobus, id est supplantator vitiorum. Inde Joannes, id est, omnia gratiae divinae ascribens contra superbiam 143Dvitae, quae solet oriri ex virtutibus.

VERS. 2.-- Transfiguratus est. Speciem quam habebat per naturam, ostendit non amittens carnem quam assumpserat voluntate. Illud autem corpus quod habuit per naturam, dedit discipulis suis in coena, non mortale, non corruptibile. Resplenduit facies. In exemplum futurae beatitudinis et claritatis, quam videbunt justi, prius sublatis impiis. Vestimenta autem, etc. Facta sunt alba quod non ante, quia nondum apparuit quod enimus, sed cum apparuerit, similes ei erimus. Marcus ait, qualia fullo super terram non potest facere. Id est, Deus in hac vita tam claras nequit facere, quia nullus sine peccato. Nullus mundator sui in terra faciet, quod Deus in coelo, 144Amundans vestem suam ab omni inquinamento. Fullo est doctor animarum, vel mundator sui corporis.

VERS. 3.-- Et ecce. Quales vel quid locuti, Lucas dicit: Visi in majestate, et dicebant excessum ejus quem completurus erat in Jerusalem (Luc. IX), id est, passionem et resurrectionem. Significant oracula, etc., usque ad quod si Petrus, videns glorificatam humanitatem, tanto afficitur gaudio, ut nunquam velit ab ejus intuitu secerni, quid putandum est de his qui Divinitatem videre meruerunt.

VERS. 5.-- Adhuc eo loquente. Ut discat qui tabernaculum quaesivit, in resurrectione non tegmine domorum, sed Spiritus sancti gratia protegendos esse. Ecce nubes lucida, etc. Sicut baptizato Domino, 144Bsic et clarificato mysterium sanctae Trinitatis ostenditur: quia gloriam ejus quam in baptismo confitemur credendo, in resurrectione laudabimus videndo. Spiritus sanctus ibi in columba, hic in nube lucida. Quia qui modo simplici corde fidem servat, tunc aperta visione quod crediderit contemplabitur, et ea gratia qua illustrabitur, protegetur. Et ecce vox, etc. Quia imprudenter quaesivit, responsionem Domini non meruit: sed vox Patris de Filio testatur, docetque Petrum, et in eo caeteros veritatem. Hic est Filius. Moyses et Elias sunt servi, et nobiscum debent in cordibus Domino praeparare tabernaculum. Ipsum audite. Hoc jam Pater praecipit, quod Moyses incarnationi testimonium dans praedixit: Prophetam suscitabit (Deut. XVIII), etc. Quasi diceret: 144CIste est quem Moyses promisit.

VERS. 6.-- Et audientes discipuli ceciderunt in faciem. Altiorem enim sensu unam utriusque intellexerunt Deitatem. Timuerunt, vel quia se errasse cognoverunt, vel quia nubes operuit eos, vel quia vocem Patris audierunt.

VERS. 7.-- Surgite, etc. Pulso metu, doctrina datur, quo manente non audiretur.

VERS. 8.-- Levantes autem oculos, etc. Ablata nube vident Jesum, et Moysen et Eliam evanuisse: quia postquam legis et prophetarum umbra discessit, quae velamento suo apostolos texerat, verum lumen in Evangelio reperitur. Nisi solum Jesum. Si enim Moyses et Elias cum Jesu perseverassent, vox Patris videretur incerta, cui potius dedisset testimonium.

144DVERS. 9.-- Nemini dixeritis nisi, etc. Ne gloria futuri regni praeostensa incredibilis esset, et apud rudes sequens crux scandalum faceret.

VERS. 10.-- Quod Eliam oporteat, etc. Traditio Pharisaeorum est juxta Malachiam prophetam, quod Elias veniet ante adventum Salvatoris, et reducet corda patrum ad filios et filiorum ad patres, et restituet omnia in antiquum statum. Aestimant ergo apostoli transformationem gloriae ultimam hanc esse quam viderant et dicunt. Si jam venisti in gloria, quomodo praecursor tuus non apparet, maxime quia et Eliam viderant recessisse.

VERS. 13.-- Tunc intellexerunt, etc. Ex indicio 145Apassionis suae quam saepe dixerat, et praecursoris sui quam completam videbant.

VERS. 14.-- Et cum venisset. Loca rebus congruunt, etc., usque ad infirmos confortat, docet, castigat. Quia lunaticus est. Hunc Marcus surdum et mutum dicit, etc., usque ad per creaturam infamet creatorem.

VERS. 15.-- Et obtuli, etc. Latenter accusat discipulos, cum saepe impossibilitas curandi non vitio curantium, sed eorum qui curandi sunt, proveniat.

VERS. 16.-- Respondens. Non taedio superatus, qui mitis est in verba erumpit, sed sicut medicus aegroto contra sua praecepta agenti.

VERS. 17.-- Et increpavit. Quia iratus est non homini, sed vitio: et per unum hominem Judaeos arguit 145Binfidelitatis. Unde addit: Afferte illum huc ad me. Et exiit ab eo daemonium. Marcus dicit, etc., usque ad quam prius quando possidebat quietus.

VERS. 19.-- Propter incredulitatem. Ecce quando non exaudimur, non est impossibilitas praestantis, sed culpa deprecantis. Nam ex opposito si fides vestra tam fervens esset quam tribulata, magis et magis calesceret. Sicut granum sinapis. Fidem perfectam grano sinapis comparat, quae sit in facie humilis, et in pectore fervens: vilis videntibus, et nullarum virium apparens, sed pressuris trita, quod intus habet ostendit.

VERS. 20.-- Hoc autem genus. Quomodo protervitas diaboli superetur ostendit, ut graviora quaeque sciamus jejuniis et orationibus superanda: et iram 145CDei in nos hoc remedio posse placari. Nisi per orationem. Ista carnalium voluptatum mutabilitas non mutatur, etc., usque ad unde dicitur: Sine intermissione orate (I Thess. V).

VERS. 21.-- Filius hominis. Semper laetis miscet tristia, ut cum venerint quasi praevisa minus moleste ferantur.

VERS. 22.-- Et contristati. Hoc non de infidelitate provenit, sed de dilectione: quia nihil humile de eo patiuntur audire.

VERS. 23.-- Et cum venissent Capharnaum. Per Augustum Caesarem Judaea facta est tributaria, etc., usque ad utrum reddat tributa, an contradicat Caesari.

VERS. 24.-- Quid tibi videtur. Antequam Petrus 145Dsuggerat, Dominus interrogat ne scandalizentur discipuli ad postulationem tributi, cum videant eum nosse quae eo absente gesta sunt. Reges terrae. Dominus noster, et secundum carnem filius regis erat ex David natus, et omnipotentis verbum: ergo ut filius regum tributa non debebat, sed pro humilitate carnis decet eum implere omnem justitiam.

VERS. 25.-- Ergo liberi, etc. Liberi sunt filii regni in omni regno, tunc multo magis liberi sunt filii illius regni, sub quo sunt omnia regna in quolibet terreno regno.

CHAPTER XVII.

VERS. 1.-- And after days, etc. Mark says the same, but Luke eight. These therefore set down the middle days, Luke adds, etc., as far as and in the world to come they shall be parted in every way, when he shall hide them in the hiding of his face from the disturbance of men.

Peter. The names of these men are fitting. Each ought to be Peter, that is, firm in faith. Then James, that is, the supplanter of vices. Then John, that is, one ascribing all things to divine grace against the pride 143Dof life, which is wont to arise out of the virtues.

VERS. 2.-- He was transfigured. The form which he had by nature he showed, not losing the flesh which he had assumed by will. But that body which he had by nature he gave to his disciples at the supper, not mortal, not corruptible. His face did shine. As a pattern of the blessedness and brightness to come, which the just shall see, the wicked being first taken away. And his garments, etc. They were made white, which they were not before, because it hath not yet appeared what we shall be, but when it shall appear, we shall be like to him. Mark says, such as a fuller upon earth cannot make. That is, God cannot in this life make them so bright, because no one is without sin. No cleanser of himself upon earth will do what God does in heaven, 144Acleansing his garment from every defilement. The fuller is a teacher of souls, or one who cleanses his own body.

VERS. 3.-- And behold. Of what sort they were, or what they spoke, Luke says: Seen in majesty, and they spoke of his decease which he was to accomplish in Jerusalem (Luc. IX), that is, his passion and resurrection. They signify the oracles, etc., as far as that if Peter, seeing the humanity glorified, is affected with so great joy that he would never wish to be parted from the sight of it, what is to be thought of those who have deserved to see the Godhead.

VERS. 5.-- As he was yet speaking. That he who sought a tabernacle may learn that in the resurrection they are to be sheltered not by the roof of houses, but by the grace of the Holy Spirit. Behold a bright cloud, etc. As when the Lord was baptized, 144Bso also when he was glorified the mystery of the holy Trinity is shown: because his glory, which in baptism we confess by believing, in the resurrection we shall praise by seeing. The Holy Spirit there in a dove, here in a bright cloud. Because he who now keeps the faith with a simple heart will then contemplate in open vision what he has believed, and will be sheltered by that grace by which he will be enlightened. And behold a voice, etc. Because he asked imprudently, he did not deserve an answer from the Lord: but the voice of the Father bears witness concerning the Son, and teaches Peter, and in him the rest, the truth. This is the Son. Moses and Elias are servants, and together with us they ought to prepare in their hearts a tabernacle for the Lord. Hear ye him. This the Father now enjoins, which Moses, giving testimony to the incarnation, foretold: He shall raise up a prophet (Deut. XVIII), etc. As if he said: 144CThis is he whom Moses promised.

VERS. 6.-- And the disciples hearing fell upon their face. For they understood, higher than their sense, the one Godhead of both. They feared, either because they knew that they had erred, or because the cloud covered them, or because they heard the voice of the Father.

VERS. 7.-- Arise, etc. Fear being driven off, doctrine is given, which while fear remained would not be heard.

VERS. 8.-- And lifting up their eyes, etc. The cloud being taken away, they see Jesus, and that Moses and Elias had vanished: because after the shadow of the law and of the prophets departed, which had covered the apostles with its veil, the true light is found in the Gospel. But only Jesus. For if Moses and Elias had remained with Jesus, the voice of the Father would seem uncertain, to which of them it had rather given testimony.

144DVERS. 9.-- Tell it to no man unless, etc. [Vulg. Mt 17:9 Nemini dixeritis visionem, "Tell the vision to no man", and Migne prints nisi where the received text has visionem] Lest the glory of the kingdom to come, being shown beforehand, should be past belief, and lest among the untaught the cross that followed should make a scandal.

VERS. 10.-- That Elias must, etc. It is the tradition of the Pharisees, according to Malachi the prophet, that Elias will come before the coming of the Saviour, and will turn back the hearts of the fathers to the sons and of the sons to the fathers, and will restore all things to their ancient state. The apostles therefore judge that this transformation of glory which they had seen is the last, and they say: If thou hast already come in glory, how does thy forerunner not appear, especially because they had seen Elias too depart.

VERS. 13.-- Then they understood, etc. From the token 145Aof his own passion, which he had often spoken of, and of his forerunner's, which they saw fulfilled.

VERS. 14.-- And when he was come. The places agree with the things, etc., as far as he comforts the weak, teaches, chastens. For he is a lunatic. Mark calls him deaf and dumb, etc., as far as that through the creature he may defame the creator.

VERS. 15.-- And I brought him, etc. He secretly accuses the disciples, whereas often the impossibility of healing comes not from the fault of those who heal, but of those who are to be healed.

VERS. 16.-- Answering. Not overcome by weariness does he who is meek break out into words, but as a physician does with a sick man who acts against his own prescriptions.

VERS. 17.-- And he rebuked. Because he was angry not with the man, but with the vice: and through one man he convicts the Jews 145Bof unbelief. Whence he adds: Bring him hither to me. And the demon went out of him. Mark says, etc., as far as than before, when it possessed him quietly.

VERS. 19.-- Because of unbelief. Behold, when we are not heard, it is no impossibility on the part of him who grants, but the fault of him who prays. For on the contrary, if your faith were as fervent as it is troubled, it would grow warm more and more. As a grain of mustard seed. He compares perfect faith to a grain of mustard seed, which is lowly in appearance and fervent in the breast: mean to those who see it, and appearing of no strength, but when bruised by pressures it shows what it has within.

VERS. 20.-- But this kind. He shows how the insolence of the devil is overcome, that we may know that all the graver things are to be overcome by fastings and prayers, and that the anger 145Cof God against us can be appeased by this remedy. But by prayer. This changeableness of carnal pleasures is not changed, etc., as far as whence it is said: Pray without ceasing (I Thess. V).

VERS. 21.-- The Son of man. He always mixes sad things with glad, that when they have come they may be borne less grievously, as things foreseen.

VERS. 22.-- And they were sorrowful. This came not from unbelief, but from love: because they cannot bear to hear anything lowly of him.

VERS. 23.-- And when they were come to Capharnaum. Through Augustus Caesar Judaea was made tributary, etc., as far as whether he pays the tribute, or gainsays Caesar.

VERS. 24.-- What is thy opinion. Before Peter 145Dcan suggest it, the Lord asks, lest the disciples be scandalized at the demand of the tribute, since they see that he knows the things that were done in his absence. The kings of the earth. Our Lord was, according to the flesh also, a king's son, born of David, and the Word of the Almighty: therefore, as a son of kings, he did not owe tribute, but for the lowliness of the flesh it becomes him to fulfil all justice.

VERS. 25.-- Then the sons are free, etc. The sons of a kingdom are free in every kingdom, then much more are the sons of that kingdom free under which all kingdoms are, in any earthly kingdom whatever.

CAPUT XVIII.

VERS. 1.-- Quis putas, etc. Ex aequalitate pretii 146Aqua in tributi redditione Domino fuerat comparatus, arbitrati sunt Petrum omnibus apostolis praelatum. In regno coelorum. Vel regnum Dei, doctrina est Evangelii, cui obedire simpliciter et sine ulla retractatione debemus, sicut puer non contradicit doctoribus.

VERS. 2.-- Et advocans. Secundum Marcum puerum Jesus complectitur, significans humiles dignos suo complexu et dilectione. Parvulum, etc. Parvulum aetate, ut similitudinem demonstraret. Vel se statuit in medio qui non venit ministrari, sed ministrare. Vel parvulum, id est, Spiritum sanctum posuit in cordibus eorum, ut humilitate superbiam vitarent.

VERS. 4.-- Quicunque ergo humiliaverit, etc. Invitat 146Bdiscipulos suos, et omnes alios Dominus, ut quod puer habet per naturam, id est innocentiam et humilitatem, hoc habeant discipuli et omnes alii per virtutem. Sicut parvulus iste. Innocens erit sicut iste parvulus, qui non perseverat in ira: Jesus non meminit: visam mulierem non cupit.

VERS. 6.-- Qui autem scandalizaverit. Etsi hoc generaliter dicitur contra omnes, etc., usque ad nos oportet consulere expedit, etc. Expedit. Quia multo melius est pro culpa brevem suscipere poenam quam aeternis servari cruciatibus. Non enim judicabit Deus bis in idipsum.

VERS. 7.-- Vae homini. Quia non judicatur secundum utilitatem quae inde provenit, sed secundum exitum quem intendit.

146CVERS. 8.-- Si autem manus tua. Docuit supra ne scandalizemus credentes, etc., usque ad per occasionem pietatis pateas scandalis. Bonum tibi est, etc. Melius est vitam solitariam ducere, quam ob vitae praesentis necessaria vitam aeternam perdere.

VERS. 10.-- Videte ne contemnatis. Sic praecipio vobis severitatem dimittendi omnes amicitias, etc., usque ad salutem infirmorum quantum potestis. Quia angeli eorum. Cur non sunt contemnendi? quia pro eis quotidie mittuntur angeli. (HIER.) Magna dignitas animarum, ut unaquaeque habeat ab ortu nativitatis in custodiam sui angelum delegatum. Semper vident, etc. Visione Dei manifesta perfruuntur, etc., usque ad intra ipsum currunt qui ubique est.

VERS. 12.-- Si fuerint alicui centum, etc. Dominus 146Dcentum oves habuit, etc., usque ad quibus ad perfectionem unitas deest. Nonne relinquet, etc. (GREG.) Quia seipsum exinanivit formam servi accipiens, etc., usque ad cum tantum sit si quod male gessit damnat injustus.

VERS. 13.-- Gaudebit, etc. Magis Dominus gaudet de nostra reparatione (quia magis nos gaudere facit) quam de angelorum immutabilitate. Major enim Dei virtus et humilitas in nostra reparatione cognoscitur quam in nostra creatione.

VERS. 15.-- Si autem peccaverit in te, etc. Si enim in Deum, non est nostri arbitrii. Hoc ordine scandala vitare debemus ne quem laedamus: et peccantem 147Azelo justitiae corripiamus, et poenitenti misericordiae viscera pandamus. Corripe eum, etc. Non passim jubet peccanti dimittere; sed poenitenti. Ita peccat qui videns fratrem peccare tacet: sicut qui poenitenti non indulget. Nam quod dixit: si poenitet dimitte, praemisit, si peccaverit increpa. Nec difficilis sit venia nec remissa indulgentia.

VERS. 17.-- Dic Ecclesiae. Ut detestationi eum habeat, etc., usque ad damnantur humanam sententiam divina sententia corroborari. Sicut Ethnicus. Detestabilior est qui sub nomine fidelis agit, etc., usque ad per furta et per perjuria.

VERS. 18.-- Amen dico, etc. Adeo vestra sententia confirmatur divina vel ad recipiendum poenitentem, vel ad abjiciendum contumacem.

147BVERS. 20.-- Si duo. Typice. Ubi spiritus anima et corpus consenserint, ut non sit inter eos bellum, carne cupiente contra spiritum et spiritu contra carnem, quidquid petunt fiet. Quia si corpus vult quae spiritus vult, tantum bonarum rerum est petitio. Ibi sum in me. Omnis sermo scilicet ad concordiam provocat et pacem quam adeo commendat, ut dicat Deum in medio esse eorum, quia pax et charitas sedem sibi in pacificis constituit, et unitatis merito omnia petenda impetranda esse confirmat.

VERS. 23.-- Ideo assimilatum. Ut sciatis dimittendum esse conservis, ostendam per similitudinem, quia si ille rex et dominus dimisit servo, multo magis homo conservis suis.

VERS. 25.-- Et uxorem. Eum non solum populum, 147Csed etiam praelatos et subditos simul cum tota familia vel substantia eorum pro magnitudine delictorum tradidit exteris nationibus: ut quod noluerunt voto, solvant tormento. Jussit pro cupiditate et operibus quasi uxore et filiis poenas solvere.

VERS. 27.-- Misertus autem. Misericors Dominus non solum captivitatem Judaicam absolvit, sed etiam peccatorum omnium veniam in adventu Christi se dare promisit, si persisterent in incepto. Dimisit ei. Id est, pondere carnalium observationum absolvit, et debitum dimisit, id est priores transgressiones.

VERS. 28.-- Invenit unum. Gentilem populum, quasi sibi debitorem fatigabat: gratiam Christi non esse eis ad salutem dicens, carnales observantias ab eis non modo persuasionibus, sed etiam tormentis 147Dexspectabat.

VERS. 32.-- Serve nequam. Saepe debita dimisi tibi, quando in angustiis rogasti; sed liberatus compassionem non habuisti, quin potius prophetas et apostolos persequeris, et ingratus et de justitia praesumens spernis gentes, indignas tua societate in Christo judicans.

CHAPTER XVIII.

VERS. 1.-- Who, thinkest thou, etc. From the equality of the price 146Aby which, in the paying of the tribute, he had been reckoned with the Lord, they judged that Peter was set above all the apostles. In the kingdom of heaven. Or the kingdom of God is the teaching of the Gospel, which we ought to obey simply and without any drawing back, as a child does not contradict his teachers.

VERS. 2.-- And calling to him. According to Mark, Jesus embraces the child, signifying that the humble are worthy of his embrace and of his love. A little one, etc. A little one in age, that he might show a likeness. Or he set himself in the midst, who came not to be ministered unto, but to minister. Or a little one, that is, he set the Holy Spirit in their hearts, that by humility they might shun pride.

VERS. 4.-- Whosoever therefore shall humble himself, etc. The Lord invites 146Bhis disciples, and all others, that what a child has by nature, that is, innocence and humility, this the disciples and all others may have by virtue. As this little child. He will be innocent as this little child, who does not persist in anger: Jesus [Jesus; read laesus, "when hurt"] does not remember: having seen a woman he does not desire her.

VERS. 6.-- But he that shall scandalize. Although this is said generally against all, etc., as far as we ought to take counsel, it is expedient, etc. It is expedient. Because it is much better to undergo a short punishment for a fault than to be kept for eternal torments. For God will not judge twice for the same thing.

VERS. 7.-- Woe to the man. Because he is not judged according to the profit that comes of it, but according to the outcome that he intends.

146CVERS. 8.-- And if thy hand. He taught above that we should not scandalize believers, etc., as far as that under the occasion of piety thou shouldst lie open to scandals. It is good for thee, etc. It is better to lead a solitary life than for the necessaries of the present life to lose life eternal.

VERS. 10.-- See that you despise not. Thus I enjoin upon you the severity of putting away all friendships, etc., as far as the salvation of the weak as far as you can. For their angels. Why are they not to be despised? because for them angels are daily sent. (HIER.) Great is the dignity of souls, that each one should have, from the beginning of its birth, an angel deputed to guard it. They always see, etc. They enjoy the manifest vision of God, etc., as far as they run within him who is everywhere.

VERS. 12.-- If a man have a hundred, etc. The Lord 146Dhad a hundred sheep, etc., as far as to whom, for perfection, unity is wanting. Doth he not leave, etc. (GREG.) Because he emptied himself, taking the form of a servant, etc., as far as whereas it is only so much, if the unjust man condemns what he has done amiss.

VERS. 13.-- He shall rejoice, etc. [Vulg. Mt 18:13 gaudet super eam magis, "he rejoiceth for it more": the present, where Migne prints the future Gaudebit — the gloss itself reverts to the present gaudet in its next words] The Lord rejoices more over our restoration (because he makes us rejoice the more) than over the unchangeableness of the angels. For the power and the humility of God are known to be greater in our restoration than in our creation.

VERS. 15.-- But if he shall offend against thee, etc. For if against God, it is not in our judgment. In this order we ought to shun scandals, that we hurt no one: and that we rebuke the sinner 147Awith a zeal for justice, and open the bowels of mercy to him that repents. Rebuke him, etc. He does not bid us forgive the sinner indiscriminately; but him that repents. He sins thus, who seeing his brother sin keeps silent: as does he who does not pardon the penitent. For what he said, "if he repents, forgive," he prefaced by "if he shall have sinned, rebuke." Let pardon be neither hard, nor indulgence slack.

VERS. 17.-- Tell the Church. That it may hold him in detestation, etc., as far as they are condemned, that a human sentence is confirmed by the divine sentence. As the heathen. More detestable is he who acts under the name of a believer, etc., as far as through thefts and through perjuries.

VERS. 18.-- Amen I say, etc. So far is your sentence confirmed by the divine, whether for receiving the penitent, or for casting off the obstinate.

147BVERS. 20.-- If two. Typically. Where the spirit, the soul and the body have agreed, so that there is no war between them, the flesh lusting against the spirit and the spirit against the flesh, whatsoever they ask shall be done. Because if the body wills what the spirit wills, the asking is only for good things. There am I in me. [Vulg. Mt 18:20 ibi sum in medio eorum, "there am I in the midst of them" — the words the gloss goes on to quote, ut dicat Deum in medio esse eorum] That is, every discourse calls to concord and to the peace which he so far commends that he says God is in the midst of them, because peace and charity set up their seat in the peaceable, and he affirms that by the merit of unity all things that are to be asked are to be obtained.

VERS. 23.-- Therefore is likened. That you may know that one must forgive one's fellow servants, I will show by a likeness, that if that king and lord forgave his servant, much more must a man forgive his fellow servants.

VERS. 25.-- And his wife. Him, not only the people, 147Cbut also the prelates and the subjects, together with all their household or substance, he delivered over to foreign nations for the greatness of their offences: that what they would not pay by vow, they might pay by torment. He commanded them to pay the penalties for their covetousness and their works, as for a wife and children.

VERS. 27.-- And moved with pity. The merciful Lord not only loosed the Jewish captivity, but also promised that at the coming of Christ he would give pardon of all sins, if they persisted in what they had begun. He forgave him. That is, he loosed him from the weight of carnal observances, and forgave the debt, that is, his former transgressions.

VERS. 28.-- He found one. The Gentile people he wearied, as though it were his debtor: saying that the grace of Christ was not unto salvation for them, not only by persuasions, but even by torments 147Dhe exacted carnal observances from them.

VERS. 32.-- Thou wicked servant. Often have I forgiven thee thy debts, when thou didst ask in thy straits; but being set free thou hadst no compassion, nay rather thou persecutest the prophets and the apostles, and, ungrateful and presuming upon justice, thou despisest the Gentiles, judging them unworthy of thy fellowship in Christ.

CAPUT XIX.

VERS. 3.-- Et accesserunt. Quasi complexione capiatur. Si diceret: Dimittenda est quacunque ex causa, et ducenda alia: pudicitiae praedicator contra se facit. Si dicit non est dimittenda, contra doctrinam Moysi et Dei facit. Ideo responsum temperat naturalem legem, primamque Dei sententiam secundo 148Aopponens, quae non voluntate Dei, sed peccantium necessitate concessa est.

VERS. 4.-- Et feminam. Non ait feminas, ut unum conjugium sit non plura, quod ex repudio priorum quaerebatur.

VERS. 5.-- Adhaerebit uxori. Si malum moribus, si luxuriosa, seu quidlibet hujusmodi sit uxor, velimus nolimus sustinenda est. Cum enim liberi essemus, volentes nos subjecimus servituti. Si ancilla libero, vel servus liberae supponitur, non reputatur conjugium postquam detectum fuerit: sic et de consanguineis. Et erunt duo in carne. Praemium nuptiarum est ex duobus una caro: Castitas conjuncta spiritui unus spiritus efficitur. Salubri consilio Dei factum est, ut portionem sui corporis vir amplecteretur 148Bin femina, ne a se putaretur diversum esse, quod de se cognosceret fabricatum.

VERS. 6.-- Homo non separet. Homo separat, quando pro desiderio secundae primam dimittit: Deus separat (qui et conjungit) quando ex consensu propter cultum Dei, eo quod tempus in arcto sit, sic habemus uxores quasi non habentes.

VERS. 7.-- Quid ergo Moyses, etc. (CHRYS.) Displicet peccatoribus doctrina, etc., usque ad veritatis interpretatio taceatur.

VERS. 9.-- Et aliam duxerit maechatur. Et qui dimissam. Quia poterat accidere, ut aliquis calumniam faceret innocenti, et ob secundam crimen impingeret primae uxori: sic priorem dimittere jubet, ut ea vivente secundam non habeat. Et ne uxor 148Cidem faciat viro eadem cautela praecipitur ne secundum habeat virum. Et quia adultera non timet opprobrium, viro dicitur, quod si talem ducit, sub adulterii crimine sit, dicit Scriptura: Qui adulteram tenet stultus et impius est (Prov. XVIII).

VERS. 10.-- Non expedit. Videbant grave pondus uxorum, quod iracundae, ebriosae et hujusmodi, quae quidem sunt sustinendae, quia, cum essemus liberi, subjecimus nos servituti.

VERS. 12.-- Sunt enim. Tria sunt genera eunuchorum, duo carnalia, eorum qui sic nascuntur, et qui captivitate et deliciis dominarum abscissi sunt. Tertium spirituale, qui, eum possent esse viri, propter Christum continent. Qui facti sunt. Inter hos computantur etiam hi qui specie religionis simulant 148Dcastitatem. Qui potest. Vox est hortantis non jubentis, quasi unusquisque vires suas consideret, an queat virginitatis et pudicitiae implere praecepta.

VERS. 14.-- Sinite parvulos. Si sancti sunt futuri, cur vetatis venire ad patrem? Si mali, cur sententiam profertis ante culpam. Talium. Nam quales sunt modo, meum quidem est, quod honorate: quales post futuri sunt, ipsorum est, quod miseremini. Non ait istorum sed talium: ut ostendant non aetatem regnare, sed mores: et his qui similem habent innocentiam et simplicitatem, praemium promittit.

VERS. 15.-- Et cum imposuisset. Imponit manus parvulis, quando humilibus auxilium gratiae tribuit.

VERS. 16.-- Et ecce unus. (BED.) Audierat ille 149Asimiles parvulo condignos esse regno, vultque certius non per parabolas sed aperte exponi, quibus meritis vitam possit consequi. (HIER.) Iste adolescens et dives et superbus erat. Et secundum alium evangelistam eum non voto discentis sed tentantis interrogat.

VERS. 17.-- Quid me interrogas de bono? Quia bonum dixit magistrum, et non Deum, vel Dei Filium confessus est: dicit quemlibet sanctum hominem comparatione Dei non esse bonum. Christus autem non renuit testimonium bonitatis, sed magistri absque Deo excludit errorem. Non se magistrum bonum esse negat, sed magistrum absque Deo nullum bonum esse dicit. Serva mandata. Iste quaesierat, quid faciam? contra non ait, disce: quia Deus eum docuerat factor rationalis creaturae: ostendens 149Bquia ille non est bonus qui quaerit de bono, sed qui facit quod bonum est.

VERS. 20.-- Dicit ei adolescens. (BED.) Non putandus iste (ut quidam aiunt) vel Dominum tentasse, vel de sua vita mentitus esse, sed simpliciter ut vixit, confessus est: alioquin (ut alius Evangelista ait) non, intuitus arcana cordis ejus, diligere eum diceretur Jesus. Diligit enim eos qui mandata legis custodiunt, sed quod ibi minus est his qui perfecti volunt esse, adjungit; unde sequitur: Si vis perfectus esse. Omnia haec. (HIER.) Mentitur. Si enim diligeret proximum suum sicut seipsum, opere complesset, non esset tristis cum sua pauperibus dare juberetur.

VERS. 23.-- Dives difficile. Divites non manentes, sed qui esse desierunt intrabunt. De omnibus tutius 149Cest, nec habere nec amare divitias.

VERS. 24.-- Quam divitem. (ISID.) Nullus dives intrabit, id est, confidens in divitiis, ut Jesus secundum Marcum exponit.

VERS. 25.-- Mirabantur. Quia in numero divitum deputabant omnes qui amant divitias, etsi non habeant. Aliter enim cum sit major turba pauperum, non dicerent: Quis poterit salvus esse?

VERS. 26.-- Deum. Qui ab amore terrenorum ad charitatem aeternorum convertit: non quod cupidi et superbi intrent.

VERS. 28.-- In regeneratione. Duae sunt regenerationes, prima ex aqua et Spiritu sancto, secunda in resurrectione. Ita duae adoptiones et duae resurrectiones. Filius hominis. Id est, cum servi forma 149Dquae judicata est judiciariam exercuerit potestatem, eritis et vos mecum judices. Super sedes duodecim. Per duodecim sedes, universitas judicantium. Per duodecim tribus, universitas judicandorum intelligitur. Qui reliquerunt omnia, et secuti sunt Dominum, hi judices erunt: qui licita habentes recte usi sunt, judicabuntur, quibus dicetur: Venite, benedicti Patris mei: Esurivi enim et dedistis mihi manducare. Duo enim sunt ordines bonorum.

VERS. 29.-- Centuplum. Id est, qui carnalia pro Domino dimiserit, spiritualia accipiet quae comparatione et merito sui ita erunt, quasi parvo numero centenarius comparetur. Marcus ait: Centies tantum nunc in tempore hoc cum persecutionibus fratres et 150Asorores, et in futuro vitam aeternam (Marc. X), id est, in hac vita persecutionibus plena gaudium regni degustant, et omnium electorum sincera dilectione fruuntur. Sed quia multi tepescunt vel retrocedunt, subdit: Multi autem erunt primi (Luc. XV), etc.

CHAPTER XIX.

VERS. 3.-- And they came. As though he might be caught in a dilemma. If he should say: She is to be put away for any cause whatever, and another is to be married, the preacher of chastity acts against himself. If he says she is not to be put away, he acts against the teaching of Moses and of God. Therefore he tempers his answer, setting the natural law and the first sentence of God over against the second, 148Awhich was granted not by the will of God, but by the necessity of those who sin.

VERS. 4.-- And female. He does not say females, that there may be one marriage and not many, which was being sought by the putting away of former wives.

VERS. 5.-- He shall cleave to a wife. If a wife be evil in her ways, if wanton, or of any such sort whatever, willing or unwilling we must bear with her. For when we were free, we willingly subjected ourselves to servitude. If a bondwoman is put under a free man, or a bondman under a free woman, it is not reckoned a marriage after it has been found out: so also concerning kindred. And they shall be two in flesh. The reward of marriage is one flesh out of two: chastity joined to the spirit is made one spirit. It was done by the wholesome counsel of God that a man should embrace a portion of his own body 148Bin the woman, lest that should be thought different from himself which he knew to have been fashioned out of himself.

VERS. 6.-- Let not man separate. Man separates, when out of desire for a second he puts away the first: God separates (who also joins) when by consent, for the worship of God, because the time is short, we so have wives as though not having them.

VERS. 7.-- Why then did Moses, etc. (CHRYS.) Doctrine displeases sinners, etc., as far as that the interpretation of the truth be passed over in silence.

VERS. 9.-- And shall marry another, committeth adultery. And he that shall marry her that is put away. Because it could happen that someone should bring a false charge against an innocent woman, and for the sake of a second fasten a crime upon his first wife: so he bids him put away the first in such a way that while she lives he may not have a second. And lest a wife 148Cshould do the same to her husband, the same caution is enjoined, that she should not have a second husband. And because an adulteress does not fear reproach, it is said to the husband that if he marries such a one he is under the charge of adultery, the Scripture says: He that keepeth an adulteress is foolish and wicked (Prov. XVIII).

VERS. 10.-- It is not expedient. They saw the heavy burden of wives, that they are wrathful, given to drink and the like, who nevertheless must be borne with, because, when we were free, we subjected ourselves to servitude.

VERS. 12.-- For there are. There are three kinds of eunuchs, two carnal: of those who are so born, and of those who have been cut by captivity and by the pleasures of their mistresses. The third is spiritual: those who, though they could be husbands, are continent for Christ's sake. Who were made so. Among these are reckoned also those who under the appearance of religion feign 148Dchastity. He that can. It is the voice of one exhorting, not of one commanding, as though each should weigh his own strength, whether he is able to fulfil the precepts of virginity and of chastity.

VERS. 14.-- Suffer the little children. If they are to be holy, why do you forbid them to come to the Father? If evil, why do you pronounce sentence before the fault. Of such. For as to what sort they now are, that indeed is mine, in that you honour them: as to what sort they are afterwards to be, that is their own, in that you have pity on them. He does not say, of these, but, of such: that they may show that it is not age that reigns, but character: and to those who have a like innocence and simplicity he promises the reward.

VERS. 15.-- And when he had laid on. He lays hands upon little ones, when he bestows the help of grace upon the humble.

VERS. 16.-- And behold one. (BED.) He had heard 149Athat those like a little child are worthy of the kingdom, and he wishes it to be set forth more certainly, not by parables but openly, by what merits he might obtain life. (HIER.) This young man was both rich and proud. And according to another evangelist he questions him not with the intent of one learning but of one tempting.

VERS. 17.-- Why askest thou me concerning good? Because he called him a good master, and did not confess him God, or the Son of God: he says that any holy man whatever is not good in comparison with God. But Christ does not refuse the testimony of goodness, but shuts out the error of a master apart from God. He does not deny that he is a good master, but he says that no master apart from God is good. Keep the commandments. This man had asked, what shall I do? in return he does not say, learn: because God, the maker of the rational creature, had taught him: showing 149Bthat he is not good who asks about the good, but he who does what is good.

VERS. 20.-- The young man saith to him. (BED.) [Vulg. Mt 19:20 Dicit illi adolescens, and Migne prints ei where the received text has illi] This man is not to be thought (as some say) either to have tempted the Lord, or to have lied about his own life, but he confessed simply as he had lived: otherwise (as another Evangelist says) Jesus, looking into the secrets of his heart, would not be said to love him. For he loves those who keep the commandments of the law, but what is lacking there he adds for those who wish to be perfect; whence there follows: If thou wilt be perfect. All these. (HIER.) He lies. For if he loved his neighbour as himself, he would have fulfilled it in deed, and would not have been sad when he was bidden to give his own goods to the poor.

VERS. 23.-- A rich man hardly. Not the rich who remain such, but those who have ceased to be such, shall enter. Of all things it is safer 149Cneither to have nor to love riches.

VERS. 24.-- Than for a rich man. (ISID.) No rich man shall enter, that is, no one trusting in riches, as Jesus expounds it according to Mark.

VERS. 25.-- They wondered. Because they counted in the number of the rich all who love riches, even if they do not have them. For otherwise, since the crowd of the poor is greater, they would not say: Who shall be able to be saved?

VERS. 26.-- God. Who turns them from the love of earthly things to the charity of eternal things: not that the covetous and the proud enter.

VERS. 28.-- In the regeneration. There are two regenerations, the first of water and of the Holy Spirit, the second in the resurrection. So there are two adoptions and two resurrections. The Son of man. That is, when the form of a servant 149Dwhich was judged shall have exercised judicial power, you also shall be judges with me. Upon twelve seats. By the twelve seats, the whole company of those who judge. By the twelve tribes is understood the whole company of those who are to be judged. Those who left all things and followed the Lord, these shall be judges: those who, having lawful goods, used them rightly, shall be judged, to whom it shall be said: Come, ye blessed of my Father: For I was hungry, and you gave me to eat. For there are two orders of the good.

VERS. 29.-- A hundredfold. That is, he who shall have left carnal things for the Lord shall receive spiritual things, which in comparison and by their own worth will be such as if a hundred were set against a small number. Mark says: A hundred times as much now in this time, with persecutions, brethren and 150Asisters, and in the world to come life everlasting (Marc. X), that is, in this life full of persecutions they taste beforehand the joy of the kingdom, and enjoy the sincere love of all the elect. But because many grow lukewarm or fall back, he adds: But many shall be first (Luc. XV), etc.

CAPUT XX.

VERS. 1.-- Simile est regnum. Ostendit per parabolam, quia primi erunt novissimi, et novissimi primi. Pater iste familias conditor noster est, qui habet vineam, universalem Ecclesiam, quae ab Abel usque ad ultimum electum qui in fine nasciturus est quotquot sanctos protulit, tot palmites misit. Hic pater exiit ad excolendam vineam mane hora tertia, sexta, nona, undecima operarios conduxit a mundi hujus initio usque ad finem: ad erudiendam fidelium 150Bplebem praedicatores mittere non desistit. Simile est regnum. Non homini solum, sed toti negotio ab homine gesto, et in similibus similiter. Primo mane. Manifeste coepit a primo justo aeterna mercede praedicatores ad plantandam Ecclesiam invitare.

VERS. 2.-- Conventione. Id est, confirmata promissione quod ad imaginem Dei reformandi essent, si Ecclesiam aedificando mandatis decalogi insisterent.

Ex denario. (HIER.) Denarius habet imaginem regis, etc., usque ad vel in ultimis resipiscite.

A novissimis usque, etc. Quia qui in adventu Domini vocati sunt ad regnum, sine aliqua tarditate perveniunt, aequales his qui ab initio mundi coeperunt, omnes quidem idem coelum, sed stella differt a stella in claritate. A novissimis incoepit, dum prius 150Clatronem quam Petrum in paradisum introduxit. Quia prius aliquando remunerantur sero venientes, quia prius de corpore exeunt, quam qui in pueritia vocati sunt.

VERS. 15.-- Aut non licet. Stulta quaestio contra bonitatem Dei. Quaerendum esset, non si daret quod non deberet: sed si non quod deberet. An oculus tuus, etc. Vere Judaei pravam intentionem habuerunt qui prohibuerunt apostolos gentibus loqui, ut salvae fierent.

VERS. 16.-- Sic erunt novissimi primi. (AUG.) Id est, Judaei de capite vertentur in caudam: et gentes de cauda in caput. Unde Moyses in Deuteronomii libro: Advena qui tecum moratur, ascendet super te: ille erit in caput, et tu eris in caudam. Sed tamen 150Dnec omnes primi nec omnes novissimi ad numerum electorum pertinent.

VERS. 18.-- Ecce ascendimus, etc. (HIER.) Hoc saepe eis dixerat, etc., usque ad resurrecturum non dubitent.

VERS. 19.-- Ad illudendum, etc. Ideo Dominus in mundo noluit prosperari sed gravia pati, ut ostenderet nos qui per delectationem cecidimus cum qua amaritudine redire debeamus.

VERS. 20.-- Tunc accessit ad eum. Audita resurrectione putavit eum illico (immemor secundi adventus) regnaturum, et aviditate feminea praesentia cupit, immemor futurorum.

VERS. 21.-- Dic ut, etc. Bonum desiderium, sed 151Ainconsiderata petitio. Et ideo non voluntas, non propositum culpatur, sed ignorantia: et si impetrare non debet simplicitas, non meretur confundi.

VERS. 22.-- Nescitis. Qui sedem gloriae petitis vel quaeritis, quam nondum meruistis, delectat culmen honoris quos nondum exercuit via laboris. Potestis. Et hoc sciebat, quod ejus passionem poterant imitari; sed hoc dicit, ut eo interrogante, et illis respondentibus, omnes audiamus: quia nemo potest cum Christo regnare, nisi passionem ejus sit imitatus.

VERS. 23.-- Calicem. Ut Jacobus ab Herode truncatus, et Joannes in ferventis olei dolium missus, et in Pathmos relegatus, animo non defuit martyrium. Non est meum dare. Hoc est regnum coelorum non est dantis sed accipientis. Non enim personarum acceptio 151Best apud Deum: sed quicunque dignus est accipit, quod non paratum personae est, sed vitae. Si itaque tales estis qui consequamini, quod victoribus pater paravit, accipietis. Vel, non est meum dare superbis, quod adhuc estis, sed aliis quibus paratum est. Ergo et vos nolite esse quod estis, sed estote alii, id est, humiliamini qui jam vultis exaltari, et est paratum vobis.

VERS. 24.-- Et audientes decem. Ut illi carnaliter petiverunt esse super omnes, sic et isti carnaliter contristati sunt esse aliquem ante se.

VERS. 30.-- Et ecce duo caeci. De istis unum commemorat Marcus, quia fuerat in illa civitate famosissimus: qui ex magna felicitate ejectus notissimae erat miseriae. Caecus nempe et mendicus, unde et nomen 151Cejus memorat, et nomen patris ejus Barthimaeus Thimaei filius, quod in aliis non facile occurrit, nisi in Jairo, qui et in illo loco nobilis fuit. Famositas autem illuminati, magnam famam miraculo comparavit. Veniens Jericho unum illuminat quod Lucas ait, quia morti propinquans lumen Evangelii praedicavit Judaeis, exiens duos, quia post resurrectionem Judaeis, et gentibus. De quibus Marcus unum ponit, qui gentibus scribit.

CHAPTER XX.

VERS. 1.-- The kingdom is like. He shows by a parable that the first shall be last, and the last first. This father of the household is our Creator, who has a vineyard, the universal Church, which from Abel to the last of the elect who is to be born at the end has sent forth as many branches as it has brought forth saints. This father went out to dress his vineyard in the morning, at the third hour, the sixth, the ninth, the eleventh he hired workmen, from the beginning of this world to its end: he does not cease to send preachers to school the people of the faithful. 150BThe kingdom is like. Not to the man only, but to the whole business carried on by the man, and so likewise in like cases. Early in the morning. Plainly he began from the first just man to invite preachers, with an everlasting wage, to plant the Church.

VERS. 2.-- By agreement. That is, by a confirmed promise that they should be reformed to the image of God, if by building up the Church they should press on in the commandments of the decalogue.

For a penny. (HIER.) The penny bears the image of the king, etc., as far as or in your last days come to your senses.

From the last even unto, etc. Because those who at the Lord's coming have been called to the kingdom arrive without any lateness, equal to those who began from the beginning of the world, all indeed have the same heaven, but star differs from star in brightness. He began from the last, when he brought the thief into paradise 150Cbefore Peter. Because those who come late are sometimes rewarded first, because they go forth out of the body before those who were called in boyhood.

VERS. 15.-- Or is it not lawful. A foolish question against the goodness of God. The thing to be asked would be, not whether he gave what he did not owe: but whether he did not give what he did owe. Is thy eye, etc. Truly the Jews had a crooked intent, who forbade the apostles to speak to the Gentiles, that they might be saved.

VERS. 16.-- So the last shall be first. (AUG.) That is, the Jews shall be turned from the head into the tail: and the Gentiles from the tail into the head. Whence Moses in the book of Deuteronomy: The stranger that sojourneth with thee shall go up above thee: he shall be for the head, and thou shalt be for the tail. But yet 150Dneither all the first nor all the last belong to the number of the elect.

VERS. 18.-- Behold we go up, etc. (HIER.) This he had often told them, etc., as far as let them not doubt that he would rise again.

VERS. 19.-- To be mocked, etc. Therefore the Lord would not prosper in the world but suffer heavy things, that he might show us who fell through delight with what bitterness we ought to return.

VERS. 20.-- Then came to him. Having heard of the resurrection she thought that he would reign on the spot (forgetful of the second coming), and with a woman's greed she desires things present, forgetful of things to come.

VERS. 21.-- Say that, etc. A good desire, but 151Aan unconsidered petition. And therefore not the will, not the purpose is blamed, but the ignorance: and if simplicity ought not to obtain what it asks, it does not deserve to be put to confusion.

VERS. 22.-- You know not. You who ask or seek the seat of glory which you have not yet deserved, the summit of honour delights those whom the way of labour has not yet exercised. You can. And this he knew, that they were able to imitate his passion; but he says this so that, with him questioning and them answering, we may all hear: that no one can reign with Christ unless he has imitated his passion.

VERS. 23.-- The chalice. As James was beheaded by Herod, and John was sent into a cauldron of boiling oil and banished to Patmos, martyrdom was not wanting to his mind. It is not mine to give. That is, the kingdom of heaven is not the giver's but the receiver's. For there is no respect of persons 151Bwith God: but whosoever is worthy receives that which is prepared not for the person, but for the life. If therefore you are such as may obtain it, you shall receive what the Father has prepared for the victors. Or, it is not mine to give to the proud, which you still are, but to others for whom it is prepared. Therefore be not you either what you are, but be others, that is, be humbled, you who already wish to be exalted, and it is prepared for you.

VERS. 24.-- And the ten hearing it. As those asked carnally to be above all, so these too were carnally grieved that anyone should be before them.

VERS. 30.-- And behold two blind men. Of these Mark commemorates one, because he had been most famous in that city: who, cast out from great prosperity, was of a most notorious misery. Blind indeed and a beggar, whence he records both his name 151Cand the name of his father, Bartimeus the son of Timeus, which does not easily occur in the others, save in Jairus, who was noble in that place too. But the notoriety of the man enlightened won great fame for the miracle. Coming to Jericho he enlightens one, which Luke tells, because drawing near to death he preached the light of the Gospel to the Jews, going out, two, because after the resurrection to the Jews and to the Gentiles. Of whom Mark sets down one, who writes to the Gentiles.

CAPUT XXI.

VERS. 1.-- Et cum appropinquassent. Hic praetermittit Matthaeus ea quae facta sunt antequam intraret Jerosolymam, quae alii prolixius tractant. Et cum appropinquassent. Dominus spiritualiter Jerusalem 151Dappropinquavit quando incarnatus, Ecclesiam in qua est visio pacis visitavit, quam per confessionem sibi reconciliavit. Bethphage enim domus buccae dicitur. Bethphage. Bethphage erat viculus sacerdotum in monte Oliveti, in cujus montis latere est civitas Bethania. Bethphage, domus buccae. Bethania, domus obedientiae. Has Dominus pergens, Jerusalem sua praesentia sublimat, quia multos ante passionem docendo donis piae confessionis et obedientiae implevit. Sitae autem sunt illae civitates in monte Olivarum, id est, in Christo Domino, a quo unctio et lux omnibus venit; unde: Non potest civitas abscondi super montem posita.--Ad montem Oliveti: tunc Jesus, etc. Ad sublimitatem misericordiae respiciens, 152Aἔλεος misericordia interpretatur. Olei natura lucis ministra est, et laborum et dolorum solamen, et excellit caeteris liquoribus. Misit. Quia lucernam quam accepit, non vult sub modio abscondi. Duos discipulos. Duo propter scientiam veritatis et munditiam operis, et sacramentum geminae dilectionis praedicaturi. Quidam dicunt hos duos Petrum et Philippum, qui primum gentiles ad Christum adduxerunt, Philippus Samariam quam asinam interpretantur, Petrus Cornelium quasi Samariae pullum.

VERS. 2.-- Et pullum. Pullus est populus gentium cui nullus doctorum frenum correctionis imposuit, liber et lascivus, qui et ipse vinculis peccatorum irretitus erat. Omnes enim peccaverunt et egent gratia Dei (Rom. III). Alii de solo pullo: Matthaeus 152Bqui Hebraeis scribit, et de asina dicit docens non desperari salutem Hebraeis si poeniteant. Marcus dicit pullum ante januam in bivio inventum. Janua Christus, extra quam populus gentium stabat ligatus: non in una via fidei, sed in diversis erroribus.

VERS. 5.-- Dicite filiae Sion. Filia Sion, est Jerusalem sita in monte Sion. Mystice. Ecclesia pertinens ad supernam Jerusalem quae est mater omnium nostrum, cujus non minima pars tunc erat in populo Israel. Rex. Ne dicatis: Non habemus regem, nisi Caesarem (Joan. XIX). Tibi. Si credis; contra te si non credis. Super asinam, etc. Non ad litteram super utrumque sedit, sed in praecordiis utriusque populi. Potuit Dominus super utrumque ascendere, descendens de uno, et ascendens super 152Calium.

VERS. 7.-- Et adduxerunt. Sic et apostoli mundum virtute Christi a potestate diaboli solverunt, et imposuerunt vestimenta super eum. Sed caetera turba, id est, Judaei vestimenta, id est, legis mandata sternunt, quia a Christianis erant conculcanda, in via, id est, in Christo. Super eos. Vestes apostolorum sunt praecepta divina, et gratia spiritualis, quibus turpitudo carnis nostrae tegitur. His prius nudi populi, sed modo per apostolos ornati Christum habent sessorem. Minor turba non ponit vestes super asinam, sed in via nec ubi offendat: quia sancti martyres carnem exuentes, simplicioribus viam per exemplum suo sanguine parant.

VERS. 8.-- Ramos de arboribus, etc. Ramos vel 152Dfrondes caedunt, quia verba et sententias de libris Patrum excerperunt vel exempla.

VERS. 9.-- Osanna. (HIER.) Osi, id est, salva, salvifica. Anna interjectio deprecantis, quasi salva obsecro. Osanna totum Hebraicum est, et significat quod adventus Christi salus mundi sit: unde sequitur: Benedictus. Osanna, id est salva. In excelsis. Quia adventus Christi non tantum hominum sed totius mundi salus est, terrena conjungens coelestibus, ut in nomine Jesu omne genu flectatur coelestium, terrestrium et infernorum (Phil. II). In excelsis. Quia et angelorum salus quorum numerum implet.

VERS. 10.-- Cum intrasset. Frequenter ingressus est civitatem Jerusalem, sed non cum his laudibus: 153Anon rex vocatur, quod semper fugerat, nisi modo cum ascendit passurus. Quod ideo factum est, ut amplius illorum adversum se excitaret invidiam, quia jam tempus passionis instabat.

VERS. 12.-- Et ejiciebat. (AUG.) Ejecerat et ante alia vice, quod Joannes solus dicit: sed de hac ultima ejectione dicunt et alii. Mensas numulariorum. Quia de omnibus partibus ad templum veniebant, vendebant sacerdotes non habentibus omnia animalia, etiam columbas quas pauperes offerrent, ut vendita postea reciperent oblationes. Sed quia aliqui nihil habebant unde emerent, posuerunt numularios, qui mutuo darent: et quia lex vetat usuram, pro ea varias species, id est, poma, uvas et hujuscemodi accipiebant; hos ejicit. Sic et mystice quotidie de 153BEcclesia ejicit. Et habet unius oriminis reos vendentes et ementes ecclesiastica officia. Et cathedras vendentium. Quae magistrorum sunt, id est, sacerdotium eorum destruit qui de impositione manus, per quam Spiritus sanctus datur, pretium accipiunt. Hinc est, quod haeresis Simonis damnatur. Ad litteram autem absurdum est dicere institores columbarum in cathedris sedere.

VERS. 13.-- Latronum. Latro est qui lucra sectatur, qui et non dantes corporaliter persequitur, et dantes necat spiritualiter, et dum laesionem proximi cogitat, quasi latro sedet in spelunca.

VERS. 14.-- Et accesserunt. Mystice. Simplices peccatores ad se per poenitentiam accedentes, prius caecos 153Cin cognitione et claudos in operatione illuminando, et in vitam rectitudinis dirigendo quotidie sanat.

VERS. 15.-- Mirabilia quae, etc. Ut flagello unius et vilis tot ejici, mensas subverti, quo nihil mirabilius egit. Igneum et sidereum aliquid radiat ex ejus oculis, lucet in facie majestas deitatis.

VERS. 16.-- Dixit eis. Non patet calumniae, non, ait, bene faciunt vel male, vel aetati parcite, sed temperat, et se tacente verba puerorum testimonio Scripturae confirmat. Infantium et lac. Testes miraculorum. Christi infantes dicuntur, non aetate, sed simplicitate. Lactentes quasi lacte, id est, miraculorum suavitate delectati, quae dicuntur lac, panis vero doctrina perfecta.

VERS. 17.-- Abiit foras. Quia pauper, nullique 153Dadulatus, nullum in tanta urbe invenit hospitem, sed apud Lazarum receptus est.

VERS. 19.-- Fici arborem. Sicut multa dixit in parabolis, ita et fecit. Cur enim in ficu fructum quaereret, cujus tempus nondum esse quilibet sciret, et ob id aeternae sterilitati damnaret, nisi quia plebem quam docebat verbo terruit hic facto; ne quis habens folia, id est verba justitiae sine fructu operum, mereatur excidi. Folia. Traditiones Pharisaeorum sine fructu veritatis, unde alius evangelista: nondum erat tempus salvationis Israel, eo quod populus gentium nondum subintrasset, vel quia praeteriit tempus fidei, cum spretus ad gentes transiit. Nunquam ex te fructus. Non facit injuriam creator 154Apossidenti, si creatura sua ad arbitrium suum utitur, et ad utilitatem aliorum.

VERS. 20.-- Et videntes. Passurus Dominus et scandalum crucis portaturus, discipulorum animos debuit signi anticipatione signare, unde et mirabantur, quod ficus aruit: potuit eadem virtute inimicos siccare nisi per patientiam eorum exspectasset salutem.

VERS. 21.-- Fidem. Perfectam fidem habet, qui per dilectionem operatur: hic potest montes transferre. Alii de peccato veniam obtinent si ipsi aliis dimittunt.

VERS. 22.-- Accipietis. Et si differatur ad tempus opportunum, et si non his quibus petitur tamen qui digne petunt.

154BVERS. 24.-- Interrogabo, etc. Poterat aperte confutare eos: sed ideo interrogat, ut suo vel silentio vel sententia damnentur, et in qua potestate hoc facit cognoscant per se, secundum illud: Deus conteret dentes eorum in ore ipsorum.

VERS. 27.-- Nec ego dico vobis. Duabus de causis occultatur veritas, vel quia non sunt capaces auditores, unde: Habeo multa vobis dicere, sed non potestis portare modo; vel quia sunt contemptores, unde Nolite sanctum dare canibus.

VERS. 28.-- Homo quidam habebat duos filios, et accedens, etc. Facta sacerdotibus cauta responsione, statim infert parabolam, quae et illos impietatis arguat, et ad gentes regnum Dei transferendum doceat.

154CVERS. 31.-- Dicunt ei. In quibusdam codicibus habetur, pro primus, novissimus. Intelligunt enim veritatem, sed tergiversando nolunt dicere quod sentiunt, ut de baptismo Joannis.

VERS. 32.-- Joannes in via justitiae. Joannem Judaei contemnentes, nec viam justitiae comprehenderunt, nec de operibus malis poenitentiam egerunt. Vos autem. (GREG.) Quia aperte nolebant respondere sapientiae, in parabolis capiuntur.

VERS. 33.-- Vineam. De qua Isaias (cap. IV). Vinea Domini saba, etc. Fodit in ea torcular. In fossione timor notatur, quia praecepta illa legalia non amore sed timore servabant.

VERS. 34.-- Tempus fructuum appropinquasset, 154Detc. Bene tempus fructuum, non fructus: quia nullus fuit in illo contumaci populo.

VERS. 37.-- Novissime, etc. Vide patrisfamilias patientiam qui exspectat ad poenitentiam. Verebuntur. Quasi faciam quod meum est, ipsi videant quod suum est.

VERS. 38.-- Hic est haeres. Patet principes sacerdotum non per ignorantiam, sed per invidiam occidisse Jesum, cum scirent eum esse, cui dicitur: Postula a me, et dabo tibi gentes haereditatem tuam (Psal. II); unde et consulentes sibi aiebant: Si dimittimus eum sic (Joan. II), etc. Habebimus haereditatem. Haereditas Christi quam resurgens possedit, Ecclesia est, quam moliebantur praeripere Judaei: 155Adum fide ejus exstincta justitiam quae ex lege est, gentibus persuadere conabantur.

VERS. 39.-- Ejecerunt extra vineam. Marcus mutat hunc ordinem, dicens: Occiderunt et ejecerunt extra vineam (Luc. XX), quasi vile cadaver. In quo notat pertinaciam, quia occiso Domino et resuscitato, a suis finibus eum excluserunt, et gentibus miserunt.

VERS. 42.-- Aedificantes. Qui de subdita plebe quasi Deo domum struere videbantur.

Et qui ceciderit, etc. Qui credit, et tamen peccat: cadit super illum lapis et non conteritur: sed constringitur, quia per patientiam ad salutem reservatur, sed cui lapis irruit, id est, qui negat, penitus conteritur, ut nec testa remaneat; vel cadit super 155Beum qui modo contemnit et injuriis afficit, necdum penitus interiit, sed quassatur ut non ambulet rectus. Cadit super illum lapis, quando veniens in judicio opprimit, cum dicit: Ite in ignem aeternum.

CHAPTER XXI.

VERS. 1.-- And when they drew nigh. Here Matthew passes over the things that were done before he entered Jerusalem, which the others treat at greater length. And when they drew nigh. The Lord drew nigh to Jerusalem 151Dspiritually when he was made flesh, he visited the Church, in which is the vision of peace, and reconciled her to himself by confession. For Bethphage is called the house of the mouth. Bethphage. Bethphage was a hamlet of priests on mount Olivet, on the side of which mountain is the town of Bethany. Bethphage, the house of the mouth. Bethany, the house of obedience. These the Lord, journeying to Jerusalem, exalts by his presence, because before his passion he filled many by his teaching with the gifts of devout confession and of obedience. And those towns are set on the mount of Olives, that is, in Christ the Lord, from whom anointing and light come to all; whence: A city cannot be hid, set upon a mountain.--To mount Olivet: then Jesus, etc. Looking to the loftiness of mercy, 152Aἔλεος is interpreted mercy. The nature of oil is to be the servant of light, and the solace of labours and of pains, and it excels the other liquids. He sent. Because he does not wish the lamp which he received to be hidden under a bushel. Two disciples. Two, as being about to preach the knowledge of truth and cleanness of work, and the sacrament of the twofold love. Some say that these two are Peter and Philip, who first brought Gentiles to Christ, Philip, Samaria, which they interpret as the she-ass, Peter, Cornelius, as it were the colt of Samaria.

VERS. 2.-- And the colt. The colt is the people of the Gentiles, on which none of the teachers had put the bridle of correction, free and wanton, which itself also was ensnared in the bonds of sins. For all have sinned and need the grace of God (Rom. III) [Vulg. Rom 3:23 egent gloria Dei, "need the glory of God"]. Others, of the colt only: Matthew, 152Bwho writes to the Hebrews, speaks also of the she-ass, teaching that salvation is not to be despaired of for the Hebrews if they repent. Mark says that the colt was found before the gate where two ways met. The gate is Christ, outside which the people of the Gentiles stood bound: not in the one way of faith, but in divers errors.

VERS. 5.-- Tell ye the daughter of Sion. The daughter of Sion is Jerusalem, set on mount Sion. Mystically. The Church belonging to the Jerusalem above, which is the mother of us all, of which no small part was then in the people of Israel. King. Lest you say: We have no king but Caesar (Joan. XIX). To thee. If thou believest; against thee if thou believest not. Upon a she-ass, etc. He did not sit upon both according to the letter, but in the hearts of both peoples. The Lord could go up upon both, coming down from the one, and going up upon 152Cthe other.

VERS. 7.-- And they brought. So the apostles also loosed the world by the power of Christ from the power of the devil, and laid their garments upon it. But the rest of the crowd, that is, the Jews, strew their garments, that is, the commandments of the law, because they were to be trodden underfoot by Christians, in the way, that is, in Christ. Upon them. The garments of the apostles are the divine precepts and spiritual grace, by which the foulness of our flesh is covered. Naked of these before, but now adorned by the apostles, the peoples have Christ for their rider. The lesser crowd does not put its garments upon the she-ass, but in the way, and not where he might stumble: because the holy martyrs, putting off the flesh, prepare a way for the simpler sort by the example of their own blood.

VERS. 8.-- Branches from the trees, etc. They cut branches or 152Dleafy boughs, because they culled words and sentences, or examples, from the books of the Fathers.

VERS. 9.-- Osanna. (HIER.) Osi, that is, save, make safe. Anna is the interjection of one entreating, as though, save, I beseech. Osanna is wholly Hebrew, and it signifies that the coming of Christ is the salvation of the world: whence there follows: Blessed. Osanna, that is, save. In the highest. Because the coming of Christ is the salvation not of men only but of the whole world, joining earthly things to heavenly, that in the name of Jesus every knee should bow, of things in heaven, of things on earth, and of things under the earth (Phil. II). In the highest. Because it is the salvation of the angels also, whose number he fills up.

VERS. 10.-- When he had entered. He entered the city of Jerusalem often, but not with these praises: 153Ahe is not called king, which he had always fled, save only now when he goes up to suffer. And this was done for this reason, that he might the more stir up their envy against himself, because the time of the passion was now at hand.

VERS. 12.-- And he cast out. (AUG.) He had cast them out at another time before, which John alone tells: but of this last casting out the others also speak. The tables of the money-changers. Because they came to the temple from all quarters, the priests sold all the animals to those who had them not, even the doves which the poor were to offer, so that afterwards, when these had been sold, they might get back the offerings. But because some had nothing wherewith to buy, they set up money-changers who might make loans: and because the law forbids usury, in its place they took various kinds of goods, that is, apples, grapes and the like; these he casts out. So too mystically he casts them daily out of 153Bthe Church. And he holds guilty of one and the same crime both those who sell and those who buy ecclesiastical offices. And the chairs of them that sold. Which belong to the masters, that is, he destroys the priesthood of those who take a price for the laying on of the hand, by which the Holy Spirit is given. Hence it is that the heresy of Simon is condemned. But according to the letter it is absurd to say that dealers in doves sat in chairs.

VERS. 13.-- Of thieves. He is a thief who follows after gain, who both persecutes bodily those who do not give, and slays spiritually those who do give, and while he plots the hurt of his neighbour, sits like a thief in a den.

VERS. 14.-- And there came. Mystically. He daily heals simple sinners coming to him by penance, enlightening them first when they are blind 153Cin knowledge and lame in work, and directing them into the life of uprightness.

VERS. 15.-- The wonderful things which, etc. That by the scourge of one man, and he of low account, so many should be cast out and the tables overturned, than which he did nothing more wonderful. Something fiery and starlike shines out from his eyes, the majesty of the godhead gleams in his face.

VERS. 16.-- He said to them. He does not lie open to a false charge, he does not say, they do well or ill, or, spare their age, but he holds the mean, and, himself keeping silence, confirms the words of the children by the testimony of Scripture. Of infants and sucklings. Witnesses of the miracles. They are called Christ's infants, not by age, but by simplicity. Sucklings, as delighted, so to speak, by milk, that is, by the sweetness of the miracles, which are called milk, whereas bread is perfect doctrine.

VERS. 17.-- He went forth abroad. Because he was poor and flattered 153Dno man, he found no host in so great a city, but was received in the house of Lazarus.

VERS. 19.-- The fig tree. As he said many things in parables, so he also did them. For why should he seek fruit on a fig tree whose season anyone would know was not yet come, and for that condemn it to everlasting barrenness, unless it were that he here terrified by a deed the people whom he was teaching by word; lest anyone having leaves, that is, words of justice without the fruit of works, should deserve to be cut down. Leaves. The traditions of the Pharisees without the fruit of truth, whence another evangelist: the time of the salvation of Israel was not yet, because the people of the Gentiles had not yet come in, or because the time of faith has passed by, when, being despised, he went over to the Gentiles. Never fruit from thee. The Creator does no wrong 154Ato the possessor if he uses his own creature at his own will, and for the profit of others.

VERS. 20.-- And seeing it. Since the Lord was to suffer and to bear the scandal of the cross, he had to mark the minds of the disciples by the anticipation of a sign, whence they wondered too that the fig tree had withered: he could by the same power have dried up his enemies, had he not by his patience awaited their salvation.

VERS. 21.-- Faith. He has perfect faith who works by love: he can remove mountains. Others obtain pardon of sin if they themselves forgive others.

VERS. 22.-- You shall receive. Even if it be deferred to a fitting time, and even if not to those for whom it is asked, yet to those who ask worthily.

154BVERS. 24.-- I will ask, etc. He could have refuted them openly: but he asks for this reason, that they may be condemned either by their own silence or by their own sentence, and may know of themselves by what power he does this, according to that saying: God shall break their teeth in their mouth.

VERS. 27.-- Neither do I tell you. For two causes the truth is hidden, either because the hearers are not capable, whence: I have many things to say to you, but you cannot bear them now; or because they are despisers, whence Give not that which is holy to dogs.

VERS. 28.-- A certain man had two sons, and coming, etc. When he had made a wary answer to the priests, he at once brings in a parable, both to convict them of impiety and to teach that the kingdom of God is to be carried over to the Gentiles.

154CVERS. 31.-- They say to him. In certain codices there stands, instead of the first, the last. For they understand the truth, but by shifting about they are unwilling to say what they think, as in the case of John's baptism.

VERS. 32.-- John in the way of justice. The Jews, despising John, neither grasped the way of justice, nor did penance for their evil works. But you. (GREG.) Because they were unwilling to answer wisdom openly, they are caught in parables.

VERS. 33.-- A vineyard. Of which Isaias (cap. IV). The vineyard of the Lord of Sabaoth, etc. He dug in it a winepress. By the digging fear is marked, because they kept those commandments of the law not out of love but out of fear.

VERS. 34.-- When the time of the fruits drew nigh, 154Detc. Rightly the time of the fruits, not of the fruit: because there was none in that stubborn people.

VERS. 37.-- Last of all, etc. See the patience of the father of the household, who waits for repentance. They will reverence. As though, I shall do what is mine, let them see to what is theirs.

VERS. 38.-- This is the heir. It is plain that the chief priests killed Jesus not through ignorance but through envy, since they knew him to be the one to whom it is said: Ask of me, and I will give thee the Gentiles for thine inheritance (Psal. II); whence also, taking counsel for themselves, they said: If we let him alone so (Joan. II), etc. We shall have his inheritance. The inheritance of Christ, which he possessed in rising again, is the Church, which the Jews were striving to snatch away: 155Awhile, his faith being put out, they endeavoured to persuade the Gentiles of the justice that is of the law.

VERS. 39.-- They cast him forth out of the vineyard. Mark changes this order, saying: They killed him and cast him forth out of the vineyard (Luc. XX), as a worthless corpse. In which he marks their obstinacy, because when the Lord had been killed and raised again, they shut him out from their own borders and sent him to the Gentiles.

VERS. 42.-- The builders. Who seemed to be building a house for God, as it were, out of the people subject to them.
