And he that shall fall, etc. He who believes and yet sins: the stone falls upon him and he is not ground to powder, but he is bound tight, because through patience he is kept for salvation; but he upon whom the stone rushes down, that is, he who denies, is utterly ground to powder, so that not even a shard remains; or it falls upon 155Bhim who now despises and does injuries, and he has not yet utterly perished, but is shaken so that he does not walk upright. The stone falls upon him when, coming in judgment, it crushes him, when he says: Go into everlasting fire. [Vulg. Mt 25:41 Discedite a me maledicti in ignem aeternum, "Depart from me, ye cursed, into everlasting fire"]

CAPUT XXII.

VERS. 2.-- Simile factum. Iterum arguit per parabolas: non tacet quia exasperati sunt, sed pejorem iram Dei per aliam parabolam dicit eis esse venturam. Nuptias. Nuptiarum thalamus est virginis uterus. De invitatis ad nuptias solus Matthaeus narrat. Aliud simile ex parte Lucas dicit de coena, id est, aeterno convivio, de quo nemo ingressus repellitur, sicut de praesenti Ecclesia.

155CVERS. 4.-- Tauri. Quia prius prophetae, et post apostoli ab infidelibus passi sunt: qui vobis modo sunt in exemplum quid sit vobis credendum. Venite, fide, caeteris concordando. Tauri sunt patres Veteris Testamenti, quibus indulta est licentia inimicos quasi cornu feriendi. Altilia ab alendo, id est pinguia interna charitate praecones Novi Testamenti, contemplationis penna superna petentes. Occisa, vel ab eo quod fuerant, vel per mortem carnis jam in requie positi.

VERS. 5.-- Alius in villam. In villam ire est terreno labori immoderate incumbere. Negotiari vero, terrenis lucris inhiare.

VERS. 7.-- Rex autem. Supra homo dum invitabat et opera clementiae agebat, nunc in ultione rex tan 155Dtum. Et missis, etc. Missis exercitibus, id est, angelis per quos judicium exercebit, persequentes perdit: et civitatem, id est, carnem in qua habitaverunt cum anima in gehenna cremabit.

VERS. 8.-- Nuptiae quidem paratae. Omne sacramentum incarnationis peractum est, nativitas et passio.

Ad exitus, etc. Actiones sunt viae: exitus, defectus actionum: quia plerumque facile convertuntur quos in terrenis actibus prospera nulla comitantur.

VERS. 11.-- Ut videret discumbentes. Id est, videri faciat judicandorum merita, et discernat. Non restitum veste nuptiali. Confutatis autem Pharisaeis 156Asupponit non omnes qui sunt in Ecclesia, esse dignos regno, ne torpeant sui.

VERS. 12.-- Amice quomodo huc intrasti non habens vestem nuptialem? Amicus, quia ad nuptias invitatus. Quomodo, impudentiae arguit.

VERS. 13.-- Ligatis manibus et pedibus. Ligantur poena qui hic a malo noluerunt ligari, id est, cohiberi. Manus quae non extensae ad eleemosynam. Pedes tardi ad bonum. Ibi singula membra poenis subjicientur, quae hic singulis vitiis servierunt. Exteriores, etc. Quia hic interiores habent tenebras cordis.

VERS. 14.-- Multi autem sunt vocati, pauci vero electi. Repulso uno, in quo omne corpus malorum exprimitur, generalis sententia infertur. Multi autem 156Bsunt vocati. Quasi dicat: Et si de uno dixi, de multis est intelligendum.

VERS. 16.-- Cum Herodianis dicentes. Tanquam ignotis, ut facilius deciperent, quia timebant turbas. Quod per se non audent, praesidis manibus attentant. Cum Herodianis. Id est, militibus Herodis. Sub Caesare Augusto quando descriptio facta est, Judaea facta est tributaria. His tributis praepositus est Herodes Antipatris filius alienigena et proselytus, rex Judaeis ab Augusto constitutus. Erat ergo seditio in populo, aliis dicentibus debere dari tributa, pro securitate et quiete, cum Romani pro omnibus militent. Pharisaeis contra nitentibus, non debere populum Dei qui decimas solvit, et caetera quae lex dicit, humanis legibus subjacere. Mittunt igitur Pharisaei 156Csi possent decipere.

Non est tibi cura, etc. Illuc provocant, ut Deum magis timeat quam Caesarem: et ideo non debere solvi tributa dicat, ut Herodiani quasi auctorem seditionis teneant.

VERS. 18.-- Hypocritae. Quia aliud sunt, et aliud simulant.

VERS. 21.-- Reddite ergo. Vel sicut Caesar exigit impressionem suae imaginis, sic et Deus animam lumine sui vultus signatam.

VERS. 23.-- Sadducaei, etc. Duae erant haereses in Judaeis. Pharisaeorum qui praeferebant justitiam traditionum et observationum: et Sadducaeorum, qui et ipsi vindicabant sibi, quod nondum erant, id est 156Djusti. Sadducaei namque justi interpretantur, qui resurrectionem corporum non credunt, et animas interire putant cum corpore, turpitudinem fabulae fingunt, quae deliramenti eos arguat qui resurrectionem asserunt. Potest autem fieri ut in gente eorum hoc aliquando acciderit.

VERS. 29.-- Erratis. Ideo quia nescitis Scripturas, Unde sequitur, ut nesciant virtutem Dei.

VERS. 30.-- In resurrectione. Resurgent quidem corpora, quae possent nubere et nubi: quod de his dicitur, qui habent membra genitalia: sed alia ratione non nubent.

VERS. 31.-- Non legistis. In Moyse quem recipitis, alioquin posset apertiora de resurrectione testimonia 157Aprophetarum dare: sed prophetas Sadducaei non recipiunt.

VERS. 32.-- Deus Abraham, etc. Qui jam mortui erant, quando haec dicebat, ergo per hoc animas probat permanere post mortem: non enim diceretur Deus non exsistentium. Et sic introducitur et corporum resurrectio, quae cum animabus bona vel mala gesserunt: Deus Abraham, Deus Isaac et Deus Jacob. Per hoc probat aeternitatem animarum, et statim infert: Non est Deus mortuorum, etc., ut cum probaverit animas permanere post mortem, consequenter introduceretur etiam corporum resurrectio, quae cum animabus bona vel mala gesserunt.

VERS. 34.-- Silentium. Nota venena invidiae superari posse, sed difficile conquiescere. Convenerunt. 157BUt multitudine vincerent quem non poterant ratione. In tentando conveniunt qui in doctrina contrarii sunt. Jam confutati erant in ostensione denarii, jam viderant etiam Sadducaeos obmutescere, sed tamen malevolentia et livor nutrit impudentiam.

VERS. 35.-- Tentans eum. Marcus ait, quod ei sapienter respondenti Dominus dixerit: Non longe es a regno Dei. Potuit enim fieri, ut qui tentans venit, responso Dominico conversus sit. Vel tentatio non est mala quasi volentis decipere, sed causa volentis amplius experiri ignotum.

VERS. 37.-- Diliges Dominum Deum tuum. Fictam conscientiam interrogantis Dominus primo responso percutit. Deus tuus unus est. Non assumes nomen Dei tui in vanum. Observa diem sabbati. Haec tria ad dilectionem 157CDei pertinent: Cujus cogites unitatem, veritatem, voluntatem. Ex toto corde. Integritas fidei exprimitur. In tota anima, perfecta obedientia, exigitur. In tota mente, perseverantia ostenditur.

VERS. 42.-- De Christo. Sive venit, sive futurum creditis, quem purum hominem asseritis.

VERS. 44.-- Dixit. Istud dicere est aequalem sibi filium generare. Domino. Non secundum quod de eo natus, sed quod de patre semper fuit.

VERS. 45.-- Quomodo. Repugnantia sunt haec secundum vestrum intellectum.

VERS. 46.-- Et nemo poterat, etc. Nota venena invidiae superari posse, sed difficile conquiescere.

CHAPTER XXII.

VERS. 2.-- It was made like. Again he convicts them by parables: he is not silent because they are exasperated, but by another parable he tells them that a worse anger of God is to come. A marriage feast. The bridal chamber of the marriage is the virgin's womb. Of those invited to the marriage Matthew alone tells. Luke tells another likeness in part, of a supper, that is, of the eternal banquet, from which no one who has entered is driven out, as he is from the present Church.

155CVERS. 4.-- The oxen. Because first the prophets, and afterwards the apostles, suffered from unbelievers: who are now to you for an example of what you must believe. Come, by faith, by agreeing with the rest. The oxen are the fathers of the Old Testament, to whom leave was granted to strike their enemies as with a horn. The fatlings, from feeding, that is, the heralds of the New Testament, fat with inward charity, seeking things on high on the wing of contemplation. Slain, either from what they had been, or already set in rest through the death of the flesh.

VERS. 5.-- Another to his farm. To go to a farm is to lie immoderately upon earthly labour. But to trade is to gape after earthly gains.

VERS. 7.-- And the king. Above, he was a man while he was inviting and doing works of clemency, now in taking vengeance he is king only 155D. And having sent, etc. Having sent his armies, that is, the angels by whom he will exercise judgment, he destroys the persecutors: and the city, that is, the flesh in which they dwelt, he will burn with the soul in hell.

VERS. 8.-- The marriage indeed is ready. The whole sacrament of the incarnation is accomplished, the nativity and the passion.

To the outgoings, etc. Actions are the ways: the outgoings are the failing of actions: because for the most part those are easily converted whom no prosperity attends in earthly doings.

VERS. 11.-- That he might see the guests. [Vulg. Mt 22:11 ut viderent discumbentes, plural, "that they might see"] That is, that he may make the merits of those to be judged be seen, and may discern them. Not clothed with a wedding garment. Now, the Pharisees having been refuted, he adds 156Athat not all who are in the Church are worthy of the kingdom, lest his own grow slothful.

VERS. 12.-- Friend, how camest thou in hither not having a wedding garment? A friend, because invited to the marriage. How, he convicts him of shamelessness.

VERS. 13.-- His hands and feet being bound. They are bound by punishment who here would not be bound from evil, that is, restrained. The hands which were not stretched out to almsgiving. The feet slow to good. There each several member shall be subjected to punishments, which here served each several vice. Outer, etc. Because here they have the inward darkness of the heart.

VERS. 14.-- But many are called, but few chosen. [Vulg. Mt 22:14 Multi enim sunt vocati, "for many are called"] One being cast out, in whom the whole body of the wicked is set forth, a general sentence is brought in. But many 156Bare called. As though he should say: Even if I have spoken of one, it is to be understood of many.

VERS. 16.-- With the Herodians, saying. As though to men unknown, that they might the more easily deceive, because they feared the crowds. What they dare not by themselves, they attempt by the governor's hands. With the Herodians. That is, with the soldiers of Herod. Under Caesar Augustus, when the enrolment was made, Judea was made tributary. Over these tributes was set Herod the son of Antipater, an alien and a proselyte, appointed king for the Jews by Augustus. There was therefore sedition among the people, some saying that tributes ought to be given, for security and quiet, since the Romans bear arms for all. The Pharisees striving against it, that the people of God, which pays tithes and the rest that the law prescribes, ought not to lie under human laws. The Pharisees therefore send 156Cto see whether they might deceive him.

Thou hast no care, etc. To this they provoke him, that he should fear God more than Caesar: and therefore should say that tributes ought not to be paid, so that the Herodians might hold him as the author of sedition.

VERS. 18.-- Hypocrites. Because they are one thing and feign another.

VERS. 21.-- Render therefore. Or, as Caesar exacts the stamping of his own image, so also does God the soul signed with the light of his countenance.

VERS. 23.-- Sadducees, etc. There were two heresies among the Jews. That of the Pharisees, who set forward the justice of traditions and observances: and that of the Sadducees, who also claimed for themselves what they were not yet, that is, 156Djust men. For Sadducees is interpreted just men, they do not believe the resurrection of bodies, and think that souls perish with the body, they invent the foulness of a fable to convict of raving those who assert the resurrection. But it may be that this once happened among their nation.

VERS. 29.-- You err. For this reason, because you know not the Scriptures, Whence there follows, that they know not the power of God.

VERS. 30.-- In the resurrection. Bodies shall indeed rise which could marry and be given in marriage: which is said of those who have the members of generation: but for another reason they shall not marry.

VERS. 31.-- Have you not read. In Moses, whom you receive, otherwise he could have given plainer testimonies of the resurrection 157Afrom the prophets: but the Sadducees do not receive the prophets.

VERS. 32.-- The God of Abraham, etc. Who were already dead when he said these things, therefore by this he proves that souls remain after death: for he would not be called the God of those that do not exist. And thus the resurrection of bodies also is brought in, which together with the souls did good or evil: The God of Abraham, the God of Isaac and the God of Jacob. By this he proves the eternity of souls, and at once brings in: He is not the God of the dead, etc., so that when he had proved that souls remain after death, the resurrection of bodies also, which together with the souls did good or evil, might consequently be brought in.

VERS. 34.-- Silence. Note that the poisons of envy can be overcome, but with difficulty are laid to rest. They came together. 157BThat by their multitude they might overcome him whom they could not by reason. In tempting him those come together who in doctrine are contrary to one another. They had already been refuted in the showing of the penny, they had already seen the Sadducees too struck dumb, but yet ill-will and spite nourish shamelessness.

VERS. 35.-- Tempting him. Mark says that the Lord said to him, when he had answered wisely: Thou art not far from the kingdom of God. For it could come to pass that he who came tempting was converted by the Lord's answer. Or the temptation is not an evil one, as of a man wishing to deceive, but by reason of a man wishing to try out further what he does not know.

VERS. 37.-- Thou shalt love the Lord thy God. With his first answer the Lord strikes the feigned conscience of the questioner. Thy God is one. Thou shalt not take the name of thy God in vain. Observe the day of the sabbath. These three belong to the love 157Cof God: That thou shouldst think upon his unity, his truth, his will. With thy whole heart. The integrity of faith is expressed. In the whole soul, perfect obedience is required. In the whole mind, perseverance is shown.

VERS. 42.-- Of Christ. Whether he has come, or whether you believe he is to come, whom you assert to be mere man.

VERS. 44.-- He said. To say that is to beget a Son equal to himself. To my Lord. Not according to that whereby he was born of him, but according to that whereby he always was of the Father.

VERS. 45.-- How. These things are contradictory according to your understanding.

VERS. 46.-- And no man could, etc. Note that the poisons of envy can be overcome, but with difficulty are laid to rest.

CAPUT XXIII.

157DVERS. 1.-- Tunc locutus est. Illis confutatis qui dolosi sunt, instruit eos qui idonei sunt, nihilominus hortans eos subjici illis propter sacerdotium et nominis dignitatem: non opera, sed doctrinam considerantes.

VERS. 4.-- Onera gravia. Legis mandata permistis suis traditionibus, secundum ea suadentes vivere: et non venire ad facilem et delectabilem gratiam Christi; unde supra: Venite ad me qui laboratis et onerati estis.

VERS. 5.-- Omnia vero. Dixit tales doctores audiendos esse, non imitandos: nunc ostendit causam propter quam non possunt credere Christo, id est quia omnia faciunt ut videantur ab hominibus. Non 158Apotest enim Christo coelestia praedicanti credere, qui gloriam terrenam cupit.

VERS. 8.-- Vos autem. Duplici ratione ab his attendere jubet, ne eorum simulatione seducamur, vel ne aemulatione inflammemur.

VERS. 9.-- Unus est. Qui illuminat hominem, quod non alius homo, sed tantum exercet, docendo non intellectum praestat. Pater vester. Deus dicitur Pater et Magister natura, homo indulgentia. Pater, ut honor aetati deferatur, non ut auctor vitae habeatur. Magister, ex consortio veri magistri tanquam ejus nuntius: et pro reverentia ejus a quo mittitur, honoratur.

VERS. 13.-- Clauditis. Vel malo exemplo, vel mysteria Scripturarum tollendo. Exemplo enim et 158Bdoctrina eorum qui magistri sunt, alii quidem Christum non recipiunt.

VERS. 15.-- Circumitis. In toto orbe Evangelio detrahitis: et si quis vobis adhaeret, duplae poenae filius est: quia neque remissionem peccatorum accipit, et in eorum societatem incidit qui Christum sunt persecuti. Quisquis ejus Filius dicitur cujus opera agit. proselytum. Proselyti sunt de gentibus in Synagogam recepti, quorum raritas per unum significatur, quia vix aliquis post Christum.

VERS. 16.-- Quicunque. Pharisaei qui omnia fecerunt propter lucrum dicebant esse etiam sanctiora oblata quam templum vel altare: ut homines promptiores essent ad offerenda dona, quam ad preces fundendas in templo vel justitias faciendas. In quo 158Cnon Dei timorem, sed suam cupiditatem attendebant. Ideo arguit eos Dominus stultitiae et fraudulentiae.

VERS. 22.-- In coelo. Per creaturam: ubi non putatis perjurium esse, jurat in coelo, id est, per subjectam creaturam jurat, et per divinitatem praesidentem creaturae.

VERS. 23.-- Vae vobis. Et hic arguit eos avaritiae: quia nihil praetermittunt de his quae ad commodum suum sunt. Qui decimatis. Quia Deus praeceperat omnium rerum decimas offerri propter alimoniam sacerdotum. Nota quod per eleemosynam aliqua impunitas non emitur in iniquitate manentibus.

158DHaec oportuit. Ne eleemosynas fructum terrae videretur respuere, haec addidit.

VERS. 24.-- Duces caeci. Opinione religionis in minimis quae lucrum habent diligentiam ostenditis: sed magna praecepta de judicio, fide et misericordia devoratis et negligitis. Excolantes. Mystice. Culicis nomine seditiosus homicida Barabbas signatur: quia hoc animal strependo inquietat et sanguine delectatur. Cameli nomine propter humilitatem ad subeunda onera, Dominus intelligitur. Colunt ergo Judaei culicem, in est, minima observant, quia Barabbam qui non solverat sabbatum, dimiserunt, et camelum glutiunt, id est, majora contemnunt: quia Christum spiritualiter sabbatum insinuantem per judicium et 159Amisericordiam et fidem (quae illi contemnunt) occiderunt.

VERS. 25.-- Vae vobis. Diversis similitudinibus simulationis et mendacii arguit pharisaeos qui aliud hominibus ostendunt, aliud domi agunt. Qui mundatis. Frustra sanctitatem exterius praetenditis, sicut si quis calicem mundet exterius, intus autem relinquat sordes: cum ejus usus sit interior. Lucas ita. Stulte, qui fecit quod deforis est etiam id quod intus est, fecit (Luc. XI), id est, qui utramque naturam hominis fecit, utramque mundari voluit. Nemo igitur putet fornicationem et hujusmodi corporalia vitia solummodo gravia esse, et avaritiam, et superbiam, et iram, et caetera spiritualia levia esse.

VERS. 26.-- Calicis. Per similitudinem, non quod 159Bin calice et paropside sit eorum superstitio.

VERS. 29.-- Qui aedificatis. Extruitis memoriam occisorum propter favorem vulgi, ut justi videamini, arguitis etiam facinora patrum vestrorum.

VERS. 31.-- Itaque. Quia scelera patrum detestamini pro specie quidem sanctitatis; dum Christum persequendo et apostolos eadem facitis, facto probatis vos esse filios non solum carne, sed imitatione iniqua: et patet eos scientes peccare. Id est contra vos loquimini dum patres damnatis: similiter enim facitis.

VERS. 32.-- Et vos. Probato quod filii sunt homicidarum, ponit id ad quod tendebat: Et vos implete, quod illis defuit implete, illi prophetas, vos Dominum prophetarum occidite. Non jubet, sed quod 159Cfacturi sunt ostendit.

VERS. 33.-- Quomodo fugietis. (HIER.) Sepulcra sanctorum aedificando, an potius corda a malitia mundando. Liberabunt vos sancti, quorum sepulcra ornatis? Non certe. Sancti non sunt amici inimicis Dei: frustra sanctos honorant, qui sanctitatem spernunt.

VERS. 34.-- Sapientes. Qui sciunt quem proferant sermonem. Scribas. In lege peritos.

VERS. 35.-- Ut veniat super vos. Non hos tantum praesentes dicit, sed omnem generationem malorum praecedentem et futuram: quia omnes una civitas sunt, unum corpus diaboli. Abel justi. Justitia Abel approbata est per munerum acceptionem. Usque ad sanguinem. Cur usque adhuc, cum multi post hunc 159Doccisi ante natalem Christi, et Christo nato mox pueri ab hac generatione sunt perempti? Sed quia Abel pastor ovium fuit necatusque in agro: Zacharias sacerdos necatus est in atrio templi: utriusque gradus martyres et laicos et officio altaris mancipatos, sub eorum voluit intimare vocabulis. Zacharias qui occisus est a rege Joas inter templum et altare, ut historia Regum narrat, filius fuit Joiadae, et in Evangelio quo utuntur Nazaraei, pro filio Barachiae legitur filius Joiadae. Sed ut justitia Joiadae sacerdotis praedicetur, ipse Barachias vocatur, id est, benedictus Domini. Iste Zacharias de duodecim prophetis unus erat, sicut pater Joannis de prophetis unus erat.

160AVERS. 36.-- Super generationem. Quae a primo parricida usque ad ultimum perditionis filium computatur: quasi, similes erunt in poena sicut sunt et in culpa.

VERS. 37.-- Jerusalem. Quasi dicat: Rei estis sanguinis, et merito, quia multoties misi ad vos congregandos, et noluistis. Quoties. Ac si dicat: Ego volui, et tu noluisti, et quotquot congregavi mea voluntate semper efficaci te nolente feci, quia semper ingrata fuisti. Gallina. Hoc animal magnum affectum habet in filios, ita ut eorum infirmitate affecta infirmetur, et eos alis protegens contra milvum pugnet. Sic Dei sapientia per carnem infirmata, protegit nos et defendit a diabolo.

VERS. 38.-- Relinquetur. Civitas auxilio Dei nudata 160Bsuae ditioni relinquetur, quae bene derelicta est, cum a Deo derelinquitur.

VERS. 39.-- Benedictus qui venit. Hoc turbae dixerunt Domino venienti Jerusalem. Quod autem his transactis hic Dominus dicit, cogit mystice intelligi de adventu charitatis suae, ubi confitebuntur Filium quem crucifixerunt.

CHAPTER XXIII.

157DVERS. 1.-- Then he spoke. Those being refuted who are crafty, he instructs those who are fit, none the less exhorting them to be subject to those men for the sake of the priesthood and the dignity of the name: considering not their works, but their teaching.

VERS. 4.-- Heavy burdens. The commandments of the law, with their own traditions mixed in, persuading men to live according to these: and not to come to the easy and delightful grace of Christ; whence above: Come to me, you that labour and are burdened.

VERS. 5.-- But all things. He said that such teachers are to be heard, not imitated: now he shows the cause for which they cannot believe Christ, that is, because they do all things to be seen of men. For he who 158Adesires earthly glory cannot believe Christ preaching heavenly things.

VERS. 8.-- But you. For a twofold reason he bids us take heed of them, lest we be led astray by their dissembling, or lest we be inflamed with emulation.

VERS. 9.-- One is. Who enlightens man, which no other man does, but only trains him, by teaching he does not bestow understanding. Your Father. God is called Father and Master by nature, man by indulgence. Father, that honour may be paid to age, not that he be held the author of life. Master, from fellowship with the true Master, as his messenger: and he is honoured out of reverence for him by whom he is sent.

VERS. 13.-- You shut. Either by evil example, or by taking away the mysteries of the Scriptures. For by the example and 158Bteaching of those who are masters, others indeed do not receive Christ.

VERS. 15.-- You go about. In the whole world you disparage the Gospel: and if anyone cleaves to you, he is the son of a twofold punishment: because he neither receives the remission of sins, and he falls into the fellowship of those who have persecuted Christ. Everyone is called the son of him whose works he does. a proselyte. Proselytes are those received from among the Gentiles into the Synagogue, whose rarity is signified by the one, because scarcely any after Christ.

VERS. 16.-- Whosoever. The Pharisees, who did all things for gain, used to say that the offerings were even holier than the temple or the altar: so that men might be readier to offer gifts than to pour out prayers in the temple or to do works of justice. In which 158Cthey were looking not to the fear of God, but to their own greed. Therefore the Lord convicts them of folly and of fraud.

VERS. 22.-- By heaven. By the creature: where you do not think there is any perjury, he swears by heaven, that is, he swears by the creature that is subject, and by the divinity that presides over the creature.

VERS. 23.-- Woe to you. Here too he convicts them of avarice: because they pass over nothing of the things that are for their own advantage. You that tithe. Because God had commanded the tithes of all things to be offered for the sustenance of the priests. Note that by almsgiving no impunity is bought for those who remain in iniquity.

158DThese things ye ought. Lest he should seem to spurn alms, the fruit of the earth, he added these words.

VERS. 24.-- Blind guides. Under the opinion of religion you show diligence in the least things which have gain in them: but the great commandments concerning judgment, faith and mercy you devour and neglect. Straining out. Mystically. By the name of the gnat the seditious murderer Barabbas is signified: because this creature disquiets by its buzzing and delights in blood. By the name of the camel, for its lowliness in taking up burdens, the Lord is understood. The Jews therefore strain the gnat, that is in est, they observe the least things, because they let Barabbas go, who had not broken the sabbath, and they swallow the camel, that is, they despise the greater things: because they killed Christ, who was setting forth the sabbath spiritually through judgment and 159Amercy and faith (which things they despise).

VERS. 25.-- Woe to you. By divers likenesses he convicts the pharisees of dissembling and of lying, who show one thing to men and do another at home. You that make clean. In vain do you hold out holiness outwardly, as if a man should cleanse a cup on the outside, but leave the filth within: whereas the use of it is within. Luke thus. Fool, he that made that which is without made also that which is within (Luc. XI) [Vulg. Lc 11:40 Stulti nonne qui fecit quod deforis est, etiam id quod deintus est fecit?, a question addressed to many: "Ye fools, did not he that made that which is without make also that which is within?"], that is, he who made both natures of man willed both to be cleansed. Let no one therefore think that fornication and bodily vices of this kind are alone grievous, and that avarice, and pride, and wrath, and the other spiritual vices are light.

VERS. 26.-- Of the cup. By way of likeness, not that 159Btheir superstition is in the cup and the dish.

VERS. 29.-- You that build. You raise up a memorial of the slain for the favour of the common people, that you may seem just, you even accuse the crimes of your fathers.

VERS. 31.-- Wherefore. Because you detest the crimes of your fathers under an appearance indeed of holiness; while by persecuting Christ and the apostles you do the same things, you prove by the deed that you are their sons not only in flesh, but in wicked imitation: and it is plain that they sin knowingly. That is, you speak against yourselves while you condemn your fathers: for you do the like.

VERS. 32.-- And you. When it has been proved that they are the sons of murderers, he sets down that to which he was tending: And fill you up, fill up what was lacking to them, they slew the prophets, do you slay the Lord of the prophets. He does not command, but shows what 159Cthey are about to do.

VERS. 33.-- How will you flee. (HIER.) By building the sepulchres of the saints, or rather by cleansing your hearts from malice. Will the saints, whose sepulchres you adorn, set you free? Certainly not. The saints are not friends to the enemies of God: in vain do they honour the saints who despise holiness.

VERS. 34.-- Wise men. Who know what discourse they utter. Scribes. Men skilled in the law.

VERS. 35.-- That upon you may come. He speaks not of these present only, but of the whole generation of the wicked, going before and to come: because all are one city, one body of the devil. Of just Abel. The justice of Abel was approved by the acceptance of his gifts. Even to the blood. Why only thus far, since many were slain after him 159Dbefore the birth of Christ, and, when Christ was born, the children were straightway destroyed by this generation? But because Abel was a shepherd of sheep and was slain in the field: Zacharias a priest was slain in the court of the temple: he willed to signify under their names the martyrs of both ranks, both laymen and those bound to the office of the altar. Zacharias, who was slain by king Joas between the temple and the altar, as the history of the Kings relates, was the son of Joiada, and in the Gospel which the Nazaraeans use, instead of the son of Barachias there is read the son of Joiada. But that the justice of Joiada the priest may be proclaimed, he himself is called Barachias, that is, blessed of the Lord. This Zacharias was one of the twelve prophets, as the father of John was one of the prophets.

160AVERS. 36.-- Upon the generation. Which is reckoned from the first parricide to the last son of perdition: as though, they shall be alike in punishment as they are also in guilt.

VERS. 37.-- Jerusalem. As though he should say: You are guilty of blood, and deservedly, because many times I sent to gather you, and you would not. How often. As if he should say: I would, and thou wouldst not, and as many as I gathered, I did by my own will, which is always effectual, while thou wouldst not, because thou wast always ungrateful. A hen. This creature has great affection toward her young, so that, touched by their weakness, she grows weak, and, covering them with her wings, fights against the kite. So the wisdom of God, made weak through the flesh, protects us and defends us from the devil.

VERS. 38.-- It shall be left. The city, stripped of the help of God, 160Bshall be left to its own dominion, and it is well left desolate, when it is forsaken by God.

VERS. 39.-- Blessed is he that cometh. This the crowds said to the Lord as he came to Jerusalem. But that the Lord says this here, when these things are past, compels it to be understood mystically of the coming of his charity, where they shall confess the Son whom they crucified.

CAPUT XXIV.

VERS. 2.-- Non relinquetur hic lapis super lapidem. Mystice. Recedente Domino de templo omnia legis aedificia et compositio mandatorum ista destructa est, ut nihil possit a Judaeis impleri. Divinitus autem provisum est, ut crescente Evangelii gratia templum illud cum suis caeremoniis tolleretur, ne 160Cquis infirmus in fide illis stantibus Judaismi occasionem haberet. Idem qui comminatus est verbo, ostendit signo, dum egreditur ab illis facta comminatione.

VERS. 3.-- Sedente autem. Ubi Marcus ait, contra templum. Sedet contra templum cum de ruina ejus et excidio gentis disputat ut et corporis situs verbis suis congruat. Mystice. Sedet in monte Oliveti, id est in Ecclesia quietus manens superborum detestatur arrogantiam. Dic nobis. Quia dixit omnia destruenda, tempus et signa destructionis quaerunt. Tria quaerunt: quo tempore destruenda Jerusalem sit? quo venturus Christus? quo consummatio saeculi ventura sit?

VERS. 4.-- Videte. Cum haec fient, ne putemus 160Dinstare diem Domini, cujus signum perspicue ponitur in sequentibus. Praemunit ne turbentur vel deficiant per deceptionem, ut per Simonem Magum, vel per tribulationem et persecutionem, ut per Neronem. Nota haec venientia ante excidium Jerusalem similia sunt et eadem fere cum his quae fient in consummatione saeculi, et loquitur ita de uno quasi de alio.

VERS. 5.-- multi. Imminente Jerusalem excidio multi fuerunt principes qui se dicerent esse Christos, et tempus libertatis prope esse. Multi in Ecclesia temporibus apostolorum, qui praeter alia falsa dicerent instare diem Domini.

VERS. 6.-- Audituri. Haec in Judaea a passione Domini 161Aabundaverunt: quibus ne territi apostoli Jerusalem et Judaeam deserant admonentur, quia non statim finis, sed post quadraginta duos annos.

VERS. 8.-- Haec autem. Haec autem omnia sunt initia, non consummatio quasi praeconia Antichristi, vel excidii urbis. Haec partim ad eversionem Judaeorum, partim ad diem judicii pertinent, quia ita apostoli interrogaverunt de utroque.

VERS. 9.-- Tunc tradent. Quo merito haec Judaeis eveniant subdit, qui post occisionem Christi, nominis et fidei ejus praecones persecuti sunt.

VERS. 11.-- Et multi pseudoprophetae. Quidam hoc ad eversionem Judaeorum referunt. Sed tunc inter eos, quia nullus erat fidelis, ad quem fieret exhortatio; melius hoc accipitur de haereticis, quorum 161Bprimus Simon Magus fuit. Extremus major omnibus, Antichristus est. Diligenter intuendum est, quod signorum ad excidium urbis, quod ad diem Domini referatur. Respondit enim Dominus, quae ex illo tempore erant secutura sive de excidio Jerusalem sive de adventu suo.

VERS. 14.-- Et praedicabitur. Quia noverat Dominus corda discipulorum de excidio et perditione suae gentis contristanda, haec solatio relevat ut scirent multo plures socios gaudii aeterni de toto orbe colligendos. Adhuc tenete hoc signum, quod non instet dies Domini vel desolatio Jerusalem antequam apostoli toto orbe dispersi sint, praeter duos Jacobos, qui primitus in Judaea interfecti sunt. Dispersi, inquam, ut si dolerent de perditione suae gentis, solatium 161Chabeant plures socios de omni gente collectos.

VERS. 15.-- Abominationem, etc. Vel si de excidio urbis, abominatio, id est idolum. Pilatus enim posuit imaginem Caesaris in templo desolato. Vel hoc dicitur de statua Adriani equestri, quae in ipso sanctorum loco longo tempore stetit.

VERS. 16.-- Tunc qui in Judaea. Haec ad litteram facta sunt, cum, appropinquante exterminio Judaeorum, oraculo moniti omnes Christiani fugerunt in regnum Agrippae qui parebat Romanis.

VERS. 22.-- Et nisi. Quae ergo spes electis, nisi gratia Dei, quae dat patientiam piis, ut citius demat potentiam impiis? Quanto enim erit haec tribulatio gravior, tanto erit brevitate moderatior. Vel dierum 161Dnomine, ipsa mala significantur. Breviati fuissent. Non mensura, sed numero, ne mora temporum fides concutiatur credentium. Erunt enim haec mala tribus annis, et dimidio tantum. Ecce hic Christus. Habet unaquaeque haeresis, vel certas mundi partes, unde dicitur: Ecce hic et ecce illic. Vel in occultis aut in obscuris conventiculis curiositatem hominum decipit, unde dicitur: Ecce in cubilibus, ecce in deserto. Multi erunt qui non credent Christum esse communem salutem omnium, sed quorumdam, unde dicitur: hic aut illic.

VERS. 24.-- Si fieri posset. Id est, si aliquo modo fieri posset, ut Dei praefinitio mutaretur, et ideo non de veris intelligi potest electis. Electi. Non quod divina 162Aelectio frustretur, sed qui judicio humano videntur electi.

VERS. 26.-- Ecce in deserto. Multi etiam credunt non venire in publicum, sed in deserto. Multi credunt Christum dedisse secretiora praecepta fidei aliis, non omnibus, unde dicitur, in penetralibus. Nolite credere, quod Christus in deserto vel in penetralibus, sed quod fides ejus ab Oriente in Occidentem in catholicis fulget clara et manifesta. Ne ergo credatur schismaticis. Nomine Orientis et Occidentis totum orbem designat.

VERS. 31.-- A summis coelorum. Ne quis putaret a quatuor plagis terrae, et non a cunctis finibus ejus, simul et mediterraneis regionibus electos esse congregandos, subditur: A summo terrae usque ad 162Bsummum coeli, id est, ab extremis terrae finibus per directum usque ad ultimos terminos ejus: ubi longe aspicientibus circulus coeli terrae videtur insidere.

VERS. 32.-- Ab arbore. Mystice. A populo Israel, in quo caecitas ex parte contigit: et fuit ficus damnata. Sed cum de arido ligno ramus fidei et charitatis in eo viruerit, et folia, id est, verba praedicationis exorta fuerint, prope est aestas, quae exspectatur, id est, aeterna serenitas et renascentium desiderata novitas. Cum enim tandem ablata perfidia Israel omnis salvus fiet, prope esse diem judicii non dubitandum est.

VERS. 35.-- Coelum et terra. Innovabuntur, deposita priori forma, permanente autem substantia, 162Cunde dicitur: Terra in aeternum stabit.

VERS. 36.-- De die. Ne forte auditis signis, quaererent etiam illius diei certum terminum, ait: De die novissima, post quot annos vel dies ventura sit, nolite esse solliciti, nulli diffinitum est, quod vel in die vel in nocte judicium futurum sit, sed pro manifestatione dicitur dies, vel pro occultatione nox. Nemo scit, etc. In quibusdam codicibus additur, neque Filius. Qui quidem omnia scit cum Patre, sed non facit homines scire, sicut Apostolus ait: In eo esse absconditos thesauros sapientiae et scientiae (II Col. II). Non facit scire, quia inutiliter sciretur. Sed semper simus incerti de adventu judicis, ut sic quotidie vivamus quasi in alia die judicandi simus. Neque electi angeli quemquam scire fecerunt. Vel 162Dnulli sanctorum angelorum concessit Deus notitiam diei hujus, neque filio adoptivo, id est, neque ulli sanctorum hominum.

VERS. 37.-- Sicut autem. Subitum adventus sui diem plurimis affirmat exemplis: nam et fulguri cito omnia transvolanti comparavit et diebus Noe, vel Loth secundum alium evangelistam aequiparat.

VERS. 38.-- Comedentes et bibentes, nubentes, etc. Non alimenta et nuptiae, sed immoderatus licitorum usus arguitur. Post bella, fames, terraemotus, etc., tunc brevis sequetur pax, ut fidelis probetur, an transactis malis speret judicem venturum, unde dicitur: Cum enim dixerint pax et securitas, tunc repentinus eis superveniet interitus (I Thess. V).

163AVERS. 40.-- Tunc, etc. Lucas, in illa nocte, id est, in illa tribulatione qua probantur, in qua alii permanent, alii cadunt. Duo erunt. Duae differentiae praedictorum sunt in Ecclesia, quasi in agro laborantium, eorum qui sincere Christum annuntiant, et eorum qui verbum Dei adulterant.

VERS. 41.-- Duae molentes. Duae differentiae sequentium orbem rerum mobilium, qui feminino genere designantur, quia majorum consilio reguntur feminae a viris. Duo in lecto. Qui otium eligunt et quietem, quae significantur nomine lecti, nec saecularibus, nec ecclesiasticis negotiis occupati. Unus assumetur. Qui propter Deum continere studet, ut sine sollicitudine vivens cogitet quae Dei sunt. Qui autem humana laude vel aliqua vitiorum corruptione monasticam 163Bvitam elegerit, relinquetur a Deo. Non sunt alia genera hominum, quam haec tria in Ecclesia. Unde Ezechiel tres viros liberatos vidit, Noe, Daniel et Job, id est rectores ut Noe, continentes ut Daniel, conjugatos ut Job. Et secundum haec tria genera hominum, tres supponit parabolas. In prima agit de laborantibus in agro, quod est ubi dicit: Vigilate ergo. In secunda agit de exsistentibus in lecto, quod est ubi dicit: Simile est regnum coelorum decem virginibus. In tertia agit de molentibus in mola, quod est ubi dicit: Sicut enim homo peregre proficiscens, etc.

VERS. 42.-- Vigilate ergo. (HIER.) Non dixit quia nescimus, sed quia nescitis, ut ostendat se diem judicii non ignorare.

163CVERS. 43.-- Si sciret, etc. Nesciente patrefamilias fur domum perfodit, quia dum a sui custodia spiritus dormit, improvisa mors carnis habitaculum irrumpit, et ad supplicium trahit. Furi autem resisteret si vigilaret, quia adventum judicis occulte venientem praecavens occurreret poenitendo.

VERS. 45.-- Quis putas est fidelis servus. Tribus similitudinibus, fulguris, furis, et submersionis Pentapolis, terrorem judicii introduxit communiter ad omnes, ut vigilarent et parati essent. Nunc specialiter ad praelatos loquitur qui humiles, prudentes, non appetentes bonum propter lucrum temporale, sed verbo et exemplo et opere debent pascere oves. Quis putas, quasi dicat: Admonendi estis. Nam quam rarus est Domino propter Dominum serviens, oves 163DChristi non ad lucrum, sed amore Christi pascens. Prudens. In futuro sibi providens. Quem constituit. Quam rarus qui sit vocatus a Deo tanquam Aaron et non magis se ingesserit, et qui non magis se pascat quam oves.

VERS. 46.-- Beatus ille. Aeterna autem beatitudine glorificandus est qui Domino veniente ad judicium repertus fuerit fideliter annonam verbi ministrans et in cura gregis vigilans. Rarus invenitur fidelis vel prudens, sed qui talis invenitur beatus est.

VERS. 47.-- Super. Id est, super omnia coelestis regni gaudia, caeteros, id est, bonos auditores faciet discumbere, et transiens ministrabit eis.

VERS. 49.-- Percutere conservos. Injuriis afficere 164Avel infirmorum conscientias pravo verbo vel exemplo vitiare. Unde Lucas ait servos vel ancillas (Luc. XII), quorum quanta infirmior est aetas vel sexus, tanto facilior est ruina.

VERS. 51.-- Cum hypocritis, etc. Cum illis scilicet qui erant in agro et qui molebant, et nihilominus derelicti sunt. Qui dicuntur hypocritae, et in agro, et in mola, id est, qui bona videbantur facere, sed exitus diversae voluntatis apparuit. Fletus Per similitudinem membrorum magnitudo ostenditur tormentorum.

CHAPTER XXIV.

VERS. 2.-- There shall not be left here a stone upon a stone. Mystically. When the Lord withdrew from the temple, all the buildings of the law and that framing of the commandments was destroyed, so that nothing can be fulfilled by the Jews. And it was divinely provided that, as the grace of the Gospel increased, that temple with its ceremonies should be taken away, lest 160Canyone weak in faith should, while those things stood, have occasion of Judaism. The same who threatened by word showed it by a sign, when he goes out from them after the threat has been made.

VERS. 3.-- And as he sat. Where Mark says, over against the temple. He sits over against the temple when he discourses of its ruin and of the destruction of the nation, so that the posture of his body also may agree with his words. Mystically. He sits on mount Olivet, that is, remaining at rest in the Church he detests the arrogance of the proud. Tell us. Because he had said that all things were to be destroyed, they ask the time and the signs of the destruction. They ask three things: at what time is Jerusalem to be destroyed? when is Christ to come? when is the consummation of the world to come?

VERS. 4.-- Take heed. When these things shall come to pass, let us not think 160Dthat the day of the Lord is at hand, the sign of which is plainly set down in what follows. He forearms them lest they be troubled or fail through deception, as through Simon Magus, or through tribulation and persecution, as through Nero. Note that these things which come before the destruction of Jerusalem are like, and almost the same as, those which shall come to pass at the consummation of the world, and he speaks of the one as though of the other.

VERS. 5.-- many. When the destruction of Jerusalem was at hand there were many leaders who said that they were Christs, and that the time of liberty was near. Many in the Church in the times of the apostles, who besides other falsehoods said that the day of the Lord was at hand.

VERS. 6.-- You shall hear. These things abounded in Judea from the passion of the Lord 161Aonward: and lest the apostles, terrified by them, should forsake Jerusalem and Judea, they are admonished that the end is not at once, but after forty-two years.

VERS. 8.-- And all these things. But all these things are beginnings, not the consummation, being as it were the heralds of Antichrist, or of the destruction of the city. These belong partly to the overthrow of the Jews, partly to the day of judgment, because the apostles asked in that way about both.

VERS. 9.-- Then shall they deliver you up. He adds by what desert these things befall the Jews, who after the killing of Christ persecuted the heralds of his name and of his faith.

VERS. 11.-- And many false prophets. Some refer this to the overthrow of the Jews. But then among them, because there was no believer to whom the exhortation might be made; this is better taken of the heretics, of whom the 161Bfirst was Simon Magus. The last, greater than all, is Antichrist. It is to be diligently considered which of the signs is to be referred to the destruction of the city, and which to the day of the Lord. For the Lord answered concerning the things that were to follow from that time, whether of the destruction of Jerusalem or of his own coming.

VERS. 14.-- And it shall be preached. Because the Lord knew that the hearts of the disciples were to be saddened over the destruction and ruin of their own nation, he lifts them up with this consolation, that they might know that far more companions of eternal joy were to be gathered out of the whole world. Hold moreover this sign, that neither the day of the Lord nor the desolation of Jerusalem is at hand before the apostles have been scattered through the whole world, except the two Jameses, who were killed first in Judea. Scattered, I say, so that if they should grieve over the ruin of their own nation, they might have as a solace 161Cmore companions gathered out of every nation.

VERS. 15.-- The abomination, etc. Or if it be said of the destruction of the city, the abomination, that is, an idol. For Pilate set the image of Caesar in the desolated temple. Or this is said of the equestrian statue of Hadrian, which stood a long time in the very place of the holy things.

VERS. 16.-- Then they that are in Judea. These things were done according to the letter, when, as the extermination of the Jews drew near, all the Christians, warned by an oracle, fled into the kingdom of Agrippa, who was obedient to the Romans.

VERS. 22.-- And unless. What hope then is there for the elect, save the grace of God, which gives patience to the godly, that it may the sooner take away power from the ungodly? For by how much this tribulation shall be the heavier, by so much shall it be the more moderate in its shortness. Or by the name 161Dof days the evils themselves are signified. Had been shortened. Not in measure, but in number, lest by the delay of the times the faith of believers be shaken. For these evils shall last three years and a half only. Behold, here is Christ. Each several heresy has also its own fixed parts of the world, whence it is said: Lo here, and lo there. Or in hidden or in obscure conventicles it deceives the curiosity of men, whence it is said: Behold, in the chambers, behold, in the desert. There shall be many who will not believe that Christ is the common salvation of all, but of some only, whence it is said: here or there.

VERS. 24.-- If it could be done. [Vulg. Mt 24:24 (si fieri potest), present indicative, "if it can be done"] That is, if in any way it could come to pass that God's predetermination were changed, and therefore it cannot be understood of the true elect. The elect. Not that the divine 162Aelection is made void, but those who by human judgment seem elect.

VERS. 26.-- Behold, in the desert. Many also believe that he does not come into the open, but into the desert. Many believe that Christ gave the more secret precepts of the faith to some, not to all, whence it is said, in the inner chambers. Do not believe, that Christ is in the desert or in the inner chambers, but that his faith shines clear and manifest from the East to the West among catholics. Let no credit therefore be given to schismatics. By the name of East and West he designates the whole world.

VERS. 31.-- From the utmost parts of the heavens. Lest anyone should think that the elect were to be gathered from the four quarters of the earth, and not from all its bounds, and from the inland regions besides, it is added: From the utmost part of the earth to 162Bthe utmost part of heaven, that is, from the farthest bounds of the earth straight on to its uttermost limits: where to those who look from far off the circle of heaven seems to rest upon the earth.

VERS. 32.-- From the tree. Mystically. From the people of Israel, in whom blindness has happened in part: and it was the fig tree that was condemned. But when out of the dry wood the branch of faith and charity shall have grown green in him, and the leaves, that is, the words of preaching, shall have sprung forth, the summer which is looked for is near, that is, eternal calm and the longed-for newness of those born again. For when at last, unbelief being taken away, all Israel shall be saved, it must not be doubted that the day of judgment is near.

VERS. 35.-- Heaven and earth. They shall be made new, the former shape laid aside, but the substance remaining, 162Cwhence it is said: The earth shall stand for ever. [Vulg. Ecl 1:4 terra autem in aeternum stat, present, "the earth standeth for ever"]

VERS. 36.-- Of the day. Lest perhaps, when the signs had been heard, they should seek also the fixed limit of that day, he says: Of the last day, after how many years or days it is to come, be not solicitous, it has been defined to no one whether the judgment shall be by day or by night, but the day is spoken of for manifestation, or the night for concealment. No one knows, etc. In certain codices there is added, neither the Son. Who indeed knows all things with the Father, but does not make men know, as the Apostle says: That in him are hidden the treasures of wisdom and of knowledge (II Col. II). He does not make it known, because it would be known to no profit. But let us always be uncertain of the coming of the judge, that we may so live every day as though we were to be judged on the next. Nor have the elect angels made anyone know it. Or 162DGod has granted the knowledge of this day to none of the holy angels, nor to an adopted son, that is, nor to any of holy men.

VERS. 37.-- But as. He affirms the suddenness of the day of his coming by very many examples: for he both compared it to the lightning that flies swiftly over all things, and likens it to the days of Noe, or of Lot according to another evangelist.

VERS. 38.-- Eating and drinking, marrying, etc. Not foods and marriages, but the immoderate use of lawful things is reproved. After wars, famines, earthquakes, etc., then a short peace shall follow, that the believer may be tried, whether, when the evils are past, he hopes that the judge will come, whence it is said: For when they shall say peace and security, then shall sudden destruction come upon them (I Thess. V).

163AVERS. 40.-- Then, etc. Luke, in that night, that is, in that tribulation in which they are tried, in which some stand fast, others fall. Two shall be. There are two sorts of the aforesaid in the Church, of those labouring as it were in a field, of those who sincerely announce Christ, and of those who adulterate the word of God.

VERS. 41.-- Two grinding. Two sorts of those who follow the round of changeable things, who are designated by the feminine gender, because women are governed by men, by the counsel of their betters. Two in a bed. Those who choose leisure and rest, which are signified by the name of the bed, occupied neither with secular nor with ecclesiastical business. One shall be taken. He who for God's sake strives to be continent, that living without solicitude he may think on the things that are God's. But he who has chosen the monastic 163Blife for human praise or through some corruption of vices, shall be left by God. There are no other kinds of men in the Church than these three. Whence Ezechiel saw three men delivered, Noe, Daniel and Job, that is, rulers like Noe, the continent like Daniel, the married like Job. And according to these three kinds of men he sets down three parables. In the first he treats of those labouring in the field, which is where he says: Watch ye therefore. In the second he treats of those who are in the bed, which is where he says: The kingdom of heaven is like to ten virgins. In the third he treats of those grinding at the mill, which is where he says: For even as a man going into a far country, etc.

VERS. 42.-- Watch ye therefore. (HIER.) He did not say, because we know not, but, because you know not, that he might show that he himself is not ignorant of the day of judgment.

163CVERS. 43.-- If he knew, etc. The thief breaks through the house while the father of the household knows it not, because while the spirit sleeps from its own guarding, unforeseen death bursts into the dwelling of the flesh, and drags it away to punishment. But he would withstand the thief if he watched, because, guarding beforehand against the coming of the judge who comes secretly, he would meet him by doing penance.

VERS. 45.-- Who, thinkest thou, is the faithful servant. By three likenesses, of the lightning, of the thief, and of the drowning of the Pentapolis, he brought in the terror of judgment to all in common, that they might watch and be ready. Now he speaks especially to prelates, who ought to be humble, prudent, not seeking the good for the sake of temporal gain, but to feed the sheep by word and by example and by work. Who, thinkest thou, as though he should say: You are to be admonished. For how rare is he that serves the Lord for the Lord's sake, feeding the sheep 163Dof Christ not for gain, but out of love of Christ. Prudent. Providing for himself in what is to come. Whom he hath appointed. How rare is he who is called by God as Aaron was and has not rather thrust himself forward, and who does not feed himself rather than the sheep.

VERS. 46.-- Blessed is that servant. But he is to be glorified with eternal blessedness who, when the Lord comes to judgment, shall be found faithfully dispensing the allowance of the word and watching in the care of the flock. Rarely is one found faithful or prudent, but he who is found such is blessed.

VERS. 47.-- Over. That is, over all the joys of the heavenly kingdom he will make the rest, that is, the good hearers, sit down, and passing by he will minister to them.

VERS. 49.-- To strike his fellow servants. To do them injuries, 164Aor to corrupt the consciences of the weak by a crooked word or example. Whence Luke says menservants or maidservants (Luc. XII), whose fall is by so much the easier as their age or sex is the weaker.

VERS. 51.-- With the hypocrites, etc. With those, namely, who were in the field and who were grinding, and were nevertheless left behind. Who are called hypocrites both in the field and at the mill, that is, who seemed to do good things, but the outcome of a different will appeared. Weeping By a likeness of the members the greatness of the torments is shown.

CAPUT XXV.

VERS. 1.-- Decem virginibus. Virgines sunt omnes qui se a luxu saeculi continent. Et sponsae. Omnes simul sunt sponsa quae modo desponsata est virgo a corruptione saeculi. Nubet, cum mortalis immortali 164Bconjungetur.

VERS. 2.-- Quinque. Quia quinquepertita est continentia in carnalibus illecebris, videlicet ut in quinque sensibus contineant se a voluptatibus.

VERS. 5.-- Moram autem faciente sponso. Quia non est parum temporis inter priorem et secundum adventum Christi. Dormitaverunt omnes et dormierunt. Quia postea suscitandi.

VERS. 6.-- Media autem nocte clamor factus est. Id est, nullo sciente vel sperante. Unde Dies Domini sicut fur in nocte veniet (I Thess. III).

VERS. 8.-- Date nobis de oleo, etc. Quorum facta aliena fulciuntur, laude eadem subtracta deficiunt, et de consuetudine id semper inquirunt, unde gaudere animus solet. Itaque hominum qui corda non 164Cvident, testimonium volunt habere apud Deum, qui cordis inspector est.

Exstinguuntur. Id est, adventu judicis intus obscurantur, et a Deo non habent mercedem, quia receperunt laudem.

VERS. 9.-- Ne forte. Quia quisque pro se rationem reddet, nec alieno testimonio juvatur quis apud Deum, cui secreta patent, et vix sibi quisque sufficit ut ei perhibeat testimonium conscientia sua. Ne forte unusquisque pro suis operibus mercedem suscipiet, nec possunt in die judicii aliorum virtutes, aliorum vitia sublevare. Ad vendentes. Vendentes sunt adulatores, quia dant laudem et accipiunt aliquam mercedem.

VERS. 10.-- Dum autem. Inclinantibus se illis in 164Dea quae foris sunt, et solita gaudia requirentibus, cum interna non noverunt, venit ille qui judicat. Clausa est. Post judicium magna est ejus severitas, cujus est ante ineffabilis misericordia.

VERS. 11.-- Novissime vero. Per veram poenitentiam, per infructuosas lacrymas pulsant foris relictae. Non dicitur quod emerunt oleum: quia nullo jam remanente gaudio de alienis laudibus, in angustiis redeunt ad implorationem Dei.

VERS. 13.-- Vigilate. Videlicet ut testimonium bonae conscientiae nobis praeparemus, ne cum fatuis virginibus foris remaneamus. Nescitis diem neque horam. Non solum diem judicii, sed diem mortis cujusque, vel horam in qua qui paratus invenitur, 165Aet tunc paratus invenietur. Et quia paratos nos invenire desiderat, subjicit aliam de eadem re parabolam.

VERS. 14.-- Peregre proficiscens vocavit servos suos, etc. Locus carnis terra est, ergo Christi carnem nostram ad peregrina duxit, quando coelum ascendit.

VERS. 15.-- Et profectus est statim. Non locum mutans, sed liberam eis potestatem operandi permittens.

VERS. 16.-- Operatus est in eis. A visibilibus se custodiendo, alios pro posse exhortando, ut de visibilibus ad invisibilia transeant. Vel utrique sexui praedicando, et de bonis moribus informando geminavit accepta talenta.

165BVERS. 19.-- Posuit autem. Coepit singulorum actus discutere accusante conscientia unumquemque vel excusante.

VERS. 20.-- Ecce alia quinque. Quotquot ab errore convertit, tot Domino obtulit, testimonium sibi dante conscientia, quod per gratiam sibi datam non tantum sibi profuit, sed etiam quos potuit lucrifecit.

VERS. 21.-- Super pauca. Pauca sunt bona praesentis vitae quamvis multa videantur, quia non sine alicujus molestiae admistione sunt. Multa autem bona aeterna, quae sunt sine omni corruptionis molestia. Intra in gaudium Domini tui. Admistus choris angelorum: sic intus laetare de munere, ut jam non sit quod exterius doleas de corruptione.

165CVERS. 22.-- Qui duo talenta acceperat. Iste viribus suis quidquid in lege Dei didicerat, in Evangelio duplicavit. Sive conscientiam et opera praesentis vitae futurae beatitudinis typos intellexit.

VERS. 24.-- Durus es. Quia ardua est via quae ducit ad vitam, et quia quanto gradus altior, tanto casus gravior.

VERS. 25.-- Et timens abii, etc. Et timens aggredi altioris vitae gradum, ne alterius salutem quaerens ipse periclitarer. Abscondi talentum tuum. Non praedicavi quod te donante intellexi.

VERS. 26.-- Sciebas quia, etc. Quare ista cogitatio non incussit tibi timorem ut scires me mea diligentius quaesiturum? Quod dixit pro excusatione vertitur in culpam.

165DVERS. 27.-- Nummulariis. Vel caeteris doctoribus, ut apostolis et episcopis. Vel, cunctis credentibus qui possunt pecuniam duplicare.

VERS. 28.-- Et date ei, etc. Hoc in Ecclesia fieri plerumque videmus, quia plerique dum bene ministrant exteriora quae accipiunt per adjunctam gratiam ad intellectum quoque mysticum perducuntur.

VERS. 29.-- Omni enim, etc. Multi enim natura licet sapientes et acuti, si sint desides, perdunt bonum naturae, et praemium quod erat eis promissum, videtur transire ad alios qui quod habent minus, per naturam acquirunt per industriam. Vel qui habet fidem et bonam voluntatem, etiam si minus habet in opere dabitur ei a bono judice. Sed qui in fide non 166Ahabet etiam virtutes quas naturaliter habet, perdit sine qua non sunt virtutes. Vel qui habet amorem verbi, datur ei sensus intelligendi. Qui vero non habet, etiam si naturali studio callet, deficit. Vel qui non habet charitatem, amittit omne bonum quod habet. Nota quod hoc quod cuique datur in rebus mundanis vel spiritualibus, pro talento imputatur de quo est rationem redditurus.

VERS. 31.-- Cum autem. Tradendus in proximo cruci praemisit gloriam triumphantis, ut secutura scandala hac promissione compensaret. Post parabolas de fine mundi nunc exsequitur Dominus modum futuri judicii.

VERS. 32.-- Oves ab, etc. Duo ordines erunt in judicio electorum scilicet et reproborum: sed electorum 166Balii judicabunt, quibus dicitur: Sedebitis et vos super sedes duodecim judicantes tribus Israel. Alii judicabuntur quibus dicitur: Esurivi et dedistis mihi manducare. Item reproborum alii qui extra Ecclesiam sunt, non judicabuntur: alii qui judicabuntur, quibus dicitur: Esurivi, et non dedistis mihi manducare, etc.

VERS. 33.-- Haedos. Haedus est animal petulcum et olidum, qui pro peccatis offerebatur in lege, et significat peccatores: nec dicit capras quae possunt habere fetus.

VERS. 34.-- Benedicti Patris. Beneficio Patris mei promoti ad obtinendam gloriam, in qua sine corruptione regnetis, ad quam praedestinati estis a constitutione mundi.

166CVERS. 37.-- Tunc respondebunt. Non diffidentes de verbis Domini, sed vel stupentes pro magnitudine majestatis regis; vel quia parum videtur illis omne bonum quod fecerunt, unde dicitur: Non sunt condignae passiones hujus temporis ad futuram gloriam (Rom. VIII), etc.

VERS. 40.-- De his fratribus. Fratres mei et mater sunt qui faciunt voluntatem Patris mei.

VERS. 42.-- Non dedistis, etc. Quid meretur qui aliena rapuit, si aeternaliter damnatur quid de suo non dedit?

VERS. 44.-- Domine, quando, etc. Excusare se satagunt quasi Deum fallere possint qui homines fallebant. Sed non habent excusationem qui converti a malis et agere eleemosynas noluerunt; unde subdit: 166DQuandiu non fecistis uni, etc.

VERS. 46.-- In supplicium aeternum. Frustra ergo Origenes spondet liberationem post multos annos malis hominibus et ipsis daemonibus. In supplicium aeternum. (AUG.) Qua in re misericordior profecto fuit Origenes qui et ipsum diabolum atque angelos ejus post graviora pro meritis et diuturniora supplicia ex illis cruciatibus eruendos atque sociandos sanctis angelis credidit. Sed illum et propter hoc et propter alia nonnulla, et maxime propter alternantes sine cessatione beatitudines et miserias et statutis saeculorum intervallis, ab illis ad istas itus ac reditus interminabiles non immerito reprobavit Ecclesia.

CHAPTER XXV.

VERS. 1.-- Ten virgins. The virgins are all who refrain themselves from the luxury of the world. And the bride. All together are the bride, who has now been espoused a virgin away from the corruption of the world. She shall be wedded, when the mortal shall be joined to the 164Bimmortal.

VERS. 2.-- Five. Because continence in carnal enticements is fivefold, namely that in the five senses they should refrain themselves from pleasures.

VERS. 5.-- And the bridegroom tarrying. Because there is no little time between the first and the second coming of Christ. They all slumbered and slept. Because they are afterwards to be raised up.

VERS. 6.-- And at midnight there was a cry made. That is, with no one knowing or hoping for it. Whence The day of the Lord shall come as a thief in the night (I Thess. III).

VERS. 8.-- Give us of your oil, etc. Those whose deeds are propped up by what is another's fail when that same praise is withdrawn, and out of habit they always seek that whence the mind is wont to rejoice. And so they wish to have before God, who is the searcher of the heart, the testimony of men, who do not 164Csee hearts.

They are going out. That is, at the coming of the judge they are darkened within, and they have no reward from God, because they have received their praise.

VERS. 9.-- Lest perhaps. Because each shall render an account for himself, nor is anyone helped before God by another's testimony, since to him secret things lie open, and each scarcely suffices for himself, that his own conscience may bear him witness. Lest perhaps each one shall receive a reward for his own works, nor in the day of judgment can the virtues of some relieve the vices of others. To them that sell. They that sell are flatterers, because they give praise and receive some reward.

VERS. 10.-- But whilst. While they bend themselves toward 164Dthe things that are without, and seek their accustomed joys, since they have not known the things within, he comes who judges. It was shut. After the judgment great is the severity of him whose mercy before it is unspeakable.

VERS. 11.-- But last of all. By true penance, by fruitless tears, those left outside knock. It is not said that they bought oil: because, no joy from the praises of others now remaining, in their straits they return to the imploring of God.

VERS. 13.-- Watch ye. Namely, that we may prepare for ourselves the testimony of a good conscience, lest with the foolish virgins we remain outside. You know not the day nor the hour. Not only the day of judgment, but the day of each man's death, or the hour in which he who is found ready 165Ashall then also be found ready. And because he desires to find us ready, he adds another parable on the same matter.

VERS. 14.-- Going into a far country he called his servants, etc. The place of the flesh is the earth, therefore he led the flesh of Christ, our own, unto foreign parts, when he ascended into heaven.

VERS. 15.-- And immediately he took his journey. Not changing place, but allowing them a free power of working.

VERS. 16.-- He traded with them. By keeping himself from visible things, by exhorting others as he was able, that they might pass over from visible things to invisible. Or by preaching to both sexes, and by forming them in good morals, he doubled the talents he had received.

165BVERS. 19.-- But he reckoned. He began to examine the deeds of each, their conscience accusing or excusing every one.

VERS. 20.-- Behold other five. As many as he converted from error, so many he offered to the Lord, his conscience bearing him witness that by the grace given him he not only profited himself, but also gained those whom he could.

VERS. 21.-- Over a few things. The good things of the present life are few, although they seem many, because they are not without an admixture of some trouble. But the eternal good things are many, which are without any trouble of corruption. Enter into the joy of thy Lord. Mingled with the choirs of angels: so rejoice within over the gift, that there may now be nothing without over which thou shouldst grieve for corruption.

165CVERS. 22.-- He that had received two talents. This man by his own powers doubled in the Gospel whatever he had learned in the law of God. Or he understood the conscience and the works of the present life as figures of the blessedness to come.

VERS. 24.-- Thou art a hard man. Because the way that leads to life is steep, and because the higher the step, the heavier the fall.

VERS. 25.-- And being afraid I went away, etc. And fearing to attempt the step of a higher life, lest, seeking another's salvation, I myself should be endangered. I hid thy talent. I did not preach what I understood by thy gift.

VERS. 26.-- Thou knewest that, etc. Why did that thought not strike fear into thee, so that thou shouldst know that I would seek my own more diligently? What he said by way of excuse is turned into blame.

165DVERS. 27.-- To the bankers. Or to the other teachers, as to the apostles and bishops. Or, to all believers who are able to double the money.

VERS. 28.-- And give it to him, etc. We see this for the most part done in the Church, because very many, while they minister well the outward things which they receive, are led on by an added grace to the mystical understanding also.

VERS. 29.-- For to everyone, etc. For many, though wise and sharp by nature, if they are slothful, lose the good of nature, and the reward that had been promised them seems to pass over to others, who acquire by industry what they have less of by nature. Or he who has faith and a good will, even if he has less in work, it shall be given to him by the good judge. But he who has it not in faith 166Aloses even the virtues which he has naturally, without which [faith] there are no virtues. Or he who has love of the word, to him is given the sense of understanding. But he who has it not, even if he is skilled by natural study, fails. Or he who has not charity loses every good that he has. Note that whatever is given to each in worldly or in spiritual things is reckoned for a talent of which he is to render an account.

VERS. 31.-- And when. Being about to be delivered to the cross very soon, he set before them the glory of him who triumphs, that he might make up by this promise for the scandals that were to follow. After the parables about the end of the world, the Lord now sets forth the manner of the judgment to come.

VERS. 32.-- The sheep from, etc. There shall be two orders in the judgment, namely of the elect and of the reprobate: but of the elect 166Bsome shall judge, to whom it is said: You also shall sit upon twelve seats, judging the tribes of Israel. Others shall be judged, to whom it is said: I was hungry and you gave me to eat. Likewise of the reprobate, some who are outside the Church shall not be judged: others who shall be judged, to whom it is said: I was hungry, and you gave me not to eat, etc.

VERS. 33.-- The goats. The he-goat is a butting and rank-smelling animal, which was offered for sins in the law, and it signifies sinners: nor does he say she-goats, which can have young.

VERS. 34.-- Blessed of the Father. Advanced by the benefit of my Father to the obtaining of glory, in which you may reign without corruption, to which you have been predestined from the foundation of the world.

166CVERS. 37.-- Then shall they answer. Not distrusting the words of the Lord, but either amazed at the greatness of the king's majesty; or because every good thing that they did seems little to them, whence it is said: The sufferings of this time are not worthy to be compared with the glory to come (Rom. VIII), etc.

VERS. 40.-- Of these brethren. My brethren and my mother are they who do the will of my Father.

VERS. 42.-- You gave me not, etc. What does he deserve who has seized what belongs to another, if he is eternally damned who has not given anything of his own?

VERS. 44.-- Lord, when, etc. They busy themselves to excuse themselves, as though they who were deceiving men could deceive God. But they have no excuse who would not be converted from evils and do alms; whence he adds: 166DAs long as you did it not to one, etc.

VERS. 46.-- Into everlasting punishment. In vain therefore does Origen promise deliverance after many years to evil men and to the demons themselves. Into everlasting punishment. (AUG.) In which matter Origen was assuredly the more merciful, who believed that the devil himself too and his angels, after punishments heavier according to their deserts and of longer duration, were to be rescued out of those torments and joined to the holy angels. But the Church has not undeservedly condemned him, both for this and for sundry other things, and chiefly for the blessednesses and miseries alternating without ceasing, and for the endless goings and returnings from those to these at fixed intervals of ages.

CAPUT XXVI.

  • 167A

VERS. 1.-- Et factum est cum consummasset. De consummatione saeculi et discretione judicii: vel ab initio Evangelii usque ad passionem omnia faciendo et praedicando compleverat. Post sermonem quo se venturum in claritate praedixit, passurum se ostendit, ut sacramentum crucis admistum esse gloriae aeternitatis admoneat.

VERS. 6.-- Cum autem. Habito consilio ordo narrationis illuc transit, tunc abiit unus de duodecim, ut factum Judae conjungatur ad consilium sacerdotum. Redit autem ad eum diem, qui erat ante sex dies paschae dicens: Cum autem, etc. Simonis. Permanet pristinum nomen ut virtus curantis appareat. Per eam domum accipe illam partem populi quae credidit 167BDomino et curata est. Simon, id est obediens.

VERS. 7.-- Accessit. (HIER.) Diligenter observa, quae de duabus super caput. Siquidem peccatrix, super pedes, et ea quae dicitur non fuisse peccatrix, super caput. Mulier habens. Maria Magdalena soror Lazari modo justa et familiaris, quae quondam peccatrix venit et pedes lacrymis lavit, nunc caput et pedes unxit, pedes nunc, ut Joannes dicit. Unguenti pretiosi. Marcus: nardi spicati, id est, de spicis et foliis confecti, quod pretiosius est. Joannes nardi pistici, id est, fidelis, non adulterati aliis herbis: πίστις Graece, fides Latine. Mystice. Haec devotio Mariae fidem significat Ecclesiae, quae dum deitatem Christi praedicat, caput ungit: dum humanitatem, pedes. De hoc unguento in domo Simonis leprosi in 167CBethania dicunt, et Matthaeus et Marcus et Joannes. Lucas vero aliud dicit simile huic factum ante in domo alterius Simonis pharisaei non leprosi, neque in Bethania, sed de eadem muliere quae tunc accessit peccatrix.

VERS. 8.-- Discipuli indignati. Joannes dicit haec Judam locutum, et hoc gratia cupiditatis: alii potuerunt verbis ejus consentire, vel etiam idem dicere, sed propter curam pauperum.

VERS. 11.-- Me autem, etc. Corporali praesentia, alioquin dicit alibi: Ecce ego vobiscum sum omnibus diebus usque ad consummationem saeculi.

VERS. 12.-- Mittens enim. Officium est sepulturae quod vos perditionem esse putatis. Nec mirum, si mihi dat fidei odorem, pro qua mox fusurus sum sanguinem.

167DVERS. 15.-- Quid vultis. Quasi vile mancipium tradens, ponit in potestate ementium quantum velint dare.

VERS. 17.-- Ubi vis, etc. Non erat eis certa domus: in quo improbatur aedificatio superfluorum.

VERS. 18.-- Ad quemdam. Eumdem significat, quem Marcus et Lucas dicunt patremfamilias. Qui ergo ait ad quemdam, tanquam ex persona sua (et non Domini) studio brevitatis illum compendio insinuavit. Ad quemdam. Non ad quemlibet, sed ad aliquem certum, quem ex persona sua loquens recte posuisset. Modo autem cum verba Domini posuit, Ite in civitatem: interponit ipse, ad quemdam: non 168Aquia Dominus hoc dixerit, sed ut ipse nobis insinuaret tacito nomine proprio fuisse ibi quemdam ad quem mittebantur.

VERS. 19.-- Et fecerunt discipuli sicuti. In alio Evangelio scribitur: Invenerunt coenaculum grande stratum. Coenaculum, lex spiritualis est, quae de angustiis litterae egrediens in sublimi loco recipit Salvatorem. Nam qui litteram sequitur in imo pascha facit. Sed qui aquae bajulum, id est praeconem gratiae, in domum Ecclesiae sequitur, hic per spiritum litteram transcendens in alto mentis Christo refectionem parat.

VERS. 21.-- Quia unus. Qui praedixit de passione, praedicit de proditore, ut videns se non latere poeniteret facti. Mittit crimen in numero, ut agat conscius 168Bpoenitentiam, non tamen designat specialiter, ne impudentior fieret.

VERS. 22.-- Coeperunt singuli. Quia plus credunt magistro quam sibi: et timentes etiam fragilitatem suam, quaerunt de peccato cujus non habent conscientiam.

VERS. 23.-- Qui intingit. Aliis contristatis et a cibo manum retrahentibus, ea impudentia qua tradidit, manum cum magistro mittit, ut audacia bonam conscientiam mentiretur. Ecce humilitas et patientia, qua proditorem ad mensam admittit. In paropside. Marcus: in catino. Paropsis est vas escarum quadrangulum a paribus absidibus, id est, ab aequis lateribus dictum: catinum vas fictile aptum ad immittendum liquorem, et potuit fieri ut mensa fictile vas 168Cet quadrangulum contineret. Poena praedicitur, ut quem pudor non vicerat, corrigant denuntiata supplicia.

VERS. 24.-- Bonum erat ei. Multo melius esset omnino non esse quam male esse, usitate et simpliciter hoc dicit. Non potest enim ei bene esse qui non est. An diabolo dicit non nasci ad peccatum: an etiam bonum illi erat ut Christo non nasceretur per vocationem, ne esset apostata per proditionem? Vel solemus dicere simpliciter, melius esset non esse quam male esse.

Rabbi. Alii dicunt, Domine, iste, rabbi. Quasi minus peccatum habens prodere magistrum quam Dominum, vel blandiendo.

VERS. 25.-- Tu dixisti. Nec hic aperte dicitur an 168Dipse esset: potest enim intelligi, quasi dicat, non ego dixi.

VERS. 26.-- Coenantibus autem, etc. Finitis solemnibus gaudiis veteris paschae quae in memoriam liberationis populi Dei de Aegypto agebantur, transit ad novum quod in sui memoriam Ecclesiam frequentare volebat. Ut videlicet pro carne agni ac sanguine, sui corporis et sanguinis sacramentum substitueret, ipsumque esse monstraret, cui juravit Dominus et non poenitebit eum: tu es sacerdos (Psal. CIX), etc. Accepit. (HIER.) Postquam typicum pascha fuerat impletum, et agni carnes cum apostolis comederat, assumit panem. Et benedixit ac fregit. Quia hominem assumptum ita morti subdidit, ut ei immortalitatis 169Apotentiam inesse, et ideo cito resurrecturum esse monstraret. Fregit, ut ostendat corporis sui fractionem non sine sua sponte fore. Benedixit prius, quia naturam quam assumpsit cum Patre et Spiritu sancto, gratia divinae virtutis implevit. Ideo post coenam corpus suum dedit, quia necesse erat pascha typicum prius consummari, et sic veteri paschae sacramenta post substitui. Nos vero pro reverentia tanti sacramenti prius reficimur Dominicae passionis sacramentis, quam corporalibus escis.

VERS. 27.-- Gratias egit. Ostendit quod quisque facere debet in flagello culpae propriae, cum ipse aequanimiter tulit alienae: quia panis corpus confirmat, vinum sanguinem operatur.

VERS. 29.-- Non bibam. Non delectabor caeremoniis 169Bhujus populi, in quibus sacra paschalis agni praecipua sunt. Post passionem non assumam carnem, donec assumam eam novam quae modo mortalis est.

Novum in regno Patris. Dicens illud novum, hoc vetus, ostendit corpus: scilicet, de veteri Adam quod immortalitate immutabitur. Vobiscum, per hoc et suis immortalitas promittitur, etsi non eodem tempore. De hoc genimine, quia eadem corpora resurrectura. Erit autem dies meae resurrectionis, cum in regno Dei, id est, immortalitatis gloria sublimatus, de salute ejusdem populi spirituali gratia regenerati, novo vobiscum gaudio perfundar.

VERS. 30.-- Et hymno. Hoc est quod psalmus dicit: Edent pauperes et saturabuntur (Psal. XXI), etc. Vel hymnum secundum Joannem Patri gratias agens 169Ccantabat, in quo pro se et discipulis et credituris precabatur. Exierunt in montem, etc. Ut per sacramentorum perceptionem et suam intercessionem ad altiora charismata quibus corde perungamur nos conscendere doceat: ubi laboris refectio, doloris solatium.

VERS. 31.-- Omnes vos scandalum pati. Praedicit illis quid passuri sunt, ut post eum tum non desperent, sed poenitentia liberentur.

VERS. 32.-- Postquam. Sicut praedixit locum passionis, sic praedicit locum resurrectionis, ut sicut sunt tristes ex passione, sic laetentur glorificati resurrectione vel visione. Praecedam vos, etc. Ut infirmitatem eorum sponsione reditus sui consolaretur.

169DVERS. 33.-- Etsi omnes. Non est mendacium nec temeritas, sed fides et ardens dilectio, intantum ut imbecillitatem suam et fidem verborum Dei non contueretur.

VERS. 36.-- Et orem. Proxima morte apte orat in valle pinguedinis, quia per humilitatem et charitatis pinguedinem pro nobis subiit mortem.

VERS. 37.-- Coepit contristari. Non timore passionis, qui ad hoc venerat, sed pro scandalo apostolorum et perditione impiorum. (HIER.) Coepit contristari, ut veritatem assumpti hominis probaret. Vere contristatus est, sed non passio ejus animo dominatur: verum propassio est, unde ait: Coepit 170Acontristari. Contristatur autem non timore patiendi, qui ad hoc venerat, sed propter infelicem Judam et scandalum apostolorum et ejectionem Judaeorum et eversionem Jerusalem.

VERS. 38.-- Tristis. Anima est quae timet et tristatur. Petrus inferior non timet, dicens: Animam meam ponam pro te: quia ut homo vim mortis ignorat. Christus timet, quia, ut Deus in corpore constitutus, fragilitatem carnis exponit: qui corpus suscepit omnia debuit subire quae corporis sunt.
