Usque ad mortem. (HIL.) Non propter mortem, sed donec apostolos sua liberet passione. Non mors, sed tempus mortis in metu est, quia post virtutem resurrectionis, fides est firmanda credentium.

VERS. 39.-- Si possibile est. Hoc non pro timore, 170Bsed pro misericordia, ne Judaei, qui excusationem ignorantiae non habent, non occidant; unde ait: iste, id est populi Judaeorum non simpliciter calix: Si possibile est, ut sine interitu Judaeorum credant gentiles, passionem recuso. Sin autem illi excaecandi sunt ut gentes intrent, fiat non mea, sed tua voluntas. Non sicut ego volo. (AUG.) Transfigurat in se suos, qui nihil aliud vult quam Pater, ut ibi: Esurivi et dedistis mihi manducare. Et quid me persequeris? Docet autem eos privatam voluntatem corrigere et dirigere juxta divinam. (AMBR.) Hominem quem veritate corporis demonstrabat, aequabat affectu, ut diceret: Sed non sicut ego volo, sed sicut tu. Suscepit voluntatem meam, suscepit tristitiam meam. 170CConfidenter tristitiam nomino qui crucem praedico.

VERS. 41.-- Caro autem. Non de se utique hoc dicit, sed de his qui dixerunt se nunquam negaturos.

VERS. 42.-- Iterum secundo. Secundo orat, id est, si non potest Ninive salvari aliter, nisi aruerit cucurbita, id est populus Judaeorum, qui prius fuit ejus tabernaculum, ut me occidat.

VERS. 44.-- Oravit tertio. Tribus vicibus oravit, ut et nos a praeteritis peccatis veniam, et a poenitentibus malis tutelam, et a futuris periculis cautelam oremus, et ut omnem orationem ad Patrem et Filium et Spiritum sanctum dirigamus. Item sicut est trina tentatio cupiditatis, ita et timoris. Est enim concupiscentia carnis, concupiscentia oculorum et ambitio saeculi. Est et timor mortis, et timor utilitatis, 170Det timor doloris, contra quae omnia docet nos debere muniri oratione. Unde et propter trinam tentationem passionis potest intelligi Dominus ter orasse.

VERS. 47.-- Cum gladiis et fustibus. Factum congruit mutatae menti. Jam enim venit cum fustibus qui quandiu cum pace fuit tranquillius se egit.

VERS. 48.-- Dedit eis signum. Putabat signa quae viderat non divinitus, sed magice facta: et quem in monte transformatum audierat, timebat ne tali modo nunc de manibus eorum laberetur: et ideo dabat signum. Quemcunque osculatus. Habebat adhuc aliquid de verecundia discipuli, cum non eum palam tradidit persecutionibus, sed per signum osculi.

171AVERS. 49.-- Et osculatus est. Suscepit Dominus osculum non quo simulare nos doceat, sed ne proditionem fugere videatur, unde: Cum his qui oderunt pacem, eram pacificus (Psal. CXIX).

VERS. 52.-- Gladio peribunt. Gladio illo quo igneus vertitur ante paradisum, id est, divinae vindictae, et gladio spiritus, id est, verbi Dei.

VERS. 53.-- An putas quia, etc. Quasi dicat non indigeo auxilio apostolorum duodecim qui possum habere duodecim legiones angelorum, in quibus sunt septuaginta duo millia quibus omnium gentium lingua exprimitur. Innuit autem non esse verendum, si contra Jesum omnes nationes insurgant, cum multo fortiores ipse habeat exercitus angelorum. Aliter: Hic numerus significat omne genus hominum 171Bcum Romano imperio contra Judaeos certaturum. Hi sunt angeli Dei, qui post resurrectionem anno quadragesimo secundo, cum Tito et Vespasiano perdiderunt, homicidas illos, et civitatem illorum succenderunt.

VERS. 55.-- Existis cum gladiis. Stultum est cum gladiis et fustibus quaerere eum qui ultro se tradit, et in nocte per proditorem investigare quasi latitantem, qui quotidie in templo docebat. Sed in tenebris congregamini, quia potestas vestra in tenebris.

VERS. 57.-- Ad Caipham. Convenit nomen actioni. Caiphas, id est, investigator vel sagax ad implendam, scilicet doli sui nequitiam. Vel vomens ore, quia impudens ad proferendum mendacium et ad petendum homicidium.

171CVERS. 58.-- A longe. Merito a longe, qui in proximo fuerat negaturus, significat Ecclesiam imitaturam passionem Christi longe differenter, quia Ecclesia patitur pro se, sed Christus pro Ecclesia.

VERS. 64.-- Tu dixisti. Marcus: Ego sum: quod idem est: Et est similis contra Pilatum et Caipham responsio, ut a propria sententia condemnentur. Filium hominis. Me in forma quam judicatis exaltatum et habentem nomen quod est super omne nomen.

VERS. 65.-- Scidit vestimenta. Furor qui fecit de solio surgere, facit et vestem scindere. Magno mysterio: cum vestis Domini nec a militibus scindi potuit. Significat enim sacerdotium eorum pro sceleribus pontificatum esse penitus solvendum: sed soliditas 171DEcclesiae (quae vestis Dei dicitur) usque in finem saeculi indirupta manebit. Mos est Judaeorum cum aliquid blasphemiae audiunt, scindunt vestimenta.

Quod Paulus et Barnabas fecerunt in Licaonia, cum dicti sunt Jupiter et Apollo.

VERS. 67.-- Tunc exspuerunt. Sicut tunc consputus est salivis infidelium, ita nunc opprobriis exhonoratur falsorum fidelium, et colaphis, id est, blasphemiis eorumdem caeditur.

VERS. 68.-- Prophetiza. Dicunt contumeliam ei qui se prophetam haberi voluit a populis. Velaverunt enim faciem ejus secundum Marcum, non ut eorum scelera non videat, sed ut a seipsis gratiam 172Acognitionis ejus abscondant. Sicut velum fuit super faciem Moysi, quod usque hodie manet.

VERS. 69.-- Una ancilla. Cur prima ancilla prodit cum viri magis poterant cognoscere, nisi ut iste sexus ostendatur in necem Domini peccasse: et per passionem redemptus. Et ideo, mulier prima rerurrectionis mysterium accepit: et mandata custodivit, ut veterem praevaricationis aboleret errorem. Sicut per feminam hostis antiquus primum parentem seduxit, ita per mulierem ad magistrum negandum quem Christum Filium Dei confessus fuerat, principem Ecclesiae compellit.

VERS. 72.-- Non novi hominem. Quam noxia pravorum colloquia, quae cogunt Petrum negare Christum, vel hominem se nosse, quem confessus est 172Binter discipulos Dei Filium.

VERS. 73.-- Qui stabant. Joannes et Lucas unum dicunt cognatum Malchi. Sed vel est plurale pro singulari. Vel unus erat qui eum viderat, et praecipue dicebat, quem secuti caeteri similiter arguebant.

VERS. 74.-- Et continuo gallus cantavit. Post tertiam negationem sequitur galli cantus. Sacramenta rerum per statum temporum designantur. Media nocte negat, in galli cantu poenitet, post resurrectionem sub luce quem ter negaverat ter se amare professus est: quia quod in tenebris oblivionis erravit, speratae jam lucis rememoratione correxit: et ejusdem verae lucis praesentia plene quidquid mutaverat, erexit.

VERS. 75.-- Recordatus est. In alio Evangelio dicitur, 172CRespexit Dominus Petrum (Luc. XXII), et intuitu provocavit ad lacrymas, quasi in mentem ei reducens quoties negaverat, et quod ei praedixerat, nec potuit in tenebris permanere, quem lux mundi respexit. Et egressus. Ab impiorum consilio seclusus culpam lavat, quod non intus. Ob hoc haesitare permissus est ut in Ecclesiae principe remedium poenitentiae conderetur, et nemo auderet de sua virtute confidere.

CHAPTER XXVI.

  • 167A

VERS. 1.-- And it came to pass when he had finished. Concerning the consummation of the world and the separation of the judgment: or, from the beginning of the Gospel until the passion he had accomplished all things by doing and by preaching. After the discourse in which he foretold that he would come in brightness, he showed that he would suffer, that he might give warning that the sacrament of the cross is mingled with the glory of eternity.

VERS. 6.-- And when. The counsel having been held, the order of the narrative passes over to that place, then went one of the twelve, so that the deed of Judas may be joined to the counsel of the priests. But he returns to that day which was before the six days of the Pasch, saying: And when, etc. Of Simon. The former name remains, that the power of him who heals may appear. By that house understand that part of the people which believed 167Bthe Lord and was healed. Simon, that is, obedient.

VERS. 7.-- There came near. (HIER.) Observe diligently what is said of the two women concerning the head. For indeed the sinful woman, upon the feet, and she who is said not to have been a sinner, upon the head. A woman having. Mary Magdalen, the sister of Lazarus, now just and of his household, who once came a sinner and washed his feet with tears, now anointed his head and his feet, the feet now, as John says. Of precious ointment. Mark: of spikenard, that is, made of the spikes and the leaves, which is more precious. John, of pistic nard, that is, faithful, not adulterated with other herbs: πίστις in Greek, faith in Latin. Mystically. This devotion of Mary signifies the faith of the Church, which, while it preaches the Godhead of Christ, anoints the head: while it preaches his humanity, the feet. Of this ointment in the house of Simon the leper in 167CBethany both Matthew and Mark and John speak. But Luke tells of another thing like to this, done before in the house of another Simon, a Pharisee, not a leper, nor in Bethany, but concerning the same woman who then came a sinner.

VERS. 8.-- The disciples had indignation. John says that Judas spoke these things, and this for the sake of covetousness: the others could agree with his words, or even say the same, but out of care for the poor.

VERS. 11.-- But me, etc. In bodily presence, otherwise he says elsewhere: Behold I am with you all days, even to the consummation of the world.

VERS. 12.-- For she in pouring. It is an office of burial, which you think to be waste. Nor is it a wonder if she gives me the odour of faith, for which I am soon to pour out my blood.

167DVERS. 15.-- What will you give. Betraying him as though a worthless chattel, he puts it in the power of the buyers how much they will give.

VERS. 17.-- Where wilt thou, etc. There was no fixed house for them: wherein the building of superfluous things is condemned.

VERS. 18.-- To a certain man. He signifies the same man whom Mark and Luke call the master of the house. He therefore who says to a certain man, as though in his own person (and not the Lord's), for the sake of brevity indicated him in a summary way. To a certain man. Not to anyone whatever, but to some certain man, whom, speaking in his own person, he would rightly have set down. But now, when he has set down the words of the Lord, Go ye into the city: he himself puts in, to a certain man: not 168Abecause the Lord said this, but that he himself might indicate to us, the proper name being left unspoken, that there was there a certain man to whom they were sent.

VERS. 19.-- And the disciples did even as. In another Gospel it is written: They found a large upper room furnished [Vulg. Mc 14:15 Et ipse vobis demonstrabit coenaculum grande, stratum, "and he will show you a large upper room furnished"]. The upper room is the spiritual law, which, going forth from the straits of the letter, receives the Saviour in a lofty place. For he who follows the letter keeps the Pasch in the lowest place. But he who follows the water-bearer, that is, the herald of grace, into the house of the Church, he, transcending the letter by the spirit, prepares a refreshment for Christ in the height of the mind.

VERS. 21.-- That one of you. He who foretold concerning the passion foretells concerning the betrayer, that seeing himself not to be hidden he might repent of the deed. He casts the charge upon the number, that the guilty one might do 168Bpenance, yet he does not point him out particularly, lest he should become more shameless.

VERS. 22.-- They began every one. Because they believe the master more than themselves: and fearing also their own frailty, they ask concerning a sin of which they have no knowledge in themselves.

VERS. 23.-- He that dippeth. While the others were made sorrowful and were drawing back their hand from the food, with that shamelessness with which he betrayed him he puts his hand in with the master, that by boldness he might feign a good conscience. Behold the humility and the patience with which he admits the betrayer to the table. In the dish. Mark: in the bowl. A paropsis is a four-cornered vessel for food, so called from equal apsides, that is, from equal sides: a catinum is an earthen vessel fit for pouring in liquid, and it could happen that the table held a vessel both earthen 168Cand four-cornered. The penalty is foretold, that the punishments declared might correct him whom shame had not overcome.

VERS. 24.-- It were better for him. It would be much better not to be at all than to be in evil case, he says this in the customary and simple way. For it cannot be well with him who is not. Or does he say to the devil, not to be born unto sin: or was it also good for him that he should not be born to Christ by calling, lest he should be an apostate by betrayal? Or we are wont to say simply, it would be better not to be than to be in evil case.

Rabbi. The others say, Lord, this man, Rabbi. As though it were a lesser sin to betray a master than the Lord, or else by way of flattery.

VERS. 25.-- Thou hast said it. Nor is it openly said here whether 168Dit was he: for it can be understood as though he were saying, I did not say it.

VERS. 26.-- And whilst they were at supper, etc. The solemn joys of the old Pasch being ended, which were kept in memory of the deliverance of the people of God out of Egypt, he passes over to the new, which he willed the Church to frequent in memory of himself. Namely, that in place of the flesh and blood of the lamb he might substitute the sacrament of his own body and blood, and might show that he is the one to whom the Lord hath sworn, and he will not repent: thou art a priest (Psal. CIX), etc. He took. (HIER.) After the typical Pasch had been fulfilled, and he had eaten the flesh of the lamb with the apostles, he takes bread. And blessed and broke. Because he so subjected the man he had assumed to death, that he showed the power of immortality 169Ato be in him, and that therefore he would rise again quickly. He broke, that he might show that the breaking of his body would not be without his own will. He blessed first, because the nature which he assumed he filled, together with the Father and the Holy Spirit, with the grace of divine power. Therefore he gave his body after supper, because it was necessary that the typical Pasch should first be completed, and that so the sacraments should afterwards be substituted for the old Pasch. But we, for reverence of so great a sacrament, are refreshed with the sacraments of the Lord's passion before bodily food.

VERS. 27.-- He gave thanks. He shows what each one ought to do under the scourge of his own fault, since he himself bore with an even mind the scourge of another's: because bread strengthens the body, wine produces blood.

VERS. 29.-- I will not drink. I will not take delight in the ceremonies 169Bof this people, among which the rites of the paschal lamb are chief. After the passion I will not take up flesh, until I take it up new, which is now mortal.

New in the kingdom of the Father. Calling that new, and this old, he shows the body: namely, that which is of the old Adam, which shall be changed by immortality. With you, by this immortality is promised to his own also, although not at the same time. Of this fruit, because the same bodies are to rise again. But the day of my resurrection will be when, exalted in the kingdom of God, that is, in the glory of immortality, I shall be filled with a new joy together with you over the salvation of that same people regenerated by spiritual grace.

VERS. 30.-- And a hymn. This is what the psalm says: The poor shall eat and shall be filled (Psal. XXI), etc. Or, according to John, he sang a hymn giving thanks to the Father, 169Cin which he prayed for himself and for the disciples and for those who should believe. They went out unto the mount, etc. That through the receiving of the sacraments and through his own intercession he may teach us to ascend to higher gifts, with which we are anointed in heart: where is the refreshment of labour, the solace of sorrow.

VERS. 31.-- That you all suffer scandal [Vulg. Mt 26:31 Omnes vos scandalum patiemini, "you shall all be scandalized"]. He foretells to them what they are to suffer, that afterwards they may not then despair, but be set free by penance.

VERS. 32.-- After. As he foretold the place of the passion, so he foretells the place of the resurrection, that as they are sorrowful from the passion, so they may rejoice, glorified by the resurrection or by the vision. I will go before you, etc. That he might console their weakness by the promise of his own return.

169DVERS. 33.-- And if all. It is not a lie nor rashness, but faith and burning love, to such a degree that he did not look to his own weakness nor to the trustworthiness of the words of God.

VERS. 36.-- And pray. Death being close at hand, he fitly prays in the valley of fatness, because through humility and the fatness of charity he underwent death for us.

VERS. 37.-- He began to grow sorrowful. Not from fear of the passion, since he had come for this, but on account of the scandal of the apostles and the perdition of the ungodly. (HIER.) He began to grow sorrowful, that he might prove the truth of the man he had assumed. He was truly made sorrowful, but the passion does not rule over his mind: rather it is a propassion, whence he says: He began 170Ato grow sorrowful. But he is made sorrowful not from fear of suffering, since he had come for this, but on account of the unhappy Judas and the scandal of the apostles and the casting out of the Jews and the overthrow of Jerusalem.

VERS. 38.-- Sorrowful. It is the soul that fears and is made sorrowful. Peter, the lesser, does not fear, saying: I will lay down my life for thee: because, as a man, he does not know the force of death. Christ fears, because, as God set within a body, he sets forth the frailty of the flesh: he who took a body was bound to undergo all things that belong to a body.

Even unto death. (HIL.) Not because of death, but until he should free the apostles by his own passion. It is not death, but the time of death, that is in fear, because after the power of the resurrection the faith of believers is to be strengthened.

VERS. 39.-- If it be possible. This not out of fear, 170Bbut out of mercy, lest the Jews, who have not the excuse of ignorance, should not kill him [non occidant; read occidant, "should kill him"]; whence he says: this, that is, the cup of the people of the Jews, not simply a cup: If it be possible that the Gentiles may believe without the destruction of the Jews, I refuse the passion. But if they are to be blinded that the Gentiles may come in, let not my will, but thine be done. Not as I will. (AUG.) He transfigures his own into himself, who wills nothing other than the Father, as in that place: I was hungry, and you gave me to eat. And why persecutest thou me? But he teaches them to correct their own private will and to direct it according to the divine. (AMBR.) The man whom he showed forth by the truth of a body, he made equal in affection, so as to say: But not as I will, but as thou [Vulg. Mt 26:39 verumtamen non sicut ego volo, sed sicut tu]. He took up my will, he took up my sorrow. 170CConfidently do I name sorrow, I who preach the cross.

VERS. 41.-- But the flesh. He says this assuredly not of himself, but of those who said that they would never deny him.

VERS. 42.-- Again a second time. He prays a second time, that is, if Nineveh cannot be saved otherwise, unless the gourd wither, that is, the people of the Jews, who before were his tabernacle, so that it may kill me.

VERS. 44.-- He prayed the third time. He prayed three times over, that we too may pray for pardon from past sins, and for protection from the evils we repent of, and for wariness against future perils, and that we may direct every prayer to the Father and the Son and the Holy Spirit. Likewise, as there is a threefold temptation of desire, so also of fear. For there is the concupiscence of the flesh, the concupiscence of the eyes and the ambition of the world. There is also the fear of death, and the fear of advantage, 170Dand the fear of pain, against all which he teaches that we ought to be fortified by prayer. Whence also, on account of the threefold temptation of the passion, it can be understood that the Lord prayed three times.

VERS. 47.-- With swords and clubs. The deed agrees with the changed mind. For now he comes with clubs who, so long as he was at peace, bore himself more quietly.

VERS. 48.-- He gave them a sign [Vulg. Mt 26:48 dedit illis signum]. He thought that the signs which he had seen were done not by divine power, but by magic: and him whom he had heard was transfigured upon the mountain he feared lest in some such way he should now slip out of their hands: and therefore he gave a sign. Whomsoever I shall kiss. He still had something of the modesty of a disciple, in that he did not betray him openly to persecutions, but by the sign of a kiss.

171AVERS. 49.-- And he kissed him. The Lord received the kiss, not that he might teach us to dissemble, but lest he should seem to flee the betrayal, whence: With them that hated peace I was peaceable (Psal. CXIX).

VERS. 52.-- They shall perish with the sword. With that sword which, being of fire, is turned before paradise, that is, of divine vengeance, and with the sword of the spirit, that is, of the word of God.

VERS. 53.-- Thinkest thou that, etc. As if he should say, I have no need of the help of the twelve apostles, I who can have twelve legions of angels, in which are seventy-two thousand, by which the tongue of all nations is expressed. But he intimates that it is not to be feared if all nations rise up against Jesus, since he himself has far stronger armies of angels. Otherwise: This number signifies the whole race of men 171Bthat was to contend with the Roman empire against the Jews. These are the angels of God, who in the forty-second year after the resurrection, together with Titus and Vespasian, destroyed those murderers and burned their city.

VERS. 55.-- You are come out with swords. It is foolish to seek with swords and clubs him who gives himself up of his own accord, and to track down by night through a betrayer, as though he were in hiding, one who taught daily in the temple. But you gather together in the darkness, because your power is in the darkness.

VERS. 57.-- To Caiphas. The name agrees with the deed. Caiphas, that is, a searcher out, or shrewd to fulfil, namely, the wickedness of his own guile. Or, one vomiting with the mouth, because he was shameless in uttering a lie and in demanding a murder.

171CVERS. 58.-- Afar off. Rightly afar off, he who was about to deny him close at hand, he signifies the Church, which would imitate the passion of Christ far differently, because the Church suffers for itself, but Christ for the Church.

VERS. 64.-- Thou hast said it. Mark: I am: which is the same: And there is a like answer against Pilate and against Caiphas, that they may be condemned out of their own sentence. The Son of man. Me, exalted in the form which you judge, and having the name which is above every name.

VERS. 65.-- He rent his garments. The fury which made him rise from his seat makes him also rend his garment. By a great mystery: since the garment of the Lord could not be rent even by the soldiers. For it signifies that their priesthood, the pontificate, was for their crimes to be utterly dissolved: but the solidity 171Dof the Church (which is called the garment of God) will remain unbroken to the end of the world. It is the custom of the Jews, when they hear anything of blasphemy, they rend their garments.

Which Paul and Barnabas did in Lycaonia, when they were called Jupiter and Apollo.

VERS. 67.-- Then they spit. As he was then spit upon with the spittle of unbelievers, so now he is dishonoured with the reproaches of false believers, and is beaten with buffets, that is, with the blasphemies of the same men.

VERS. 68.-- Prophesy. They speak an insult to him who willed to be held a prophet by the peoples. For they veiled his face, according to Mark, not that he might not see their crimes, but that they might hide from themselves the grace 172Aof the knowledge of him. As there was a veil upon the face of Moses, which remains to this day.

VERS. 69.-- One maidservant. Why does a maidservant betray him first, when the men were better able to recognize him, unless that this sex might be shown to have sinned unto the death of the Lord: and redeemed through the passion. And therefore a woman first received the mystery of the resurrection: and kept the commands, that she might abolish the old error of the transgression. As through a woman the ancient enemy seduced the first parent, so through a woman he drives the prince of the Church to deny the master whom he had confessed to be Christ the Son of God.

VERS. 72.-- I know not the man. How harmful are the conversations of the wicked, which compel Peter to deny Christ, or that he knew the man whom he confessed 172Bamong the disciples to be the Son of God.

VERS. 73.-- They that stood by. John and Luke speak of one, a kinsman of Malchus. But either it is a plural for a singular. Or there was one who had seen him and was speaking chiefly, whom the rest, following him, accused in like manner.

VERS. 74.-- And immediately the cock crew. After the third denial the cock's crowing follows. The sacraments of things are signified by the state of the times. At midnight he denies, at the cock's crowing he repents, after the resurrection, in the light, he professed three times that he loved him whom he had three times denied: because what he had erred in the darkness of forgetfulness, he corrected by the remembrance of the light already hoped for: and by the presence of that same true light he fully raised up whatever he had changed.

VERS. 75.-- He remembered. In another Gospel it is said, 172CThe Lord looked upon Peter (Luc. XXII), and by his look drew him to tears, as though bringing back to his mind how often he had denied, and what he had foretold to him, nor could he remain in darkness upon whom the light of the world had looked. And going forth. Shut off from the counsel of the ungodly, he washes away his fault, which he did not do within. For this cause he was permitted to waver, that in the prince of the Church a remedy of penance might be established, and that no one should dare to trust in his own strength.

CAPUT XXVII.

VERS. 1.-- Mane autem. Matthaeus et Marcus contexerunt narrationem de his quae cum Domino acta sunt usque ad mane, sed postea redeunt usque ad negationem Petri narrandam: qua terminata redeunt 172Diterum ad mane, ut inde caetera contexerent.

VERS. 4. Peccavi tradens, etc. Quasi in potestate sua esset mutare persecutorum sententiam: licet autem mutaverit voluntatem, tamen primae voluntatis exitum non mutavit. Quid ad nos. Quid ad nos pertinet de hoc? tu videris, tu scias quid fecisti. Vel quid videris pertinere ad nos. Quid ad nos pertinet pretium acceptum: tu istud probaveras, tu inde fac quod vis. Qui ad mortem peccant, tantam habent ignorantiam et caecitatem, ut nec turbentur in sceleribus suis, nec poenitentiae dolore crucientur.

VERS. 5.-- Et projectis. Sicut poenitentia ejus fuit infructuosa, sic et haec oblatio in templo est exsecrabilis. Et abiens. (HIER.) Nihil profuit Judae egisse poenitentiam, per quam scelus corrigere non 173Apotuit. Sed quando frater sic peccat in fratrem, ut emendare valeat quod peccavit, potest ei dimitti. Sin autem permanent opera, frustra voce assumitur poenitentia. Hoc est quod de eo dicitur: Et oratio ejus fiat in peccatum (Psal. CVIII): ut non solum emendare nequiverit proditionis nefas, sed proprii homicidii scelus addiderit, unde scelestior.

VERS. 6.-- Non licet eos. Vere colentes culicem et camelum glutientes: si enim ideo non mittunt pecuniam in corbonam, id est, in gazophylacium, id est, inter dona Dei, quia pretium sanguinis est, cur ergo ipse sanguis effunditur? Et quid praemii eis, si inde ager emitur in sepulturam pauperum?

VERS. 9.-- Per Jeremiam. (HIER.) Hoc non in Jeremia, sed in Zacharia legitur: tamen aliis verbis. 173BLegi nuper Jeremiae apocryphum, in quo haec verba scripta reperi. Sed tamen magis mihi videtur hoc sumptum de Zacharia more apostolorum, qui verborum ordine praetermisso, sensus tantum de Veteri Testamento proferunt.

A filiis Israel, etc. A quibus minus oportuerat, qui magnificare debuerant.

VERS. 11.-- Tu es rex Judaeorum. Secundum Lucam duo objecerant, scilicet quod prohibebat dari censum Caesari, et quod diceret se regem. De regno ergo Pilatus, quia nescit, interrogat. De tributis: quia audierat eum respondisse: Reddite quae sunt Caesaris Caesari (Zac. II): quasi apertum mendacium invidorum negligit. Unde Marcus: Et non erant convenientia testimonia eorum (Marc. XXIV). In 173Choc quod Pilatus nihil aliud criminis interrogat, nisi an sit rex Judaeorum: arguuntur impietatis Judaei, qui nec falso invenire potuerunt quod objicerent Salvatori.

VERS. 16.-- Qui dicebatur Barabbas. Barabbas in Evangelio secundum Hebraeos filius patris vel magister eorum interpretatur. Ipse est Antichristus, quem illi Christo praeferunt, quibus dicitur: Vos ex patre diabolo estis (Joan. VIII). Pilatus qui os malleatoris interpretatur, diabolum significat, qui est malleus universae terrae, per quem Dominus vasa sua permittit probari: et ad ultimum conterit, quando in extrema damnatione confringet.

VERS. 17.-- Quem vultis. Populo volens satisfacere dat optionem quamvis non dubitet Christum 173Deligendum sciens per invidiam traditum. Qui dicitur Christus. Idem quod Marcus ait: regem Judaeorum, quorum rex Christus vocabatur.

VERS. 19.-- Pro tribunali. Tribunal sedes judicum, ut solium regum, cathedra doctorum. Uxor ejus. Uxor viri gentilis hoc in visione intelligit, quod Judaei vigilantes intelligere noluerunt. Nihil tibi. Nunc demum diabolus intelligens per Christum se spolia amissurum, sicut primum per mulierem mortem intulit; ita modo per mulierem vult Christum de manibus Judaeorum liberare, ne per mortem ejus mortis amittat imperium.

VERS. 21.-- Barabbam, etc. Filium diaboli elegerunt pro Filio Dei, ut Salvatorem cum sua salute perderent, 174Aet proditorem liberarent, quae sua petitio hodie etiam haeret eis, amissa gente et loco et libertate: quia datorem libertatis perdere voluerunt.

VERS. 22.-- Quid igitur faciam de Jesu. Multas quaerebat occasiones, ut eum cum benevolentia populi liberaret. Primo, latronem justo conferens. Deinde: Quid ergo faciam de Jesu, qui dicitur Christus? Et cum dicerent: crucifigatur, non statim acquievit, sed juxta suggestionem uxoris ait: Quid enim mali fecit? Quo dicto Pilatus absolvit Christum.

VERS. 24.-- Lavit manus. Gentilem populum ab impietate Judaeorum alienum designans, qui clamaverunt: Crucifige, crucifige eum.

VERS. 25.-- Sanguis ejus. Perseverat usque hodie imprecatio, et sanguis Christi non aufertur 174Bab eis.

VERS. 26.-- Tunc dimisit illis Barabbam. Barabbas latro seditiosus et homicidiorum auctor, dimissus est populo Judaeorum. (ISID.) Diabolus qui jam olim ob superbiam a patria lucis expulsus, et in tenebrarum carcerem fuerat missus. Et ideo Judaei pacem habere non possunt, quia seditionum principem eligere maluerunt. Vel Barabbas typum gerebat Antichristi.

Jesum vero flagellatum. Ideo credendus est Pilatus Jesum flagellasse et militibus ad illudendum dedisse, ut satiati poenis ejus et opprobriis, Judaei ejus mortem ultra non sitirent. Jesum vero. Serviens Romanis legibus, quae crucifigendos prius jubent flagellari.

174CVERS. 28.-- Chlamydem coccineam. (HIER.) In chlamyde coccinea opera gentium cruenta sustentat. In corona spinea, maledictum solvit antiquum. In calamo, venenata occidit animalia. Vel calamum tenet in manu, ut sacrilegium scriberet Judaeorum. In suis vestibus non illuditur, sed in his quae propter peccata nostra portavit. Cum crucifigeretur suum recepit ornamentum statim: quia elementa turbantur, et Creatori testimonium dat creatura.

VERS. 32.-- Cyrenaeum. Ecce non Hebraeus, sed alienigena opprobrio Christi subditur, ut plenitudo sacramentorum transire a circumcisione ad praeputium ostendatur.

VERS. 33.-- Golgotha. Syrum est, et interpretatur Calvariae, non ob calvitium Adae quem mentiuntur 174Dibi sepultum, sed ob decollationem damnatorum. Adam juxta Hebron in libro Jesu legitur sepultus.

VERS. 34.-- Cum felle mistum. Alius evangelista dicit mirrhatum, id est, amarissimum. Et potuit utraque esse commistio fellis et myrrhae. Et cum gustasset noluit. Hoc Marcus tacet: noluit bibere. Hoc est quod Marcus ait: non accepit, scilicet ut biberet.

VERS. 35.-- Diviserunt. Hoc Joannes distinctius: vestimenta in quatuor partes ut Ecclesia in quatuor partibus mundi signetur. Tunica sortita significat omnium partium unitatem vinculo charitatis, quae sorte, id est, gratia Dei provenit, non humano arbitrio.

VERS. 37.-- Causam. Pro quam eum Judaei Pilato 175Atradiderunt. Rex per crucem non perdidit, sed confirmavit imperium, velint nolint Judaei.

VERS. 38.-- Unus a dextris. Unus a dextris per quem mortificatio significatur carnis pro coelesti gloria: unus a sinistris, per quem iterum significatur mortificatio carnis pro aliqua mundi causa.

VERS. 39.-- Blasphemabant. Praedictum erat in libro Sapientiae (Cap. II): Morte turpissima condemnemus eum. Erit enim respectus in sermonibus illius. Si enim vere Filius Dei est, suscipiet illum et liberabit illum de manu contrariorum. Quia ergo crucifigebatur, et non liberabatur, credebant illum non esse Dei Filium: propterea pendenti in ligno insultant, et caput agitant:

VERS. 40.-- Si Filius Dei es. Si insultantibus cedens 175Bdescenderet de cruce, virtutem patientiae non demonstraret.

VERS. 42.-- Et credemus ei. Fraudulenta promissio: majus facit surgens de sepulcro, et non creditis: si ergo de cruce descenderet, non crederetis. Sed hoc daemones immiserunt; statim enim senserunt virtutem crucis, et suas vires fractas et hoc agunt ut de cruce descendat. Sed sciens hoc Dominus, permanet ut diabolum destruat.

VERS. 44.-- Idipsum autem et latrones. Prius uterque blasphemavit, deinde visis signis unus credidit. Ita uterque populus Deum prius blasphemavit: sed alter signis territus poenituit, et nunc Judaeos arguit.

VERS. 45.-- A sexta autem hora. Nota, sole a centro mundi recessuro crucifigitur Dominus, quia 175Cpropter peccata moritur, et Adam post meridiem cum peccasset vocem Domini audivit. Et ordo est rationis, ut quo tempore praevaricanti Adae clausit, eo tempore poenitenti latroni januam reseret paradisi. Quod Marcus ait hora tertia crucifixisse Dominum, ideo est, ut Judaeos qui se excusant, magis crucifixisse ostendat linguis, quam milites quorum manibus factum est: qui hora tertia ut crucifigeretur inclamaverunt. Quando enim crucifixus est, hora erat quasi sexta, id est non adhuc plene sexta.

VERS. 46.-- Et circa horam nonam. Cum inclinata est dies ad vesperam et tepefactus est sol a fervore, passionem consummat, ostendens se mori propter peccata nostra quibus a divina luce et dilectione in hanc noctem cecidimus. Mane surrexit Dominus, 175Ddocens resuscitatos in anima se esse producturum in lucem aeternae felicitatis. Eli, Eli, lamma. Quorum suscipit naturam, eorumdem plorat miseriam. In quo ostendit quantum ipsi flere debent qui peccant. Ne mireris verborum humilitatem et querimonias derelicti, cum formam servi sciens scandalum crucis videas.

VERS. 47.-- Quidam autem. Non omnes, forsitan Romani, Hebraei sermonis ignorantes proprietatem. Vel aliqui Judaei Christum minorem Elia reputantes, ut ejus auxilium deprecetur.

VERS. 49.-- Sine videamus. Quia vocem Domini male intelligebant, frustra Eliae exspectabant adventum.

176AVERS. 51.-- Et ecce velum templi. Ut arca testamenti et omnia sacramenta legis quae tegebantur appareant, et ad gentes transeant. Josephus ait virtutes angelicas praesides quondam templi tunc pariter conclamasse: transeamus de his sedibus. Velum quod dicebatur exterius: quia nunc ex parte videmus; cum autem venerit quod perfectum est, tunc velum interius dirumpetur. Terra mota est. Omnia elementa suum Dominum crucifixum demonstrant. Terra movetur, Dominum suum pendentem ferre non sustinens. Petrae. Id est corda gentilium, vel vaticinia prophetarum, qui petrae dicuntur a petra Christo, ut quidquid in eis duro legis velamine clausum erat, scissum pateret gentibus.

VERS. 52.-- Et monumenta. Et tamen cum monumenta 176Baperta sunt, non ante surrexerunt, quam Dominus resurgeret, ut esset primogenitus ex multis fratribus. Corpora sanctorum. Hi credendi sunt cum Domino ascendente similiter ascendisse cum corpore.

VERS. 53.-- In sanctam civitatem. Jerusalem quae sancta dicitur propter templum, et Sancta sanctorum, quod nulla alia.

VERS. 54.-- Centurio autem. Hi in scandalo crucis confitentur Deum. Arius in Ecclesia praedicat creaturam, quem caeci Judaei nec tot miraculis credunt. In centurione fides Ecclesiae designatur, quae velo coelestium mysteriorum per mortem Domini reserato, Jesum Filium Dei tacente Synagoga confirmat.

VERS. 55.-- Mulieres multae. Matthaeus et Lucas dicunt Mariam Magdalenam a longe stetisse. Joannes 176Cvero juxta crucem, quia tanto intervallo aberat, ut juxta diceretur: quia praesto erat in conspectu ejus, et longe comparatione turbae propioris. Possumus etiam dicere quod illae quae erant cum matre Domini postquam eam commendavit discipulo, abire coeperint jam, ut a densitate turbae eruerent se et caetera quae facta sunt longius intuerentur. Ministrantes ei. Consuetudinis Hebraicae erat, ut mulieres de substantia sua praeceptoribus suis ministrarent: quod Dominus accepit, ut exemplum daret apostolis.

VERS. 56.-- Jacobi. Jacobus Alphaei minor dicitur ad differentiam Jacobi, qui inter primos apostolos vocatus est et electus a Domino.

VERS. 57.-- Homo dives. Quia ad praesidem nisi dives accedere non poterat, non quod divitias commendet 176Devangelista. Dictus est et decurio, quia fuit de ordine curiae et officium curae ministrabat, per hoc potuit habere facultatem, per hoc quod discipulus, hoc ministerio dignus fuit. Discipulus erat Jesu. Hic accessit ad Pilatum. Etsi occultus, vitans prius inimicitias Judaeorum, in hoc extremo officio nihil curat de illis.

VERS. 59.-- In syndone munda. Mystice. Sindone munda Jesum involvit, qui pura mente eum suscepit. Hinc etiam mos Ecclesiae habet, ut sacrificium altaris non serico, non panno tincto, sed puro lineo celebretur.

VERS. 60.-- In monumento suo novo. Quia si in monumento, ubi alius positus esset, poneretur, alius 177Afortasse resurrexisse fingeretur. In petra, ne si ex multis lapidibus aedificatum esset, suffossis tumuli fundamentis ablatus furto diceretur.

VERS. 61.-- Erant autem ibi. Aliis notis Jesu ad sua redeuntibus, solae mulieres quae arctius amabant, obsequium funeris inspiciebant, exspectantes quod promiserat Jesus: et ideo primae resurrectionem vident.

VERS. 63.-- Post tres. A parte totum, sexti diei quo crucifixus est cum sua nocte pro uno die. Sabbatum integre. Tertius dies a parte prima, id est, nocte totius cum suo diurno, et sic est triduum.

VERS. 66.-- Munierunt sepulcrum. Diligentia eorum nostrae profuit fidei. Nam quanto amplius servabatur, tanto magis virtus resurrectionis ostenditur.

CHAPTER XXVII.

VERS. 1.-- And in the morning. Matthew and Mark have woven together the narrative of the things that were done with the Lord up to the morning, but afterwards they go back to tell the denial of Peter: which being ended, they return 172Dagain to the morning, that from there they might weave together the rest.

VERS. 4. I have sinned in betraying, etc. As though it were in his own power to change the sentence of the persecutors: but although he changed his will, yet he did not change the outcome of his first will. What is that to us. What does it concern us about this? look thou to it, do thou know what thou hast done. Or, what dost thou see as pertaining to us. What does the price received concern us: thou hadst approved of that, do thou with it what thou wilt. They who sin unto death have so great ignorance and blindness that they are neither troubled in their crimes, nor tormented with the pain of repentance.

VERS. 5.-- And casting down. As his repentance was fruitless, so also this offering in the temple is execrable. And going away. (HIER.) It profited Judas nothing to have done penance, by which he could not 173Acorrect his crime. But when a brother so sins against a brother that he is able to amend what he has sinned, it can be forgiven him. But if the works remain, penance is taken up with the voice in vain. This is what is said of him: And let his prayer be turned to sin (Psal. CVIII): so that not only was he unable to amend the wickedness of the betrayal, but he added the crime of his own murder, whence he was the more wicked.

VERS. 6.-- It is not lawful for them. Truly straining out the gnat and swallowing the camel: for if for this reason they do not put the money into the corbona, that is, into the treasury, that is, among the gifts of God, because it is the price of blood, why then is the blood itself poured out? And what reward is theirs, if from it a field is bought for the burial of the poor?

VERS. 9.-- By Jeremias. (HIER.) This is read not in Jeremias, but in Zacharias: yet in other words. 173BI lately read an apocryphon of Jeremias, in which I found these words written. But nevertheless it seems to me rather that this was taken from Zacharias, after the manner of the apostles, who, the order of the words being passed over, bring forward only the meanings out of the Old Testament.

Of the children of Israel, etc. From whom it was less fitting, who ought to have magnified him.

VERS. 11.-- Thou art the king of the Jews. According to Luke they had brought two charges, namely that he forbade tribute to be given to Caesar, and that he said he was a king. Concerning the kingdom, therefore, Pilate asks, because he does not know it. Concerning the tributes: because he had heard that he had answered: Render to Caesar the things that are Caesar's (Zac. II): he disregards, as it were, the open lie of the envious. Whence Mark: And their testimonies were not agreeing (Marc. XXIV) [Vulg. Mc 14:56 et convenientia testimonia non erant, without eorum]. In 173Cthis, that Pilate asks of no other crime except whether he is the king of the Jews: the Jews are convicted of impiety, who could not even falsely find what they might lay against the Saviour.

VERS. 16.-- Who was called Barabbas. Barabbas, in the Gospel according to the Hebrews, is interpreted son of the father or their master. He himself is Antichrist, whom they prefer to Christ, to whom it is said: You are of your father the devil (Joan. VIII). Pilate, who is interpreted the mouth of the hammerer, signifies the devil, who is the hammer of the whole earth, by whom the Lord permits his own vessels to be tried: and at the last he grinds him down, when in the final damnation he shall break him in pieces.

VERS. 17.-- Whom will you. Wishing to satisfy the people, he gives the choice, although he does not doubt that Christ 173Dis to be chosen, knowing that he had been handed over through envy. Who is called Christ. The same as what Mark says: the king of the Jews, whose king Christ was called.

VERS. 19.-- For the judgment seat. The tribunal is the seat of judges, as the throne is of kings, the chair of teachers. His wife. The wife of a Gentile man understands in a vision that which the Jews, waking, would not understand. Have thou nothing to do with that just man. Now at last the devil, understanding that through Christ he was to lose his spoils, just as at the first he brought in death through a woman; so now through a woman he wishes to free Christ out of the hands of the Jews, lest through his death he lose the empire of death.

VERS. 21.-- Barabbas, etc. They chose the son of the devil instead of the Son of God, that they might destroy the Saviour together with their own salvation, 174Aand set free a betrayer, which petition of theirs cleaves to them even to this day, their nation and their place and their liberty being lost: because they willed to destroy the giver of liberty.

VERS. 22.-- What therefore shall I do with Jesus. He sought many occasions to set him free with the goodwill of the people. First, comparing a robber with a just man. Then: What then shall I do with Jesus, who is called Christ? [Vulg. Mt 27:22 Quid igitur faciam de Jesu, qui dicitur Christus?; Migne's own lemma above prints igitur] And when they said: let him be crucified, he did not at once yield, but according to his wife's suggestion he says: Why, what evil hath he done? By which saying Pilate absolved Christ.

VERS. 24.-- He washed his hands. Signifying the Gentile people, a stranger to the impiety of the Jews, who cried out: Crucify him, crucify him.

VERS. 25.-- His blood. The imprecation persists to this day, and the blood of Christ is not taken away 174Bfrom them.

VERS. 26.-- Then he released to them Barabbas. Barabbas, a seditious robber and the author of murders, was released to the people of the Jews. (ISID.) The devil, who long since had for his pride been driven out of the fatherland of light, and had been sent into the prison of darkness. And therefore the Jews cannot have peace, because they chose rather the prince of seditions. Or, Barabbas bore the type of Antichrist.

But Jesus having been scourged [Vulg. Mt 27:26 Jesum autem flagellatum]. Therefore it is to be believed that Pilate scourged Jesus and gave him to the soldiers to be mocked, that the Jews, sated with his punishments and his reproaches, might thirst no further for his death. But Jesus. Serving the Roman laws, which command that those who are to be crucified be first scourged.

174CVERS. 28.-- A scarlet cloak. (HIER.) In the scarlet cloak he bears up the bloody works of the Gentiles. In the crown of thorns he looses the ancient curse. In the reed he kills the venomous beasts. Or he holds the reed in his hand, that he might write the sacrilege of the Jews. He is not mocked in his own garments, but in those which he bore for our sins. When he was crucified he received his own adornment at once: because the elements are troubled, and the creature gives testimony to the Creator.

VERS. 32.-- A man of Cyrene. Behold, not a Hebrew, but a foreigner is put under the reproach of Christ, that the fulness of the sacraments might be shown to pass over from circumcision to uncircumcision.

VERS. 33.-- Golgotha. It is Syriac, and is interpreted of Calvary, not because of the baldness of Adam, whom they falsely say was buried 174Dthere, but because of the beheading of the condemned. Adam is read in the book of Josue to have been buried near Hebron.

VERS. 34.-- Mingled with gall. Another evangelist says myrrhed, that is, most bitter. And there could have been a mingling of both, of gall and of myrrh. And when he had tasted, he would not. This Mark passes over in silence: he would not drink. This is what Mark says: he took it not, namely, that he should drink it.

VERS. 35.-- They divided. This John more distinctly: the garments into four parts, that the Church may be signified in the four parts of the world. The coat allotted signifies the unity of all the parts by the bond of charity, which comes about by lot, that is, by the grace of God, not by human choice.

VERS. 37.-- The cause. For which the Jews handed him over 175Ato Pilate. The King through the cross did not lose, but confirmed, his empire, whether the Jews will or will not.

VERS. 38.-- One on the right hand. One on the right hand, by whom is signified the mortification of the flesh for the sake of heavenly glory: one on the left hand, by whom again is signified the mortification of the flesh for the sake of some cause of the world.

VERS. 39.-- They blasphemed. It had been foretold in the book of Wisdom (Cap. II): Let us condemn him to a most shameful death. For there shall be respect in his words. For if he be truly the Son of God, he will take him up and will deliver him from the hand of his adversaries [Vulg. Sap 2:20 erit enim ei respectus ex sermonibus illius, and Sap 2:18 Si enim est verus filius Dei, suscipiet illum, et liberabit eum de manibus contrariorum]. Because therefore he was being crucified and was not delivered, they believed that he was not the Son of God: therefore they insult him as he hangs upon the tree, and shake their heads:

VERS. 40.-- If thou be the Son of God. If, yielding to those who insulted him, 175Bhe had come down from the cross, he would not have shown the power of patience.

VERS. 42.-- And we will believe him [Vulg. Mt 27:42 et credimus ei, the present tense]. A fraudulent promise: he does a greater thing in rising from the tomb, and you do not believe: if therefore he should come down from the cross, you would not believe. But the demons put this in; for they felt at once the power of the cross, and their own strength broken, and they do this that he may come down from the cross. But the Lord, knowing this, remains, that he may destroy the devil.

VERS. 44.-- And the selfsame thing the robbers also. At first both blasphemed, then, the signs being seen, one believed. So both peoples first blasphemed God: but the one, terrified by the signs, repented, and now convicts the Jews.

VERS. 45.-- And from the sixth hour. Note that the Lord is crucified when the sun is about to withdraw from the centre of the world, because 175Che dies for sins, and Adam, when he had sinned, heard the voice of the Lord after midday. And it is the order of reason that at the time at which he shut the gate of paradise against the transgressing Adam, at that time he should unlock it to the penitent robber. That Mark says the Lord was crucified at the third hour is for this reason, that he may show that the Jews, who excuse themselves, crucified him rather with their tongues than the soldiers by whose hands it was done: who at the third hour cried out that he should be crucified. For when he was crucified, it was about the sixth hour, that is, not yet fully the sixth.

VERS. 46.-- And about the ninth hour. When the day had declined toward evening and the sun had grown cool from its heat, he completes the passion, showing that he dies for our sins, by which we have fallen from the divine light and love into this night. In the morning the Lord rose again, 175Dteaching that he would lead forth those raised up in soul into the light of eternal happiness. Eli, Eli, lamma. Whose nature he takes upon him, of the same men he bewails the misery. Wherein he shows how much they themselves ought to weep who sin. Marvel not at the lowliness of the words and at the complaints of one forsaken, since, knowing the form of a servant, thou seest the scandal of the cross.

VERS. 47.-- And certain. Not all, perhaps Romans, not knowing the property of the Hebrew speech. Or some Jews, reckoning Christ less than Elias, so that he should beg his help.

VERS. 49.-- Let be, let us see. Because they understood the voice of the Lord amiss, they waited in vain for the coming of Elias.

176AVERS. 51.-- And behold the veil of the temple. That the ark of the testament and all the sacraments of the law, which were being covered, might appear and might pass over to the Gentiles. Josephus says that the angelic powers, once the guardians of the temple, then cried out together: let us pass over from these seats. The veil which was called the outer one: because now we see in part; but when that which is perfect shall come, then the inner veil shall be rent asunder. The earth was moved. All the elements show forth their Lord crucified. The earth is moved, not enduring to bear its Lord as he hangs. The rocks. That is, the hearts of the Gentiles, or the prophecies of the prophets, who are called rocks from the rock Christ, that whatever in them was shut up under the hard veil of the law might, being rent, lie open to the Gentiles.

VERS. 52.-- And the monuments. And yet, although the monuments 176Bwere opened, they did not rise before the Lord rose again, that he might be the firstborn among many brethren. The bodies of the saints. These are to be believed to have ascended in like manner with the body when the Lord ascended.

VERS. 53.-- Into the holy city. Jerusalem, which is called holy because of the temple, and the Holy of holies, which no other city has.

VERS. 54.-- And the centurion. These men confess God in the scandal of the cross. Arius in the Church preaches a creature, whom the blind Jews do not believe even for so many miracles. In the centurion is signified the faith of the Church, which, the veil of the heavenly mysteries being unlocked by the death of the Lord, affirms Jesus to be the Son of God while the Synagogue is silent.

VERS. 55.-- Many women. Matthew and Luke say that Mary Magdalen stood afar off. But John 176Csays she was beside the cross, because she was distant by such an interval that she could be said to be beside it: because she was at hand in his sight, and afar off by comparison with the nearer crowd. We can also say that those women who were with the mother of the Lord, after he had commended her to the disciple, had already begun to go away, that they might draw themselves out of the density of the crowd and behold from farther off the other things that were done. Ministering unto him. It was of the Hebrew custom that women should minister of their own substance to their teachers: which the Lord accepted, that he might give an example to the apostles.

VERS. 56.-- Of James. James the son of Alphaeus is called the less, to distinguish him from that James who was called among the first apostles and chosen by the Lord.

VERS. 57.-- A rich man. Because none but a rich man could approach the governor, not that the evangelist commends 176Driches. He was called also a decurion, because he was of the order of the curia and discharged the office of the curia, by this he could have the means, by this, that he was a disciple, he was worthy of this ministry. He was a disciple of Jesus. He went to Pilate. Although in secret, avoiding beforehand the enmities of the Jews, in this last office he cares nothing for them.

VERS. 59.-- In a clean linen cloth. Mystically. He wraps Jesus in a clean linen cloth who receives him with a pure mind. Hence also the custom of the Church holds that the sacrifice of the altar be celebrated not upon silk, not upon dyed cloth, but upon pure linen.

VERS. 60.-- In his own new monument. Because, if he were laid in a monument where another had been laid, another 177Aperhaps would be feigned to have risen again. In the rock, lest, if it had been built of many stones, he should be said to have been taken away by theft, the foundations of the tomb being undermined.

VERS. 61.-- And there were there. While the other acquaintances of Jesus were returning to their own, the women alone, who loved more closely, were watching the service of the burial, awaiting what Jesus had promised: and therefore they are the first to see the resurrection.

VERS. 63.-- After three. The whole from a part, of the sixth day, on which he was crucified, with its own night, for one day. The sabbath entire. The third day from its first part, that is, from the night, for the whole with its own daytime, and so there is a space of three days.

VERS. 66.-- They made the sepulchre sure. Their diligence profited our faith. For the more it was guarded, the more is the power of the resurrection shown.

CAPUT XXVIII.

  • 177B

VERS. 1.-- Vespere, etc. (HIER.) Quod diversa tempora istarum mulierum, etc., usque ad adire non potuerunt, mane adeunt. Maria Magdalene, et altera Maria. Duae unius nominis et devotionis mulieres, duas significant plebes pari devotione Christi passionem et resurrectionem amplectentes.

VERS. 2.-- Terrae motus. Sicut in passione et in resurrectione fit motus terrae: significat autem corda terrenorum per fidem passionis et resurrectionis ad poenitentiam movenda. Revolvit lapidem. Clauso potuit exire sepulcro, qui clauso natus est virginis utero. Revolutio lapidis significat reserationem sacramentorum Christi, quae velo litterae tegebantur: 177Cquo ablato, resurrectio, mortis abolitio, vita aeterna coepit toto orbe praedicari.

VERS. 3.-- Sicut fulgur. In candore vestis significabat blandum justis, in terrore fulguris, significat terribilem futurum reprobis.

VERS. 5.-- Nolite timere. Quae vestros concives videtis. Illi timeant qui ad eorum societatem non pertinent.

VERS. 7.-- Et cito euntes. Nec concessum est nobis hoc gaudium occulto cordis tenere, sed similiter amantibus pandere.

VERS. 8.-- Cum timore et gaudio. Duplex est affectus in eis, timoris et gaudii. Alter de magnitudine miraculi, alter ex desiderio resurgentis, et uterque gradum femineum concitabat.

177DVERS. 9.-- Et ecce Jesus, etc. Sic omnibus iter virtutum inchoantibus adjuvando occurrit Christus. Et tenuerunt. Superius dictum est clauso exisse monumento, ut immortale corpus intelligeres: hic tenentur pedes, ut veram carnem comprobes. Et quia tantam visionem tremebant, primum pellitur timor: Nolite timere, ut mente placida possint audire quae dicuntur. Istae tenent pedes quae adorant resuscitatum: sed illa quae nesciebat adhuc resurrexisse 178AFilium Dei, audit merito: Noli me tangere, nondum enim ascendi ad Patrem meum (Joan XX).

VERS. 10.-- Nuntiate. Ut mulier mortifera serpentis verba narravit, ita modo verba vivificatoris, ut a qua tunc mors, modo ab ea vita. Fratribus meis. Ante etiam servos vocavit, modo fratres, ut post resurrectionem eamdem humanitatem resumpsisse, et illos idem sperare doceat.

VERS. 12.-- Pecuniam copiosam. (HIER.) Qui rebus Ecclesiae abutuntur in aliis rebus quibus suam expleant voluntatem, similes sunt scribarum et Pharisaeorum et sacerdotum redimentium mendacium et sanguinem Salvatoris.

VERS. 13.-- Furati sunt eum nobis dormientibus. (AUG.) Si dormierunt, quomodo viderunt? Si non 178Bviderunt, quomodo testes esse potuerunt? Avaritia quae captivavit discipulum comitem Domini, decepit et milites custodes sepulcri: et sic Judaeos seduxit, unde subdit: Et divulgatum est.

VERS. 16.-- Abierunt. A Jerusalem in Galilaeam. In montem: quia corpus de communi terra susceptum super omnia terrena erat elevatum. Et monet nos, ut si eum videre volumus, ad superna de infimis elevemur. Praecessit Christus primitiae dormientium, sequuntur alii in suo ordine, et videntes eum adorant, id est, sine fine laudant.

VERS. 17.-- Quidam autem dubitaverunt, ut Lucas ait: existimantes se spiritum videre, quem moriendo tradidit.

VERS. 18.-- Et accedens. Et ideo pius magister et 178Cillos ad fidem confortat, et dubios ad fidem vocat intimans ad quantam pervenerit gloriam. Data est. Hoc non de coaeterna Patris divinitate, sed de assumpta dicit humanitate, in qua minor angelis, in qua etiam gloria et honore coronatus est et omnibus superpositus.

VERS. 19.-- Euntes. Qui jam in lucem venit, quod hactenus quibusdam de causis dubium hominibus fuerat. Docete. Congruus ordo. Primo enim docendus est auditor. Deinde fidei sacramentis imbuendus, tunc ad servanda mandata instruendus. Quia nisi prius anima fidem recipiat, non est dandus baptismus, nec valet mundari, si post non insistat bonis operibus.

VERS. 20.-- Et ecce ego vobiscum sum. Quae autem 178Dmerces piae conversationis, quodve pignus futurae beatitudinis etiam in praesenti sit fidelibus, subdit: Ecce ego vobiscum sum, etc. Quod apostolis per successiones universali Ecclesiae promittit. Nota quod usque in finem saeculi non sunt desituri qui divina mansione sunt digni.

(AUG.) Prima sabbati diluculo, etc., usque ad dicente eis angelo: Sic veniet quemadmodum vidistis eum euntem in coelum (Act. I).

CHAPTER XXVIII.

  • 177B

VERS. 1.-- In the evening, etc. (HIER.) That the different times of these women, etc., as far as they could not approach, they approach in the morning. Mary Magdalen, and the other Mary. Two women of one name and of one devotion signify two peoples embracing with equal devotion the passion and the resurrection of Christ.

VERS. 2.-- An earthquake. As in the passion, so also in the resurrection there is a movement of the earth: and it signifies that the hearts of earthly men are to be moved to penance through faith in the passion and the resurrection. He rolled back the stone. He could go out from the sepulchre while it was closed, who was born from the virgin's womb while it was closed. The rolling back of the stone signifies the unlocking of the sacraments of Christ, which were covered by the veil of the letter: 177Cwhich being taken away, the resurrection, the abolition of death, life eternal began to be preached throughout the whole world.

VERS. 3.-- As lightning. In the whiteness of the garment he signified that he would be gentle to the just, in the terror of the lightning he signifies that he will be terrible to the reprobate.

VERS. 5.-- Fear not. You who see your own fellow citizens. Let those fear who do not belong to their fellowship.

VERS. 7.-- And going quickly. Nor is it granted to us to keep this joy in the secret of the heart, but to lay it open to those who love in like manner.

VERS. 8.-- With fear and joy. The affection in them is twofold, of fear and of joy. The one from the greatness of the miracle, the other from desire of him who was rising again, and each urged on the womanly step.

177DVERS. 9.-- And behold Jesus, etc. So Christ meets with his help all who begin the way of the virtues. And they held. It was said above that he went out while the monument was closed, that thou mightest understand the body to be immortal: here his feet are held, that thou mightest prove the flesh to be true. And because they trembled at so great a vision, fear is first driven out: Fear not, that with a peaceful mind they might be able to hear the things that are said. These women hold his feet, who adore him raised up again: but she who did not yet know that the Son of God 178Ahad risen again deservedly hears: Touch me not, for I am not yet ascended to my Father (Joan XX).

VERS. 10.-- Tell. As a woman told the death-bearing words of the serpent, so now the words of the life-giver, that from her from whom death then came, now life may come. To my brethren. Before he called them servants also, now brethren, that he may teach that after the resurrection he has taken up again the same humanity, and that they may hope for the same.

VERS. 12.-- A great sum of money. (HIER.) Those who misuse the goods of the Church upon other things by which they may fulfil their own will are like to the scribes and the Pharisees and the priests who bought a lie and the blood of the Saviour.

VERS. 13.-- They stole him away while we were asleep. (AUG.) If they slept, how did they see? If they did not 178Bsee, how could they be witnesses? The avarice which took captive the disciple, the companion of the Lord, deceived also the soldiers who guarded the sepulchre: and so it seduced the Jews, whence he adds: And it was spread abroad.

VERS. 16.-- They went away. From Jerusalem into Galilee. Into a mountain: because the body taken up from the common earth had been lifted above all earthly things. And he admonishes us that, if we wish to see him, we should be lifted from the lowest things to the things above. Christ went before, the firstfruits of them that sleep, the others follow in their own order, and seeing him they adore him, that is, they praise him without end.

VERS. 17.-- But some doubted, as Luke says: thinking that they saw a spirit, which he gave up in dying.

VERS. 18.-- And coming near. And therefore the loving master both 178Cstrengthens them in the faith, and calls the doubting to faith, intimating to how great a glory he had come. It is given. He says this not of the coeternal Godhead of the Father, but of the humanity he assumed, in which he is less than the angels, in which also he was crowned with glory and honour and set above all things.

VERS. 19.-- Going. He who has now come into the light, which until now had for certain causes been doubtful to men. Teach. A fitting order. For first the hearer is to be taught. Then he is to be steeped in the sacraments of the faith, then to be instructed in the keeping of the commandments. Because unless the soul first receive the faith, baptism is not to be given, nor is it of avail to be cleansed, if afterwards he does not press on in good works.

VERS. 20.-- And behold I am with you. But what is the reward 178Dof a holy manner of life, and what the pledge of the blessedness to come which the faithful have even in the present, he adds: Behold I am with you, etc. Which he promises to the apostles, and through their successions to the universal Church. Note that until the end of the world there will not cease to be men who are worthy of the divine dwelling.

(AUG.) On the first day of the week at daybreak, etc., as far as the angel saying to them: So shall he come as you have seen him going into heaven (Act. I).

The Latin is Migne's printing of Evangelium secundum Matthaeum (PL 114, coll. 63A–178D), by Anselm of Laon and his school, from the Corpus Corporum transcription. Parenthetical references in small type stand on Migne's page — editorial identifications inherited from the edition he reprinted or supplied by his shop, not necessarily the author's own; the author's own citations run in the prose itself. Every gilt column mark is an address: migne.app/pl/114/63a resolves to the first.
